# Research Intake

Leads for research code whose capabilities belong in the preamble.

Each entry names existing research code that provides mathematical capability not yet owned by the preamble.
The preamble absorbs each capability through its owned category, object, morphism, and functor structure, with maintained computation behind a private adapter.
Do not import the research code directly and do not reimplement its algorithm locally.
Use the intake to select the correct owner, trace the required construction, and link the resulting TODO item and complaint where applicable.

Add a lead with source location, mathematical capability, intended preamble owner, and current status.
Remove a lead when the preamble owns the capability and specimens prove it, with evidence in the delivery commit.

## Reference implementations

Where to look first for existing algorithms before writing new code.
Check these before claiming a computation has no maintained implementation.

## Intake report: https://github.com/taklab-org/CAP_finding_monodromy — 2026-09-15

Hard codes are data, not method.
Generalize data and you get 4 reusable rigorous capabilities:

**1. Rigorous singular locus — hard code: polynomial**

* Hard: $S = \{4455\ldots\} = 0$, line $2(x-x0)+(y-y0)=0$

* General: For any flat connection $\nabla = d - \sum A_i dz_i$ with rational $A_i$, enclose $\operatorname{Sing}(\nabla) \cap L$ for any generic line $L$ via `kv::allsol` interval Newton.
  Input: rational equations.
  Output: disjoint interval boxes containing each intersection point.
  Method is general.

**2. Rigorous fundamental matrix at base point — hard code: $N=4$, $N_{\mathrm{trunc}}=41$, $\mathrm{lam0}=2^{-10}$, formulas for $a_{lm}$**

* Hard: `phi1..4` for this GKZ with $a_{lm}=(2l+4m)!/(l+m)!l!(m!)^3$

* General: For any regular-singular Pfaffian system $d\Phi = \Omega \Phi$ with series $\sum a_{m} z^{m+\text{regular exp}} (\log z)^k$, enclose $\Phi(b0)$ by truncated sum + majorant bound.

  * Needs: coefficient growth estimate $|a_m| \leq C \beta^m$, truncation $N$, radius $|b0|<1/\beta$.
    Theorems 3.4-3.7 are just $\beta_N,\gamma_N,\delta_i$ for this $a_{lm}$.
    Same proof gives $\beta,\delta(N)$ for any hypergeometric/GKZ ratio.

  * Operation: `fundamental_matrix(nabla, b0, N) -> interval matrix` with guaranteed $|\mathrm{error}| < \delta$.

**3. Rigorous connection matrices — hard code: `matA,matB` as rational functions of $x,y$**

* Hard: K3 family $h=1+20x+9y$, $A,B$ built from `a..em,B0..C3`

* General: Any integrable $\Omega = A(x)dx + B(x)dy$, $A,B \in \operatorname{Mat}_n( C(x))$ with simple poles on $S$.
  Input: rational matrix functions.
  `variable_coefficients.hpp` is just the evaluator $\Omega(x,y)$.
  Replace evaluator and code runs.

**4. Rigorous analytic continuation / monodromy — hard code: 6 circles with centers $cx,cy$ and $r$, order 15, DD**

* Hard: $x=cx+r e^{\pm i\pi t}, y=cy-2r e^{\pm i\pi t}$, 3 segments per $\Sigma_i$

* General: For any path $\gamma:[0,1]\to B$ piecewise smooth, validated ODE $d\Phi/dt = (dx/dt A + dy/dt B) \Phi$.

  * Integrator `kv::odelong_maffine` is fully general: Taylor order $p$, interval/`dd`/`interval<dd>`, affine arithmetic remainder.
    Hard codes are $p=15$ and `dd`.

  * Operation: `transport(nabla, gamma, Phi0) -> interval matrix Phi1` with $\Phi1 \ni \text{actual analytic continuation}$.
    Iterate over $\gamma_{i,1}*\ldots*\gamma_{i,k}$ to get $M_i = \Phi0^{-1} \Phi1$.

  * Parallel over 4 columns via OpenMP is general rank $n$.

**General object:**

```text
Input:  (B = C^k \ S, rank n, Omega in Mat_n(C(B)) integrable, regular singular, base b0, presentation of Phi(b0))
Functor: LocalSystem(nabla): pi1(B,b0) -> GL_n(C)
Method:  Phi(b0) via 2 + transport via 4
Output: interval M_i with width < 0.5 if M_i in GL_n(Z), certifies integer monodromy
```

What does not generalize automatically: the closed-form $\delta_i$ proof for 2, and the choice of $\gamma$ that generates $\pi_1$.
The rigorous transport 4 does.

## Fundamental semantic language required — verbatim — 2026-09-15

You are right.
Reformulation in preamble semantic language — only categories, functors, and their objects/morphisms — not data:

**Base language (must exist to name anything)**

* $\mathsf{Sets}$, $NN$ — $\dim = 2$ is an element $2 : NN$, not an object

* $\mathsf{CommRings}$, $\mathsf{CommAlgebras}(C)$ — $C$, $C[z1..zk]$, $C(X)$

* $\mathsf{Modules}(R)$ — free module $E = O_U^n$ is object with axiom $\mathrm{Free}$ of rank $n: NN$; $n$ is not a category

* $\mathsf{Schemes}/C$, $\mathsf{Smooth}(C)$ — $X = A^k_C$ is object of $\mathsf{Schemes}/C$; $S \subset X$ is object of $\mathsf{Divisors}(X)$, not a primitive; $U = X \setminus S$ via open immersion $j: U \hookrightarrow X$

**Sheaf language**

* $\mathsf{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{Zar}})$, $\mathsf{QCoh}(X)$, $O_X\text{-}\mathsf{Mod}$ — quasi-coherent sheaves; $\Omega^1_X$ is object of $\mathsf{QCoh}$

* $\mathsf{AnSpaces}$, analytification functor $(-)^{\mathrm{an}}: \mathsf{Sch}/C \to \mathsf{An}$ — $X^{\mathrm{an}}$, $U^{\mathrm{an}}$

* $\mathsf{Sh}(X^{\mathrm{an}})$, $O^{\mathrm{an}}\text{-}\mathsf{Mod}$, $\mathsf{LocSys}(U^{\mathrm{an}})$ — locally constant $C$-sheaves; object of $\mathsf{LocSys}$ is the local system, not its rank

**Connection language**

* $\mathsf{Conn}(X) = (E, \nabla)$ where $E : \mathsf{QCoh}(X)$, $\nabla: E \to E \otimes \Omega^1_X$ morphism of sheaves

* $\mathsf{FlatConn}(X) \subset \mathsf{Conn}(X)$ — subcategory defined by $\nabla^2 = 0 : E \to E \otimes \Omega^2_X$ (flatness equation)

* $\mathsf{TrivFlatConn}(U)$ — Pfaffian system is not fundamental; it is trivialization $E \cong O_U^n$ of object of $\mathsf{FlatConn}(U)$.
  Matrix $\Omega = \sum A_i dz_i$ is representation of $\nabla$ in that trivialization, not a new notion.
  General integral form $\Omega \in \operatorname{Mat}_n( \Omega^1(\log S))$ is object of $\Omega^1_X(\log S)$.

**D-module language**

* $D_X\text{-}\mathsf{Mod}$, its full subcategory $D^{\mathrm{rh}}_X(S)$ — regular holonomic $D$-modules with singularities along $S$

* Functor $\mathrm{DR}: D_X\text{-}\mathsf{Mod} \to \mathsf{Sh}(X^{\mathrm{an}})$ and $\mathrm{Sol} = \operatorname{Hom}_{D}( -, O^{\mathrm{an}}) : D_X\text{-}\mathsf{Mod}^{\mathrm{op}} \to \mathsf{Sh}(X^{\mathrm{an}})$ — solution functor

* Riemann-Hilbert functor $\mathrm{RH}: D^{\mathrm{rh}}_X(S) \to \mathsf{Perv}_S(X^{\mathrm{an}})$ — equivalence.
  Its restriction to $\mathsf{FlatConn}$ is $\mathrm{RH}: \mathsf{FlatConn}^{\mathrm{rs}}(X,S) \simeq \mathsf{LocSys}(U^{\mathrm{an}})$.
  This is the functor that turns $\nabla$ into monodromy.
  Source = $D^{\mathrm{rh}}$, target = $\mathsf{LocSys}$/$\mathsf{Perv}$.

**Homotopy / monodromy language**

* $\pi_1: \mathsf{An}_* \to \mathsf{Groups}$ and fundamental groupoid $\Pi_1(U^{\mathrm{an}}) : \mathsf{Cat}$ — source of local system as functor $\Pi_1 \to \mathsf{Vect}_C$

* Equivalence $\mathsf{LocSys}(U^{\mathrm{an}}) \simeq \mathsf{Rep}(\pi_1(U^{\mathrm{an}},b0)) \simeq \mathsf{Fun}(\Pi_1, \mathsf{Vect}_C)$ — monodromy representation $\rho : \pi_1 \to \operatorname{GL}_n(C)$ is image of $\mathrm{Sol}(M)$ under this.
  Base point $b0 : \operatorname{Spec} C \to U$ is morphism, not an object.

* Path $\gamma: [0,1] \to U^{\mathrm{an}}$ is morphism in $\Pi_1$; transport $T_\gamma$ is image of $\gamma$ under $\rho$.

**What is not fundamental**

* $k=2$, $n=4$, $N_{\mathrm{trunc}}=41$, $b0=2^{-10}$, $S$ polynomial, $A_i$ rational entries — all are elements/morphisms in the above categories

* $\delta(N), \beta_N$ — analysis of series in $O^{\mathrm{an}}_{b0}$

Capabilities state as: choose $\nabla : \mathsf{FlatConn}^{\mathrm{rs}}(X,S)$, apply $\mathrm{Sol} = \mathrm{RH}( D(\nabla)) : \mathsf{LocSys}(U^{\mathrm{an}})$, evaluate at $b0$, transport along $\gamma$, read $\rho([\gamma])$.
Code replaces last two steps by validated enclosure in $IR$-lift.

## Desired capability: families as scheme morphisms — note 2026-09-15

Formulate a scheme morphism $f: X \to S$ as a family via an interface `f.as_family(...)`.

`f.as_family` must require enough data to be well-defined — it is not a bare cast.
Required data includes at least the base scheme $S$, the structure morphism $f$, and the properties that make $f$ a family: flatness, and where needed properness / smoothness / finite presentation over $S$.
Additional framing (projectivity, chosen relatively ample line bundle, marking of a section) is supplied when the construction needs it, not by default.
Without this data the object is not a family; calling `as_family` without it must fail.

Families must support period functions for continuous families of cycles and forms:

* Continuous family of cycles $\gamma_z$ — horizontal family of homology classes $\gamma : S^{\mathrm{an}} \to R^k f^{\mathrm{an}}_* C$ (section of the local system $R^k f_* C$ / Betti local system).
  $\gamma_z \in H_k(X_z, C)$ varies locally constantly.

* Continuous family of relative forms $\Omega_z$ — section $\Omega : S \to f_* \Omega^k_{X/S}$ (or relative de Rham sheaf $H^k_{\mathrm{dR}}(X/S)$).

* Period function $w(z) = \int_{\gamma_z} \Omega_z : S^{\mathrm{an}} \to C$ — pairing of the above.
  For algebraic families $w$ is holomorphic on $U = S \setminus \text{discriminant}$ and satisfies a Picard-Fuchs equation.

Required operation: `w.Picard_Fuchs()` / $\mathrm{PF}(w)$ — the regular-singular linear differential equation (Picard-Fuchs equation) satisfied by $w(z)$, as an object of $D_S\text{-}\mathsf{Mod}$ (or as $\nabla_{\mathrm{GM}}$ flat connection / Pfaffian system on the Hodge bundle $H^k_{\mathrm{dR}}(X/S)$). Construction is via the Gauss-Manin connection $\nabla_{\mathrm{GM}}: H^k_{\mathrm{dR}}(X/S) \to H^k_{\mathrm{dR}}(X/S) \otimes \Omega^1_S$.
The family must expose $H^k_{\mathrm{dR}}(X/S)$, $\nabla_{\mathrm{GM}}$, its regular singularities, and its monodromy local system, so that $\mathrm{PF}(w)$ is the annihilator of $w$ in $D_S$.
This connects families to the monodromy intake above: $\rho$ of $\nabla_{\mathrm{GM}}$ is the monodromy of periods.

Intended owners: `categories/schemes/families.py` (`f.as_family` constructor, $\operatorname{Hom}(\mathsf{Sch}/C)$), `categories/schemes/periods.py` (period pairing, Gauss-Manin), `categories/functors/gauss_manifold.py` / $D_S\text{-}\mathsf{Mod}$.
Not a free function `PicardFuchs(...)`.

## Desired capability: generatingfunctionology (Wilf Ch.1-2) — note 2026-09-15

Provide basic generatingfunctionology semantic interfaces, at least Wilf *generatingfunctionology* Chapters 1-2.

Required: formulate recurrence relations symbolically and solve them exactly in known cases, and produce ordinary generating functions (OGFs), exponential generating functions (EGFs), and associated L-functions or zeta functions.

Recurrence relation is not a Python `def` with a loop — it is an object $\mathrm{Rec}(R, a(n), \text{relation})$ in a category of sequences: $a : NN \to R$ with defining relation $\sum_{i=0}^d c_i(n) a(n+i) = b(n)$ (linear with polynomial coefficients; constant coefficients as special case) plus initial data $a(0)..a(d-1)$.
Interface lives on sequences/recurrences, not as a free solver function.
Exact solving means when the recurrence lies in a known solvable class (e.g. C-finite / constant coefficients, P-recursive / D-finite with closed hypergeometric form, rational generating function), return closed form for $a(n)$ and certified equality, not a numeric guess.
No invented `solve_recurrence` that handles only numerics.

Production of OGF / EGF is a functor:

* $\mathrm{OGF}: \mathsf{Seq}(R) \to R[[x]]$, $a(n) \mapsto A(x)= \sum_{n\geq 0} a(n) x^n$ — formal power series as object of $R[[x]]$

* $\mathrm{EGF}: \mathsf{Seq}(R) \to R[[x]]$, $a(n) \mapsto \hat{A}(x)= \sum_{n\geq 0} a(n) x^n / n!$

* Both are objects of `FormalPowerSeries(R)` with correct parent (radius, valuation, coefficient ring).
  Converting between them is not string manipulation.

Associated L-functions / zeta functions: for $a(n)$ (or arithmetic function) produce Dirichlet series $L(s,a)= \sum_{n\geq 1} a(n) n^{-s}$ as formal Dirichlet series object, and when the sequence comes from counting (e.g. $a(n)=\#\{\text{points}\}$) its zeta/Hasse-Weil incarnation.
These are objects of a category of Dirichlet series / Euler products, with Euler factor, functional equation, and convergence data retained, not a bare complex function.
Construction is $L = \mathrm{Dirichlet}(\mathrm{OGF}/\mathrm{EGF})(s)$ with preservation of base ring and abscissa.

These must be integrated closely with formal power series rings and other I-adic completions.
OGF/EGF are not a separate `GeneratingFunctions` hierarchy — they are the $(x)$-adic completion $R[[x]] = \varprojlim R[x]/(x^n)$ (and more generally $\hat{I} = \varprojlim R/I^n$ for any ideal $I$). The same I-adic completion construction that gives $C[[t]]$, $Z_p$, $m$-adic completions and the period rings must own $R[[x]]$, its universal property, its ideal of definition, and its functorial `completion` maps.
A recurrence's OGF is then $A(x)$ in that completed ring, its truncated $R[x]/(x^N)$ images are finite stages of the inverse system, and operations between OGFs (Cauchy product, Hadamard product, composition) use the completed ring's operations, not a second generating-function class.
D-finite closure (Wilf Ch.2), P-recursive $\Leftrightarrow$ D-finite equivalence, and transforms $\mathrm{Rec} \to R[[x]] \to \mathrm{Dirichlet}$ all compose through this one completion/coefficient owner.
Do not build a parallel `PowerSeriesForGF` that duplicates $R[[x]]$.

Intended owners: `categories/generating_functions/recurrences.py` (recurrence objects, `RecurrenceCategory`), `categories/rings/formal_power_series.py` (`FormalPowerSeries`, `OGF`, `EGF` functors) which delegates to the shared I-adic completion owner `categories/rings/completions.py` / `categories/completions.py`, `categories/rings/dirichlet_series.py` (`DirichletSeries`, `LFunction`, `Zeta`), and their $D\text{-}\mathsf{Mod}$ / `C-finite` solvers behind private adapters (e.g. `ore_algebra`, `sage.combinat`). No free `ogf(...)` at top level — `a.ogf()`, `a.egf()`, `a.dirichlet_series()`, `rec.solution().closed_form()` on the owning objects.

Reference: H. Wilf, *generatingfunctionology*, Ch.1 (ordinary/enumerative) and Ch.2 (exponential) — symbolic recurrence $\to$ generating function dictionary, rational / algebraic / D-finite closure.

## Desired capability: monodromy groups/representations + $\pi_1$/$H_1$ + CW + graded — note 2026-09-15

Want a semantic interface for monodromy groups and monodromy representations, computable in some cases.

This requires a well-established $\pi_1(X, x)$ interface as a functor $\pi_1: \mathsf{An}_* \to \mathsf{Groups}$ (and $\Pi_1: \mathsf{An} \to \mathsf{Cat}$ for the groupoid), not an ad-hoc method returning a string.
Monodromy group is the image $\operatorname{im}(\rho) \leq \operatorname{GL}_n(C)$ of the monodromy representation $\rho: \pi_1(U^{\mathrm{an}}, b0) \to \operatorname{GL}_n(C)$ from the intake above; monodromy representation is the functor $\rho$ itself (object of $\mathsf{Rep}(\pi_1)$ / $\mathsf{LocSys}$). Interface must be `nabla.monodromy_group()`, `nabla.monodromy_representation(b0)`, `local_system.monodromy()`, on the owning connection/local-system objects — computable via rigorous transport when $\nabla$ is regular-singular and $\pi_1$ has known generators, otherwise retained as formal object with known type.

Requires $\pi_1(X, x)$ and $H_1^{\mathrm{sing}}(X, ZZ)$ computable for a collection of concrete varieties (at least: $A^1 \setminus \{0..n\}$, $P^1 \setminus \{\text{pts}\}$, complements of discriminants, punctured planes, tori $G_m^k$, elliptic curves, abelians, configuration spaces).
Computation routes through topology, not scheme code: analytification $X \mapsto X^{\mathrm{an}}$ then singular/simplicial.

Needs a good CW complex interface: CW complex is object $K = (K^0 \subset K^1 \subset \ldots)$ with skeleta, cells $e^n_\alpha$, and attaching maps $\varphi_\alpha: S^{n-1} \to K^{n-1}$ as morphisms of spaces.
Gluing is the colimit $K^n = K^{n-1} \cup_{\coprod \varphi_\alpha} \coprod D^n_\alpha$ in $\mathsf{Top}$.
Interface must construct $K$ cell-by-cell, expose its cellular chain complex $C_*(K)$, and compute $\pi_1(K,x)$ via van Kampen from the 2-skeleton and $H_*(K)$ from $C_*$.
No bare list of cells without attaching data.

Needs a static database of all known homotopy groups of spheres $\pi_{n+k}(S^n)$ (stable and unstable ranges as tabled — e.g. Toda, Mimura, etc.) and, when possible, explicit named generators and relations (e.g. $\eta: S^3 \to S^2$ Hopf, $\eta^2$, $\nu$, $\sigma$, Whitehead products) as elements with certified relation $2\eta = 0$ after suspension, etc. This DB supplies the attaching data: a map $S^{n-1} \to K^{n-1}$ that factors through a sphere can be named as $a\cdot\eta$ etc. Without it CW gluing cannot be specified — a cell attached by $2\cdot\mathrm{id}: S^1 \to S^1$ is the datum for $RP^2$.

Needs a good interface for $ZZ^n$-graded modules: category $\mathsf{GrMod}_{ZZ^n}(R)$ with $ZZ^n$-graded objects $M = \bigoplus_{d\in ZZ^n} M_d$, morphisms preserving degree, shifts $M(d)$.
From this: complexes $\mathsf{Ch}(\mathsf{GrMod})$ (single grading + homological degree), double complexes $\mathsf{Ch}(\mathsf{Ch}(\mathsf{Gr}))$ with bidegree $(p,q)$, total complex, filtrations, and spectral sequences as objects $E_r^{p,q}$ with differentials $d_r: E_r^{p,q} \to E_r^{p+r, q-r+1}$ and convergence data $E_\infty \Rightarrow H^*(\operatorname{Tot})$.
Support at least Serre spectral sequence (fibration), Atiyah-Hirzebruch, Leray-Serre, and in some easy cases compute them: when $E_2$ is known from $H_*(\text{base}) \otimes H_*(\text{fiber})$ and differentials are forced by degree / known $k$-invariants / known $\pi_*(S^n)$, return $E_3, E_\infty$ and the associated graded of $H^*$.
This is computable when the $ZZ^n$-graded module is finite and the differential is sparse.

In some easy cases the whole pipeline must compute: $X$ concrete variety $\to$ $K = \mathrm{CW}(X^{\mathrm{an}})$ via known decomposition $\to$ $C_*(K)$ $\to$ $\pi_1(K)$, $H_1(K)$; plus $E_2$ from CW cohomology + sphere DB $\to$ Serre $\to$ $\pi_*(X)$ or $H^*(X)$ in range.
All via owned categories, not via ad-hoc Python lists of cells.

Intended owners: `categories/topology/cw_complexes.py` (`CWComplex`, `Cell`, `AttachingMap` in $\mathsf{Top}$), `categories/topology/homotopy_groups.py` (`HomotopyGroupsDB` with $\pi_{n+k}(S^n)$, generators `Hopf`, `Whitehead`), `categories/topology/fundamental_group.py` ($\pi_1$, $H_1 = \text{abelianization}$), `categories/graded/graded_modules.py` ($ZZ^n\text{-}\mathsf{GrMod}$, `GrComplex`, `DoubleComplex`), `categories/graded/spectral_sequences.py` (`SpectralSequence`, `SerreSS`) with unstable support.
Monodromy ties back to `categories/functors/local_system.py` and `categories/schemes/monodromy.py`.

## Intake: https://github.com/lairez/periods — functionality — 2026-09-15

*Periods* is a Magma package for Lairez "Computing periods of rational integrals" (arXiv:1404.5069). Implements Griffiths-Dwork / Dimca Rham-Koszul reduction for hypersurface complements.

Input $f \in Q(t,x1..xn)$ rational, `t = A.1` parameter.
Output differential operator $L \in Q[t]\langle\partial_t\rangle$ (or $t\partial_t$) annihilating periods $p(t)=\int_\gamma f(t,x)dx$, where $\gamma_t \in H_n(A^n \setminus V(q_t))$ horizontal.
$L\cdot p=0$ for all $\gamma$.

* `Periods(f : r, variant)` — periods of $f$ dx

* `Diagonal(f : r)` — diagonal $\sum [x^n y^n z^n]F \cdot t^n$ via $1/(1-t xyz)$ trick

* `LaurentSequence(f)` — constant term of Laurent powers via residues — all reduce to periods

Core steps:

* Homogenize $f \to fsf$ square-free part, degree $d$

* `InitRK(f : r, variant)` builds $R=k[x0..xn,u,v]$ with Jacobian ideal $\operatorname{Jac}(f)$ and trivial syzygy ideal `tsyz`, Groebner `jac`, `syz` — reduction basis $W_r$

* `TotDiff = ExtDiff - ExtProd` implements pole-order reduction $p/f^k \to [p'] / f^{k-1}$ modulo $\operatorname{Jac}$ + syzygies (Dimca)

* `GaussManin(f,r,L)` — at generic `t=ipoint` compute cohomology basis $H = H^n_{\mathrm{dR}}(\text{complement})$ and connection matrix $M: H' = M\cdot H$ via `HomReduceMatrix`. Loop over many `ipoint` $(100,101,\ldots)$ over many primes $p$ and rationally reconstruct $M(t)=\mathrm{mat}/\mathrm{den} \in Q(t)^{m\times m}$ via `RHAddRat/RHAddMod` + `RatInterp`/`PolInterp` (Chinese remainder)

* `Storjohann(A,b)` — high-order lifting + Keller-Gehrig + Padé to solve $A x = b$ over $k[t]$ without blow-up

* `CyclicEquation(mat/den, vec)` — cyclic vector to scalar operator $L$

Example: $f1=1/(1-(1-xy)z - t xy z(1-x)(1-y)(1-z))$ $\to$ Apery operator order 3-4. $f2..f6$ same operator via different $F$.

Magma-only, CeCILL, preliminary.
Provides no standalone D-module library — extraction is `RhamKoszul` reduction + `Storjohann` + `RatInterp` pattern.

## How periods generalize — verbatim — 2026-09-15

Hard code is choice of $f$.
Method is for any $f$:

**Fundamental object** (not hard-coded): For $f = p/q \in C(t,x1..xn) = C(S\times A^n)$ with $t$ parameter (first variable hard-coded as `A.1`), family of hypersurfaces $V_t = V(q_t) \subset A^n$, complement $U_t = A^n \setminus V_t$.
Period

$$
p_\gamma(t) = \int_{\gamma_t} f(t,x) dx1\wedge\ldots\wedge dxn ,\quad \gamma_t \in H_n(U_t, C) \text{ horizontal}
$$

is pairing $H_n \otimes H^n_{\mathrm{dR}}(U_t)$.
All $p_\gamma$ satisfy same $L \in C(t)\langle\partial_t\rangle$ — Picard-Fuchs.
`Periods(f)` returns that $L$.
`Diagonal` and `LaurentSequence` are not new — they are `Periods` after

$$
\begin{aligned}
\operatorname{Diag} F(t) &= [x^n y^n z^n]F = \operatorname{res} F(x,y,z)/ (1-txyz) \to 1/(1 - y(1+x)\ldots ) \\
\operatorname{ct}(f^n) &= \operatorname{res} f^n dx/x
\end{aligned}
$$

transforms coded in `misc/apery.m`. One computation replaces 6 examples.

**What generalises directly** — change data, same functor:

* $n = \operatorname{Rank}(A)-1$ variables, $\deg f$ arbitrary — `InitRK` builds $R=k[x0..xn,u,v]$ with $\mathrm{jac} = (\partial_i f\cdot u - \ldots)$ generally.
  Groebner `jac` + syzygy `tsyz` via `LeadingMonomialIdeal` is generic.

* $q_t$ arbitrary denominator — `prepare_fraction` takes square-free part `fsf` and degree `deg`. No Apery coefficients remain.

* $r$ pole-order bound — $r=1$ is Griffiths, $r>1$ adds syzygy filtration $W_r$.
  Algorithm is generic in $r$; completeness threshold is $r \geq n+1$ (Dimca).
  `variant {"mindeg","profile"}` is heuristic choice of $C[t]$-basis minimizing $\deg(\mathrm{den})$ — generic rational-function minimisation.

* Evaluation-interpolation — `GaussManin(f,r)` evaluates $f|_{t=\mathrm{ipoint}}$ at `ipoint=100,101,...` (or random $\bmod p$), computes $M(\mathrm{ipoint}) \in F_p^{m\times m}$ via `HomReduceMatrix`, then `RatInterp`/`PolInterp` reconstructs $M(t) \in Q(t)$ from many $F_p$ evaluations.
  Method is generic $Q(t) = \lim \mathrm{RatInterp}(F_p)$ via `RHNew` Chinese remainder.
  Replace $Q$ by any number field $K$ via `CoefficientRing(K)`.

* Linear solve — `Storjohann(A,b,prec)` solves $A x = b$ over $k[t]$ for any $A,b$; not Apery-specific.
  `PolynomialLinearAlgebra` linearises any $k[x]$-module to matrix over $k$.

**What changes category when you vary $S$:**

* $\mathrm{Periods}: \mathsf{Rat}(S\times A^n) \to D_S\text{-}\mathsf{Mod}$, $f \mapsto L = \operatorname{Ann}_{D_S}(p_\gamma)$.
  Hard: $S=A^1$.
  General: $S=A^k$ $\to$ Pfaffian system $\partial_i \Phi = M_i(t) \Phi$ (Gauss-Manin connection $\nabla_{\mathrm{GM}}: H^n_{\mathrm{dR}}(U/S) \to H^n_{\mathrm{dR}} \otimes \Omega^1_S$). `LinearHomotopies.m: GaussManinLin(f0,f1)` already computes one direction of this: family $f_t=(1-t)f0+t f1$ gives $\mathrm{mat}(t)/\mathrm{den}(t)$.
  Full multivariate is same `InitRK` with $k$ parameters and $k$ matrices.

* Target $L$ in $D_S$ is cyclic vector reduction of $\nabla_{\mathrm{GM}}$: `CyclicEquation(mat/den, vec) -> L`. Generic for any $\nabla_{\mathrm{GM}}$.

**What does not generalise without new math:**

* Single $t$ only — need $D_S$ in $k$ variables for true multivariate PF ideal, not just one $\mathrm{mat}/\mathrm{den}$

* Hypersurface complement only — general $X \to S$ proper smooth family needs Griffiths-Dwork for $P^n$ complete intersections, not just $A^n \setminus V(q)$

* No cycle choice — $L$ annihilates *all* horizontal $\gamma$; to get *specific* $p_\gamma$ need initial conditions `InitialConditionsOfCoordinate(gm,i,order)` which is only evaluation of $M$ at $t=0$ via `HomReduce`

In preamble terms: $f: U \to S$ as family (`f.as_family`), $H = R^n f_* \Omega^\bullet_{U/S}$ object of $\mathsf{QCoh}(S)$, $\nabla_{\mathrm{GM}}: H \to H \otimes \Omega^1_S$ flat connection, $L = \operatorname{Ann}(w)$ in $D_S\text{-}\mathsf{Mod}$ where $w = \int_\gamma \Omega$ section of $H$.
`periods` package is private adapter computing $\nabla_{\mathrm{GM}}$ via `Dwork` reduction + `Storjohann` + `RatInterp`, and $L$ via cyclic vector.

## Fundamental semantic language for periods generalization — verbatim — 2026-09-15

For $\mathrm{Periods}: \mathsf{Rat}(S\times A^n) \to D_S\text{-}\mathsf{Mod}$ to be statable, these must exist as categories/functors — not data:

**Base**

* $\mathsf{Sets}$, $NN$ — $n, r, \deg$ are $: NN$

* $\mathsf{CommRings}$, $\mathsf{CommAlgebras}(C)$, $\mathsf{Fields}$ — $C$, $Q(t)$, $C(t,x) = \operatorname{Frac} C[t,x]$

* $\mathsf{FctField}(C)$ — rational function $f = p/q : S\times A^n \dashrightarrow A^1$ is morphism in $\mathsf{Rat}(S\times A^n)$ (object of function field), $q$ defines divisor

**Geometry / families**

* $\mathsf{Sch}/C$, $\mathsf{Smooth}(C)$, $\mathsf{Aff}_n = A^n_C : \mathsf{Sch}/C$

* $\operatorname{Div}(X)$ — hypersurface $V(q_t) \subset A^n$ is $\operatorname{div}(q) : \operatorname{Div}$

* Open immersion $j: U = (S\times A^n) \setminus V(q) \hookrightarrow S\times A^n$, complement of divisor — object of $\mathsf{Open}(S\times A^n)$

* Morphism $f: U \to S$ via projection $\operatorname{Hom}(\mathsf{Sch}/C)$; family structure `f.as_family` requires flatness object in $\mathsf{Fam}(S)$ where $\mathsf{Fam}(S) \subset \operatorname{Hom}(\mathsf{Sch}/C)_{/S}$ defined by $\text{flat} + (\text{proper/smooth where needed})$

**Sheaf / de Rham**

* $\mathsf{QCoh}(X)$, $\mathsf{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{Zar}})$, $\Omega^1_{X/S} : \mathsf{QCoh}$ — Kähler differentials

* De Rham complex $\Omega^\bullet_{U/S} : \mathsf{Ch}(\mathsf{QCoh}(U))$ and its cohomology sheaf $H^n_{\mathrm{dR}}(U/S) = R^n f_* \Omega^\bullet_{U/S} : \mathsf{QCoh}(S)$ — object carrying periods

* Rham-Koszul/Jacobian presentation: $\operatorname{Jac}(f) = (\partial_{x_i} f) : \mathsf{QCoh}$, syzygy module $\operatorname{Syz}(\operatorname{Jac})$ — subobjects used to present $H^n_{\mathrm{dR}}$; $W_r, U_r$ filtration by pole order is filtration of this $\mathsf{QCoh}(S)$-module

* $\Omega^\bullet(\log S)$ — not new; $H$ with its Hodge filtration is object of $\mathsf{Filt}(\mathsf{QCoh})$

**Connection / D-module**

* $\mathsf{Conn}(S) = (E,\nabla)$, $E:H$, $\nabla: H \to H \otimes \Omega^1_S$ — Gauss-Manin connection $\nabla_{\mathrm{GM}}$ is object of $\mathsf{Conn}(S)$

* $\mathsf{FlatConn}(S) \subset \mathsf{Conn}(S)$ by $\nabla^2=0$

* $D_S\text{-}\mathsf{Mod}$ — $D_S = \operatorname{DiffOps}(O_S)$ sheaf of rings; flat connection $\leftrightarrow$ left $D_S$-module via $\nabla \leftrightarrow D_S$-action

* Cyclic presentation: for $w \in H$, $\operatorname{Ann}_{D_S}(w) = \{P : P\cdot w=0\} \subset D_S$ — left ideal; Picard-Fuchs operator $L = \text{generator of } \operatorname{Ann}(w)$ is element $L : D_S$, $D_S/(L)$ is quotient object.
  `CyclicEquation` is functor $\mathsf{FlatConn} \to D_S\text{-}\mathsf{Mod}$ via cyclic vector.

**Betti / pairing (to define $w$)**

* Analytification $(-)^{\mathrm{an}}: \mathsf{Sch}/C \to \mathsf{An}$ and $H_n(U_t, C) = H_n^{\mathrm{sing}}(U_t^{\mathrm{an}}, C)$ — fiber of local system $R^n f^{\mathrm{an}}_* C : \mathsf{LocSys}(S^{\mathrm{an}})$

* Pairing $\int: H_n \otimes H^n_{\mathrm{dR}} \to O^{\mathrm{an}}_S$ — morphism $w = \int_{\gamma} \Omega : S^{\mathrm{an}} \to C$ section of $H^\vee$; $\gamma$ section of $R_n f_* C$ is horizontal (local system section)

**Functor being generalised**

* $\mathrm{Periods}: \mathsf{Rat}(S\times A^n) \to \mathsf{FlatConn}(S) \to D_S\text{-}\mathsf{Mod}$, $f \mapsto (H,\nabla_{\mathrm{GM}}) \mapsto L$

* Source varies only as element $f : \mathsf{Rat}$; target category $D_S\text{-}\mathsf{Mod}$ is fixed.
  $S=A^1$ hard-coded $\to$ general $S=A^k$ gives $\nabla_{\mathrm{GM}} \in \operatorname{Mat}_m(\Omega^1_S)$ Pfaffian system, not one $\mathrm{mat}/\mathrm{den}$.
  Reduction data `EchelonForm`, `Storjohann(A,b)` are linear-algebra adapters for $\mathsf{Modules}(Q(t))$, not new categories.

**Not fundamental**

* $f1..f6$, `t= A.1`, $n=3$, $r=2$, `ipoint=100`, $p$ prime, `den`, `mat` coefficients — elements/morphisms in above

* `Diagonal, LaurentSequence` — composites $\mathsf{Rat} \to \mathsf{Rat}$ via $F \mapsto 1/(1-t xyz)F$ then `Periods`; no new functor

## Intake: Monodromy of Family of Cubic Surfaces — 2026-09-15 — verbatim

No general method beyond intake.
Paper-specific notebooks for family $w^3 = f$, $f=y^2z - x^3 + xz^2$ (cubic surface 3-fold branched over cubic curve):

* `Intersection Pattern...sagews` + `Translation to Hesse Form.sagews` — enumerates 27 lines on $V(w^3-f)$ as pairs of linear forms $[a0,a1,a2,a3]$ with $\zeta = e^{2\pi i/3}$, $\alpha: 3t^4-6t^2-1=0$ (x of flexes $\neq[0:0:1]$). Test $L_i \cap L_j \neq \emptyset \Leftrightarrow \det M_{4\times 4}(\mathrm{rows}_i,\mathrm{rows}_j)=0$ $\to$ $27\times 27$ 0/1 matrix (diag -1), finds one sextuple of pairwise skew lines $(1,5,7,12,14,18)$ as basis of $\operatorname{Pic} = H^2$ (blow-up of $P^2$ at 6 points).
  Hard-coded flex data.

* `MonodromyOfRootsControllingCubicFlexes.nb` — `NSolve`/`Animate` of roots of $3x^4 -4(-1+e^{2\pi it})x^3 -6x^2 +12(-1+e^{2\pi it})x -4(-1+e^{2\pi it})^2 -1$ (flex abscissae along loop), `rootPlot` animation.
  Naive numerics, no validated ODE.

* `UnderstandingW(E6).nb` — $6\times 6$ matrices $X, s1..s5$ for $W(E6)=O(E6)$ as reflection group on $\operatorname{Pic}$ orthogonal to $K$, $\operatorname{charpoly} (t-1)^5(t+1)$, 36 positive roots.
  Ad-hoc.

All hard-coded to $f=y^2z-x^3+xz^2$ and its Hesse form.
No `GaussManin`, `Periods`, `kv`, or reusable `CW/pi1` framework.
Intersection via `det`, flexes via `NSolve`, Weyl via explicit matrices — instances of already-intaken families `f.as_family`, $H^2_{\mathrm{dR}}(X/S)$, $\operatorname{Weyl}(O(L))$, $\pi_1$/discriminant complement, not a new abstraction to absorb.
Do not add as reference implementation.

## Intake: Fermat Jacobians — functionality — 2026-09-15 — verbatim

Yes — new vs CAP/periods.
Narrow to Fermat Jacobians $J_m = \operatorname{Jac}(y^2 = x^m-1)$ (CM by $Q(\zeta_m)$), not general $\nabla$.

**What it does** — Magma, $m$ param (mostly odd $m$, $g=(m-1)/2$; even handled):

* `FermatJacobians1_computeMTandKconn.m` (620l) — Mumford-Tate:

  * `MatrixOfNorm(m,d)`, `MatrixOfReflexNorm(d)` with CM type $\{i<d/2 : (i,d)=1\}$, `PartialMTMatrix = Norm*Reflex` — builds `MTMatrix = BuildMTMatrix(m)` by joining divisors $d\mid m$.
    `Ker(Transpose(MT))` gives multiplicative equations $\prod x_j^{v_j}=1$ for $\mathrm{MT}(J_m) \subset G_m^{\varphi(m)}$ (Deligne).
    `ListOrderCharacters` fixes order.
    $P_\gamma(\alpha)= \prod\Gamma(i/m)^2/\Gamma(2i/m) / (2\pi i)^{\#\alpha/2}$ via `Gamma(C)`, minimal polynomial `MinimalPolynomial(...,2m)` $\to$ element `true_gamma in Kconn`.

  * `compute_Kconn()` $\to$ connected monodromy field $K^{\mathrm{conn}} = Q( P_\gamma )$ (field generated by Gamma ratios).
    Uses `SplittingField(CyclotomicPolynomial(m))`.

* `FermatJacobians2_computeST.m` (272l) — Sato-Tate:

  * Tensor rep $\mathrm{STcoeff}(\tau,\alpha)= \mu(\text{target})\operatorname{Gal}(P_{\text{target}})/\mu(\text{start})P_{\text{start}}$ with $\mu(\alpha)=\prod(m-2i)/m$.
    `small_a_matrix`, `relation_from_character`: $\prod T[i, u^{-1}i] - \mathrm{STcoeff} =0$.
    `compute_component(tau)` builds ideal $I(\tau) \subset \mathrm{Kconn}[t_{ij}]$ with permutation structure $j\neq u\cdot i$, $TT^8=1$, and all `relations_from_character_eigenspace` from $\mathrm{MT}$ equations.
    Groebner $\to$ ideal of component $\mathrm{ST}^0\cdot\tau$.

* `FermatJacobians3_computeEndomorphismFields.m` + `J60.m, X20.m` — endomorphism fields via splitting field of $\operatorname{Frob}$ charpolys, counting factorisation of $p$ in $K1/K12$ etc., `HeuristicEndomorphismLatticeDescription`.

* `FastCharPolyJ60.m` / `Example649...` — `FrobCharpolyC60Jacobi(p)` : $f_p(T)=\prod(T-\alpha_a)$ where $\alpha_a = -\chi^a(-1) J(\chi^a,\varphi)$ Jacobi sum.
  Implemented by counting $x\in F_p$: `logs` discrete log via `PrimitiveElement`, $\mathrm{counts}[r][s] = \#\{x: \log_g(x)=r \bmod 60, \log_g(1-x)=s \bmod 2\}$, then $Ja= \sum \mathrm{counts}\cdot z^{a r+30s}$.
  Coerce $K=Q(\zeta60) \to Z$.
  Also `BaseFieldDataFromFrobCharpoly`, `EndomorphismFieldFromFrobCharpoly` via splitting field degrees.

* `J60.out, Example428...` — data for $m=60,15,10$, `Kconn` polynomials, MT equations.

**New vs intake:** None of this is in CAP (rigorous ODE) or Lairez (Griffiths-Dwork).
Intake has generic `monodromy(reps)/pi1` but not $\mathrm{MT}/\mathrm{ST}/K^{\mathrm{conn}}$ via Gamma/reflex norms, nor `Jacobi-sum Frobenius` for CM Jacobians.
Belongs to arithmetic Hodge / Sato-Tate, not just $\nabla_{\mathrm{GM}}$ transport.

**Generalises?** Hard-coded to $y^2=x^m-1$ CM family.
Matrices $\operatorname{Norm}(m,d)$ and $\Gamma$-product use $Q(\zeta_m)$ class field theory (Shioda, Deligne).
Same functor generalises to any CM abelian variety with explicit CM type (replace `TriplicateExponents` and reflex type), but not to general $J(C)$ without CM. `FrobCharpoly` Jacobi method generalises to any Fermat-type $x^m+y^n=1$.

Add as intake if you want `MumfordTate/SatoTate/EndomorphismField` as owned $\mathsf{Hodge} \to \mathsf{Groups}$ functors; otherwise no reusable $D\text{-}\mathsf{mod}$ piece beyond what `families/periods` already covers.

## Fundamental objects for Fermat Jacobians generalization — verbatim — 2026-09-15

For $J_m = \operatorname{Jac}(y^2=x^m-1)$ MT/ST to be statable, these must exist as categories/functors — not $m=60$:

**Base**

* $\mathsf{Sets}$, $NN$ — $m,g = \varphi(m)/2, d\mid m$ are $:NN$

* $\mathsf{Fields}$, $\mathsf{NumberFields}$, $\mathsf{CMFields} \subset \mathsf{Fields}$ — $Q(\zeta_m)=\operatorname{SplittingField}(\Phi_m) : \mathsf{Fields}$, its subfields $Q(\zeta_d)$

* $\mathsf{AbVar}/Q : \mathsf{Cat}$ — abelian variety $J_m : \mathsf{AbVar}$, Jacobian functor $\operatorname{Jac}: \mathsf{Curves} \to \mathsf{AbVar}$; $\mathrm{CM}$ is property ($\operatorname{End}^0(J_m) \supset Q(\zeta_m)$), not a category.
  $g = \dim J_m$ is rank of $H^1$, not an object

* $\mathsf{HodgeStr}(Q)$ — pure $Q$-Hodge structure of weight 1; functor $H^1_B: \mathsf{AbVar} \to \mathsf{HodgeStr}$, $H^1_{\mathrm{dR}}(J_m) : \mathsf{Vect}_{Q(\zeta_m)}$

**Torus / character language (where `MTMatrix` lives)**

* $\mathsf{Tori}_Q : \mathsf{Cat}$ — torus $T_K = \operatorname{Res}_{K/Q} G_m : \mathsf{Tori}$, its character group $X^*(T_K) = \bigoplus_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow C} Z\cdot\chi_\sigma$

* Homomorphism of tori $\operatorname{Norm}_{m,d}: T_{Q(\zeta_m)} \to T_{Q(\zeta_d)}$ — morphism in $\mathsf{Tori}$.
  Matrix $\mathrm{MatrixOfNorm}(m,d) : \operatorname{Mat}_{\varphi(d)\times\varphi(m)}(Z)$ is its $X_*$ representation.
  Not fundamental; the functor $\operatorname{Res}$ is.

* CM type $\Phi = \{i<d/2 : (i,d)=1\} \subset \operatorname{Hom}(Q(\zeta_d),C) : \mathsf{Sets}(\mathsf{Sets})$ — datum defining reflex.
  Reflex norm $N_{\Phi^*}: T_{Q(\zeta_d)} \to T_{Q(\zeta_d)}$ — morphism `MatrixOfReflexNorm(d)`

* Product $\mathrm{PartialMT} = \operatorname{Norm} \circ N_{\Phi^*} : T_{Q(\zeta_m)} \to T_{Q(\zeta_d)}$ — $\mathrm{MT}(J_m)$ is subtorus $\bigcap_d \ker( \text{character} )$ where $\ker$ is kernel in $\mathsf{Tori}$.
  Equation $\prod x_j^{v_j}=1$ is element $v \in \ker( \mathrm{Transpose}(\mathrm{BuildMTMatrix}(m)) : Z^{\sum\varphi(d)} \to Z^{\varphi(m)})$; `B = Basis(Ker)` is $X^*(T/Q)$ basis.
  The matrix is presentation of $X^*(\mathrm{MT})$.

**Hodge / Mumford-Tate language**

* $\mathrm{MumfordTate}: \mathsf{AbVar} \to \mathsf{SubTori}_{\mathrm{GSp}(H^1_B)}$ functor — $\mathrm{MT}(J_m) = \operatorname{Stab}_{\mathrm{GSp}}(\text{Hodge tensors}) =$ smallest $Q$-torus containing Hodge cocharacter $\mu: G_m \to \operatorname{GL}(H^1_B\otimes C)$.
  For CM, $\mathrm{MT} \subset T_{Q(\zeta_m)}$ via `Norm/Reflex` description above.
  General $A$ CM by $E$ uses $\Phi_A$ and reflex $\Phi^*$.

* Field $K^{\mathrm{conn}} \subset Q^{\mathrm{ab}}$ — connected monodromy field.
  Object $K^{\mathrm{conn}} : \mathsf{Fields}$ is field generated by normalized Gamma ratios $P_\gamma(\alpha)= \prod\Gamma(i/m)^2/\Gamma(2i/m) / (2\pi i)^{\#\alpha/2} : C^\times$ for $\alpha \in X^*(\mathrm{MT})$.
  `MinimalPolynomial(P_gamma,2m)` and `Roots(-,K^{conn})` pick the $Q^{\mathrm{ab}}$-realisation.
  Not fundamental; $\Gamma$ value is $C$ element, $K^{\mathrm{conn}}$ is subfield of period field.

**Galois / Sato-Tate language**

* $\ell$-adic representation $\rho_{\ell}: \operatorname{Gal}_Q \to \mathrm{GSp}(H^1_{\ell}) : \mathsf{Groups}$ and Zariski closure $G_{\ell} : \mathsf{GrpSch}/Q_\ell$; $G_{\ell}^0 = \mathrm{MT} \otimes Q_\ell$ after $K^{\mathrm{conn}}$ (Mumford-Tate conjecture, theorem for CM)

* Sato-Tate group $\mathrm{ST}(J_m) \subset \mathrm{USp}(2g)$ — maximal compact of $\mathrm{MT}\otimes R$, object of $\mathsf{CompactLieGroups}$; $\mathrm{ST}^0$ is identity component, $\pi_0(\mathrm{ST})=\operatorname{Gal}(K^{\mathrm{conn}}/Q)$.
  `compute_component(tau)` builds ideal $I(\tau) \subset K^{\mathrm{conn}}[t_{ij}]$ whose variety is $\tau\cdot\mathrm{ST}^0$ as algebraic set $\prod T[i,u^{-1}i] - \mathrm{STcoeff}(\tau,\alpha)=0$ plus $TT^8=1, T[i,j]=0$ for $j\neq u\cdot i$.
  This is coordinate ring of $\mathrm{ST}$ in $\mathsf{AffVar}_{K^{\mathrm{conn}}}$.

* Endomorphism algebra $\operatorname{End}^0(J_m)=\operatorname{End}(J_m)\otimes Q : \mathsf{Algebras}_Q$ and field $\mathrm{EndField} \subset K^{\mathrm{conn}}$ where all endomorphisms defined — object of $\mathsf{Fields}$; `Frobenius` conjugacy $\operatorname{Frob}_p : H^1_{\ell} \to H^1_{\ell}$ with charpoly $f_p(T)=\det(T-\operatorname{Frob}_p|H^1) = \prod(T+\chi^a(-1)J(\chi^a,\varphi))$ where Jacobi sum $J(\chi^a,\varphi)= \sum_{x} \chi^a(x)\varphi(1-x) : Q(\zeta_m)$ is period.
  `FastCharPoly` counts $x\in F_p$ via $\log_g$ discrete log is evaluation of this character, not a new functor.

**Not fundamental**

* $m=15,60,20$, $z_m=\zeta_m : C$, `g, coprimes_to_m`, `OrderCharacters`, `ipoint`, $p=1021$ — elements/morphisms in above

* $X,Y,Z$ matrices $s1..s5$ in `UnderstandingW(E6)` — specific $\operatorname{Weyl}(O(H^2))$ elements, instance of $\operatorname{Weyl}(\mathsf{Lattice})$ already in intake

## Desired capability: honest and operationalized $Q_\ell$, étale cohomology, Galois cohomology as specialization — computable in some cases — note 2026-09-16

Need honest and operationalized $Q_\ell$, étale cohomology, and Galois cohomology as a specialization — not a formal $H^1_\ell$ string — and it must be computable in some cases via effective models.

* $Q_\ell$ is not $Q$ with $\ell$ appended.
  It is the $\ell$-adic completion $Q_\ell = (\varprojlim Z/\ell^n) \otimes Q$ as object of $\mathsf{Fields}$ with its $\ell$-adic topology, valuation, and absolute Galois action.
  Need $Z_\ell$, $Q_\ell$, $Q_\ell^{\mathrm{ur}}$ as coefficient rings for sheaves, with $\ell$-adic lisse sheaves $Q_\ell(n)$ as objects of $\mathsf{Sh}(X_{\text{ét}}, Q_\ell)$ obtained via inverse system $(Z/\ell^n)_{n}$ and tensor with $Q_\ell$.
  Without this, $\rho_\ell: \operatorname{Gal}_Q \to \mathrm{GSp}(H^1_\ell)$ cannot be stated.

* Étale cohomology $H^*_{\text{ét}}(X_{\text{ét}}, Q_\ell) : \mathsf{GrMod}$ is sheaf cohomology of the étale site $X_{\text{ét}}$ as graded object $H^*_{\text{ét}} = \bigoplus_i H^i_{\text{ét}}$ with $H^i_{\text{ét}} = H^i(R\Gamma(X_{\text{ét}},-))$ and uniform interface $H^*_{\text{ét}}.\mathtt{graded\_piece}(i) = H^i_{\text{ét}}$, not a family of separate $H^i$ requests.
  Requires site $(C_X, J_{\text{ét}})$ where $C_X = \text{Ét}_{/X}$ and $J_{\text{ét}}$ is Grothendieck topology given by covering families $\{U_i \to U\}$ jointly surjective, equivalently sieves $S \subset h_U$.
  Sheaf condition is $F(U) \cong \lim_{S} F$ for covering sieves.
  This gives topos $\mathsf{Sh}(X_{\text{ét}})$ and derived $R\Gamma(X_{\text{ét}},-): D(\mathsf{Sh})\to D(\mathsf{Ab})$ whose cohomology is the graded $H^*_{\text{ét}} = H^*(R\Gamma) : \mathsf{GrMod}$ with $H^*_{\text{ét}}.\mathtt{graded\_piece}(i)=H^i_{\text{ét}}$.

* Galois cohomology as graded specialization: for $X = \operatorname{Spec} K$ ($K$ field, e.g. $K=Q$), $X_{\text{ét}}$ is the site of finite étale $\operatorname{Spec} L \to \operatorname{Spec} K$ (finite separable extensions), equivalent to finite discrete $\operatorname{Gal}_K$-sets.
  Then $\mathsf{Sh}((\operatorname{Spec} K)_{\text{ét}}) \simeq \operatorname{Gal}_K\text{-}\mathsf{Sets}$ (discrete $\operatorname{Gal}_K$-modules), and $H^*_{\text{ét}}(\operatorname{Spec} K, F) : \mathsf{GrMod}$ with $H^*_{\text{ét}}.\mathtt{graded\_piece}(i)=H^i_{\text{ét}}(\operatorname{Spec} K,F)=H^i(\operatorname{Gal}_K,F_{K^{\mathrm{sep}}})$ — graded $H^*(\operatorname{Gal}_K,-)$ is the value of the same site cohomology functor at the terminal object.
  Need this identification as theorem on graded $H^*$, not as separate definition.
  Without sites, $H^*(\operatorname{Gal},-)$ and $H^*_{\text{ét}}$ are two unrelated functors.

* Requires: category $\mathsf{Sites}$ with objects $(C, J)$ (small category + Grothendieck topology via sieves/covering families), morphisms of sites (continuous functors), associated sheaf functor $a: \mathsf{PSh}(C) \to \mathsf{Sh}(C,J)$, and cohomology $R\Gamma$.
  Specializations: Zariski site $(\mathsf{Open}(X), J_{\mathrm{Zar}})$, étale site $(\text{Ét}_{/X}, J_{\text{ét}})$, pro-étale, fppf — each is an object of $\mathsf{Sites}$.
  The same formalism must own Zariski, étale, and Galois as instances.

* Operationalized and computable in some cases (not merely honest): for $X: \mathsf{Sch}/F_q$ finite type with a finite affine/étale cover $U=\{U_i\to X\}$ and $F : \mathsf{Sh}(X_{\text{ét}},Q_\ell)$ constructible lisse (e.g. $Q_\ell(n)$ via $Z/\ell^n$ system $\varprojlim F_n \otimes Q_\ell$), $R\Gamma(X_{\text{ét}},F)$ must be an effective object $EC: \mathsf{Ch}^{\mathrm{eff}}$ with finite $\ell$-adic coefficients via Čech $\check{C}(U,F)$ as $\operatorname{Tot}$ and graded $H^*_{\text{ét}}(X,Q_\ell)=H^*(EC) : \mathsf{GrMod}$ with $H^*_{\text{ét}}.\mathtt{graded\_piece}(i)=H^i(EC)$ and $\operatorname{Frob}$ action, computable via `X.etale_cohomology(Q_ell) : GrMod` with $H^*.\mathtt{graded\_piece}(i)$ and comparable to $R\Gamma_c$ already intaken for zeta/Weil.
  In the smooth proper case, comparison $H^*_{\text{ét}}(X_{\bar F_q},Q_\ell) \cong H^*_{\mathrm{MW/rigid}}(X)\otimes Q_\ell : \mathsf{GrMod}$ via Monsky-Washnitzer / rigid cohomology gives a private computational adapter when the site model is not yet effective; for general singular $X$, retain $R\Gamma$ as formal $D$ with known type.
  Without this, $H^*_{\text{ét}}$ remains a definition with no computational route, and the zeta/Weil and Lefschetz-trace leads would have no honest cohomology to compute against.

Intended owners: `categories/topology/sites.py` (`Site`, `GrothendieckTopology`, `Sieve`, `CoveringFamily` with axioms), `categories/topology/sheaves_on_site.py` ($\mathsf{Sh}(C,J)$), `categories/etale/etale_site.py` ($X_{\text{ét}}$), `categories/etale/ladic_sheaves.py` ($Q_\ell$, $Q_\ell(n)$ lisse), `categories/galois/galois_cohomology.py` ($H^*(\operatorname{Gal}_K,-)=H^*_{\text{ét}}(\operatorname{Spec} K,-) : \mathsf{GrMod}$ as graded specialization, not a parallel definition), `categories/etale/effective.py` ($R\Gamma$ as effective $\mathsf{Ch}$ via $\check{C}(U,F)$ and MW/rigid adapter), `categories/graded/graded_modules.py` ($\mathsf{GrMod}$ with `graded_piece(i)` and `euler_characteristic : GrMod_{ZZ} -> ZZ` via $\chi(H^*) = \sum (-1)^i \operatorname{rank} H^*.\mathtt{graded\_piece}(i)$), `categories/graded/euler_characteristic.py` (`Euler characteristic from ZZ-graded objects in complete generality`). Not a free `etale_cohomology(X, Q_ell)` function — `X.etale_site().cohomology(Q_ell) : GrMod` with $H^*.\mathtt{graded\_piece}(i)=H^i_{\text{ét}}$, `Spec(K).etale_cohomology(Q_ell) : GrMod`, with `X.etale_cohomology(Q_ell).effective_complex()` when computable; do not add `cohomology(i, Q_ell)` returning a bare $H^i$.
Almost all cohomology requested in this intake is requested as graded $H^* : \mathsf{GrMod}$ with uniform `H.graded_piece(i)`, not as $H^i$; Euler characteristics just come from $\mathsf{GrMod}_{ZZ}$ in complete generality via $\chi(H^*) = \sum (-1)^i \operatorname{rank} H^*.\mathtt{graded\_piece}(i)$ for any $H^* : \mathsf{GrMod}_{ZZ}$, with $\chi(H^*_{c,\text{ét}}) = \sum (-1)^i b_i$ as instance.

## Desired capability: Gauss-Manin, six functors, derived pushforwards — note 2026-09-15

Need the Gauss-Manin connection, some basic six-functor formalism for sheaves, and derived functors — particularly right-derived pushforwards.

* Gauss-Manin connection $\nabla_{\mathrm{GM}}: H^k_{\mathrm{dR}}(X/S) \to H^k_{\mathrm{dR}}(X/S) \otimes \Omega^1_S$ is not an ad-hoc matrix $M(t)$ from `periods`. It is the flat connection on the relative de Rham cohomology sheaf $H^k_{\mathrm{dR}}(X/S) = R^k f_* \Omega^\bullet_{X/S}$ obtained as the $d_1$ differential of the Hodge-de Rham spectral sequence or, equivalently, as the connection induced by the derived pushforward of the relative de Rham complex.
  Construction lives in $D_S\text{-}\mathsf{Mod}$ via $\nabla_{\mathrm{GM}} \leftrightarrow D_S$-action; $\mathrm{PF}(w)$ is $\operatorname{Ann}_{D_S}(w)$ for $w = \int_\gamma \Omega$.
  Need $\nabla_{\mathrm{GM}}$ as object of $\mathsf{FlatConn}(S)$ with its regular singularities and Griffiths transversality, not just $\mathrm{mat}/\mathrm{den}$.

* Six functors for sheaves: for any morphism $f: X \to S$ need functors between derived categories $D(\mathsf{Sh}(X)), D(\mathsf{Sh}(S))$ (étale, coherent, analytic — same formalism via sites):

  * $f^*: D(S) \to D(X)$ (pullback, left adjoint to $f_*$), $f_*: D(X) \to D(S)$ (pushforward)

  * $f_!: D(X) \to D(S)$ (pushforward with proper support), $f^!: D(S) \to D(X)$ (exceptional pullback, right adjoint to $f_!$)

  * $\otimes, \operatorname{Hom}$ internal tensor and internal hom in $D$

  * Adjunctions $f^* \dashv f_*$, $f_! \dashv f^!$, projection formula $f_!(F \otimes f^*G) \cong f_!F \otimes G$, base change, duality.
    These are required to state `f.as_family` cohomology, dualizing complexes, and $R f_*$ compatibility.

* Derived functors — particularly right-derived pushforwards: $R f_*: D(\mathsf{Sh}(X)) \to D(\mathsf{Sh}(S))$ is right derivation of $f_*: \mathsf{Sh}(X) \to \mathsf{Sh}(S)$ via injective resolutions.
  Need $R^i f_* = H^i(R f_*)$ as objects $R^i f_* \Omega^\bullet$, $R^i f_* Q_\ell$ etc., with $R f_*$ preserving constructibility under proper/base-change hypotheses.
  Period sheaf $H^k_{\mathrm{dR}}(X/S) = R^k f_* \Omega^\bullet_{X/S}$ and $R^k f^{\mathrm{an}}_* Q_\ell$, $R^k f^{\mathrm{an}}_* C$ (Betti local system) are all instances of the same $R f_*$.
  The comparison $R^k f_*^{\mathrm{dR}} \otimes C \cong R^k f^{\mathrm{an}}_* C \otimes O^{\mathrm{an}}$ is the comparison isomorphism that makes $\nabla_{\mathrm{GM}}$ and monodromy agree.
  Without $R f_*$ as derived functor, each $H^k$ is a separate ad-hoc construction.

* Requires: category $\mathsf{Derived}(C)$ for Grothendieck abelian $C = \mathsf{Sh}(X_{\text{ét}}), \mathsf{QCoh}, \mathsf{Mod}_{D_S}$, functors $L f^*, R f_*, R f_!, f^!$ as triangulated functors with adjunctions, and the full six-functor package (not just $R f_*$). Specializations: $R\Gamma(X, -) = R p_*$ for $p: X \to \operatorname{Spec} k$; $H^i_{\text{ét}} = R^i\Gamma$; $H^i(\operatorname{Gal}, -)$ is $R^i\Gamma$ for $\operatorname{Spec} K$.

Intended owners: `categories/functors/gauss_manifold.py` (`GaussManin` as $\nabla_{\mathrm{GM}}$ on $H_{\mathrm{dR}}$), `categories/sheaves/six_functors.py` ($f^*, R f_*, R f_!, f^!, \otimes, \operatorname{Hom}$ with adjunctions), `categories/derived/derived_category.py` ($D(-)$, $R f_*$ via injectives, $L f^*$), `categories/derived/pushforward.py` ($R^i f_*$ as cohomology sheaves).
Not free `pushforward(f, sheaf)` with no derived structure — `f.derived_pushforward(sheaf)` in $D(\mathsf{Sh})$ on the site objects, with $R^i$ as $H^i$.

## Desired capability: p-curvature — note 2026-09-15

Need $p$-curvature.

For a flat connection $\nabla: E \to E \otimes \Omega^1_{X/S}$ on a smooth $S$-scheme $X$ in characteristic $p>0$ (or reduction mod $p$ of a characteristic-zero connection), the $p$-curvature is the $O_X$-linear map

$$
\psi_p(\nabla): T_{X/S} \to \operatorname{End}_{O_X}(E),\quad \psi_p(D) = \nabla(D)^p - \nabla(D^{[p]})
$$

where $D^{[p]}$ is the $p$-th iterate (restricted Lie algebra structure on derivations) and $\nabla(D)^p$ is $p$-th iterate as differential operator.
For $X = \operatorname{Spec} R[t]$ and $\nabla = d + A dt$, this is $\psi_p(\partial_t) = (\partial_t + A)^p - (\partial_t^p + A^{(p)})$ as $p$-linear operator.
Vanishing $\psi_p = 0$ is Cartier descent: $\nabla$ has full set of horizontal sections $E^{\nabla}$ with $E \cong F^* E^{\nabla}$ via Frobenius $F: X \to X^{(1)}$.
The $p$-curvature measures obstruction to descending $\nabla$ along Frobenius and controls Grothendieck-Katz $p$-curvature conjecture: $\nabla$ has algebraic solutions iff $\psi_p \equiv 0 \bmod p$ for almost all $p$.

Requires: category $\mathsf{Conn}(X/S)$ in characteristic $p$, restricted Lie algebra $T_{X/S}$ with $D \mapsto D^{[p]}$, Frobenius twist $X^{(1)}$, Cartier operator, and functor $\psi_p: \mathsf{Conn} \to \mathsf{Higgs}_{X^{(1)}}$ to Higgs fields ($\operatorname{End}(E)$-valued 1-forms).
Need $\psi_p$ as morphism $T_{X/S} \to \operatorname{End}(E)$ on the connection object, not a bare matrix $A_p$.
Instances: Gauss-Manin $\nabla_{\mathrm{GM}}$ reduced mod $p$, its $p$-curvature is the obstruction whose vanishing detects algebraicity of periods; for $\mathrm{PF}(w)$ operator $L \in D_S$, $\psi_p(L)$ is its $p$-curvature as $p$-linear operator on $D_S$-module.

Intended owners: `categories/connections/p_curvature.py` (`p_curvature(nabla): Higgs`), `categories/characteristic_p/cartier.py` (`Frobenius`, `Cartier descent`), `categories/connections/characteristic_p.py` ($\mathsf{Conn}_p$, $\psi_p$ with $D^{[p]}$). Not a free `p_curvature(matrix)` — `nabla.p_curvature(D)` on the connection object in $\mathsf{FlatConn}(X/S)$ over $F_p$.

## Desired capability: zeta functions of varieties over $F_q$ and Weil verification — note 2026-09-15

Need to work with zeta functions of varieties over $F_q$, and at least for (some) curves, affine spaces, projective spaces, Grassmannians, etc. where point counts have explicit formulas, get closed forms and verify all Weil conjectures hold for them.

* Zeta function is not a Python `float` from $\exp(\sum N_r T^r/r)$ numerically.
  It is the formal series/exponential

  $$
  Z(X/F_q, T) = \exp( \sum_{r\geq 1} |X(F_{q^r})| T^r / r ) \in 1 + T\cdot Q[[T]]
  $$

  equivalently Euler product $\prod_{x\in|X|} (1 - T^{\deg x})^{-1}$, object of $Q(T)$ (rational function when Weil holds) with its $T$-adic parent $Q[[T]]$.
  Interface lives on the variety: `X.zeta()` returns $Z$ as element $Z : Q(T)$ with numerator/denominator $P_i(T)$, not a bare power series guess.

* Concrete point counts with closed forms (must be exact, not enumerating $F_{q^r}$ for large $r$):

  * $A^n: |A^n(F_{q^r})| = q^{n r}$ $\to$ $Z = 1/(1 - q^n T)$

  * $P^n: |P^n(F_{q^r})| = (q^{(n+1)r}-1)/(q^r-1) = 1+q^r+\cdots+q^{nr}$ $\to$ $Z = 1/((1-T)(1-qT)\cdots(1-q^n T))$

  * $\operatorname{Gr}(k,n): |\operatorname{Gr}(k,n)(F_{q^r})| = \text{Gaussian binomial } \binom{n}{k}_{q^r}$ with $q$-factorial formula $\to$ $Z = \prod_{i=0}^{k(n-k)} 1/(1-q^i T)^{c_i}$ with explicit $c_i$ or product of $q$-integers; closed via $q$-binomial rationality

  * Some curves: $A^1$ minus $n$ points, elliptic curve $E: y^2 = x^3+ax+b$ with $|E(F_{q^r})| = q^r+1 - \alpha^r - \bar\alpha^r$ ($\alpha\bar\alpha = q$), hyperelliptic of low genus where $L$-polynomial is known; at least need interface `Curve.point_count(r)` that is exact and $Z$-rational.

* Must get closed forms: $Z(X,T) = \prod_{i=0}^{2 \dim X} P_i(T)^{(-1)^{i+1}}$ with $P_i(T) = \det(1 - T\cdot\operatorname{Frob} \mid H^*_{c,\text{ét}}(X_{\bar F_q}, Q_\ell).\mathtt{graded\_piece}(i)) \in Z[T]$ ($H^*_{c,\text{ét}} : \mathsf{GrMod}$ with $H^*.\mathtt{graded\_piece}(i)=H^i_{c,\text{ét}}$), $P_0 = 1-T$, $P_{2n}=1-q^n T$ for connected $X$.
  Construction is $X \to (N_r = |X(F_{q^r})|) \to Z = \exp$ with rational reconstruction (compare coefficients $N_r$ with $\log Z$ expansion), not numeric `exp`.

* Must verify all Weil conjectures for these concrete $X$ and return certificates, not just claim:

  * (W1) Rationality: $Z \in Q(T)$ — check $Z$ is rational function with $Z \in 1+T\cdot Z[[T]]$

  * (W2) Functional equation: $Z(X, 1/(q^n T)) = \pm q^{n\chi/2} T^{\chi} Z(X,T)$ with $\chi = \sum (-1)^i b_i$, $n=\dim X$; verify as identity in $Q(T)$

  * (W3) Riemann hypothesis: $P_i(T)=\prod(1-\alpha_{ij} T)$ with $|\alpha_{ij}| = q^{w/2}$ for $w=i$ (purity) — verify by factoring $P_i$ over $C$ and checking $|\alpha| = q^{i/2}$ via `QQbar` absolute value, or via $\ell$-adic weights.
    For concrete cases this is checkable: $P_i$ split with known roots $q^{j}$ or Weil numbers $\alpha, \bar\alpha$.

  * (W4) Betti numbers: $\deg P_i = b_i = \dim H^*_{c,\text{ét}}.\mathtt{graded\_piece}(i)$ — compare with known $b_i$ from $\ell$-adic graded $H^*_{c,\text{ét}} : \mathsf{GrMod}$ (e.g. $b_i(P^n)=1$ for even $i\leq 2n$ else $0$; $b_i(\operatorname{Gr})$ via Schubert cells).

* Requires: site cohomology $R\Gamma_c(X_{\text{ét}}, Q_\ell)$ from six-functor $R f_!$ already noted, trace formula $N_r = \sum (-1)^i \operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Frob}^r \mid H^*_c.\mathtt{graded\_piece}(i))$ with $H^*_c : \mathsf{GrMod}$, and the comparison $Z = \prod \det(1-T\cdot\operatorname{Frob}\mid H^*_c.\mathtt{graded\_piece}(i))^{(-1)^{i+1}}$.
  The same $R f_!$ and $Q_\ell$ owners above must supply $H^*_c : \mathsf{GrMod}$ and $\operatorname{Frob}$ action on each `graded_piece(i)`.

Intended owners: `categories/zeta/zeta.py` (`Variety.zeta(): Q(T)` via $N_r$), `categories/varieties/point_counts.py` (`X.point_count(r): NN` exact for $A^n$, $P^n$, $\operatorname{Gr}(k,n)$, some curves), `categories/etale/weil.py` (`WeilVerification` with `rationality`, `functional_equation`, `rh_roots`, `betti` checks).
Not a free `zeta_via_brute_force(X,q)` — `X.zeta()` on the variety object in $\mathsf{Sch}/F_q$ with $Q_\ell$-cohomology behind the same site, and `Z.verify_weil()` returning certificates for the concrete families.

## Fundamental objects to even state Weil conjectures — verbatim — 2026-09-15

To state $W(X/F_q)$ you need these as categories/functors — not $q=5$, $X=P^2$:

**Base**

* $\mathsf{FinFields}$ — object $F_q : \mathsf{Fields}$ with $q=p^a : NN$, $\mathrm{Fr}_q: \operatorname{Spec} \bar F_q \to \operatorname{Spec} \bar F_q$ Frobenius morphism $x\mapsto x^q$; $\operatorname{Gal}(\bar F_q/F_q)= \hat Z\cdot\mathrm{Fr}_q : \mathsf{Groups}$

* $\mathsf{Sch}/F_q : \mathsf{Cat}$ — variety $X : \mathsf{Sch}/F_q$ finite type, $\dim X = n : NN$ (element), base-change $X_{\bar F_q}=X\times_{F_q}\bar F_q : \mathsf{Sch}/\bar F_q$; $|X(F_{q^r})| = \operatorname{Hom}_{\mathsf{Sch}/F_q}(\operatorname{Spec} F_{q^r}, X) : \mathsf{Sets}$ has $\mathrm{cardinality} : NN$

**Counting / zeta (no cohomology yet)**

* Formal series $Q[[T]]$ = $(T)$-adic completion of $Q[T]$; subobject $1+T\cdot Q[[T]]$ and $Q(T)=\operatorname{Frac} Q[T] : \mathsf{Fields}$

* Euler product as identity in $Q[[T]]$: for $|X|$ closed points, $\deg x = [\kappa(x):F_q]$

  $$
  Z(X,T)=\exp( \sum_{r\geq 1} N_r T^r/r ) = \prod_{x\in|X|} (1-T^{\deg x})^{-1} : Q[[T]]
  $$

  where $N_r = |X(F_{q^r})|$.
  This is definition of $Z$, element $Z : Q[[T]]$.
  Rationality $Z \in Q(T)$ is first Weil statement.

**Cohomology (to state factorisation)**

* Site $X_{\text{ét}} : \mathsf{Sites}$ and $\ell\neq p$, $Q_\ell : \mathsf{Fields}$ as before; $\mathsf{Sh}(X_{\text{ét}},Q_\ell) : \mathsf{AbCat}$ and derived $R\Gamma_c = R(p_!) : D(\mathsf{Sh}) \to D(\mathsf{Vect}_{Q_\ell})$ for $p: X\to\operatorname{Spec} F_q$ (proper-support pushforward from six functors).
  Object $H^*_{c,\text{ét}}(X_{\bar F_q}, Q_\ell) = H^*(R\Gamma_c(Q_\ell)) : \mathsf{GrMod}_{Q_\ell}$ with $H^*_{c,\text{ét}}.\mathtt{graded\_piece}(i)=H^i_{c,\text{ét}}=H^i(R\Gamma_c) : \mathsf{Vect}_{Q_\ell}$ finite-dimensional, with continuous $\operatorname{Gal}(\bar F_q/F_q)$-action; $\mathrm{Fr}_q$ acts as $\operatorname{Frob} : H^*_c.\mathtt{graded\_piece}(i) \to H^*_c.\mathtt{graded\_piece}(i)$

* Grothendieck-Lefschetz trace formula (must exist to link counting to cohomology):

  $$
  N_r = \sum_{i=0}^{2n} (-1)^i \operatorname{Tr}( \mathrm{Fr}_q^r \mid H^*_c.\mathtt{graded\_piece}(i) ) \quad \text{with } H^*_c : \mathsf{GrMod}
  $$

  Without $R f_!$ and $\operatorname{Tr}$ on the graded $H^*_c$ this is not statable.

**Factorisation and statements**

* From trace, $Z$ factors as

  $$
  Z(X,T)= \prod_{i=0}^{2n} P_i(T)^{(-1)^{i+1}},\quad P_i(T)=\det(1-T\cdot\mathrm{Fr}_q \mid H^*_c.\mathtt{graded\_piece}(i)) \in Z[T] : \mathsf{Poly}(Z) \quad \text{with } H^*_c : \mathsf{GrMod}
  $$

  Need $\mathsf{Poly}(Z) \to Q(T)$ and $\deg P_i = b_i = \dim H^*_c.\mathtt{graded\_piece}(i) : NN$ (fourth Weil/Betti).
  This is rationality refinement.

* Functional equation needs Poincaré duality for $H^*_c$ ($f^! Q_\ell \cong Q_\ell(n)[2n]$) and $f_! \dashv f^!$ on graded $H^*_c$:

  $$
  \begin{aligned}
  &Z(X, 1/(q^n T)) = \pm q^{n\chi/2} T^{\chi} Z(X,T),\quad \chi=\chi(H^*_{c,\text{ét}}) = \sum(-1)^i b_i : ZZ \\
  &\text{with } b_i = \operatorname{rank} H^*_{c,\text{ét}}.\mathtt{graded\_piece}(i)\text{, Euler characteristic of the } ZZ\text{-graded object } H^*_{c,\text{ét}} : \mathsf{GrMod}_{ZZ} \text{ (or } \mathsf{GrMod}_{Q_\ell}\text{) in complete generality} \\
  &\text{— } \chi \text{ just comes from } \mathsf{GrMod}_{ZZ} \text{ via } \chi(H^*) = \sum (-1)^i \operatorname{rank} H^*.\mathtt{graded\_piece}(i)\text{, not specific to étale}
  \end{aligned}
  $$

  as identity in $Q(T)$ (needs $\otimes$ and dualizing complex).

* Riemann hypothesis needs algebraic numbers and weights: $P_i(T)=\prod_j (1-\alpha_{ij} T)$, $\alpha_{ij} : \bar Q \subset C$ via chosen $\bar Q\hookrightarrow C$, condition $|\alpha_{ij}| = q^{i/2}$ for all embeddings $|\cdot|: \bar Q\to C$ (Weil numbers of weight $i$). Needs $\mathsf{Fields}$, $\mathsf{AlgClosure}$, $\mathrm{Abs}: C\to R_{\geq 0}$, and $\mathrm{Weight}$ as $NN$ element.
  No numerics — $|\alpha|=q^{i/2}$ is equality in $R$.

**Not fundamental**

* $q=7$, $X=A^n,P^n,\operatorname{Gr}(k,n)$, $N_r = q^{nr}$ or $q$-binomial, explicit $P_i = 1-q^j T$ — elements $:NN$ and morphisms $\operatorname{Spec} F_{q^r}\to X$ whose counts give closed $Z = 1/(1-q^n T)$ etc.; the functor $X \mapsto Z(X)$ and its factorisation are.

## Desired capability: operationalize Lefschetz trace for Frob — note 2026-09-15

Need a way to operationalize the Lefschetz trace formula, especially on étale cohomology for the trace of Frobenius.

Need $\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Frob}^r \mid H^*_{c,\text{ét}}(X_{\bar F_q}, Q_\ell).\mathtt{graded\_piece}(i)) : Q_\ell$ as computable morphism on the graded cohomology $H^*_{c,\text{ét}} : \mathsf{GrMod}$, not a formal symbol.
Formula

$$
N_r = \sum_{i=0}^{2n} (-1)^i \operatorname{Tr}( \mathrm{Fr}_q^r \mid H^*_{c}.\mathtt{graded\_piece}(i) ) \quad \text{with } H^*_c : \mathsf{GrMod}
$$

must be executable: from $X : \mathsf{Sch}/F_q$ produce graded $H^*_c : \mathsf{GrMod}$ with $H^*_c.\mathtt{graded\_piece}(i)=H^i_c : \mathsf{Vect}_{Q_\ell}$ and $\operatorname{Frob} : \operatorname{End}(H^*_c.\mathtt{graded\_piece}(i))$ (via $X_{\text{ét}}$, $R\Gamma_c = R(p_!)$, $Q_\ell(n)$), compute its trace via $\mathsf{Vect}_{Q_\ell}$ linear algebra, and compare with $|X(F_{q^r})| : NN$ (finite-set count).
This is the bridge between counting and cohomology that makes $Z(X,T)=\prod P_i(T)^{(-1)^{i+1}}$ effective, where $P_i(T)=\det(1 - T\cdot\operatorname{Frob} \mid H^*_c.\mathtt{graded\_piece}(i))$.

Requires: $\operatorname{Frob} : X_{\bar F_q} \to X_{\bar F_q}$ as $\mathrm{Fr}_q \times \mathrm{id}$ on $X\times_{F_q}\bar F_q$, its action $\operatorname{Frob}^*: H^*_c.\mathtt{graded\_piece}(i) \to H^*_c.\mathtt{graded\_piece}(i)$ via functoriality of $R\Gamma_c$ on graded $H^*_c : \mathsf{GrMod}$, and $\operatorname{Tr}: \operatorname{End}(V) \to Q_\ell$ on $\mathsf{Vect}_{Q_\ell}$ (finite-dimensional).
Operationalization means: when $H^*_c : \mathsf{GrMod}$ is presented (e.g. via known cell decomposition for $A^n, P^n, \operatorname{Gr}$ or via Monsky-Washnitzer / crystalline $R\Gamma_c$ with Frobenius lift for general $X$), actually return matrix $M_i = \operatorname{Frob}|_{H^*_c.\mathtt{graded\_piece}(i)} : \operatorname{Mat}_{b_i}(Q_\ell)$ and compute $\operatorname{Tr}(M_i^r)$, $\det(1 - T M_i)$ exactly in $Z[T]$.
Not a placeholder `trace_of_frob` stub.

For concrete families ($A^n, P^n, \operatorname{Gr}(k,n)$ and some curves) the trace is already known from explicit $P_i$: e.g. $\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Frob}\mid H^*_c.\mathtt{graded\_piece}(2j))=q^j$, otherwise $0$; for $\operatorname{Gr}$, $\operatorname{Tr}$ is $q^{\text{something}}$ via Schubert.
The operational trace must reproduce those $N_r$ via the sum, certifying the formula for those $X$.
For general $X$, the trace is the computational content of Monsky-Washnitzer / rigid cohomology $H^*_{\mathrm{MW}}: \mathsf{GrMod}$ with Frobenius lift, behind the same $R\Gamma_c$ interface — private adapter, but `X.etale_cohomology(Q_ell) : GrMod` with $H^*.\mathtt{graded\_piece}(i)$ and `Frob.matrix()` stay owned.

Intended owners: `categories/etale/trace_formula.py` (`LefschetzTrace` with $\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Frob}^r\mid H^*_c.\mathtt{graded\_piece}(i))$ and $N_r = \sum (-1)^i \operatorname{Tr}$), `categories/etale/frobenius.py` ($\mathrm{Frobenius} : \operatorname{End}(H^*_c.\mathtt{graded\_piece}(i))$ as $R\Gamma_c(\mathrm{Fr}_q)$ on graded $H^*_c$), `categories/etale/monsky_washnitzer.py` (private MW adapter for general $X$ when $R\Gamma_c$ not yet known).
Not a free `trace_frobenius(X)` — `X.etale_cohomology_c(Q_ell) : GrMod` with `X.etale_cohomology_c(Q_ell).graded_piece(i).frobenius().trace(r)` on the graded cohomology, with `X.point_count(r)` on the variety object for comparison; do not add `etale_cohomology_c(i, Q_ell)` returning bare $H^i$.

## Desired capability: $HH(A)$ — Hochschild (co)homology — note 2026-09-16

Support $HH(A)$ — Hochschild (co)homology of an (associative, possibly dg) algebra $A$.

* $HH(A)$ is not a bare list of groups computed by a helper.
  Homology is the homology of the Hochschild complex $C(A): \ldots \to A^{\otimes n+1} \to A^{\otimes n} \to \ldots$ with differential $b(a0\otimes\ldots\otimes an)= \sum (-1)^i \ldots + (-1)^n a_n a_0 \otimes \ldots$, as object $HH_*(A) = \bigoplus_n HH_n(A) : \mathsf{GrMod}$ ($HH_0 = A/[A,A]$, etc.) with $HH_*(A).\mathtt{graded\_piece}(n)=HH_n(A)$.
  For dg $A$, $C(A)$ is the derived tensor $C(A)= A \otimes^{L}_{A\otimes A^{\mathrm{op}}} A : \mathsf{Ch}$, i.e. $HH_*(A)= \operatorname{Tor}^{A\otimes A^{\mathrm{op}}}_*(A,A) : \mathsf{GrMod}$.
  Need $HH_*(A)$ as functor $HH_*: \mathsf{Alg}_{\mathrm{dg}} \to \mathsf{GrMod}$ (or $D(\mathsf{Ab})$), with $HH_*(A)$ computed as $H_*(C(A))$ and $HH_*(A).\mathtt{graded\_piece}(n)=H_n(C(A))$.
  Cohomology is $HH^*(A) = \bigoplus_n HH^n(A) : \mathsf{GrAlg}$ with $HH^n(A)=\operatorname{Ext}^n_{A^e}(A,A)=H^n(R\operatorname{Hom}_{A^e}(A,A)) : \mathsf{Mod}_k$ and $HH^*(A).\mathtt{graded\_piece}(n)=HH^n(A)$, with cup product $HH^p\otimes HH^q\to HH^{p+q}$ via Yoneda $\operatorname{Ext}^p\otimes \operatorname{Ext}^q\to \operatorname{Ext}^{p+q}$ and Gerstenhaber bracket $[-,-]: HH^p\otimes HH^q\to HH^{p+q-1}$.
  Need $HH^*(A)$ as functor $HH^*: \mathsf{Alg}_{\mathrm{dg}} \to \mathsf{GrAlg}$ with $HH^*(A)=H^*(R\operatorname{Hom}_{A^e}(A,A)) : \mathsf{GrAlg}$.

* Requires: category $\mathsf{Alg}_{\mathrm{dg}}(k)$ (dg algebras over $k$ with $k$ a commutative ring), enveloping algebra $A^e = A\otimes A^{\mathrm{op}} : \mathsf{Alg}$, $\mathsf{Bimod}_A = \mathsf{Mod}_{A^e}$, and derived $\otimes^L$ and $R\operatorname{Hom}$.
  Homology uses $\otimes^L : HH_*(A)=\operatorname{Tor}^{A^e}_*(A,A)$; cohomology uses $R\operatorname{Hom} : HH^*(A)=\operatorname{Ext}^*_{A^e}(A,A)$.
  Interface must be `A.hochschild_complex() -> C(A) : Ch(k)` and `A.hochschild_homology() -> HH_*(A) : GrMod` with $HH_*(A).\mathtt{graded\_piece}(n)=HH_n(A)$ and `A.hochschild_cohomology() -> HH^*(A) : GrAlg` with $HH^*.\mathtt{graded\_piece}(n)=HH^n(A)$, with $HH_0 = \text{abelianization}$ and $HH^0 = \text{center } Z(A)$.

* Expected compatibilities (at least for smooth cases): HKR isomorphisms — homology $HH_n(A) \cong \Omega^n_{X/k}$ for smooth commutative $A = O(X)$ as $\mathsf{GrMod}$ with Connes differential $B: HH_n \to HH_{n+1}$ matching $d_{\mathrm{dR}}$, and cohomology $HH^n(A) \cong \mathrm{PolyVect}^n_{X/k} = \wedge^n T_{X/k}$ as $\mathsf{GrAlg}$ with Gerstenhaber bracket matching Schouten bracket $[-,-]_S$ and cup product matching wedge $\wedge$.
  For scheme $X$, $HH_*(X)=HH_*(\mathsf{Perf}(X))$ with $HH_*(X) \cong \bigoplus H^i(X, \Omega^j)$ and $HH^*(X)=HH^*(\mathsf{Perf}(X))$ with $HH^*(X) \cong \bigoplus H^i(X, \wedge^j T_X)$ as $\mathsf{GrMod}$/$\mathsf{GrAlg}$.
  Need $HH_*$ and $HH^*$ to compose with $\mathsf{Perf}$ and $\mathsf{QCoh}$ via Morita invariance $HH_*(A) \cong HH_*(\mathsf{Perf}_A)$ and $HH^*(A) \cong HH^*(\mathsf{Perf}_A)$.

* In some cases (smooth proper $X$ of dimension $n$, e.g. $P^n, \operatorname{Gr}(k,n),$ smooth curves) $HH_*(X) : \mathsf{GrMod}$ and $HH^*(X) : \mathsf{GrAlg}$ are finite and explicitly computable via HKR, and must be verified.
  For general $A$, $HH_*$ and $HH^*$ are retained as formal (co)complexes with known graded type, not forced to compute.

Intended owners: `categories/homology/hochschild.py` (`HochschildComplex` $C(A)$, $HH_*(A)=\operatorname{Tor}^{A^e}_*$ as $\mathsf{GrMod}$ with `graded_piece(n)` and $HH^*(A)=\operatorname{Ext}^*_{A^e}$ as $\mathsf{GrAlg}$ with `graded_piece(n)`, Gerstenhaber and cup), `categories/algebras/dg_algebras.py` (`DgAlgebra` with $A^e$, $\mathsf{Bimod}$ and $\otimes^L$/$R\operatorname{Hom}$), `categories/schemes/hochschild_kostant_rosenberg.py` (HKR $HH_n(O_X) \cong \Omega^n$ and $HH^n(O_X) \cong \mathrm{PolyVect}^n$). Not a free `hochschild_homology(A)` — `A.hochschild_complex() : Ch`, `A.hochschild_homology() : GrMod` with `A.hochschild_homology().graded_piece(n)` and `A.hochschild_cohomology() : GrAlg` with `A.hochschild_cohomology().graded_piece(n)`, with `Perf(X).hh()` and `Perf(X).hh_cohomology()` for schemes; do not add `hochschild_homology(n)` returning bare $HH_n$ or `hochschild_cohomology(n)` returning bare $HH^n$.

## Desired capability: right-derived Hom and left-derived tensor — Ext, Tor, derived tensor products — note 2026-09-15

> One also needs basic right-derived functor machinery for (at least) R-modules, for (at least) Hom and tensor (so Ext and Tor).
> Derived tensor products are especially important

Need basic right-derived functor machinery for $R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod}$ for Hom and tensor — $\operatorname{Ext}$ and $\operatorname{Tor}$ — derived tensor products especially.

* $R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod} : \mathsf{AbCat}$ Grothendieck abelian (for $R : \mathsf{CommRings}$ or associative $R : \mathsf{Rings}$) with enough injectives and enough projectives/flats.
  Need derived category $D(R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod}) : \mathsf{TriCat}$ as Verdier localization $D = K(R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod})[\mathrm{qis}^{-1}]$ (homotopy category of complexes localized at quasi-isomorphisms), with shift $[1]$, distinguished triangles from mapping cones, and cohomology functors $H^n: D \to R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod}$.
  Interface lives on $D(R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod})$, not as a free `derived_category(R)` helper returning bare complexes.

* Right-derived Hom: $\operatorname{Hom}_R(-,-): R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod}^{\mathrm{op}} \times R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod} \to \mathsf{Ab}$ left exact in second variable; its right derivation $R\operatorname{Hom}_R: D(R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod})^{\mathrm{op}} \times D(R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod}) \to D(\mathsf{Ab})$ via injective (or $K$-injective) resolutions $M \to I^\bullet$, $R\operatorname{Hom}(M,N)=\operatorname{Hom}^\bullet(M,I^\bullet)$ (or $\operatorname{Hom}^\bullet(P^\bullet,I^\bullet)$ for unbounded).
  Then $\operatorname{Ext}^n_R(M,N) = H^n(R\operatorname{Hom}_R(M,N)) = R^n \operatorname{Hom}_R(M,N) : \mathsf{Ab}$ (and $R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod}$ when $R$ commutative), with $\operatorname{Ext}^0 = \operatorname{Hom}$, long exact sequences from $D$ triangles, and functoriality $\operatorname{Ext}^n: R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod}^{\mathrm{op}}\times R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod} \to R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod}$.
  Need `M.rhom(N) : D(Ab)` and `M.ext(n,N) : Ab` on the module objects in $D$, not a free `ext(M,N,n)` with no derived parent.
  Requires `InjectiveResolution` and `KInjective` presentation; Yoneda $\operatorname{Ext}$ via extensions is comparison, not replacement.

* Left-derived tensor: $\otimes_R: R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod} \times R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod} \to R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod}$ (or $\mathsf{Mod}\text{-}R \times R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod} \to \mathsf{Ab}$) right exact; its left derivation $\otimes^L_R: D(R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod}) \times D(R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod}) \to D(R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod})$ (resp.
  $D(\mathsf{Mod}\text{-}R)\times D(R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod})\to D(\mathsf{Ab})$) via flat / projective / $K$-flat resolutions $P^\bullet \to M$, $M\otimes^L N = P^\bullet\otimes N$ total complex.
  Then $\operatorname{Tor}^R_n(M,N) = H^{-n}(M\otimes^L N) = L_n(\otimes)(M,N) : \mathsf{Ab}$ (homological grading $\operatorname{Tor}_n = H_n$), with $\operatorname{Tor}_0 = \otimes$, balanced via either argument's resolution, long exact sequences, and base-change compatibility.
  Need `M.derived_tensor(N) : D(R-Mod)` object $M\otimes^L_R N$ and `M.tor(n,N) : Ab` on the module objects, with $\otimes^L$ as bifunctor on $D$, not a bare `tor(M,N)`.

* Derived tensor products especially: need $\otimes^L$ as symmetric monoidal structure on $D(R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod})$ when $R$ commutative (unit $R$, associator, braiding from complexes), internal Hom $R\operatorname{Hom}$ right adjoint to $\otimes^L$ ($\operatorname{Hom}_D(L\otimes^L M,N) \cong \operatorname{Hom}_D(L,R\operatorname{Hom}(M,N))$), projection formula and base-change for $R\to S$, and for schemes $L f^*$ / $\otimes^L_{O_X}$ on $D(\mathsf{QCoh}(X))$.
  All $HH(A)=A\otimes^L_{A^e} A$ and $R\Gamma$, $R f_*$ compatibility above must compose through this same $\otimes^L$/$R\operatorname{Hom}$ machinery, not a parallel `hochschild_tensor`.

* In concrete computable cases (finite projective resolutions over $ZZ$, $k[x]$, $k[x]/(x^n)$, PID) $\operatorname{Ext}$ and $\operatorname{Tor}$ must be explicitly computable via the resolution and return owned modules with presentation and class maps, and verify $\operatorname{Ext}^1(Z/n, Z/m)=Z/\gcd(n,m)$ etc. For general $R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod}$, $\operatorname{Ext}$/$\operatorname{Tor}$ retained as formal derived objects with known type.

Intended owners: `categories/derived/derived_category.py` ($D(R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod})$ with $\mathrm{qis}$ localization, $H^n$), `categories/homological/ext_tor.py` ($R\operatorname{Hom}$, $\operatorname{Ext}^n = R^n \operatorname{Hom}$, $\otimes^L$, $\operatorname{Tor}_n = L_n(\otimes)$ via $K$-injective/$K$-flat resolutions), `categories/derived/tensor_product.py` (`derived_tensor_product` as monoidal on $D$). Not a free `Ext(M,N)` or `Tor(M,N)` — `M.rhom(N)`, `M.ext(n,N)`, `M.derived_tensor(N)` / `M.tensor_L(N)` on the module/complex objects in $D(R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod})$, with $D(\mathsf{Ab})$ / $D(R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod})$ as the ambient.

## Intake: https://www-fourier.univ-grenoble-alpes.fr/~sergerar/Kenzo/kenzo-demo.html — functionality — 2026-09-15 — verbatim

Kenzo (Sergeraert et al., EAT$\to$Kenzo, incl.
Dousson, Romero, Siret) implements Constructive Algebraic Topology via effective homology.
Demo page http://www-fourier.univ-grenoble-alpes.fr/~sergerar/Kenzo/kenzo-demo.html shows three computations from actual Lisp listing on PC Isabelle (GDR Medicis), with file-list 36 modules: classes, macros, chain-complexes, effective-homology, homology-groups, cones, tensor-products, coalgebras, cobar, algebras, bar, simplicial-sets/mrphs, suspensions, disk-pasting, cartesian-products, eilenberg-zilber, kan, simplicial-groups, fibrations, loop-spaces, classifying-spaces, k-pi-n, serre, whitehead, etc.

* $H5(\Omega^3(\operatorname{Moore}(Z/2,4))) = Z/2^5$ — Moore space $\operatorname{Moore}(Z/2,4)$ as simplicial set `m` via `(moore 2 4)`, then `o3m = loop-space(m,3)` as simplicial group `[K30 Simplicial-Group]`, then `(homology o3m 5)` computes boundary matrices in dimensions 5 (rank 23) and 6 (rank 53) via effective homology $\to$ 5 copies $Z/2$ in 2 minutes.

* $H5(\Omega\Omega(S^3 \cup_2 D^3)) = Z + Z/2^6$ — `s3 = sphere 3` $\to$ `os3 = loop-space(s3)` $\to$ attach 3-disk $D^3 = \Delta^3$ with faces 0,2 identified to fundamental simplex of loop space, faces 1,3 collapsed $\to$ space $S^3 \cup_2 D^3$ (attach by degree 2), then double loop $\Omega\Omega(\ldots)$ and `(homology ... 5)` gives $Z + Z/2^6$ in 40 seconds.

* $\pi7(P\infty R/P2R) = Z/2 + Z/4$ — $P\infty R$ has cellular model with one cell $E_n$ each dimension, `P3 = r-proj-space 3` as $P\infty R$ collapsed 2-skeleton; verify $H_*(P3)$ to dim 9 then iterative Postnikov/Whitehead fibrations: `ch5 = chml-clss(x4,5)` cohomology class degree 5 $\to$ `f5 = z2-whitehead(x4,ch5)` fibration `[K249 Fibration]` $\to$ `x6 = fibration-total(f5)` $\to$ `(homology x6 6)` gives $\pi6(P3)=Z/2$ in 1s, repeat to `x?` with `ch6` etc. to $\pi7 = Z/2+Z/4$ in 20 hours total; also examples $\pi4(\Sigma K(A4,1)) = Z/12$ vs literature $Z/4$ (Mikhailov-Wu correction) and generalizations listed in Kenzo overview.

What generalizes: hard code is choice of spaces ($\operatorname{Moore}(Z/2,4)$, $S^3\cup_2 D^3$, $P\infty R/P2R$); method is general effective homology for any locally effective simplicial set.

## How Kenzo generalizes — what is new vs intake — verbatim — 2026-09-15

Hard codes are data (particular spaces), not method.
Existing intake already has: CW complexes $K = (K^0\subset K^1\subset\ldots)$ with attaching maps $\varphi_\alpha: S^{n-1}\to K^{n-1}$ and cellular $C_*(K)$ finite effective chain complex; static DB $\pi_{n+k}(S^n)$ (Hopf $\eta,\nu,\sigma$, Whitehead); $ZZ^n\text{-}\mathsf{GrMod}$ complexes/double/SerreSS for finite sparse $E2$; computable $\pi_1(X,x)=\pi_1(K)$ via van Kampen on 2-skeleton and $H_*$ via $C_*$ for concrete varieties via analytification.
None of that computes homology of *infinite* complexes such as $\Omega^3 \operatorname{Moore}$, $\Omega^n X$, $K(\pi,n)$, $X^I$, or arbitrary fibrations — $C_*(\Omega^3 \operatorname{Moore})$ has infinitely many non-degenerate simplices in degree 5 (rank 23 after reduction vs. infinite before).

What Kenzo does that is new and belongs in preamble:

**1. Effective homology as object** — not method.
Object with effective homology is triple $(X, \mathrm{EC}, \varepsilon)$ where $X : \mathsf{sSet}$ (locally effective simplicial set: each $X_n : \mathsf{Sets}$ and face/degeneracy are computable), $C_*(X) : \mathsf{Ch}_{ZZ}$ its normalized chain complex (possibly infinite type), $\mathrm{EC} : \mathsf{Ch}_{ZZ}$ effective ($\mathrm{EC}_n$ finitely generated free finite rank in each degree with computable differential/boundary matrices), and $\varepsilon : C_*(X) \Leftrightarrow \mathrm{EC}$ strong chain equivalence — span of two reductions $C_*(X) \Leftarrow \hat{C} \Rightarrow \mathrm{EC}$.
Reduction $\rho = (f,g,h)$ with $f: C\to D$, $g: D\to C$, $h: C\to C_{+1}$ satisfying $fg=\mathrm{id}_D$, $gf + dh + hd = \mathrm{id}_C$, $fh=0$, $hg=0$, $hh=0$.
Perturbation lemmas (Basic BPL, Easy EPL) transfer reductions along twisting cochains $t: C\to A$ or differentials $\delta$.
This is the category $\mathsf{EffHom}_{\mathsf{sSet}}$ with forgetful $U: \mathsf{EffHom}\to \mathsf{sSet}$ and $\mathrm{EC}: \mathsf{EffHom}\to \mathsf{Ch}^{\text{eff}}$.
Intake has no such object; it has only finite CW $C_*$.

**2. Simplicial Kan model** — $\mathsf{sSet}$ with `Kan` axiom (horn fillers) via simplicial groups $G(X)=\text{Kan loop group}$ and $\bar{W}(G)$ classifying space.
Functors $G: \mathsf{sSet}_* \to \mathsf{sGrp}$ and $\bar{W}: \mathsf{sGrp} \to \mathsf{sSet}_*$ with $G \dashv \bar{W}$ and $\Omega|X| \simeq |G X|$ geometric.
Operations `suspension` $\Sigma$, `cone`, `disk-pasting`, `cartesian-product` $X\times Y$ with Eilenberg-Zilber reduction $C_*(X\times Y) \Leftrightarrow C_*(X)\otimes C_*(Y)$ and twisted Eilenberg-Zilber for twisted cartesian product $E = F \times_\tau B$ (fibration with twisting operator $\tau: B_{n+1}\to G_n$ satisfying Kan condition).
File modules 16-34 own this.
Intake has $\mathsf{Top}$ CW colimits but not $\mathsf{sSet}$ Kan or $\tau$.

**3. Fibrations with effective homology** — Serre fibration $F \hookrightarrow E \to B$ as $E = F \times_\tau B$ with structure group $G$ acting on $F$; chain level $C_*(E) \cong C_*(F) \otimes_t C_*(B)$ twisted tensor product with differential $d = d_F\otimes 1+1\otimes d_B + \text{perturbation } \delta_\tau$.
Effective homology of base $B$ and fiber $F$ $\to$ effective homology of total $E$ via twisted Eilenberg-Zilber + BPL (Kenzo `fibration-total` object with its `efhm`). Intake mentions Serre SS only as $E2=H_*(B)\otimes H_*(F) \Rightarrow H_*(E)$ finite/sparse determinable; Kenzo's is constructive cycle-level reduction, not spectral sequence table.

**4. Loop/classifying effective homology** — $\Omega X = G X$ loop space as simplicial group (Kan) with cobar construction at chain level: $C_*(\Omega X)$ effective via $\Omega C_*(X)$ cobar on coalgebra $C_*(X)$ (modules 12-15 bar/cobar, coalgebras).
Iterated loops $\Omega^n X$ require iterated cobar with effective homology at each step — demo's $\Omega^3 \operatorname{Moore}(Z/2,4)$ is this iteration.
Classifying $B G = \bar{W} G$ similarly via bar construction.
No finite CW model exists for $\Omega^n X$ in general; effective homology is the only computable model.

**5. Eilenberg-MacLane effective homology** — $K(\pi,n)$ via `k-pi-n` module: Dold-Kan + bar construction inductively, with $\mathrm{EC}$ effective and Smith normal form `smith` for homology invariants $H_q(K(\pi,n))$.
Prerequisite for Postnikov k-invariants and for $H_{*}$ of EM spaces in towers.

**6. Whitehead/Postnikov tower for $\pi_n$** — For simply connected finite $X : \mathsf{sSet}$ with effective homology, Kenzo builds Whitehead tower $\cdots\to X_{n+1}\to X_n\to\cdots\to X_1=X$ where $X_{n+1} = \text{homotopy fiber of k-invariant } \kappa_n \in H^{n+1}(X_n; \pi_n(X))$ classified by `k-pi-n` twisting.
Each $X_n$ gets effective homology via fibration 3; then $\pi_n(X) = H_n(X_n)$ as $\operatorname{Ext}$?
