  Correspondence $(E,\varphi) \leftrightarrow (E,\nabla=\partial_E+\varphi+\ldots) \leftrightarrow \rho$ via harmonic metric and $D''= \bar\partial_E+\varphi$.
  Interface same moduli functors already intaken via $M_B$, plus $M_{Dol}, M_{dR}$ as $\mathsf{Sch}$ with same `GITQuotient` and `HiggsField` as morphism $\varphi$.

* Operationalize Riemann-Roch computations, index theorems (Atiyah-Singer, Gauss-Bonnet): RR is not $\chi(E)=\int\operatorname{ch}(E)\operatorname{Todd}$.
  It is the equality of $K$-theory pushforward $f_!: K_0(X)\to K_0(Y)$ (for proper $f: X\to Y$) with cohomological $\operatorname{ch}(f_!E)=f_*(\operatorname{ch}(E)\cdot\operatorname{Todd}(T_f))$ as Chow/cohomology pushforward (Grothendieck-RR), specialization to $X\to\operatorname{Spec} k$ gives $\chi(X,E)=\int_X \operatorname{ch}(E) \operatorname{Todd}(T_X)$ as $\mathsf{Ab}$ integer.
  Operationalize as `X.riemann_roch(E)` $\to\chi$ via $K_0$ object $E: K_0(X)$ and $\operatorname{ch}: K_0\to A^*(X)_Q$ (Chow) and $\operatorname{Todd}: \mathsf{Vect}\to A^*$.
  Atiyah-Singer index: for elliptic differential operator $D: \Gamma(E)\to\Gamma(F)$ on compact oriented Riemannian $X : \mathsf{RiemMfd}$ with symbol $\sigma(D): \pi^*E\to\pi^*F$ over $T^*X\setminus 0$, analytic index $\operatorname{ind}(D)=\dim\ker D - \dim\operatorname{coker} D : ZZ$ equals topological $\operatorname{ind}_t(\sigma(D))=\int_{T^*X} \operatorname{ch}([\sigma])\cdot\operatorname{Todd}(T_X\otimes C)$.
  Gauss-Bonnet is specialization $D=d+d^*$ or Euler $\chi(X)=\int_X \operatorname{Pf}(\Omega/2\pi) = \langle e(T_X),[X]\rangle$.
  Interface `D.analytic_index() -> ZZ`, `sigma(D).topological_index() -> ZZ` with `D=symbol_map(D)` agreement testable when $D$ is `DiffOp` object of `PDiffOp(X;E,F)` with $\sigma(D): \operatorname{Sym}(T^*X)\to\operatorname{Hom}(E,F)$ polynomial symbol.

* Compute full Poincaré series as OGFs (formal power series) and thus extra Betti numbers: for $X : \mathsf{Top}$ with $b_i=\dim H_i(X;Q)$ (or $b_i(X;F_p)$), $P_X(t)=\sum_{i\geq 0} b_i t^i : ZZ[[t]]$ as `OGF` object of `FormalPowerSeries(ZZ)` = (t)-adic completion $ZZ[[t]]=\lim Z[t]/(t^n)$ already intaken via $R[[x]]$ and $(x)$-adic, not a list $[b_0,\ldots]$.
  Extra Betti via $P_X(t)= (1-t)^{-?}$ rational when $X$ fibration or Koszul.
  Interface `X.poincare_series() -> P_X : ZZ[[t]]` with $b_i = [t^i]P$ and $\chi= P(-1)$.

* Operationalize calculus with Chern classes and Chern characters: for $E : \mathsf{VecBun}(X)$ rank $r$, total Chern $c(E)=1+c_1(E)+\ldots+c_r(E) : A^*(X)$ with $c_i\in A^i$, Whitney $c(E\oplus F)=c(E)c(F)$, $c(L)=1+c_1(L)$ for line $L$ with $c_1: \operatorname{Pic}(X)\to A^1$ via `divisor`. Chern character $\operatorname{ch}(E)= \sum \exp(x_i)= r + c_1 + (c_1^2-2c_2)/2 + \ldots : A^*(X)_Q$ with $x_i$ Chern roots via splitting $\pi: \operatorname{Fl}(E)\to X$ with $\pi^*E = \bigoplus L_i$, $\operatorname{ch}: K_0(X)\to A^*(X)_Q$ ring homomorphism $\operatorname{ch}(E\otimes F)=\operatorname{ch}(E)\operatorname{ch}(F)$.
  Interface `E.chern_class(i) -> c_i : Chow`, `E.chern_character() -> ch : Chow_Q` as $\mathsf{GrMod}$ graded, with $c(E\oplus F)$ relation test.

* Get $H^*(X;ZZ)/\operatorname{tors}$ for at least 4-manifolds, as graded group but ideally ring/$ZZ$-algebra, and thus check homeomorphism via Freedman: for closed simply-connected topological 4-manifold $X : \mathsf{Top4Mfd}$, $H_2(X;ZZ)\cong ZZ^{b_2}\oplus\operatorname{Tors}$ is free when simply-connected (by $H_1=0$, $\operatorname{Tors} H^2\cong\operatorname{Tors} H_1=0$), intersection form $Q_X: H_2\times H_2\to ZZ$ via $(a,b)\mapsto\langle a\cup b,[X]\rangle$ as symmetric unimodular bilinear $Q_X : \mathsf{Lattices}$ ($\det=\pm 1$ by Poincaré duality), with parity `even` iff $w_2(X)=0$ (spin) else `odd`. $H^*(X;ZZ)/\operatorname{tors} \cong ZZ \oplus 0 \oplus H^2 \oplus 0 \oplus ZZ$ as $\mathsf{GrAlg}$ with $\cup: H^2\times H^2\to H^4\cong ZZ$ equal to $Q_X$.
  Freedman theorem: homeomorphism class of simply-connected closed topological 4-manifold is determined by $Q_X$ (isometry class) and $\operatorname{ks}(X)\in Z/2$ (Kirby-Siebenmann, zero when smoothable).
  Interface `X.intersection_lattice() -> Q_X : Lattices` and `X.cohomology_ring_mod_tors() -> H^*/tors : GrAlg` with $\cup$ product via $C^*(X)$ effective `EC` cup product $\smile: C^p\otimes C^q\to C^{p+q}$ on `EC` cochains.

* Decide when smooth manifold is spinnable via $w_2$ and refinements: $X$ closed $n$-manifold $X : \mathsf{SmMfd}$ is spin $\Leftrightarrow$ $w_2(TX)=0 \in H^2(X;F_2)$ via $w_2$ universal from $B\mathrm{SO}_n$ ($w: B\mathrm{SO}_n\to K(Z/2,2)$), plus when $w_2\neq 0$ distinction $\mathrm{spin}^c$.
  Rohlin: for smooth closed spin 4-manifold $\sigma(X)\equiv 0 \bmod 16$ where $\sigma=b^+_2 - b^-_2$ signature of $Q_X$; contrapositive $\sigma\neq 0 \bmod 16 \Rightarrow \text{not spin}$.
  Kervaire–Milnor on exotic spheres $\Theta_n$ via $bP_{n+1}$ etc.; Freedman–Kirby on topological spin with $\operatorname{ks}$ obstruction for smoothing: closed topological 4-manifold smoothable iff $\operatorname{ks}(X)=0$ after correcting $\sigma$ parity.
  Interface `X.is_spin() -> Bool` via `w_2(X).is_zero() : H^2(F_2)`, `X.rohlin_invariant() -> sigma mod16`, `X.kervaire_obstruction()`.

* Arf invariant, Kirby–Siebenmann invariant, $\hat{A}$ genus: Arf $\operatorname{Arf}(q)\in Z/2$ for nondegenerate quadratic refinement $q: H^{2k+1}(M;F_2)\to Z/2$ with $q(x+y)=q(x)+q(y)+\langle x\cup y,[M]\rangle$, as $\operatorname{Arf}(H^{2k+1},q)$ for framed $(4k+1)$-manifold or characteristic $\Sigma^{2k+1}$.
  $\operatorname{ks}: \mathsf{Top4Mfd}\to Z/2$ Kirby–Siebenmann as $\operatorname{ks}(X)\in H^4(X;Z/2)\cong Z/2$ obstruction to PL structure, with $\sigma/8 \equiv \operatorname{ks} \bmod 2$ for spin with boundary.
  $\hat{A}$ genus $\hat{A}(X)=\langle\hat A(TX),[X]\rangle : Q$ with $\hat A = \prod x_i/2 / \sinh(x_i/2)$ where $x_i$ formal Pontryagin roots ($p_i = \sigma_i(x_j^2)$), as $Q$-number via $\operatorname{Todd}$-like class, zero for positive scalar curvature on spin via Lichnerowicz.
  Interface `E.arf_invariant()`, `X.kirby_siebenmann()`, `X.a_hat_genus()`.

* Enough for Hirzebruch–Riemann–Roch and Chern–Gauss–Bonnet: HRR for $X$ smooth projective $n$-fold and $E : \mathsf{VecBun}(X)$ is $\chi(X,E)=\int_X \operatorname{ch}(E)\cdot\operatorname{Todd}(T_X)$ with $\operatorname{Todd}(T_X)=\prod x_i/(1-e^{-x_i}) : A^*(X)_Q$ (Todd class of tangent), where $x_i$ Chern roots of $T_X$; specialization to $X$ curve $\chi=\deg E + \operatorname{rank}(1-g)$ etc. CGB for $X$ closed oriented $2n$-manifold $\chi(X)=\langle e(T_X),[X]\rangle = (1/(2\pi)^n)\int_X \operatorname{Pf}(\Omega)$ where $e(T_X)=c_n(T_X\otimes C)$ top Chern as Euler class, $\Omega$ curvature of Levi-Civita.
  Both as derived from $\operatorname{ch}$/$\operatorname{Todd}$/$e$ calculus plus $\int_X : A^{\dim X}\to ZZ$ via fundamental class $[X] : H_{\dim X}$.
  Interface `X.hirzebruch_riemann_roch(E)` and `X.chern_gauss_bonnet()` with numerical $\chi$ via $R\Gamma$.

* Differential operators and symbol map, and their index maps: $\operatorname{DiffOp}^m(X;E,F)$ order $m$ $P: \Gamma(E)\to\Gamma(F)$ with local expression $P= \sum_{|\alpha|\leq m} a_\alpha(x) D^\alpha$, principal symbol $\sigma_m(P): T^*X\setminus 0 \to \operatorname{Hom}(\pi^*E,\pi^*F)$ homogeneous degree $m$ via $\sigma_m(P)(x,\xi)= \sum_{|\alpha|=m} a_\alpha(x) \xi^\alpha : \operatorname{Sym}^m(T^*X)\otimes\operatorname{Hom}(E,F)$ as element $\sigma(P)\in\Gamma(T^*X, \operatorname{Hom}(\pi^*E,\pi^*F))$.
  Symbol exact sequence $0\to\operatorname{Diff}^{m-1}\to\operatorname{Diff}^m \to \operatorname{Sym}^m(T^*X)\otimes\operatorname{Hom}\to 0$.
  Index maps $\operatorname{ind}: [\sigma]\in K^0(T^*X)\to ZZ$ topological via $\operatorname{ch}([\sigma])\cdot\operatorname{Todd}$ integral, matching $\operatorname{ind}(D)$.
  Interface `P.symbol() -> sigma(P) : Sym` with `is_elliptic` $\Leftrightarrow \sigma(P)(x,\xi) \text{ invertible } \forall\xi\neq 0$, `P.index_map()`.

* Todd class $\operatorname{Todd}(E)=\prod x_i/(1-e^{-x_i})$ already for RR, similarly $L$-class, $\hat{A}$-class.

* Operational $G$-equivariant cohomology and $K$ theory: for $G : \mathsf{Groups}$ (compact Lie $S^1, \mathrm{SU}(n)$, finite), $X : G\text{-}\mathsf{Spaces}$ with $G$-action $G\times X\to X$, Borel construction $X_G = (EG\times X)/G : \mathsf{HoTop}$ with fibration $X\to X_G\to BG$, $H^*_G(X;R)=H^*(X_G;R) : \mathsf{GrMod}_R$ with $H^*_G(\mathrm{pt})=H^*(BG)$, and $K^0_G(X)=K_G(X)$ equivariant $K$ via $G$-vector bundles $\mathsf{Vec}_G(X)$ Grothendieck $K_G(X)=K_0(\mathsf{Vec}_G(X))$ with $K_G(\mathrm{pt})=R(G)$ representation ring.
  Interface `X.equivariant_cohomology(G,R) -> H^*_G : GrMod` via `EC(EG x X)/G` effective, `X.equivariant_k_theory(G) -> K_G` with $G$-bundle $E$.

* Exterior derivatives, Hodge Laplacians, Hodge star: on oriented Riemannian $X : \mathsf{RiemMfd}$ $n$-dim with metric $g$, volume $\mathrm{vol}$, Hodge $\star: \Omega^k_X\to\Omega^{n-k}_X$ via $\alpha\wedge\star\beta = \langle\alpha,\beta\rangle_g \mathrm{vol}$, exterior $d: \Omega^k\to\Omega^{k+1}$ with $d^2=0$, codifferential $\delta = (-1)^{nk+n+1}\star d\star: \Omega^k\to\Omega^{k-1}$, Laplacian $\Delta = d\delta+\delta d: \Omega^k\to\Omega^k$ self-adjoint elliptic, harmonic $H^k = \ker \Delta \cong H^k_{dR}$ via Hodge.
  Interface `Omega^k(X).hodge_star() -> Omega^{n-k}`, `Omega^k.exterior_derivative() -> Omega^{k+1}`, $\Delta$ as $\operatorname{DiffOp}^2$.

* Known cobordism rings operational and cobordant detection: $MO_* = \pi_*(MO) = F_2[x_i \mid i\neq 2^k-1]$ unoriented, $MU_* = ZZ[x_1,x_2,\ldots]$ $|x_i|=2i$ complex (Milnor), $M\mathrm{SO}_*$, $M\mathrm{Spin}_*$, $M\mathrm{SU}$, $M\mathrm{String}$, etc. as $E_\infty$ ring spectra $MO=\operatorname{Thom}(O)$, $MU=\operatorname{Thom}(U)$ with $\pi_*(MO)=\Omega^{un}_*$ etc. Ring $\Omega^{un}_*$ via Stiefel-Whitney numbers $w_{I}(M)=\langle\prod w_{i_k}(TM),[M]\rangle\in F_2$, $M$ null-cobordant iff all $w_I=0$; $\Omega^{SO}_*\otimes Q = Q[\text{Pontryagin numbers}]$ etc. Detection `M.is_cobordant(N)` $\Leftrightarrow [M]=[N]\in\Omega_* \Leftrightarrow \text{invariants equal}$ (Pontryagin/Stiefel-Whitney numbers, $\hat{A}$, $\sigma$). Interface `M.cobordism_class() -> [M] : MO_*/MU_*` with `StiefelWhitneyNumbers`, `PontryaginNumbers`.

* Cobordism categories to define TQFTs as functors: $\mathsf{Bord}_n$ objects $M^{n-1}$ closed oriented $(n-1)$-manifolds, morphisms $W: M_0\to M_1$ as $n$-bordisms $W$ with $\partial W = M_0\sqcup\bar M_1$ up to diffeomorphism, monoidal $\coprod$, symmetric via $M\amalg N \cong N\amalg M$.
  $n=1$ is intervals/circles, $n=2$ pair-of-pants.
  Extended $\mathsf{Bord}_{n,m}$ with corners.
  TQFT is symmetric monoidal functor $Z: \mathsf{Bord}_n \to \mathsf{Vect}_k$ (or $\mathsf{Ch}, \mathsf{Sp}$) with $Z(M\amalg N)=Z(M)\otimes Z(N)$, $Z(\emptyset)=k$, $Z(W): Z(M_0)\to Z(M_1)$ linear.
  Requires $\mathsf{Bord}_n$ as $\infty$-category $\mathsf{Bord}_n : \mathsf{Cat}$ with $\mathsf{Bord}_n(M_0,M_1) : \mathsf{Spaces}$ via moduli of bordisms.

* Normal bundles of immersions and embeddings: for immersion $f: M^n \looparrowright N^{n+k}$ with $df: TM\to TN$ injective bundle map, normal bundle $\nu_f = f^*TN / df(TM) : \mathsf{VecBun}(M)$ rank $k$ with $f^*TN \cong TM\oplus\nu_f$ as $\mathsf{Vect}$; for embedding $i: M\hookrightarrow N$ same with $\nu_i$ astubular neighborhood $\nu_i \cong$ neighborhood $U\subset N$ via exponential $\exp: \nu_i \to N$.
  For $M\subset R^{n+k}$ standard, $\nu$ classifies immersion up to regular homotopy via Hirsch-Smale $\operatorname{Imm}(M,N)\simeq \operatorname{Mono}(TM,TN)$.
  Self-intersection via $e(\nu)$ Euler.

Intended owners: `categories/group/cohomology.py` (`GroupCohomology` $H^*(G,M)$ via `BarRes -> Ext`, $\text{Tate } \hat{H}^*$, `transfer`, `norm`, `Ind/Res` as $D(ZZ[G])$ adjoints), `categories/group/surface_groups.py` (`SurfaceGroup(g)` $\to \pi_1\Sigma_g$ framed $\langle a_i,b_i\mid\prod[a_i,b_i]\rangle$), `categories/group/group_schemes.py` + `categories/group/lie_groups.py` (`AlgGroup`/`LieGroup`/`GrpSch` with `Lie`), `categories/higgs/nonabelian_hodge.py` (`HiggsBundle(E,phi)` semisimple, $M_{Dol}/M_{dR}/M_B$ with `harmonic_metric`), `categories/index_theory/` (`RiemannRoch`, `AtiyahSingerIndex` $\sigma\mapsto\operatorname{ind}$, `GaussBonnet` $\chi=\int e$, `HirzebruchRR` $\chi=\int\operatorname{ch}\cdot\operatorname{Todd}$, `ChernGaussBonnet`), `categories/characteristic_classes/chern.py` (`chern_class`, `chern_character`, `Todd`, `PoincareSeries` $P_X(t)\in ZZ[[t]]$ as $(t)$-adic `OGF`), `categories/topology/four_manifolds.py` (`IntersectionLattice` $Q_X$, `CohomologyRingModTors` $H^*/\operatorname{tors}$ as $\mathsf{GrAlg}_{ZZ}$, `is_homeomorphic_via_Freedman` from $Q_X,\operatorname{ks}$), `categories/topology/spinnability.py` (`is_spin` via $w_2$, $\text{Rohlin } \sigma \bmod 16$, `Kervaire-Milnor`, `Freedman-Kirby ks` $\to$ `Arf`, `KirbySiebenmann`, `a_hat_genus`), `categories/differential_operators/` (`DiffOp`, `principal_symbol`, `index_map`), `categories/equivariant/` ($\text{Borel } H^*_G$, $K_G$), `categories/hodge/hodge_star.py` ($d$, $\star$, $\delta$, $\Delta$), `categories/cobordism/rings.py` ($MO_*$, $MU_*$, $M\mathrm{SO}_*$, `cobordant(M,N)` via numbers), `categories/cobordism/bordism_category.py` ($\mathsf{Bord}_n$ $n$-category, $\mathrm{TQFT}: \mathsf{Bord}_n\to\mathsf{Vect}$ functor), `categories/topology/normal_bundles.py` (`NormalBundle(immersion)` $\to \nu_f$ with $f^*TN\cong TM\oplus\nu$). Not free `group_cohomology(G,M)` list or `chernClass(E)` bare number — `G.group_cohomology(n,M)` via `BarRes` $D(ZZ[G])$, `E.chern_class(i)` as $\mathsf{Chow}$ graded, `M.normal_bundle()` as $\mathsf{VecBun}$ with $\nu$ as quotient/ tubular.

## Desired capability: almost complex structures, integral structure, Nijenhuis tensor, curvature tensors — note 2026-09-15

One needs almost complex structures, integral such structure, the Nijenhuis tensor, Ricci/gaussian/scalar curvature tensors, etc.

* Almost complex structure is not a matrix $J$ with $J^2=-1$ as bare $\operatorname{Mat}_{2n}(R)$.
  For $M : \mathsf{SmMfd}$ real $2n$-manifold with $TM : \mathsf{VecBun}_R(M)$ rank $2n$, $J: TM\to TM$ is $J \in \operatorname{End}(TM)$ as $\mathsf{VecBun}$ endomorphism with $J^2 = -\mathrm{id}_{TM}$ as morphism $J\circ J = -\mathrm{id}$ in $\operatorname{End}(TM)$ ($J_x: T_xM \to T_xM$, $J_x^2=-1$ pointwise).
  Equivalently $G$-structure reduction of frame bundle $\operatorname{Fr}(TM)$ from $\mathrm{GL}_{2n}(R)$ to $\mathrm{GL}_n(C)$ via $J$.
  Automorphism $J$ makes each $T_xM$ a $C$-vector space ($(a+ib)\cdot v = a v + b J(v)$). Interface `M.almost_complex_structure() -> J : End(TM)` with `J.is_almost_complex()` $J^2=-\mathrm{id}$ test as $\operatorname{End}$ morphism.

* Integral structure (integrable) means $J$ comes from holomorphic atlas $M : \mathsf{ComplexMfd}$.
  Newlander-Nirenberg: $J$ integrable iff Nijenhuis tensor $N_J =0$.
  Also $J$ integrable iff $T^{0,1} = \ker(J+i) \subset TM\otimes C$ is involutive $[T^{0,1}, T^{0,1}]\subset T^{0,1}$.

* Nijenhuis tensor $N_J: \Lambda^2 TM \to TM$ as $\mathsf{VecBun}$ morphism $N_J(X,Y)= [JX,JY] - J[JX,Y] - J[X,JY] - [X,Y]$ where $X,Y \in \Gamma(TM)$ vector fields, $[-,-]$ Lie bracket of fields, $JX \in \Gamma(TM)$.
  $N_J$ is tensorial ($C^\infty(M)$-linear in $X,Y$) despite appearance, and $N_J\in\Gamma(\Lambda^2 T^*M\otimes TM)$ with $N_J=0$ iff $J$ integrable.
  More generally for $M$ with $J$, $N_J$ measures failure of $J$ to be integrable; interface `J.nijenhuis_tensor() -> N_J : Hom(Lambda^2 TM, TM)` and `J.is_integrable() <=> N_J==0` as morphism zero.

* Curvature tensors all derive from a connection $\nabla$ on $TM$ (Levi-Civita $\nabla^{LC}$ for $g$ metric, or Chern $\nabla^{Ch}$ for Hermitian $J$-compatible).
  For $R : \mathsf{RiemMfd}$ with $g$, Levi-Civita is unique torsion-free metric-compatible $\nabla: \Gamma(TM)\to\Gamma(T^*\otimes TM)$ with $\Gamma^k_{ij}$ Christoffel and $R(X,Y)Z = \nabla_X\nabla_Y Z - \nabla_Y\nabla_X Z - \nabla_{[X,Y]}Z$ as $R \in \Gamma(\Lambda^2 T^*\otimes\operatorname{End}(TM))$ Riemann $(4,0)$ via $R(W,Z,X,Y)=g(R(X,Y)Z,W)$.
  Ricci $\operatorname{Ric}(Y,Z)= \operatorname{tr}(X\mapsto R(X,Y)Z) \in \Gamma(\operatorname{Sym}^2 T^*)$ as contraction $\operatorname{Ric}_{jk}=R^i_{ijk}$, scalar $S = \operatorname{tr}_g \operatorname{Ric} = g^{jk}\operatorname{Ric}_{jk} : C^\infty(M)$ function, sectional $K(\sigma)= R(X,Y,Y,X)/(|X|^2|Y|^2-\langle X,Y\rangle^2)$ for 2-plane $\sigma=\operatorname{span}\{X,Y\}$, Gaussian $K = S/2$ when $\dim=2$ via $K = \det(\text{Weingarten})$ for surface $\Sigma\subset R^3$ vs intrinsic $K = R_{1212}/\det g$.
  Gaussian curvature already intaken as $K$ for surfaces via shape operator; now general $R, \operatorname{Ric}$.

* For almost Hermitian $(M,J,g)$ with $g(JX,JY)=g(X,Y)$, Ricci forms $\rho = \operatorname{Ric}(J\cdot,\cdot) \in \Omega^{1,1}$ and $\ast$-Ricci etc.; integrability links $\operatorname{Ric}$ to Chern classes $c_1(T^{1,0}) = [\operatorname{Ric}(\omega)/2\pi]$ via $\nabla^{Ch}$ when $J$ integrable Kähler ($\nabla^{LC}J=0$ $\Leftrightarrow$ Kähler with $\omega=g(J\cdot,\cdot)$ closed $d\omega=0$). Need operators $\nabla$, $R$, $\operatorname{Ric}$, $S$, $N_J$ as morphisms $J : \operatorname{End}(TM)$, $N_J : \operatorname{Hom}(\Lambda^2TM,TM)$, $R : \operatorname{Hom}(\Lambda^2TM\otimes TM,TM)$ / $R \in \Omega^2(\operatorname{End})$, $\operatorname{Ric} \in \operatorname{Sym}^2$, $S \in C^\infty$ on the manifold object $M : \mathsf{SmMfd}$ with $TM : \mathsf{VecBun}_R(M)$ ($TM = T_{M/R}$), not as free `curvature(matrix)` lists.

Intended owners: `categories/geometry/almost_complex.py` (`AlmostComplexStructure` $J: TM\to TM$ with $J^2=-\mathrm{id}$, `is_almost_complex`, `is_integrable via N_J==0`), `categories/geometry/complex_structures.py` (`NijenhuisTensor` $N_J : \Lambda^2TM\to TM$ with $N_J(X,Y)=[JX,JY]-J[JX,Y]-J[X,JY]-[X,Y]$, `NewlanderNirenberg`), `categories/geometry/curvature.py` (`RiemannCurvature` $R\in\Gamma(\Lambda^2\otimes\operatorname{End})$, `Ricci` $\operatorname{Ric}=\operatorname{tr} R$, `Scalar` $S=\operatorname{tr}_g \operatorname{Ric}$, `Sectional` $K(\sigma)$, `Gaussian` $K=S/2$ in 2d as $\det II$, Levi-Civita $\nabla^{LC}$ as $\mathsf{Conn}(TM)$), `categories/geometry/kahler.py` (`KahlerStructure` $\omega=g(J\cdot,\cdot)$ closed, `ChernConnection`). Not a free `nijenhuis(J)` matrix or `ricci(metric)` list — `J.nijenhuis_tensor()` and `g.riemann_tensor()`, `g.ricci()`, `g.scalar_curvature()` on the manifold/structure objects.

## Desired capability: flatness, moduli of flat connections, curvature, Levi-Civita, gauge group, Hermitian, covariant derivatives, gradient/Hamiltonian flows — note 2026-09-15

One needs flatness for connections, moduli of flat connections, curvature associated to a connection, Levi-Civita, the gauge group, Hermitian modules/vector spaces, hermitian metrics, the hermitian form associated to a lattice on $L\otimes_{ZZ} C$ (as opposed to its bilinear extension), covariant derivatives.
Basic gradient flow, Hamiltonians and Hamiltonian flows.

* Flatness for a connection $\nabla: E\to E\otimes\Omega^1_X$ on $X : \mathsf{Sm}$ ($X$ smooth scheme/manifold $C$), $E: \mathsf{VecBun}(X)$ rank $n$, is the curvature equation $F_\nabla = \nabla^2 =0 : E\to E\otimes\Omega^2_X$ ($F_\nabla\in\Gamma(\Omega^2_X\otimes\operatorname{End}(E))$). Curvature associated to any connection (flat or not) is $F_\nabla = \nabla\circ\nabla : E\to E\otimes\Omega^2$ as $F_\nabla(X,Y)=[\nabla_X,\nabla_Y]-\nabla_{[X,Y]} \in \operatorname{End}(E)$, i.e. $F_\nabla=dA+A\wedge A$ in trivialization $\nabla=d+A$ with $A\in\Omega^1(\operatorname{End})$.
  Bianchi $d_\nabla F_\nabla=0$.
  Flatness $F_\nabla=0$ $\Leftrightarrow$ local system $E^\nabla = \ker \nabla$ is $C$-local system rank $n$ via Riemann-Hilbert and $\nabla$ corresponds to representation $\rho: \pi_1\to\mathrm{GL}_n$.
  For line bundles, $F_\nabla=dA$ as $\Omega^2$ class $c_1(E)$ via $\operatorname{ch}$.
  Interface `nabla.curvature()` $\to F_\nabla : \operatorname{Hom}(E, E\otimes\Omega^2)$ and `nabla.is_flat() <=> F_nabla==0` as morphism, not as numeric `norm(F)<eps`.

* Levi-Civita $\nabla^{LC}$ is the unique torsion-free metric-compatible connection on $TM$ for $M : \mathsf{RiemMfd}$ with metric $g : \operatorname{Sym}^2 T^*M$ ($g$ positive-definite).
  Existence via Koszul $2g(\nabla_XY,Z)=\ldots$ Christoffel $\Gamma^k_{ij}$.
  $\nabla^{LC}$ has $T(\nabla^{LC})=0$ torsion $T(X,Y)=\nabla_XY-\nabla_YX-[X,Y]$ and $\nabla^{LC}g=0$.
  Curvature above gives $R^{LC}=F_{\nabla^{LC}}$ Riemann, $\operatorname{Ric}$, $S$ as before.
  For Kähler $(M,J,g)$ with $J$ integrable and $\omega=g(J\cdot,\cdot)$ closed $d\omega=0$, Levi-Civita coincides on $T^{1,0}$ with Chern $\nabla^{Ch}$ and $\nabla^{LC}J=0$.

* Moduli of flat connections $M_{flat} = \mathsf{FlatConn}(X)/\!/G$ as $G$-quotient of affine space $A = \{\nabla=\nabla_0 + A \mid A\in\Omega^1(\operatorname{End} E)\}$ by gauge $G$.
  More precisely $M^{ss}_{flat}= \{\nabla \text{ flat, semistable}\}/\sim$ via NAH when $X$ compact Kähler, but bare $M_{flat} = \{F_\nabla=0\}/G$ as Betti $M_B$ via $\mathrm{RH}: \mathsf{FlatConn}^{rs}\simeq\mathsf{LocSys}$ and $\mathsf{LocSys} \simeq \operatorname{Rep}(\pi_1)/\!/G$ already intaken as $M_B$.
  Need $M_{flat}$ as `GITQuotient(A,G)` stacky $M_{flat} : \mathsf{Stack}$ with $\pi: A^{ss}\to M_{flat}$ and $T_{[\nabla]}M_{flat} \cong H^1(X, \operatorname{End}(E)\otimes\ldots)$ deformation via $\operatorname{Ext}^1$ already intaken.
  Distinguish $M_{flat}$ (analytic quotient by $G=C^\infty$ gauge) vs $M_B$ (algebraic $/\!/G$).

* Gauge group $G = G(P)= \Gamma(X, \operatorname{Aut}(P))$ for principal $G$-bundle $P\to X$ ($P: \mathsf{Bun}_G(X)$) as $\operatorname{Maps}_G(P,G)$ or $\Gamma(X, \operatorname{Ad} P)$ where $\operatorname{Ad} P = P\times_G G$ via conjugation.
  For vector bundle $E$, $G = \Gamma(X, \mathrm{GL}(E)) = \operatorname{Aut}(E)$ as sheaf $G(U)=\mathrm{GL}(\Gamma(U,E))$.
  Lie algebra $\operatorname{Lie}(G)=\Omega^0(\operatorname{End} E)=\Gamma(\operatorname{End} E)$.
  Action on $A$: $g\cdot\nabla = g\nabla g^{-1}= g\circ\nabla\circ g^{-1}$ with infinitesimal $g=1+\varepsilon\varphi : \delta_\varphi A = -d_\nabla\varphi$.
  Quotient $A/G$ stack, stabilizer $\operatorname{Stab}(\nabla)=\operatorname{Aut}(\nabla)= \{g \mid g\cdot\nabla=\nabla\}=H^0(\operatorname{End} E)^{\nabla}$.

* Hermitian modules/vector spaces: $H : \mathsf{HermSpaces}$ / $\mathsf{HermModules}_R$ where $R=ZZ, RR, CC$ with Hilbert inner product.
  Object $H = (V, h)$ with $V : \mathsf{Vect}_C$ finite $n$ and $h: V\times V\to C$ sesquilinear $h(\lambda v,w)=\lambda h(v,w)$, $h(v,w)=\overline{h(w,v)}$, positive-definite $h(v,v)>0$ ($v\neq 0$). Morphisms are $C$-linear $f: V\to W$ with $h_W(fv,fw)=h_V$ when unitary else $f^*h_W = h_V$.
  For $R=ZZ$, $\mathsf{HermModules}_{ZZ}$ as lattices with Hermitian form after $\otimes C$.

* Hermitian metrics $h: E\times\bar E \to C$ on complex vector bundle $E\to X$ ($X : \mathsf{ComplexMfd} / \mathsf{SmMfd}$ with $C$-structure) as smooth family $h_x: E_x\times\bar E_x\to C$ positive Hermitian, i.e. section $h \in \Gamma(E^\vee\otimes\bar E^\vee)$ with $h= \bar h^t$.
  Chern connection $\nabla^h = \partial_h + \bar\partial_E$ uniquely $\nabla^h h=0$ and $(\nabla^h)^{0,1}= \bar\partial_E$.
  Interface `E.hermitian_metric() -> h : HermMetrics(E)` with `h.chern_connection() -> nabla^h : Conn(E)` and curvature $F_{\nabla^h} \in \Omega^{1,1}(\operatorname{End})$.

* Hermitian form associated to a lattice $L$ on $L\otimes_{ZZ} C$ (as opposed to its bilinear extension): $L : \mathsf{Lattices}$ integral lattice with bilinear $b: L\times L\to ZZ$ ($b(x,y)=x\cdot y$), e.g. even lattice $A_2$, $E_8$, hyperbolic $U$.
  Extend scalars $V_C = L\otimes_{ZZ} C : \mathsf{Vect}_C$ ($V_C = V_R \otimes_R C$ with $V_R=L\otimes R$). Bilinear extension $b_C: V_C\times V_C\to C$ is $C$-bilinear $b_C(v\otimes\lambda, w\otimes\mu)= \lambda\mu b(v,w)$ symmetric.
  Hermitian form $h_C: V_C\times\overline{V_C}\to C$ is instead sesquilinear $h_C(v\otimes\lambda, w\otimes\mu)= \lambda\bar\mu b(v,w)$ when $b$ already extended to $R$-bilinear and then sesquilinear via $C$-antilinear second factor, or more generally $h_C(v,w)=b_C(v, \bar w)$ with $w\mapsto\bar w$ conjugation on $C$ factor.
  Difference matters: $b_C$ is $\mathrm{GL}_n(C)$ invariant but not positive-definite; $h_C$ is $U(n)$-invariant Hermitian $h_C(v,v)\in R_{\geq 0}$ positive when $b_R$ positive.
  For period lattices $L=H^2(X,ZZ)$ of K3 $L\cong U^3\oplus E8(-1)^2$, $h_C$ gives Hodge Hermitian $\langle\alpha,\bar\beta\rangle$ on $H^{p,q}$ and Weil $i^{p-q}h_C$ positive.
  Need both `L.bilinear_form()` $b: \operatorname{Sym}^2(L^\vee)$ vs `L.hermitian_form() -> h: Herm(V_C)` as $\mathsf{Sesq}$.

* Covariant derivatives: for $\nabla: E\to E\otimes\Omega^1_X$ on $E: \mathsf{VecBun}(X)$, $\nabla_X = \langle\nabla,X\rangle : \Gamma(E)\to\Gamma(E)$ for $X\in\Gamma(TX)$, with Leibniz $\nabla(fs)= f\nabla s + s\otimes df$ and $\nabla_{fX}=f\nabla_X$.
  Iterated $\nabla^k: \Gamma(E)\to\Gamma(E\otimes\Omega^{1\otimes k})$ via $\nabla_{X_1,\ldots,X_k}$.
  For function $f: X\to R$, $\nabla f = df \in \Omega^1$ as $T^*X$; for vector field $Y$, $\nabla_X Y \in \Gamma(TX)$.
  For principal $P$, $D_A: \Gamma(\operatorname{Ad} P)\to\Gamma(\operatorname{Ad} P\otimes T^*X)$.

* Basic gradient flow $\gamma: R\to M$ on $M : \mathsf{RiemMfd}$ with $f: M\to R$ smooth: $\dot\gamma(t)= -\nabla f(\gamma(t))$ where $\nabla f = g^\sharp(df) \in \Gamma(TM)$ via musical $\sharp: T^*M\to TM$ ($df(Y)=g(\nabla f,Y)$). Stable/unstable manifolds, Morse $\operatorname{Hess} f = \nabla^2 f : \operatorname{Sym}^2 T^*M$ at $\operatorname{crit}(f)$.
  Interface `f.gradient_flow(gamma_0) -> gamma` as ODE in $C^\infty(R,M)$ via exponential map $\exp_p$.

* Hamiltonians and Hamiltonian flows: on symplectic $(M,\omega)$ $\omega\in\Omega^2(M)$ closed nondegenerate ($\omega^n$ volume when $\dim=2n$), Hamiltonian $H: M\to R$ defines $X_H \in \Gamma(TM)$ via $\iota_{X_H}\omega = dH$ ($\omega(X_H,\cdot)=dH$), i.e. $X_H = \omega^\sharp(dH)$ via $\sharp_\omega: T^*M\to TM$.
  Flow $\varphi_H^t: M\to M$ generated by $X_H$ with $d/dt\, \varphi_H^t = X_H\circ\varphi_H^t$, $\varphi_H^0=\mathrm{id}$, preserves $\omega$ ($L_{X_H}\omega=0$) and $H$ along flow when $H$ time-independent ($d/dt\, H\circ\varphi=0$ if $\partial_tH=0$). Lagrangian $\operatorname{Lagrangian}(H)=\operatorname{graph}(dH) \subset T^*M$ etc. For time-dependent $H_t: M\times R\to R$, $X_{H_t}$ similarly.
  Interface `H.hamiltonian_vector_field(omega) -> X_H`, `H.hamiltonian_flow(omega, t) -> phi^t`.

Intended owners: `categories/connections/curvature.py` ($\operatorname{curvature}(F_\nabla)=\nabla^2 : E\to E\otimes\Omega^2$, `is_flat` $\Leftrightarrow F=0$, `Bianchi`), `categories/moduli/flat_connections.py` (`ModuliFlat = FlatConn//G` as `GITQuotient` stack $M_{flat} = \{F=0\}/G$ with $T_{[\nabla]}\cong H^1(\operatorname{End})$), `categories/connections/gauge_group.py` (`GaugeGroup(P)` $=\Gamma(\operatorname{Aut} P)$ with $\operatorname{Lie}(G)=\Omega^0(\operatorname{End})$, action $g\cdot\nabla=g\nabla g^{-1}$), `categories/geometry/levi_civita.py` (`LeviCivita(g)` $\to \nabla^{LC}: \Gamma(TM)\to\Gamma(T^*\otimes TM)$ $T=0$, $\nabla g=0$), `categories/geometry/hermitian.py` (`HermitianSpace` $(V,h)$, `HermitianMetric` $h:E\times\bar E\to C$, `chern_connection` $\nabla^h$), `categories/lattices/hermitian_forms.py` (`Lattice.hermitian_form()` $\to h: V_C\times\bar V_C\to C$ vs `bilinear_form()` $\to b: V_C\times V_C\to C$ sesquilinear vs bilinear), `categories/connections/covariant_derivative.py` ($\nabla_X : \Gamma(E)\to\Gamma(E)$ $\nabla(fs)=f\nabla s+s\otimes df$), `categories/dynamics/gradient_flow.py` (`GradientFlow(f,g)` $\to \dot\gamma=-\nabla f$), `categories/dynamics/hamiltonian.py` (`Hamiltonian` $H: M\to R$ with $X_H=\omega^\sharp dH$, `hamiltonian_flow` $\varphi_H^t$ preserving $\omega$). Not free `curvature(A)` matrix or `hamiltonian_flow(H)` bare ODE — `nabla.curvature()`, `g.levi_civita()`, `P.hermitian_form()` vs `bilinear_form()`, $\nabla_X(s)$, `f.gradient_flow()`, `H.hamiltonian_flow(omega)` on the bundle/manifold/symplectic objects.

## Desired capability: Teichmüller space and Weil-Petersson metric — note 2026-09-15

Teichmüller and the Weil-Petersson metric.

* Teichmüller space is not a bare $T_g$ symbol.
  For $g: NN$, $g\geq 2$, $\Sigma_g : \mathsf{Top}$ closed orientable surface genus $g$ with $\pi=\pi_1\Sigma_g = \langle a_i,b_i\mid\prod[a_i,b_i]=1\rangle$ (already intaken as `SurfaceGroup(g)`), $T_g : \mathsf{ComplexMfd}$ is $T_g = \{J : \mathsf{AlmostComplex} \text{ on } \Sigma_g \text{ with } J \text{ integrable, orientation preserving}\}/\operatorname{Diff}_0(\Sigma_g)$ i.e. $\operatorname{ComplexStructures}(\Sigma_g)/\sim$ where $J_1\sim J_2$ if $\exists\varphi\in\operatorname{Diff}_0(\Sigma_g)$ isotopic to $\mathrm{id}$ with $\varphi^*J_2=J_1$.
  Point $[J]\in T_g$ is marked Riemann surface $X_J=(\Sigma_g,J)$ genus $g$ with marking $\Sigma_g\to X_J$ up to isotopy.
  $\dim_C T_g = 3g-3$, $\dim_R =6g-6$, contractible Stein manifold $T_g \cong C^{3g-3}$ via Bers embedding.
  Interface `SurfaceGroup(g).teichmuller_space() -> T_g : ComplexMfd` with `T_g.marked_surface() -> X_J : RiemannSurface` and $\operatorname{Diff}_0$ quotient as $\operatorname{MCG}$ action below.

* Moduli $M_g = T_g / \operatorname{MCG}_g$ where $\operatorname{MCG}_g = \operatorname{Mod}(\Sigma_g)= \operatorname{Diff}^+(\Sigma_g)/\operatorname{Diff}_0(\Sigma_g)= \operatorname{Out}(\pi_1\Sigma_g)$ mapping class group action $\operatorname{MCG}_g \curvearrowright T_g$ properly discontinuous by $\varphi\cdot[J]=[\varphi^*J]$.
  $M_g : \mathsf{DeligneMumfordStack}$ (orbifold) $\dim_C=3g-3$ with coarse moduli $M_g^{coarse}$ quasi-projective; $T_g\to M_g$ is $\operatorname{MCG}_g$-covering (orbifold universal cover) and universal curve $C_g\to T_g$ with fiber $X_J$.

* Weil-Petersson metric $g_{WP}$ is not a formal `wp_metric`. For $[J]\in T_g$, tangent $T_{[J]}T_g \cong H^1(X_J, T_{X_J}) \cong H^0(X_J, K_{X_J}^{\otimes 2})^\vee$ via Kodaira-Spencer (Bers: Beltrami differentials $\mu\in\Omega^{-1,1}=\Gamma(\bar K^{-1}\otimes K)$ modulo $\bar\partial$); cotangent $T^*_{[J]}T_g \cong Q(X_J)=H^0(X_J, K^{\otimes 2})$ quadratic differentials $q = q(z)dz^2$.
  Pairing via hyperbolic metric $\rho_J$ on $X_J$ (unique metric of curvature $-1$ in conformal class of $J$, $\rho_J = \lambda(z)|dz|^2$ with $\lambda=1/\operatorname{Im} z$ for uniformization $X_J= H/\Gamma$). Then for $\mu_1, \mu_2 \in H^1(T_X)$ corresponding to $q_1,q_2$, $g_{WP}(\mu_1, \mu_2)= \int_{X_J} \mu_1 \bar\mu_2 \rho_J^{-1}$ as $L^2$ pairing, or dually $\langle q_1,q_2\rangle_{WP}= \int_{X_J} q_1 \bar q_2 / \rho_J$.
  More invariantly $g_{WP}= \int_{X_J} |q|^2 / \rho$ after identifying $\mu = \bar q / \rho$.
  This defines Hermitian metric $h_{WP}$ on $T^{1,0}T_g$, Kähler with Kähler form $\omega_{WP}= i\partial\bar\partial \log\det$?
  Actually $\omega_{WP} = i/2\, \partial\bar\partial S$ where $S$ is Liouville?
  Equivalent via $\omega_{WP}$ closed.
  Weil-Petersson is Kähler, negatively curved, incomplete, with completion $\bar T_g$ Deligne-Mumford $\bar M_g$.

* Interface `T_g.weil_petersson_metric() -> g_{WP} : HermitianMetric` on $T_g : \mathsf{ComplexMfd}$ with $g_{WP}: T_{T_g}\times\overline{T_{T_g}}\to C$ as above, $\omega_{WP}= \operatorname{Im} g_{WP}$ Kähler $2$-form, curvature $R_{WP}$ as $\Omega^{1,1}(\operatorname{End}(T_{T_g}))$ with dual Nakano negativity, etc. Coupled to Teichmüller metric $g_T$ (Finsler via $\|q\|_1 = \int|q|$), but WP is Hermitian $L^2$.

* Pants decompositions, Fenchel-Nielsen: $T_g$ parametrized by $3g-3$ length-twist pairs $(\ell_i, \tau_i)\in R_{>0}\times R$ from maximal collection of disjoint simple closed curves $C_i$ cutting $\Sigma_g$ into $2g-2$ pairs of pants $P_j\cong S^2\setminus 3\,\text{disks}$.
  For $X_J$ with hyperbolic $\rho_J$, $\ell_i = \operatorname{length}_{\rho_J}(C_i)$ geodesic length, $\tau_i$ twist around $C_i$.
  $T_g \cong R_{>0}^{3g-3}\times R^{3g-3}$ via FN, and $\omega_{WP}= \sum_i d\ell_i \wedge d\tau_i$ (Wolpert).
  Effective model needs `CurveComplex(Sigma_g)` and `PantsDecomposition` objects.

Intended owners: `categories/teichmuller/teichmuller_space.py` (`TeichmullerSpace(g) -> T_g : ComplexMfd` as $\operatorname{ComplexStructures}/\operatorname{Diff}_0$, `ModuliSpace M_g = T_g/MCG_g` with $\operatorname{MCG}_g=\operatorname{Mod}(\Sigma_g)$ action, pants `PantsDecomposition`), `categories/teichmuller/weil_petersson.py` (`WeilPeterssonMetric` $g_{WP}$ as `HermitianMetric` on $T_g$ via $L^2$ of $Q(X)=H^0(K^{\otimes 2})$ against $\rho_J$, $\omega_{WP}$ Kähler, with `hyperbolic_metric X_J -> rho_J` via uniformization, and `fenchel_nielsen_coords` with $\omega_{WP}=\sum d\ell\wedge d\tau$). Not a free `weil_petersson(g)` number — `T_g.weil_petersson_metric()` on the Teichmüller object with $Q(X)$ and $\rho_J$ as data.

## Desired capability: slopes of vector bundles — and checking (semi)stability — note 2026-09-15

Slopes of vector bundles.
And checking (semi)stability.

* Slope is not a bare rational $\deg/\operatorname{rank}$ with $\deg$ as integer.
  For $X : \mathsf{SmProj}$ (curve, surface, higher) with polarization $H : \operatorname{AmpleDiv}(X)$ (or Kähler form $\omega$) and $E : \mathsf{VecBun}(X)$ / $\mathsf{CohSh}(X)$ torsion-free coherent, $\operatorname{rank}(E)=\operatorname{ch}_0(E) : ZZ_{>0}$ and $\deg_H(E)=c_1(E)\cdot H^{\dim X-1} : ZZ$ (intersection number via $A^1\cdot A^{\dim-1}$, or $\deg_H(E)=\int_X c_1(E)\wedge\omega^{n-1}$) — both as $\mathsf{Chow}$/cohomology classes already intaken via Chern calculus.
  Slope $\mu_H(E)= \deg_H(E)/\operatorname{rank}(E) : QQ$ as element $\mu: \mathsf{CohTorsionFree}(X) \to QQ$ (or $RR$ when $\omega$ real).
  For Higgs $(E,\varphi)$, same $\mu(E)$ with $\varphi$-invariant $F$ in semistability test; for parabolic bundles $\operatorname{par}\mu = (\deg + \sum \alpha_i\cdot \operatorname{wt}_i)/\operatorname{rank}$.

* (Semi)stability is not a string tag.
  $E$ is semistable (resp.
  stable) if for every proper nonzero subsheaf $F\subset E$ (resp.
  proper $\varphi$-invariant $F$ for Higgs, or subbundle) with $0<\operatorname{rank}(F)<\operatorname{rank}(E)$ and torsion-free quotient, $\mu_H(F) \leq \mu_H(E)$ (resp.
  $<$). Equivalently maximal destabilizing subsheaf $F_{\max} \subset E$ with $\mu(F_{\max})=\mu_{\max}(E) = \max_{F\subset E} \mu(F)$ satisfies $\mu_{\max} \leq \mu(E)$ (semistable) / $<$ (stable).
  Polystable means $E \cong \bigoplus_i E_i$ with each $E_i$ stable and $\mu(E_i)=\mu(E)$ (direct sum of stable of same slope; equivalently $E$ semistable and $\operatorname{gr}_{JH}(E)=\bigoplus \operatorname{gr}_i$ polystable Jordan–Hölder graded).
  And polystability (and checking it) is required: $E$ is polystable iff $E$ is semistable and $E \cong \operatorname{gr}_{JH}(E)$ as $\bigoplus_i$ of its Jordan–Hölder stable factors (or via Kobayashi-Hitchin: admits Hermite–Einstein $h$ with $i\Lambda F_h = \mu(E)\cdot\mathrm{id}$). Unstable means $\exists F$ with $\mu(F)>\mu(E)$.

* Checking (semi)stability must be operational for effective bundles where subsheaves are enumerable via $\mathsf{QCoh}$ $\operatorname{Sub}(F)$ as Quot scheme $\operatorname{Quot}(E,P)$ strata.
  For $E$ on curve $X$ smooth projective curve (already intaken with $\operatorname{Hilb}$ and $\operatorname{Quot}$), use existing $\operatorname{Quot}(E)$ parameterization: $\operatorname{Quot}(E) = \coprod_P \operatorname{Quot}(E,P)$ with $P$ Hilbert polynomial $P_F(m)= \operatorname{rank}(F)\cdot\deg H\cdot m + \deg(F)+ \operatorname{rank}(F)(1-g)$; each $\operatorname{Quot}(E,P)$ projective $\mathsf{Sch}$ with computable `EC` when $X$ with finite affine cover.
  Then $\mu_{\max}(E)$ is $\max_{F\in\operatorname{Quot}}$ finite over destabilizing candidates bounded by $\deg(F) \leq \operatorname{rank}(F)\cdot\mu_{\max}$ plus boundedness theorem (Grothendieck $\operatorname{Quot}$ bounded).
  For $E$ on curve, Harder-Narasimhan filtration provides certificate: unique $0=E_0\subset E_1\subset\ldots\subset E_\ell=E$ with each $\operatorname{gr}_i=E_i/E_{i-1}$ semistable and $\mu(\operatorname{gr}_1)>\ldots>\mu(\operatorname{gr}_\ell)$ strictly decreasing; then $E$ semistable $\Leftrightarrow$ $\ell=1$, stable $\Leftrightarrow$ $\ell=1$ and no proper $F$ with same $\mu$ (i.e. $E$ simple $\operatorname{End}(E)=k$). HN is computed via iterative maximal destabilizing $E_1 = F_{\max}$ and recursion.
  Interface `E.slope(H) -> mu : QQ`, `E.is_semistable(H) -> Bool` via $\mu_{\max}(E) \leq \mu(E)$ with witness $F_{\max}$ when false, `E.is_stable(H)`, `E.harder_narasimhan(H)` $\to 0\subset E_1\subset\ldots\subset E$ as filtered bundle with $\operatorname{gr}_i$ semistable.

* For $X$ higher dimension ($\dim\geq 2$) with polarization $H$, same definition but $\operatorname{Quot}$ boundedness via $\mu$-bounded family uses $\deg_H$ and $\operatorname{rank}$ as above and Bogomolov-type bounds $\Delta(E)=2rc_2-(r-1)c_1^2$ already intaken via Chern; effective check requires bounding $\deg(F)$ candidates via $F\in\operatorname{Quot}(E, c_1,d)$ finite.
  Not just curve case; Higgs $\varphi$-invariance restricts to $F$ with $\varphi(F)\subset F\otimes\Omega^1$.

* Requires: Chern data $c_1, \operatorname{rank}$ already intaken via `chern_class`; $\operatorname{Quot}$ scheme $\operatorname{Quot}(E,P) : \mathsf{Sch}$ with $\operatorname{EC}$ effective (already via Hilbert schemes lead); `Hilbert polynomial` $P: QQ[t]$ via $R\Gamma$; $\mathsf{Filt}$ from earlier `FiltCh` lead for HN filtration as filtered vector bundle.

Intended owners: `categories/vector_bundles/slope.py` ($\operatorname{slope}(E,H)=\deg_H/\operatorname{rank} : QQ$, `mu_max(E,H)` via $\operatorname{Quot}$), `categories/vector_bundles/stability.py` (`is_semistable(E,H)` $\Leftrightarrow \forall F\subset E\ \mu(F)\leq\mu(E)$ with certificate $F_{\max}$, `is_stable`, `is_polystable` $\Leftrightarrow E \cong \bigoplus_i E_i$ stable same $\mu$ / $E \cong \operatorname{gr}_{JH}(E)$ / Hermite–Einstein $i\Lambda F_h=\mu\cdot\mathrm{id}$, `harder_narasimhan(E,H)` $\to\mathsf{Filt}(E)$ with $\operatorname{gr}$ semistable and $\mu$ decreasing, `jordan_holder` for $S$-equivalence and $\operatorname{gr}_{JH}$), `categories/moduli/higgs.py` (`HiggsSemistable` via $\varphi$-invariant $F$). Not a free `is_semistable(E)` bare $\deg/\operatorname{rank}$ table — `E.slope(H)`, `E.is_semistable(H)`, `E.is_polystable(H)`, `E.harder_narasimhan(H)` on the bundle with $H: \mathsf{Ample}$ polarization and $\operatorname{Quot}(E)$ behind $\mu_{\max}$.

## Desired capability: composition series, filtrations, associated graded, semidirect, is_simple, Jordan-Hölder, Inn/Out, artinian/noetherian, subgroup poset — note 2026-09-15

Need composition series for groups, modules, algebras, etc. Need filtrations to support associated graded.
Need semidirect product support, is_simple for objects in any abelian category, Jordan-Hölder decompositions, inner and outer automorphisms of groups, need is_artinian and is_noetherian for modules and rings.
Need to support entire subgroup poset of a group as an honest poset.

* Composition series is not a list of subgroups with a name.
  For $G : \mathsf{Groups}$ (finite, profinite, $\mathsf{FPGroups}$ with `Groups().framed`), $M : R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod}$ ($R: \mathsf{Rings}$), $A : \mathsf{Alg}_R$, a composition series is $0 = G_0 \triangleleft G_1 \triangleleft \cdots \triangleleft G_n = G$ (resp.
  $0 \subset M_0 \subset \cdots \subset M_n = M$ as $R$-submodules, $0 \subset A_0 \subset \cdots$) with each factor $G_{i+1}/G_i$ simple (no nontrivial proper normal subgroup), $M_{i+1}/M_i$ simple $R$-module, etc. Length $n = \ell(G) : NN$ is Jordan-Hölder length.
  Interface `G.composition_series()` $\to 0\triangleleft G_1\triangleleft\cdots\triangleleft G : \mathsf{Filt}(\mathsf{Groups})$ with $\operatorname{gr}_i = G_{i+1}/G_i : \mathsf{GrAb}/\mathsf{Groups}$ simple, and `M.composition_series()` $\to \mathsf{Filt}(R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod})$ similarly; when $G$ finite, series exists iff $G$ finite length (trivially); when $M$ finite length $R$-module, same.
  Not a free `composition_series(G)` returning Python `list` — `G.composition_series()` as filtered object with $F^pG$ and $\operatorname{gr}_F^pG$ simple.

* Filtrations to support associated graded: for any $X : \mathsf{AbCat}$ abelian (groups with normal series, $R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod}$, $\mathsf{Alg}_R$, $\mathsf{Ch}$), filtration $F : \mathsf{Filt}(X)$ is decreasing $\cdots \supset F^pX \supset F^{p+1}X \supset \cdots$ by subobjects ($F^pX \hookrightarrow X$ mono) or increasing $0=F_0\subset F_1\subset\cdots$ with $\bigcup F_p=X$.
  Associated graded $\operatorname{gr}_F(X) = \bigoplus_p \operatorname{gr}_F^p X$ where $\operatorname{gr}_F^p X = F^pX / F^{p+1}X : X$ as $\mathsf{GrMod}_{ZZ}$ object $\operatorname{gr}: \mathsf{Filt}(X) \to \mathsf{Gr}(X)$ functor with $\operatorname{gr}_F^p$ simple when $F$ is composition series.
  Requires `FiltCh` lead already intaken for chain complexes; now extend to $\mathsf{Filt}(\mathsf{Groups})$, $\mathsf{Filt}(R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod})$, $\mathsf{Filt}(\mathsf{Alg})$ uniformly via `Subobjects` construction $\operatorname{Sub}_G$ category, not a bare `filtration` attribute.

* Semidirect product support: for $N,H : \mathsf{Groups}$ with action $\varphi: H \to \operatorname{Aut}(N)$ as group morphism $\varphi: H \to \operatorname{Aut}(N)$ already intaken via `G-action is morphism` (caller constructs $\rho: G\to\operatorname{Aut}(M)$), $N\rtimes_\varphi H : \mathsf{Groups}$ is $N \rtimes H = \{(n,h) \mid (n_1,h_1)(n_2,h_2)=(n_1\cdot\varphi_{h_1}(n_2), h_1h_2)\}$ with $N \triangleleft N\rtimes H$, $H \leq N\rtimes H$, $N\rtimes H/N \cong H$, $1\to N\to N\rtimes H\to H\to 1$ split via $s: H\to N\rtimes H, s(h)=(1,h)$.
  Interface `N.semidirect_product(H, phi)` $\to N\rtimes_\varphi H : \mathsf{Groups}$ with `inclusion` $N\hookrightarrow N\rtimes H$, `projection` $N\rtimes H\twoheadrightarrow H$, `section` $H\hookrightarrow N\rtimes H$, and detection `G.is_semidirect_product` $\Rightarrow (N,H,\varphi)$ via complement $H\leq G$ with $G=NH$, $N\cap H=1$.
  When $H$ acts on $R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod}$ similarly $M\rtimes G$.

* `is_simple` for objects in any abelian category: for $X : \mathsf{AbCat}$ (any $C$ abelian with $0$, kernels, cokernels, $\operatorname{Hom}$), `X.is_simple()` $\Leftrightarrow X\neq 0 \wedge \forall \text{ mono } i: S\hookrightarrow X\ (S=0 \vee S\cong X)$ i.e. no nonzero proper subobject $0\subset S\subset X$.
  Equivalently $X$ has exactly two subobjects $\{0,X\}$ in $\operatorname{Sub}(X)$ lattice.
  For $G : \mathsf{Groups}$ not abelian category but simple group means no nontrivial proper normal subgroup; for $R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod}$ simple means no nontrivial proper submodule.
  Interface is `X.is_simple() -> Bool` as predicate on the object in its category, with $X \in \mathsf{AbCat}$ dispatched by `X.category().is_abelian()` and $\operatorname{Sub}(X)$ lattice already via `Subobjects` construction.
  No separate `is_simple_group` vs `is_simple_module` — one `is_simple` on $C$ when $C$ abelian, plus `G.is_simple_group()` when $\mathsf{Groups}$ non-abelian variant.

* Jordan-Hölder decompositions: any finite-length $X : \mathsf{AbCat}$ has composition series and $\operatorname{gr}(X) = \bigoplus \operatorname{gr}_i$ multiset of simple factors is unique up to permutation/isomorphism (Jordan-Hölder theorem): if $0\subset X_1\subset\cdots\subset X_n=X$ and $0\subset Y_1\subset\cdots\subset Y_m=X$ are composition series, then $n=m$ and $\{\operatorname{gr}_i(X_\bullet)\} \cong \{\operatorname{gr}_j(Y_\bullet)\}$ as sets with multiplicity.
  Interface `X.jordan_holder_factors() -> Multiset(SimpleObjects)` as $\mathsf{Gr}$ or `Multiset` of simples with `X.jordan_holder_series()` returning one series and `X.length()` $\to n: NN$ plus witness that any two series have same $\operatorname{gr}$ up to permutation (via Schreier refinement).
  For groups finite, `G.composition_factors() -> Multiset(SimpleGroups)` via $\operatorname{gr}_i = G_{i+1}/G_i$.

* Inner and outer automorphisms of groups: for $G : \mathsf{Groups}$, $\operatorname{Inn}(G) = \{c_g : x\mapsto gxg^{-1} \mid g\in G\} \leq \operatorname{Aut}(G)$ as normal subgroup $\operatorname{Inn}(G) \triangleleft \operatorname{Aut}(G)$, $\operatorname{Aut}(G) : \mathsf{Groups}$ already as $\mathsf{AutCat}$, $\operatorname{Out}(G)=\operatorname{Aut}(G)/\operatorname{Inn}(G) : \mathsf{Groups}$ quotient.
  Interface `G.inner_automorphisms()` $\to \operatorname{Inn}(G) : \mathsf{Subgroups}(\operatorname{Aut}(G))$ via $g \mapsto c_g$ morphism $G\to\operatorname{Aut}(G)$ with kernel $Z(G)$, `G.outer_automorphism_group()` $\to \operatorname{Out}(G) = \operatorname{Aut}(G)/\operatorname{Inn}(G) : \mathsf{Groups}$ as quotient, and $\operatorname{Aut}(G) \to \operatorname{Out}(G)$ projection.
  Distinguish $\operatorname{Inn}$ as image of $\operatorname{Ad}: G\to\operatorname{Aut}(G)$ vs $\operatorname{Out}$ as group of outer classes.

* `is_artinian` and `is_noetherian` for modules and rings: for $M : R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod}$ ($R: \mathsf{Rings}$), `M.is_artinian()` $\Leftrightarrow$ every descending chain $M\supset M_1\supset M_2\supset\cdots$ stabilizes ($\exists n\ M_n=M_{n+1}$) $\Leftrightarrow$ every nonempty set of submodules has minimal element; `M.is_noetherian()` $\Leftrightarrow$ every ascending chain $M_1\subset M_2\subset\cdots$ stabilizes / every submodule finitely generated.
  For $R : \mathsf{Rings}$, `R.is_artinian()` as $R$-module over itself (left-artinian), `R.is_noetherian()` left-noetherian.
  When $R$ commutative PID, `is_noetherian` holds.
  For finite-length $M$, both artinian and noetherian.
  Interface `M.is_artinian()`, `M.is_noetherian()`, `R.is_artinian()`, `R.is_noetherian()` on the module/ring objects with $\operatorname{Sub}(M)$ lattice already, not a free `is_artinian(M)` function returning bare `Bool` without $\operatorname{Sub}$ data.

* Entire subgroup poset of a group as an honest poset: for $G : \mathsf{Groups}$ (finite, finitely presented with computable $\operatorname{Sub}(G)$ when finite index bound), $\operatorname{Sub}(G) = \{H \leq G\} : \mathsf{Sets}(\mathsf{Sets})$ with partial order $H \leq K \Leftrightarrow H\subseteq K$ as poset $\mathsf{Pos} = (\operatorname{Sub}(G), \subseteq)$ with $\bot = \{1\}$, $\top = G$, meets $H\wedge K = H\cap K$, joins $H\vee K = \langle H\cup K\rangle$ when computable, and lattice when $G$ finite.
  Need `Poset` object $P = \operatorname{SubPoset}(G) : \mathsf{Posets}$ with `P.elements() -> Set(Sub)`, `P.le(H,K)` $\Leftrightarrow H\leq K$, `P.cover_relations()`, `P.hasse_diagram()`, and as poset category $\operatorname{PosCat}(P) : \mathsf{Cat}$ with $\operatorname{Hom}_P(H,K)=1$ if $H\leq K$ else $\emptyset$.
  Interface `G.subgroup_poset()` $\to P : \mathsf{Posets}$ honest `Poset`, not a `list` of subgroups, with `P.as_category() -> Cat` and `P.as_poset()` roundtrip.

Intended owners: `categories/algebra/composition_series.py` (`CompositionSeries` $0=G_0\triangleleft\cdots\triangleleft G_n=G$ with $\operatorname{gr}$ simple, `length`), `categories/abelian/filtrations.py` ($\mathsf{Filt}(X)$, $\operatorname{gr}_F$, `AssociatedGraded` functor), `categories/groups/semidirect.py` (`SemidirectProduct` $N\rtimes_\varphi H$ with $\varphi: H\to\operatorname{Aut}(N)$ morphism, `split ses`), `categories/abelian/is_simple.py` (`is_simple` predicate on any $\mathsf{AbCat}$ via $\operatorname{Sub}$), `categories/algebra/jordan_holder.py` (`JordanHolder` with $\operatorname{gr}$ multiset uniqueness), `categories/groups/automorphisms.py` ($\operatorname{Inn}(G)\triangleleft\operatorname{Aut}(G)$, $\operatorname{Out}(G)=\operatorname{Aut}/\operatorname{Inn}$ via $\operatorname{Ad}$), `categories/modules/chain_conditions.py` (`is_artinian`, `is_noetherian` via ACC/DCC on $\operatorname{Sub}$), `categories/groups/subgroup_poset.py` ($\operatorname{SubPoset}(G) : \mathsf{Posets}$ with `PosetCat`). Not a free `composition_series(G)` list — `G.composition_series()`, `X.is_simple()`, `N.semidirect_product(H,phi)`, `G.subgroup_poset()` on the group/module/abelian objects.

## Desired capability: open-set categories, powersets, topologies, poset $\leftrightarrow$ poset-category fluid interface — note 2026-09-15

Need to support open set categories $\operatorname{Op}(X)$, powersets, topologies as subsets of power sets, poset structure on $P(X)$ and $\operatorname{Op}(X)$, need fluid interface between posets and poset categories.

* Open set category $\operatorname{Op}(X)$ is not a bare set of opens.
  For $X : \mathsf{Top}$ topological space with $\tau_X : \mathsf{Top}$ as set of opens $\tau_X \subset P(X)$ (below), $\operatorname{Op}(X) : \mathsf{Cat}$ is the poset category whose objects are $U \in \tau_X$ (each $U\subset X$ open) and morphisms $\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{Op}(X)}(U,V)=1$ if $U\subseteq V$ else $\emptyset$ (single inclusion $U\hookrightarrow V$ when $U\subseteq V$). Composition is inclusion transitivity.
  $\operatorname{Op}(X)$ is a poset category (thin, skeletal) with terminal $X$, initial $\emptyset$, pullbacks $U\wedge V = U\cap V$ as categorical product (meet), coproduct $U\vee V = U\cup V$ as join, and $\operatorname{Op}(X) = (\tau_X, \subseteq)$ as `PosetCat`. Interface `X.open_set_category()` $\to \operatorname{Op}(X) : \mathsf{PosCat}$ with `X.open_sets()` $\to \tau_X : \mathsf{Sets}(\mathsf{Sets})$ and `Op(X).inclusion(U,V) -> Hom` and `Op(X).as_poset()` $\to (\tau_X,\subseteq)$; for $X : \mathsf{Sch}$ with $\operatorname{Top}(X)$ Zariski, $\operatorname{Op}(X)$ is Zariski opens.

* Powerset $P(X) = 2^X : \mathsf{Sets}(\mathsf{Sets})$ is not a Python `set`. For $X : \mathsf{Sets}$ (or $X : \mathsf{Top}$ underlying set $U(X)$), $P(X) = \{A \mid A\subseteq X\} : \mathsf{Sets}$ as object $P(X) : \mathsf{Sets}$ with $|P(X)| = 2^{|X|}$ cardinality via $|P(X)| = 2^{|X|}$ when $|X| : \mathsf{Cardinal}$ computable and finite, $\emptyset\in P(X)$ as bottom, $X\in P(X)$ top, operations $\cup: P\times P\to P$ union, $\cap: P\times P\to P$ intersection, ${}^c: P\to P$ complement $A^c = X\setminus A$, $\Delta$ symmetric difference, all as morphisms in $\mathsf{Sets}$.
  Poset structure $P(X) : \mathsf{Posets}$ with $A \leq B \Leftrightarrow A\subseteq B$ via $\mathsf{PosCat}$ below; lattice $P(X)$ is Boolean lattice $B = (P(X),\subseteq,\cup,\cap,{}^c)$.
  Interface `X.powerset()` $\to P(X) : \mathsf{Sets}$ with `P(X).as_poset() -> Posets` (see fluid below) and `P(X).as_boolean_algebra()`.

* Topologies as subsets of power sets: $\tau \subset P(X)$ collection of subsets of $X$ satisfying $\emptyset\in\tau$, $X\in\tau$, $\tau$ closed under arbitrary unions $\bigcup_{i\in I}U_i \in \tau$ (finite vs arbitrary distinguished for topology vs Grothendieck $J$ already intaken) and finite intersections $U\cap V \in \tau$.
  $\tau : \mathsf{Topologies}(X)$ is object of $\operatorname{Top}(X) : \mathsf{Sets}$ where $\operatorname{Top}(X) = \{\tau \subset P(X) \mid \tau \text{ topology}\}$.
  Interface `Topology(X, tau)` constructor with `X.topology()` $\to \tau_X : \mathsf{Topologies}(X)$ for $X: \mathsf{Top}$ (its defining $\tau$), `Powerset.poset` etc. For $X$ finite, $\tau$ enumerable; for $X$ general, retained as formal $\tau$ with axioms.

* Poset structure on $P(X)$ and $\operatorname{Op}(X)$ by inclusion: both $P(X)$ and $\operatorname{Op}(X)$ (via underlying $\tau_X$) carry poset $(P, \subseteq)$ with $A\leq B$ as $A\subseteq B$, meet $A\wedge B=A\cap B$, join $A\vee B=A\cup B$, order $\leq$ as relation $R\subset P\times P$.
  Need `Poset` object $P = (E, \leq)$ with $E : \mathsf{Sets}$ elements, $\leq: E\times E\to\mathsf{Bool}$ reflexive/transitive/antisymmetric, `P.hasse`, `P.comparable`, `P.meet`, `P.join` when lattice.
  Interface `P(X).poset()` $\to (P(X),\subseteq) : \mathsf{Posets}$ and `Op(X).poset()` $\to (\tau_X,\subseteq)$ via same $\mathsf{PosCat}$; for $P(X)$ Boolean lattice, `P.dual()`, `P.intervals()` etc.

* Fluid interface between posets and poset categories: every `Poset` $P=(E,\leq): \mathsf{Posets}$ has associated thin category $\operatorname{PosCat}(P): \mathsf{Cat}$ with $\operatorname{Ob} = E$, $\operatorname{Hom}_P(x,y)=1$ if $x\leq y$ else $0$, $\mathrm{id}_x$ the $x\leq x$ witness, $\circ$ is transitivity.
  Conversely every poset category $C : \mathsf{Cat}$ thin skeletal with at most one morphism between objects has underlying poset $\operatorname{Pos}(C) = (\operatorname{Ob}(C), \leq_C)$ where $x \leq_C y \Leftrightarrow \operatorname{Hom}_C(x,y)\neq\emptyset$.
  Need functors $\operatorname{PosCat}: \mathsf{Posets} \to \mathsf{Cat}_{pos}$ and $\operatorname{Pos}: \mathsf{Cat}_{pos} \to \mathsf{Posets}$ inverse (equivalence $\mathsf{Posets} \simeq \mathsf{Cat}_{pos}$), with natural isomorphism $\operatorname{Pos}(\operatorname{PosCat}(P))\cong P$ and $\operatorname{PosCat}(\operatorname{Pos}(C))\cong C$.
  Interface must be fluid: `P.as_category()` $\to \operatorname{PosCat}(P) : \mathsf{Cat}$ and `C.as_poset()` $\to \operatorname{Pos}(C) : \mathsf{Posets}$ with `P.as_category().as_poset() == P` and `Op(X).as_poset()` / `P(X).as_category()` roundtrip, and `P.as_category().as_poset().as_category() == PosCat(P)` etc. Reuse category $\mathsf{Posets}$ lead's $\operatorname{SubPoset}(G)$ above — same $\mathsf{Posets}$ owner, so $\operatorname{Sub}(G)$ poset and $\operatorname{Op}(X)$ poset share $\mathsf{Posets}$ type, and $\mathsf{Cat}$ thin categories are objects of $\mathsf{Cat}$ already.

Intended owners: `categories/topology/open_sets.py` (`OpenSetCategory` $\operatorname{Op}(X): \mathsf{Cat}$ with `X.open_set_category()`, $\operatorname{Hom}=\operatorname{Inclusion}$), `categories/sets/powerset.py` (`Powerset` $P(X)=2^X : \mathsf{Sets}$ with $\cup,\cap,{}^c$ and `as_poset()` Boolean lattice), `categories/topology/topologies.py` (`Topology` $\tau\subset P(X)$ with $\emptyset,X\in\tau$, $\bigcup\in\tau$, $\cap\in\tau$), `categories/posets/poset_category.py` ($\mathsf{Posets}$, $\operatorname{PosCat}: \mathsf{Posets} \rightleftarrows \mathsf{Cat}_{pos} :\operatorname{Pos}$ fluid `as_category()`/`as_poset()` equivalence).
Not a free `open_sets(X)` list — `X.open_set_category()` with `Op(X).poset()` and `P(X).as_poset().as_category()` roundtrip on the space/set objects.

## Desired capability: short exact sequences $1\to A\to B\to C\to 1$ and fibrations $F\to E\to B$ classified by Ext / twisting cocycle — note 2026-09-15

Ask for a way to compute, algorithmically, given a short exact sequence of groups $1\to A\to B\to C\to 1$, computing $B$ as a twisted product/sum of $A$ and $C$ determined by an $\operatorname{Ext}$ class/cocycle.
Similarly for fibrations $F\to E\to B$.

A group extension is not an object; a short exact sequence of groups is.
A group can be isomorphic to an extension of $C$ by $A$, but $B$ alone is just a group.
The object is the SES

$$
1 \to A \xrightarrow{i} B \xrightarrow{p} C \to 1
$$

with $i: A\hookrightarrow B$ mono (identified with normal $i(A)\triangleleft B$) and $p: B\twoheadrightarrow C$ epi, $\operatorname{im} i = \ker p$.
Morphisms of SES are triples of group homomorphisms commuting with $i,p$.
Similarly $0\to A\to B\to C\to 0$ in $R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod}$ is the object in the abelian category, not a new `Extension` wrapper.
A fibration sequence $F\to E\to B$ (Serre fibration $F\hookrightarrow E\twoheadrightarrow B$ with $F$ fiber over $b0\in B$) is the object, not a `TwistedProduct` wrapper.
Constructing the SES / fibration sequence is the work; returning $B$ or $E$ alone forgets the structure.

* For groups: the SES $1\to A\to B\to C\to 1$ (e.g. $A,C : \mathsf{Groups}$ with $A$ normal in $B$, $B/A\cong C$; $A$ abelian or general, $C$ acting via $C\to\operatorname{Out}(A)$) has class $\alpha \in H^2(C,A)$ / $\operatorname{Ext}^1(C,A)$ / $H^2(C,Z(A))$.
  Choose a set-theoretic section $s: C\to B$ ($p\circ s=\mathrm{id}_C$, $s(1)=1$ after normalization), then factor set $c: C\times C\to A$, $c(x,y)=s(x)s(y)s(xy)^{-1}\in A$, with twisted multiplication on the underlying set $A\times C$ as $(a_1,c_1)(a_2,c_2)=(a_1\cdot {}^{c_1}a_2\cdot c(c_1,c_2), c_1c_2)$ where ${}^{c}a=s(c)a s(c)^{-1}$ is the $C\curvearrowright A$ action.
  Class $[c]\in H^2(C,A)$ (or $\operatorname{Ext}^1$ when $A$ abelian $Z[C]$-module, or $H^2(C,Z(A))$ for $A$ nonabelian via center) classifies the SES up to isomorphism of SES. $\operatorname{Ext}$ already intaken via $\operatorname{RHom}$ on $D(ZZ[C]\text{-}\mathsf{Mod})$ / $D(ZZ\text{-}\mathsf{Mod})$ with $\operatorname{Ext}^n=R^n \operatorname{Hom}$; given $[c]$ construct SES $1\to A\to B_c\to C\to 1$ with $B_c$ underlying set $A\times C$ and law $*_c$ and $(i_c,p_c)$ as above and $[B_c]=[c]$.
  Split case $c=0$ gives $B\cong A\rtimes C$ semidirect ($\exists s$ homomorphism); central $A\leq Z(B)$ when action trivial.
  Interface is on the SES object: `SES(1->A->B->C->1).extension_class()` $\to [c]\in H^2(C,A)$ where cocycle $c: C\times C\to A$ satisfies $dc=0$ ($c(xy,z)\cdot c(x,y)^z=c(x,yz)\cdot c(y,z)$) modulo coboundaries $[c]=[c']\Leftrightarrow\exists b:C\to A\ c'=c\cdot db$, and conversely `SES_from_cocycle(A,C,c)` $\to (1\to A\to A\times_c C\to C\to 1) : \mathsf{SES}$.
  No `GroupExtension` object — the SES is the object and $B$ is its middle term.

* Algorithmically realizing the middle term from $[c]$/$c$ uses: group cohomology $H^n(G,M)=\operatorname{Ext}_{ZZ[G]}^n(ZZ,M)$ via $\operatorname{BarRes}\to\operatorname{RHom}$ with $M:ZZ[C]\text{-}\mathsf{Mod}$ (the $C$-module $A$ via action), cocycle $c\in Z^2(C,A)=\ker d^2\subset C^2$, class $[c]\in H^2=Z^2/B^2$, and the twisted law above.
  For $A$ abelian finite with $H^2(C,A)$ via Smith over $ZZ$ when $C$ finite / $C=ZZ^n$ etc., the SES middle $B$ is constructed as $\mathsf{Groups}$ with $A\times C$ underlying set and $*_c$.

* Similarly for modules/algebras: SES $0\to A\to B\to C\to 0$ in $R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod}$ (abelian category) is determined by $\operatorname{Ext}^1_R(C,A)=H^1(\operatorname{RHom}(C,A))$ class $\xi: C\to A[1]$ in $D(R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod})$; $\xi=0$ gives split $B\cong A\oplus C$.
  Class $\xi$ is Baer sum $0\to A\to B\to C\to 0$ = pushout of $0\to K\to P\to C\to 0$ (projective presentation) along $i:K\to A$ representing $\xi$.
  Interface: SES $0\to A\to B\to C\to 0$ has `ses.extension_class()` $\to \xi\in\operatorname{Ext}^1(C,A)$, and `ses_from_class(C,A,xi)` $\to (0\to A\to B_\xi\to C\to 0)$.
  No `ModuleExtension` wrapper — the SES is the object.

* Similarly for fibrations $F\to E\to B$ (Serre fibration $F\hookrightarrow E\twoheadrightarrow B$ with $B$ path-connected, $F$ fiber over $b0\in B$): already intaken via Kenzo $F\times_\tau B$ with twisting operator $\tau: B_{*+1}\to G_*$ where $G$ acts on $F$ via $G=\operatorname{Aut}(F)$ or $\Omega B$.
  Here $E\simeq F\times_\tau B$ twisted cartesian product is the middle term of the fibration sequence, determined by $k$-invariant / twisting cocycle $\tau\in Z^{n+1}(B;\pi_n(F))$ / $H^{n+1}(B;\pi_n(F))$ when $F=K(\pi_n,n)$ iteratively (Postnikov), or more generally $[\tau]\in[B,B\operatorname{Aut}(F)]$ classifying map.
  Construction uses $\operatorname{tEZ}$ / $\operatorname{BPL}$ / $\operatorname{Bar}/\operatorname{Cobar}$ as $F\times_\tau B \Leftrightarrow F\otimes_t B$; algorithmically $B$ base $\mathsf{sSet}$ with $\operatorname{EC}$ effective, $F$ fiber with $\operatorname{EC}$, and $\tau$ simplicial twisting operator ($B_{n+1}\to F_n$, Kan condition $d_0 \tau(b)=\tau(d_0b)\cdot\partial b$), then $F\times_\tau B$ sSet with simplices $(f,b)$, $d_0(f,b)=(d_0f\cdot\tau(b), d_0b)$.
  Interface: fibration sequence $F\to E\to B$ has `fib_seq.twisting_class()` $\to [\tau]\in H^{n+1}(B;\pi_n(F))$ / $[B\to B\operatorname{Aut}(F)]$, and `fib_seq_from_twisting(F,B,tau)` $\to (F\to F\times_\tau B\to B)$.
  No `TwistedProduct` object type — the fibration sequence is the object and $F\times_\tau B$ is its total space.

* Requires: $H^n(G,M)=\operatorname{Ext}_{ZZG}$ (group cohomology), $\operatorname{Ext}^1_R(C,A)=R^1\operatorname{Hom}$ ($\operatorname{RHom}$/$D(R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod})$), $H^n(B;\pi)$ via $\mathsf{sSet}$ cohomology via $\operatorname{EC}$/$\Omega/B$, $\operatorname{Bar}$/$\operatorname{Cobar}$ for $H^2(C,A)$ as $\operatorname{Ext}$, and $\mathsf{sSet}$ twisting operator $\tau$ / $G\dashv\overline{W}$, all already intaken.
  No new cohomology — reuse $\operatorname{Ext}$/$\operatorname{Tor}$/$D$ and $\mathsf{sSet}$/$\tau$.

Intended owners: `categories/groups/short_exact_sequences.py` (`SES` $1\to A\to B\to C\to 1$ with $i: A\hookrightarrow B$, $p: B\twoheadrightarrow C$, `ses.extension_class()` $\in H^2(C,A)$ via $c\in Z^2$; constructor `ses_from_cocycle(A,C,c) -> SES` with middle $A\times_c C$), `categories/algebras/short_exact_sequences.py` (`SES` $0\to A\to B\to C\to 0$ in $R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod}$ via $\xi\in\operatorname{Ext}^1_R(C,A)$ Baer sum; `ses_from_class(C,A,xi) -> SES`), `categories/topology/fibrations.py` + `categories/homotopy/fibration_sequences.py` (`FibrationSequence` $F\to E\to B$ with $\tau: B\to G$ twisting operator, `fib_seq.twisting_class()` $\in[B,B\operatorname{Aut}(F)]$/$H^{n+1}$; constructor `fib_seq_from_twisting(F,B,tau) -> FibrationSequence` with total $F\times_\tau B$), delegating to `categories/derived/ext_tor.py` ($\operatorname{Ext}^1$, $H^2$), `categories/group/cohomology.py` ($H^2(C,A)$ via $\operatorname{Bar}$), `categories/topology/simplicial_sets.py` ($\tau$ Kan condition).
Not a free `extension(A,C,cocycle)` returning bare group — SES $1\to A\to B\to C\to 1$ is the object $B=(A\times C,*_c)$ with $i:A\hookrightarrow B$, $p:B\twoheadrightarrow C$, and $F\to E\to B$ is fibration sequence with class in $H^2$/$H^{n+1}$.

## Desired capability: Lie groups $\to$ Lie algebras, matrix groups as Lie groups, algebraic / Lie / group scheme interfaces, Ad/ad — note 2026-09-15

For $G$ a Lie group, extract $g = \operatorname{Lie}(G)$ its Lie algebra.
All standard matrix groups promoted to Lie groups, $\operatorname{Mat}_{n\times n}$ being the Lie algebra of $\mathrm{GL}_n$, etc. Need good interfaces for both algebraic groups and Lie groups and general group schemes.

* Lie functor: $\operatorname{Lie}: \mathsf{LieGroups} \to \mathsf{LieAlgebras}_R$ (over $R = RR$ or $CC$ where $\mathsf{LieGroups}$ lives) is functor sending $G : \mathsf{LieGroups}$ (smooth manifold $G : \mathsf{Man}$ with smooth $m:G\times G\to G$, $e:\operatorname{Spec} R\to G$, $\operatorname{inv}:G\to G$) to $g = \operatorname{Lie}(G) = T_e G : \mathsf{LieAlgebras}$ with Lie bracket $[X,Y] = [X^L,Y^L]_e$ from left-invariant vector fields $X^L, Y^L \in \Gamma(TG)$ ($X^L_g = dL_g(X)$). Morphism $\varphi: G\to H$ in $\mathsf{LieGroups}$ induces $\operatorname{Lie}(\varphi)=d\varphi_e: g\to h$ Lie algebra homomorphism $[d\varphi_e(X), d\varphi_e(Y)] = d\varphi_e([X,Y])$.
  Need `G.lie_algebra()` $\to g : \mathsf{LieAlgebras}$ and `phi.lie_map()` on morphisms, with $\operatorname{Lie}(G\times H)\cong\operatorname{Lie}(G)\oplus\operatorname{Lie}(H)$, $\operatorname{Lie}(G^{an})$ compatibility for algebraic $G$ below.
  Exponential $\exp_G: g \to G$ via flow of left-invariant field, with $d(\exp)_0 = \mathrm{id}_g$ and $\exp((t+s)X)=\exp(tX)\exp(sX)$, natural $\varphi\circ\exp_G = \exp_H\circ\operatorname{Lie}(\varphi)$.

* Matrix groups promoted to Lie groups (and to $\mathsf{AlgGroups}$/$\mathsf{GrpSch}$): $\mathrm{GL}_n(R) = \{M\in\operatorname{Mat}_{n\times n}(R) \mid \det M \in R^\times\} : \mathsf{LieGroups}$ with underlying manifold $\mathrm{GL}_n(R) \subset \operatorname{Mat}_{n\times n} \cong R^{n^2}$ open, $\operatorname{Lie}(\mathrm{GL}_n)=\operatorname{Mat}_{n\times n} = \mathrm{gl}_n$ with $[A,B]=AB-BA$ as $\operatorname{Mat}$ commutator; $\mathrm{SL}_n(R)=\ker(\det:\mathrm{GL}_n\to G_m)$ $\operatorname{Lie}(\mathrm{SL}_n)=\mathrm{sl}_n=\{A\mid\operatorname{tr} A=0\}$; $O_n(R)=\{M\mid M^tM=I\}$ $\operatorname{Lie}(O_n)=\mathrm{so}_n=\{A\mid A^t=-A\}$; $\mathrm{SO}_n$ identity component; $U_n=\{M\mid M^*\!M=I\}$ $\operatorname{Lie}(U_n)=u_n=\{A\mid A^*=-A\}$; $\mathrm{SU}_n=U_n\cap\mathrm{SL}_n(C)$ $\operatorname{Lie}=\mathrm{su}_n$; $\mathrm{Sp}_{2n}=\{M\mid M^t J M=J\}$ $\operatorname{Lie}=\mathrm{sp}_{2n}$ etc., with correct $R=RR,CC$.
  Each is object $G : \mathsf{LieGroups}$ with `G.underlying_manifold()` $: \mathsf{Man}$, `G.lie_algebra()` $= \mathrm{gl}_n$ etc. as above, not a bare matrix group alias.
  Also as algebraic groups $G : \mathsf{AlgGroups}/k$ ($\mathrm{GL}_n=\operatorname{Spec} k[x_{ij},\det^{-1}]$ etc.) with same $\operatorname{Lie}$ after analytification $G^{an}:\mathsf{LieGroups}$.

* Interfaces for algebraic groups / Lie groups / group schemes: distinct categories with faithful functors:

  - $\mathsf{AlgGroups}_k : \mathsf{Cat}$ — $G : \mathsf{Sch}/k$ smooth affine with $m:G\times G\to G$, $e:\operatorname{Spec} k\to G$, $\operatorname{inv}$ as $\mathsf{Sch}/k$ morphisms, e.g. $\mathrm{GL}_n, \mathrm{SL}_n, \mathrm{SO}_n, \mathrm{Sp}_{2n}, G_m, G_a$, reductive, etc.; $\mathsf{AlgGroups}_k \to \mathsf{LieGroups}$ via analytification $G \mapsto G^{an}$ when $k=RR,CC$ ($G^{an}=G(C)$ with analytic topology), `G^{an}.lie_algebra()` $= \operatorname{Lie}(G)$ as algebraic Lie algebra $\operatorname{Lie}(G)=T_eG$ same $R$-module.

  - $\mathsf{LieGroups} : \mathsf{Cat}$ as above (smooth manifold group); forgetful $\mathsf{LieGroups} \to \mathsf{Groups}$ via underlying abstract group $G \mapsto G^{abs}$.

  - $\mathsf{GrpSch}_S : \mathsf{Cat}$ — group schemes over base $S=\operatorname{Spec} R$ / $\operatorname{Spec} ZZ$ ($G: \mathsf{Sch}/S$ with group law), e.g. $\mu_n, \alpha_p$, $\mathrm{GL}_{n,ZZ}$; base-change $G_R = G\times_S \operatorname{Spec} R : \mathsf{GrpSch}_R$; $\mathsf{GrpSch}_S \to \mathsf{Groups}$ via $G \mapsto G(R)$ points; $\mathsf{AlgGroups}_k \subset \mathsf{GrpSch}_k$ smooth finite-type.
    Base-change functor $(-)_R: \mathsf{GrpSch}_S \to \mathsf{GrpSch}_R$ compatible with $\operatorname{Lie}$ when smooth ($\operatorname{Lie}(G_R)=\operatorname{Lie}(G)\otimes_S R$). Need `G.base_change(R)` $\to G_R$ and `G.analytification()` $\to G^{an}$.

* $\operatorname{Ad}$ and $\operatorname{ad}$: for $G : \mathsf{LieGroups}$ (or $G : \mathsf{AlgGroups}$ smooth) with $g=\operatorname{Lie}(G)$, adjoint representation $\operatorname{Ad}_G: G \to \mathrm{GL}(g) : \mathsf{Groups}$ is group morphism $\operatorname{Ad}_G(g)= d(c_g)_e$ where $c_g: G\to G, c_g(h)=ghg^{-1}$ conjugation, so $\operatorname{Ad}_G(g)(X)= gXg^{-1}$ for matrix groups; on Lie algebra level $\operatorname{ad}_g: g \to \mathrm{gl}(g)$ is $\operatorname{ad}_g(X)(Y)=[X,Y]$ ($\operatorname{ad} = \operatorname{Lie}(\operatorname{Ad})$). Concretely $\operatorname{ad}: \mathsf{LieAlgebras} \to \operatorname{End}(g)$ as Lie algebra morphism $\operatorname{ad}_X = [X,-]$.
  Interface `G.adjoint_representation()` $\to \operatorname{Ad}_G : G \to \operatorname{Aut}(g)$ as $\operatorname{Rep}(G)$ object $(g, \operatorname{Ad}_G) : \operatorname{Rep}(G)$ and `g.adjoint_representation()` $\to \operatorname{ad}_g : g \to \operatorname{End}(g)$ as $\operatorname{Rep}(g)$; for algebraic $G$, $\operatorname{Ad}_G : G \to \mathrm{GL}(g)$ is $\mathsf{AlgGroups}$ morphism (algebraic representation), with same analytification.

Intended owners: `categories/lie/lie_algebras.py` (`LieAlgebra` with $[,-]$, $\operatorname{ad}$), `categories/lie/lie_groups.py` (`LieGroup` with `G.lie_algebra()` $\to g$, $\exp: g\to G$, $\operatorname{Lie}: \mathsf{LieGroups}\to\mathsf{LieAlgebras}$ functor), `categories/groups/matrix_groups.py` ($\mathrm{GL}_n, \mathrm{SL}_n, O_n, \mathrm{SO}_n, U_n, \mathrm{SU}_n, \mathrm{Sp}_{2n}$ as $\mathsf{LieGroups}$ and $\mathsf{AlgGroups}$ with $\operatorname{Lie}(\mathrm{GL}_n)=\operatorname{Mat}_{n\times n}=\mathrm{gl}_n$, $\operatorname{Lie}(\mathrm{SL}_n)=\mathrm{sl}_n$ etc.), `categories/groups/algebraic_groups.py` ($\mathsf{AlgGroups}$ with $G^{an}$), `categories/groups/group_schemes.py` ($\mathsf{GrpSch}$ with $G_R$ base-change), `categories/representations/adjoint.py` ($\operatorname{Ad}_G: G\to\mathrm{GL}(g)$, $\operatorname{ad}_g: g\to\mathrm{gl}(g)$ with $\operatorname{ad}=\operatorname{Lie}(\operatorname{Ad})$). Not free `lie_algebra(G)` on bare matrix group — `G.lie_algebra()` on the $\mathsf{LieGroups}$/$\mathsf{GrpSch}$ object with $\operatorname{Mat}_{n\times n}$ as $\operatorname{Lie}(\mathrm{GL}_n)$.

## Desired capability: group cohomology pairings via composition morphisms, Zariski tangent of character varieties as group cohomology — note 2026-09-15

Use composition morphisms to define pairings in group cohomology.
Identify Zariski tangent space of character varieties with group cohomology per Goldman / https://www.math.umd.edu/~wmg/SymplecticNature.pdf.

* Pairings in group cohomology via composition morphisms: for $G : \mathsf{Groups}$ (discrete, finitely presented $G=\langle S\mid R\rangle$, profinite, Lie) and $M,N,P : ZZ[G]\text{-}\mathsf{Mod}$ ($G$-modules as $\mathsf{Ab}$ with $G$-action $\rho: G\to\operatorname{Aut}(M)$), the $\operatorname{Ext}$ description $H^n(G,M)=\operatorname{Ext}^n_{ZZ[G]}(ZZ,M)=H^n(\operatorname{RHom}_{ZZ[G]}(ZZ,M))$ already intaken via $\operatorname{BarRes} \to \operatorname{RHom}$ in $D(ZZ[G]\text{-}\mathsf{Mod})$.
  Pairings are not ad-hoc $H^p\otimes H^q\to H^{p+q}$ numbers; they are induced by composition of morphisms in $D$:

  - Cup product / Yoneda composition: $\operatorname{RHom}(ZZ,M) \otimes^L \operatorname{RHom}(ZZ,N) \to \operatorname{RHom}(ZZ, M\otimes N)$ via $\operatorname{Hom}_{ZZ[G]}(P_\bullet,M) \otimes \operatorname{Hom}_{ZZ[G]}(P_\bullet,N) \to \operatorname{Hom}_{ZZ[G]}(P_\bullet, M\otimes N)$ using Alexander-Whitney $P_\bullet \to P_\bullet\otimes P_\bullet$ on bar $B_\bullet(G)=ZZ[G^{n+1}]$ (coalgebra diagonal) and tensor $M\otimes N$ with diagonal $G$-action $g\cdot(m\otimes n)=gm\otimes gn$; on cochains $(f\cup g)(g_1,\ldots,g_{p+q}) = f(g_1,\ldots,g_p) \otimes g_{p+1}\cdot g(g_{p+1},\ldots,g_{p+q})$ etc., giving $\cup: H^p(G,M)\otimes H^q(G,N)\to H^{p+q}(G,M\otimes N)$.

  - Composition $\operatorname{Ext}^p(B,C)\otimes\operatorname{Ext}^q(A,B)\to\operatorname{Ext}^{p+q}(A,C)$ as Yoneda splice $\xi\circ\eta = \xi[ q]\circ\eta$ in $D$; for $A=B=C=ZZ$ with coefficients, this is same $\cup$.

  - Internal Hom composition $\operatorname{Hom}(M,N)\otimes\operatorname{Hom}(L,M)\to\operatorname{Hom}(L,N)$ induces $\operatorname{RHom}$ composition $\operatorname{RHom}(M,N)\otimes^L \operatorname{RHom}(L,M)\to\operatorname{RHom}(L,N)$ and on $H^0$ the $\operatorname{Ext}$ pairing $\operatorname{Ext}^p\otimes\operatorname{Ext}^q\to\operatorname{Ext}^{p+q}$.
    Interface `G.cohomology_pairing(p,q,M,N)` / `M.cup_product(N)` as morphism $H^p(G,M)\otimes H^q(G,N)\to H^{p+q}(G,M\otimes N)$ via $\operatorname{BarRes}$ $\cup$ with $M\otimes N$ diagonal, delegating to $\operatorname{Ext}$ composition already in $D$.

* Zariski tangent of character varieties / Betti moduli as $H^1$: for $\pi : \mathsf{Groups}$ finitely presented ($\pi=\pi_1(X,x)$ etc., $\pi=\langle S\mid R\rangle$ via `Framed` already) and $G : \mathsf{AlgGroups}$ / $\mathsf{LieGroups}$ reductive ($\mathrm{GL}_n, \mathrm{SL}_n, \mathrm{PGL}_n, \mathrm{Sp}_{2n}, O_n, G_2$ etc.) with representation scheme $\operatorname{Rep}(\pi,G)=\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathsf{Groups}}(\pi,G) \subset G^S$ cut by $r((g_s))=1\ \forall r\in R$, point $\rho: \pi\to G$ i.e. $\rho \in \operatorname{Rep}(\pi,G)(k)$ as object $\rho: \operatorname{Hom}(\pi,G)$, and character variety $X(\pi,G)=\operatorname{Rep}(\pi,G)/\!/G : \mathsf{Sch}/k$ `GIT` quotient by conjugation already intaken.
  The Zariski tangent at $\rho$ is

  $$
  \begin{aligned}
  T_\rho \operatorname{Rep}(\pi,G) &\cong Z^1(\pi, g_{\operatorname{Ad}\circ\rho}) && (\text{1-cocycles } u: \pi\to g,\ u(gh)=u(g)+\operatorname{Ad}_{\rho(g)}u(h)) \\
  T_\rho (G\cdot\rho) &\cong B^1(\pi, g_{\operatorname{Ad}\circ\rho}) && (\text{1-coboundaries } u(g)=\operatorname{Ad}_{\rho(g)}X - X \text{ for } X\in g) \\
  T_{[\rho]} X(\pi,G) &\cong H^1(\pi, g_{\operatorname{Ad}\circ\rho}) = Z^1/B^1 && \text{when } \rho \text{ has closed orbit / stable (and } \cong H^1 \text{ at smooth points)}
  \end{aligned}
  $$

  where $g_{\operatorname{Ad}\circ\rho} : \pi\text{-}\mathsf{Mod}$ is $g = \operatorname{Lie}(G)$ as $\pi$-module via $\pi \xrightarrow{\rho} G \xrightarrow{\operatorname{Ad}_G} \mathrm{GL}(g)$, i.e. $\operatorname{Ad}\circ\rho: \pi\to\mathrm{GL}(g)$ ($\operatorname{Ad}_G$ already above).
  For $G$ algebraic over $k$ char 0, this is Goldman's theorem: $T_{[\rho]} M_B(X,G) \cong H^1(\pi_1(X), g_{\operatorname{Ad}\circ\rho})$ as $k$-vector space, with symplectic form $\omega_{\mathrm{Goldman}}$ on $H^1$ via cup $\cup$ above composed with invariant pairing $B: g\otimes g\to k$ ($B(X,Y)=\operatorname{tr}(\operatorname{ad}_X \operatorname{ad}_Y)$ Killing) and Poincaré $H^1\otimes H^1 \to H^2(\pi, k) \cong k$ when $X$ surface (Theorem 1.1 of https://www.math.umd.edu/~wmg/SymplecticNature.pdf).
  Need `rho.adjoint_module()` $\to g_{\operatorname{Ad}\circ\rho} : \pi\text{-}\mathsf{Mod}$ (as $ZZ[\pi]\text{-}\mathsf{Mod}$ via $\operatorname{Ad}\circ\rho$) already via $\operatorname{Rep}$, then $H^1(\pi, g_{\operatorname{Ad}\circ\rho})$ via group cohomology $H^1(G,M)$ already intaken ($\operatorname{BarRes}\to\operatorname{Ext}$). Interface `rho.zariski_tangent()` $\to H^1(\pi, g_{\operatorname{Ad}\circ\rho}) : \mathsf{Vect}_k$, `rho.cocycles()` $\to Z^1$, `rho.coboundaries()` $\to B^1$, and `X(pi,G).tangent_at([rho])` $\to H^1$ with Goldman symplectic $H^1\otimes H^1 \to H^2 \to k$ via $\cup$ + $B$.

Intended owners: `categories/group/cohomology.py` (`cup_product` / `yoneda_product` $H^p\otimes H^q\to H^{p+q}$ via $\operatorname{Hom}\otimes\operatorname{Hom}\to\operatorname{Hom}$ composition $\cup$ from $\operatorname{BarRes}$ $P_\bullet\to P_\bullet\otimes P_\bullet$ and $M\otimes N$ diagonal, $\operatorname{Ext}^p\otimes\operatorname{Ext}^q\to\operatorname{Ext}^{p+q}$), `categories/representations/adjoint.py` ($\operatorname{Ad}_G$ already, $\operatorname{Ad}\circ\rho$ as $\pi$-module $g_{\operatorname{Ad}\circ\rho}$ via $\rho:\pi\to G$), `categories/moduli/character_varieties.py` ($\operatorname{Rep}(\pi,G) \subset G^S$, $X(\pi,G)=\operatorname{Rep}/\!/G$, `rho.zariski_tangent()` $\to Z^1/B^1 = H^1(\pi,g_{\operatorname{Ad}\circ\rho})$ with `Goldman symplectic` via cup+B). Not free `tangent_space(rho)` number — `rho.zariski_tangent()` as $H^1$ group cohomology with $g_{\operatorname{Ad}\circ\rho}$ coefficients via $\operatorname{BarRes}$ and Yoneda $\cup$.

## Desired capability: de Rham cohomology with coefficients in a flat vector bundle / local system (Bott & Tu), honest K(G,n) — note 2026-09-15

Honest constructions of $K(G,n)$ spaces, at least for $n=1$ and common groups $G$ where e.g. $K(G,1)\cong BG$; obtain higher $K$ spaces by loops/suspension/etc.
Plus de Rham cohomology with coefficients in flat vector bundles and cohomology with coefficients in local systems, following Bott & Tu.

* de Rham cohomology with coefficients in a flat vector bundle: for $X : \mathsf{Man}$ smooth manifold (or $X : \mathsf{SmSch}/k$ with $k=RR,CC$) and $(E,\nabla)$ flat vector bundle $E : \mathsf{VecBun}(X)$ rank $r$ with $\nabla: E\to E\otimes\Omega^1_X$ flat $F_\nabla=\nabla^2=0 : E\to E\otimes\Omega^2$ (i.e. $(E,\nabla): \mathsf{FlatConn}(X)$ already intaken), the twisted de Rham complex is

  $$
  \Omega^*(X;E,\nabla) = ( \Gamma(X, E\otimes\Omega^*_X), d_\nabla )
  $$

  with $d_\nabla: E\otimes\Omega^q \to E\otimes\Omega^{q+1}$ defined in trivialization $\nabla=d+A$ by $d_\nabla(s\otimes\omega)=\nabla(s)\wedge\omega + s\otimes d\omega = ds\otimes\omega + s\otimes d\omega + A\wedge s\otimes\omega$, satisfying $d_\nabla^2 = F_\nabla\wedge- =0$ iff $\nabla$ flat.
  Then $H^*_{dR}(X;E,\nabla) = H^*(\Omega^*(X;E), d_\nabla) : \mathsf{GrMod}$ as graded module $\bigoplus_q H^q_{dR}(X;\nabla)$.
  For trivial $\nabla=d$ this is $H^*_{dR}(X)\otimes E_x$.
  Flatness is the condition for $d_\nabla$ to be a complex; without it $d_\nabla^2\neq 0$ and cohomology is not defined.
  Interface `nabla.de_rham_complex()` $\to \Omega^*(X;E) : \mathsf{Ch}$ with $d_\nabla$, and `nabla.de_rham_cohomology(q)` $\to H^q_{dR}(X;\nabla) : \mathsf{Vect}$ as $H^q$ of that $\mathsf{Ch}$, with `nabla.is_flat()` gate $F_\nabla=0$ already.

* Cohomology with coefficients in a local system following Bott & Tu: for $L : \mathsf{LocSys}(X)$ locally constant sheaf of $k$-vector spaces ($L : \mathsf{Sh}(X^{an})$ with $L|_{U_\alpha}\cong k^{r}$), with equivalence $\mathsf{LocSys}(X^{an}) \simeq \mathsf{FlatConn}^{rs}(X)$ via Riemann-Hilbert already intaken ($L = \ker \nabla$ as $C$-local system, $E = L\otimes O^{an}$), the cohomology with local coefficients is sheaf cohomology $H^*(X;L) = R\Gamma(X,L) = H^*(X^{an}, L) : \mathsf{GrMod}$ ($R\Gamma = R(p_*)$ from six functors).
  For manifold $X$ with good cover $U=\{U_\alpha\}$ (finite open cover with all $U_{\alpha_0\ldots\alpha_p}$ contractible), Bott & Tu compute it via Čech-de Rham double complex $K^{p,q}= \check{C}^p(U, \Omega^q\otimes L)$ / $C^p(U)\otimes L$ with $D = d_\nabla + \delta_{\check{C}}$ and $H^*(X;L) \cong H^*(\operatorname{Tot}(K), D)$.
  De Rham theorem with local coefficients is the quasi-isomorphism $L \to \Omega^*(X;E,\nabla)$ as resolution $0\to L\to E \xrightarrow{\nabla} E\otimes\Omega^1 \xrightarrow{d_\nabla} \cdots$, giving $H^*_{dR}(X;\nabla) \cong H^*(X^{an};L)$ as $\mathsf{GrMod}$ isomorphism natural in $(E,\nabla)$.
  Interface `L.cohomology(q)` $\to H^q(X;L)$ via $R\Gamma$, and `nabla.de_rham_cohomology(q)` $\cong$ `L.cohomology(q)` comparison as isomorphism in $\mathsf{Vect}$, with Bott & Tu Čech-de Rham as effective $\operatorname{Tot}$ when $U$ finite good cover (reuse `EffHom` Čech already intaken).
  Not free `local_system_cohomology(L)` number — `L.cohomology()` on the $\mathsf{LocSys}$ object and `nabla.de_rham_cohomology()` on $\mathsf{FlatConn}$ with comparison `nabla.local_system().cohomology()` $\cong$ `nabla.de_rham_cohomology()`.

* Honest $K(G,n)$ spaces: for $G : \mathsf{Groups}$ (abelian when $n\geq 2$, arbitrary when $n=1$) $K(G,n) : \mathsf{HoTop}$ is Eilenberg-MacLane with $\pi_n(K(G,n))=G$, $\pi_{\neq n}=0$.
  At least for $n=1$ and common $G$ where $K(G,1)\cong BG$: $K(G,1)=BG$ for any discrete $G$ via $BG = B G = |\overline{W}(N G)|$ already intaken as classifying space `G.classifying_space()` $\to BG : \mathsf{HoTop}$ with $\Omega BG\simeq G$, $EG\to BG$.
  Common groups include $G = Z, Z/n, Z/2, S_n, B_n, F_n, \pi_1 \Sigma_g, \mathrm{GL}_n(Z)$ etc. with already intaken concrete $BG$ models $RP^\infty=B(Z/2)$, $CP^\infty=B(Z)=K(Z,2)$ no — $CP^\infty=K(Z,2)$ is $n=2$, $L_p^\infty=B(Z/p)$, $\operatorname{Gr}_n(C^\infty)=B U(n)$ not $K$, $BF_n=\bigvee^n S^1 =K(F_n,1)$ etc. So $K(G,1)$ owner reuses `ClassifyingSpace` already: $K(G,1) := BG$.
  Higher $K(G,n)$ obtained by loops/suspension/delooping:

  - Looping: $\Omega K(G,n) \simeq K(G,n-1)$ as $\mathsf{HoTop}$ ($\Omega$ already intaken via $G \dashv \overline{W}$ loop $\Omega = G$ simplicial or $\operatorname{Map}_*(S^1,-)$), so $K(G,n-1) = \Omega K(G,n)$ when $K(G,n)$ already has $\operatorname{EC}$ effective (Kenzo `loop-space`).

  - Suspension/delooping: $K(G,n) \simeq B K(G,n-1)$ as classifying space of the simplicial abelian group $K(G,n-1)$, i.e. $K(G,n)=\overline{W} K(G,n-1)$ via bar $B = \overline{W}$ already intaken ($\operatorname{Bar}$/$\overline{W}$), inductively $K(G,n)=B^n G$ as $n$-fold delooping $B^n(K(G,0))$ with $K(G,0)=G$ discrete, $K(Z,n)$ via $B^n Z$.
