  - Equivalently via Dold-Kan: $K(G,n)$ as $\Gamma( G[n] )$ where $G[n]$ is chain complex with $G$ in degree $n$, via $\operatorname{DK}: \mathsf{Ch}_{\geq0}(\mathsf{Ab}) \to \mathsf{sAb} \to \mathsf{sSet}$ forgetting to $\mathsf{sSet}$.
    Interface `EilenbergMacLane(G,n)` $\to K(G,n) : \mathsf{HoTop}$ with $K(G,1)=BG$ reuse, and `K(G,n).loop_space()` $\to K(G,n-1)$, `K(G,n).delooping()` $\to K(G,n+1) = B K(G,n)$, and $\Omega K(G,n) \cong K(G,n-1)$ as natural isomorphism in $\mathsf{HoTop}$ with $\pi_*$ verification $\pi_n(K(G,n))=G$ via effective homology $\operatorname{EC}(K(G,n))$.

Intended owners: `categories/de_rham/de_rham_coeff.py` (`FlatConn.de_rham_complex()` $\to \Omega^*(E,\nabla)$ with $d_\nabla$, $H^*_{dR}(\nabla)$), `categories/sheaves/local_systems.py` (`LocalSystem.cohomology()` $\to H^*(X;L)=R\Gamma(L)$ with Bott & Tu Čech-de Rham $\operatorname{Tot}$ and comparison $H^*_{dR}(\nabla)\cong H^*(L)$), `categories/topology/eilenberg_maclane.py` ($K(G,n)=B^n G$ with $\Omega K(G,n)\simeq K(G,n-1)$ via $B=\overline{W}$/$\Omega=G$, $K(G,1)=BG$ reusing `ClassifyingSpace`, Dold-Kan $\Gamma(G[n])$), delegating to `categories/topology/classifying_spaces.py` ($BG$), `categories/topology/simplicial_sets.py` ($\Omega$, $\overline{W}$), `categories/topology/effective_homology.py` ($\operatorname{EC}$ for $K(G,n)$). Not free `k_g_n(G,n)` — `EilenbergMacLane(G,n)` on the group object with `K(G,1)=G.classifying_space()` reuse.

## Desired capability: modular forms, automorphic forms, factors of automorphy, automorphic bundles — note 2026-09-16

Need symbolic modular forms, automorphic forms, factors of automorphy, and associated automorphic bundles when explicit and computable.

* Modular form is not a $q$-series alone.
  For $\Gamma \leq \mathrm{SL}_2(RR)$ Fuchsian (e.g. $\Gamma=\mathrm{SL}_2(ZZ)$, $\Gamma(N),\Gamma_1(N),\Gamma_0(N)$) and weight $k : ZZ$ (or $k\in\tfrac12 ZZ$ with multiplier), $f: HH\to C$ holomorphic with $f(\gamma z)=j(\gamma,z)^k f(z)$ for $j: \Gamma\times HH\to C^\times$ factor of automorphy $j(\gamma,z)=cz+d$ ($\gamma=(a\ b;c\ d)$) and $j(\gamma_1\gamma_2,z)=j(\gamma_1,\gamma_2z)j(\gamma_2,z)$ cocycle, plus holomorphy at cusps $f|_k \gamma$ bounded as $\operatorname{Im} z\to\infty$.
  Interface must be `ModularForm(Gamma,k,j)` as section $f : HH\to C$ with `f.automorphy_factor()` $\to j$ and `f.slash(k,gamma)`; $f$ is object with parent $M_k(\Gamma)$.
  $q$-expansion $f(z)=\sum_{n\geq 0} a_n q^n$, $q=e^{2\pi iz}$, is `FormalPowerSeries` coefficient view `f.q_expansion()` $\to \sum a_n q^n : R[[q]]$, not the definition.

* Factor of automorphy is not a function $j(z)$.
  For $\Gamma$ acting on $HH$ via $\gamma\cdot z=(az+b)/(cz+d)$, a factor is $j: \Gamma\times HH\to\mathrm{GL}(V)$ holomorphic with cocycle $j(\gamma_1\gamma_2,z)=j(\gamma_1,\gamma_2z)j(\gamma_2,z)$; weight $k$ is $j_k(\gamma,z)=(cz+d)^k$, Nebentypus $\chi$ is $j_{k,\chi}=\chi(d)(cz+d)^k$.
  Symbolic factors means $j$ as object `FactorOfAutomorphy(Gamma,HH,V)` with `j.cocycle(gamma_1,gamma_2,z)` test, not a bare exponent $k$.
  Composition of factors $j_1\otimes j_2$ is tensor of $\mathrm{GL}$ representations.

* Automorphic form generalization: for $G$ reductive $G=\mathrm{SL}_2, \mathrm{Sp}_{2g}, U(p,q), \mathrm{GL}_n$ with symmetric space $X=G/K$ ($HH=\mathrm{SL}_2(RR)/\mathrm{SO}(2)$), $\Gamma\subset G(Q)$ arithmetic, $j: \Gamma\times X\to\mathrm{GL}(V)$ factor as above, automorphic form is $F: G\to C$ with $F(\gamma g)=j(\gamma,g\cdot o)F(g)$, $F(gk)=\sigma(k)^{-1}F(g)$ for $K$-type $\sigma$, plus growth/K-finiteness.
  When $G=\mathrm{SL}_2$, this specializes to $f$ via $F(g)=j(g,o)^{-k} f(g\cdot o)$.
  Interface `AutomorphicForm(G,Gamma,j,sigma)` on the group $G$, not just $HH$.

* Associated automorphic bundle is not a $\mathsf{VecBun}$ with no $\Gamma$.
  For $j: \Gamma\times HH\to\mathrm{GL}(V)$ factor, bundle is $V_j = (HH\times V)/\Gamma$ where $\Gamma$ acts by $\gamma\cdot(z,v)=(\gamma z, j(\gamma,z)v)$ as $\mathsf{VecBun}$ over $X(\Gamma)=\Gamma\backslash HH$ (or $\Gamma\backslash G/K$ generally), with $\Gamma(HH,V_j) \cong \{f: HH\to V \mid f(\gamma z)=j(\gamma,z)f(z)\}$ modular forms as $H^0(X(\Gamma),V_j)$.
  When $j=j_k$, $V_j = \omega^{\otimes k}$ power of Hodge bundle $\omega = e^*\Omega^1_{E/X(\Gamma)}$ for universal elliptic $E\to X(\Gamma)$.
  Explicit and computable means transition $j$ on good cover $U_\alpha\subset X(\Gamma)$ computable via $cz+d$, with $V_j|_{U_\alpha}\cong O_{U_\alpha}\otimes V$.
  Interface `AutomorphicBundle(Gamma,j)` $\to V_j : \mathsf{VecBun}(X(\Gamma))$ with `V_j.sections() = M(Gamma,j)`; `ModularForms` app is `X(Gamma).automorphic_bundle(j).global_sections()`.

Intended owners: `categories/modular/forms.py` (`ModularForm(Gamma,k)` with $f(\gamma z)=j(\gamma,z)^k f(z)$, `q_expansion`), `categories/automorphic/forms.py` (`AutomorphicForm(G,Gamma,j)`), `categories/automorphic/factors.py` (`FactorOfAutomorphy` with cocycle $j(\gamma_1\gamma_2,z)=j(\gamma_1,\gamma_2z)j(\gamma_2,z)$), `categories/automorphic/bundles.py` (`AutomorphicBundle` $V_j=(HH\times V)/\Gamma : \mathsf{VecBun}(X(\Gamma))$ with $H^0 = M(\Gamma,j)$). Not a free `modular_form(q_series)` — `M_k(Gamma).element(q_series)` with automorphy $j$ retained.

## Desired capability: spaces of modular forms, bases, Eisenstein, q-expansions, containment and sampling — note 2026-09-16

Need $M_k(\Gamma)$ spaces, computable dimensions/bases (Eisenstein), $q$-expansions, weight/level extraction, and ability to express a given form via basis through sampling, plus containment testing.

* Space $M_k(\Gamma) : \mathsf{Vect}$ (over $Q$, $QQbar$, $C$) is not a Python `list` of $q$-series.
  For $\Gamma\leq\mathrm{SL}_2(ZZ)$ congruence ($\Gamma(N),\Gamma_1(N),\Gamma_0(N)$) or general finite-index Fuchsian, $M_k(\Gamma) = \{f: HH\to C \mid f|_k \gamma = f\ \forall\gamma\in\Gamma, \text{ holomorphic at cusps}\}$ as $\mathsf{Vect}$ with $M_k(\Gamma)\subset O(HH)$, graded ring $M_*(\Gamma)=\bigoplus_k M_k(\Gamma)$ with multiplication $M_k\otimes M_\ell\to M_{k+\ell}$ ($f\cdot g$), filtration by order at cusps $S_k\subset M_k$ cuspidal ($a_0=0$ at all cusps).
  Similarly `AutomorphicForms(G,Gamma,j)` as $\mathsf{Vect}$.
  Interface `Gamma.modular_forms(k)` $\to M_k(\Gamma) : \mathsf{Vect}$ and `M_k(Gamma).dimension()` $\to \dim : NN$ via genus formula / Riemann-Roch / trace (see modular curves below), basis `M_k(Gamma).basis()` $\to [b_i : M_k]$.

* Dimensions when known: $\dim M_k(\mathrm{SL}_2(ZZ))$ via $k\equiv 0 \bmod 4$ etc., $\dim M_k(\Gamma_0(N))$, $\dim S_k$ via `Riemann-Roch` on $X(\Gamma)$: $M_k(\Gamma) \cong H^0(X(\Gamma), \omega^{\otimes k}\otimes O(\ldots\text{cusps}\ldots))$ with $\omega$ Hodge, $\dim = \deg(\omega^{\otimes k})+1-g + h^1$ via `RR` / `Hirzebruch-RR` already intaken, plus trace formula `Eichler-Selberg`. Not enumerated by brute enumeration of `q`-series.

* Bases when known, e.g. Eisenstein series: $E_k(z)= \tfrac12\zeta(1-k)^{-1} \sum_{(c,d)=1} (cz+d)^{-k} = 1 - (2k/B_k) \sum_{n\geq 1} \sigma_{k-1}(n)q^n$ for even $k\geq 4$, $E_k\in M_k(\mathrm{SL}_2(ZZ))$ (normalized $E_4, E_6$ generate $M_*=C[E_4,E_6]$), $E_k^{\chi,\psi}$ with characters ($\chi\psi(-1)=(-1)^k$) level $N$, $E_k(N)\in M_k(\Gamma_0(N))$, oldforms $E_k(dz)$.
  Symbolic formulas and series: `E_k.q_expansion(N_terms)` $\to \sum_{n<N} a_n q^n$ as $R[[q]]$ truncated image of $R[[q]]=\lim R[q]/(q^N)$ already intaken.
  Interface `EisensteinSeries(k)` $\to E_k : M_k$, `EisensteinSeries(k,N,chi)` $\to E_{k,\chi}$, with `E_k.q_expansion()` via $\sigma_{k-1}$ divisor sum (not numeric guess).

* Weight and level extraction: for $f \in O(HH)$ presented as $q$-series or function, `f.weight()` asks $k$ with $f|_k \gamma =j(\gamma,z)^{-k}f(\gamma z)$ testing $k\in ZZ$, `f.level()` $\to N : NN$ minimal $N$ with $f\in M_k(\Gamma_0(N)) \cap \cdots$ (or $\Gamma_1(N)$, $\Gamma(N)$) via Sturm bound test $f|_k \gamma = f$ on generators of $\Gamma(N)$.
  Returns $k : ZZ$, $N : NN$ (or `Unknown` when not modular/automorphic), with certificate via generators $S=(0\ {-1};1\ 0)$, $T=(1\ 1;0\ 1)$ etc., not by string tag.

* Containment testing `is_modular`, maybe discovering modularity/automorphicity/quasi-modularity by search: given $f : HH\to C$ or $F :$ `FormalPowerSeries` ($\sum a_n q^n$), predicate $f \in M_k(\Gamma)$ is tested via `f.slash(k,gamma) == f` on generating set $\operatorname{gens}(\Gamma) \subset \mathrm{SL}_2(ZZ)$ ($S,T$ plus $\Gamma_0(N)$ coset reps) up to Sturm bound, plus cusp holomorphy ($a_n=0$ for $n<0$ in each $f|_k \alpha$, $\alpha\in\mathrm{SL}_2(ZZ)$). Discovery/search means when weight/level unknown, enumerate candidate $(k,N)$ bounded by conductor estimates ($k\leq K_{\max}$, $N\mid N_{\max}$) and test $f\in M_k(\Gamma_0(N))$ via equality in $R[[q]]/(q^{\operatorname{Sturm}(N,k)})$, returning minimal $(k,N)$ with success or `Unknown`. Quasi-modular $E_2$ is $f|_2 \gamma = f + (6/\pi i) c/(cz+d)$ as extension $\mathrm{QMod} = M_* \oplus M_*\cdot E_2 \oplus \cdots$ with derivation $D=q\, d/dq$ (Ramanujan).
  Interface `f.is_modular(Gamma,k) -> Bool` with witness `Sturm` bound, `f.find_level(K_max,N_max)` $\to (k,N)$ / `Unknown`, `f.is_quasimodular()` $\to (M_{k-2} \text{ component})$ via $E_2$.

* Expressing a given form as linear combination of basis forms via pointwise / $q$-coefficient evaluations: for known $M_k(\Gamma)$ with $d=\dim M_k$ basis $[b_i]_{i<d} : \mathsf{GrMod}$ and $f\in M_k(\Gamma)$, there exist $c_i \in k$ with $f = \sum c_i b_i$ unique.
  Determining how many evaluations are needed: Sturm bound $\operatorname{Sturm}(k,\Gamma)$ ($k\cdot[\mathrm{SL}_2(ZZ):\Gamma]/12$ etc.) is $N_0$ such that $a_n(b_i)$ for $n<N_0$ determines $f$, i.e. $N_0 = \dim M_k(\Gamma)$-many $q$-coefficients suffice generically, more precisely $N_0 = \operatorname{Sturm}(k,\Gamma)$ guarantees equality as $R[[q]]$ elements; pointwise $f(z_j)$ at $d$ generic $z_j\in HH$ with $z_i\neq z_j$ gives Vandermonde $V_{j,i}=b_i(z_j)$ invertible for Zariski-generic $z_j$.
  Interface `M_k(Gamma).coordinates(f)` $\to (c_i)$ via linear solve $V\cdot c = [f(z_j)]$ or $A\cdot c = [a_n(f)]$ where $A_{n,i}=a_n(b_i) \in \operatorname{Mat}_{N_0\times d}$, communicating required count: `M_k(Gamma).sturm_bound()` $\to N_0 : NN$, `M_k(Gamma).num_evaluations_needed()` $\to d : NN$, and method `f.as_linear_combination()` returns $[c_i]$ with certificate `Sturm` or condition number of $V$.
  Search/discovery variant uses same bound to certify modularity.

Intended owners: `categories/modular/spaces.py` ($M_k(\Gamma) : \mathsf{Vect}$ with `dimension` via `RR` on $X(\Gamma)$, `basis` with `Eisenstein`+`cusp`), `categories/modular/eisenstein.py` ($E_k, E_{k,\chi} \to M_k$ with $\sigma_{k-1}$ q-expansion $\sum a_n q^n$), `categories/modular/q_expansions.py` (`q_expansion` $: R[[q]]$ via $R[[q]]=\lim R[q]/(q^n)$), `categories/modular/containment.py` (`is_modular`, `find_level`, `is_quasimodular`, `Sturm`), `categories/rings/formal_power_series.py` ($R[[q]]$ $(q)$-adic).
Not a free `is_modular_series(series)` number — `M_k(Gamma).contains(f)` with `Sturm` and $f \in M_k(\Gamma)$ as sections of $V_{j_k}$.

## Desired capability: congruence subgroups, arithmetic subgroups, modular curves and their invariants, fundamental domains — note 2026-09-16

Need congruence subgroups and arithmetic subgroups as first-class objects, geometric models of modular curves of various levels with algebro-geometric invariants, and real fundamental domains as Euclidean/spherical/hyperbolic polytopes with visualization in low dimensions.

* Congruence subgroups as first-class objects: for $N : NN$, $\Gamma(N)=\ker(\pi_N: \mathrm{SL}_2(ZZ)\to\mathrm{SL}_2(ZZ/N))$ principal, $\Gamma_1(N)=\{ (a\ b;c\ d)\in\mathrm{SL}_2(ZZ) \mid a\equiv d\equiv 1, c\equiv 0 \bmod N\}$, $\Gamma_0(N)=\{c\equiv 0 \bmod N\}$, and $\Gamma(N)\triangleleft\Gamma_1(N)\triangleleft\Gamma_0(N)\triangleleft\mathrm{SL}_2(ZZ)$ with indices $[\mathrm{SL}_2(ZZ):\Gamma(N)]=N^3\prod_{p\mid N}(1-1/p^2)$, $[\mathrm{SL}_2:\Gamma_0]=N\prod_{p\mid N}(1+1/p)$.
  Object $\Gamma : \mathsf{CongruenceSubgroups}$ with `Gamma.level()` $\to N$, `Gamma.index()` $\to [\mathrm{SL}_2:\Gamma]$, `Gamma.generators()` $\to [S,T,\ldots]$ finite generating set, `Gamma.cusps()` $\to P^1(Q)/\Gamma$ coset enumeration, `Gamma.coset_reps()` $\to \mathrm{SL}_2(ZZ)/\Gamma$, membership $\gamma\in\Gamma$ decidable via $\gamma \bmod N$.
  Interface `Gamma0(N)`, `Gamma1(N)`, `Gamma(N)` constructors as $\mathsf{Subgroups}(\mathrm{SL}_2(ZZ))$ with $\operatorname{Sub}(G)$ lattice, not bare integers $N$.

* Arithmetic subgroups in general: for semisimple $G/Q$ (e.g. $\mathrm{SL}_2, \mathrm{Sp}_{2g}, \mathrm{SL}_n, U(p,q), \mathrm{SO}(p,q)$), $G(ZZ)=G\cap\mathrm{GL}_n(ZZ)$ and $\Gamma\subset G(Q)$ commensurable with $G(ZZ)$ (finite index in $G(ZZ)\cap gG(ZZ)g^{-1}$) as $\mathsf{ArithmeticSubgroups}(G) : \operatorname{Sub}(G(Q))$ with $\Gamma$ neat/cofinite, $\Gamma\backslash G/K$ finite volume.
  Includes $\mathrm{SL}_2(ZZ)$, $\Gamma\subset\mathrm{Sp}_{2g}(ZZ)$ Siegel, $\mathrm{SL}_n(ZZ)$, Bianchi $\mathrm{SL}_2(O_K)$ for imaginary quadratic $K$ acting on $HH^3$.
  Interface `ArithmeticSubgroup(G, level)` with `Gamma.ambient_group()` $\to G$, `Gamma.congruence_closure()`, `Gamma.is_congruence() -> Bool` (congruence subgroup problem), commensurator.

* Geometric models of modular curves of various levels: for $\Gamma$ as above, $Y(\Gamma)=\Gamma\backslash HH : \mathsf{RiemannSurface}$ (orbifold when $-I\in\Gamma$ elliptic points) and compactified $X(\Gamma)=\Gamma\backslash HH^*$ with $HH^*=HH\cup P^1(Q)$ adding cusps $C_\Gamma=P^1(Q)/\Gamma$ finite set, as smooth projective curve $X(\Gamma) : \mathsf{Curve}/Q$ (canonical model over $Q$ via $j$ and $j_N$, over $ZZ[1/N]$ integral model).
  Concrete models: $X(1)=P^1_j$ via $j$, $X_0(N)=\Gamma_0(N)\backslash HH^*$ via $q$-expansion at $\infty$, $X_1(N)$, $X(N)$ via `Tate` / `Katz-Mazur` moduli $E\to S$ elliptic with level structure $\alpha: (ZZ/N)^2 \simeq E[N]$ etc. $X(N)$ moduli functor $F_N(S)=\{E/S \text{ with } \alpha\}$ representable by $X(N)$ when $N\geq 3$.
  Interface `ModularCurve(Gamma)` $\to X(\Gamma) : \mathsf{SmoothProjectiveCurve}$ with $X(\Gamma)(C) \cong \Gamma\backslash HH^*$ as $\mathsf{AnSpaces}$, `X(Gamma).integral_model()` over $ZZ[1/N]$ when $\Gamma=\Gamma(N)$.

* Algebro-geometric invariants, known algorithms and formulas: $\text{genus } g(X(\Gamma)) = 1 + [\mathrm{SL}_2:\Gamma]/12 - e_2/4 - e_3/3 - c/2$ with $e_2,e_3$ elliptic points, $c=|C_\Gamma|$ cusps (for $\Gamma\subset\mathrm{SL}_2(ZZ)$; general via Riemann-Hurwitz $X(\Gamma)\to X(1)=P^1$). For $\Gamma_0(N)$, explicit $g(N)=1+\mu(N)/12 - \nu_2/4 - \nu_3/3 - \nu_\infty/2$ with $\mu=[\mathrm{SL}_2:\Gamma_0]$, $\nu_2,\nu_3,\nu_\infty$ via divisor counts of $N$.
  Arithmetic genus $p_a$ vs geometric $p_g=g$ (smooth), `Hodge` $h^{1,0}=g$, $h^{0,1}=g$, $H^1\cong H^0(\Omega^1)\oplus\overline{H^0(\Omega^1)}$, $\text{canonical } K_{X}= (2g-2)\cdot\mathrm{pt}$ divisor class with $\deg K=2g-2$, $H^0(K)=H^0(\Omega^1)$ dimension $g$, etc., via `Riemann-Roch` $\chi(O(D))=\deg D+1-g$.
  Algorithms: `X.genus()`, `X.hodge_numbers()`, `X.canonical_divisor()`, `X.cusp_divisor()`, `X.elliptic_points()` from $\Gamma$ coset/elliptic enumeration (Sage `Gamma0(N).genus()` etc. as private adapter).

* Real fundamental domains as subsets of other first-class spaces, e.g. Euclidean/spherical/hyperbolic polytopes: for $\Gamma$ Fuchsian acting on $HH$ ($HH^2$ hyperbolic plane $ds^2=(dx^2+dy^2)/y^2$, curvature $-1$) or $\mathrm{SL}_2(O_K)$ on $HH^3 = C\times R_{>0}$ ($ds^2=(|dz|^2+dt^2)/t^2$), Kleinian, etc., a fundamental domain $F_\Gamma\subset HH^n$ is $\operatorname{closure}\{ z \mid d(z,p_0) \leq d(\gamma z,p_0)\ \forall\gamma\neq 1\}$ Dirichlet ($p_0\in HH^n$ not fixed by $\Gamma\setminus\{1\}$) or Ford $F=\{z \mid |cz+d|\geq 1\ \forall(c\ d)\}$ for $\Gamma\subset\mathrm{SL}_2(ZZ)$.
  $F_\Gamma$ is `HyperbolicPolytope` in $HH^n$ as finite-sided convex polytope with geodesic sides (circular arcs in $HH^2$, hemispheres in $HH^3$), side-pairings $\gamma_s: s\to s'$ with $\gamma_s\in\Gamma$ and cycle relations $\prod\gamma_{s_i}=1$.
  For $\mathrm{SL}_2(ZZ)$, $F=\{|\operatorname{Re} z|\leq\tfrac12, |z|\geq 1\}$ standard.
  For $\Gamma_0(N)$, $F_{\Gamma_0}$ is $[\mathrm{SL}_2:\Gamma_0]$-fold union of $F_{\mathrm{SL}_2}$ translates via coset reps.

* Visualization in low dimensions ($HH^2, HH^3$): $F_\Gamma : \mathsf{HyperbolicPolytope}$ as subset $F_\Gamma\subset HH^n$ with `F_Gamma.sides() -> [geodesic]`, `F_Gamma.side_pairings()` $\to\Gamma$, and `F_Gamma.visualize() -> InteractivePolytope` widget bound to $F_\Gamma$ (reuse `SpectralSequence.visualize` pattern) rendering in $HH^2$ Poincaré disk / upper half-plane with arcs, and in $HH^3$ via $H^3$ upper half-space with hemispherical faces, using hyperbolic isometry $\mathrm{PSL}_2(R)$ / $\mathrm{PSL}_2(C)$ private adapter (Sage `HyperbolicPlane`, `HyperbolicSpace(3)`). Not a free `plot_fundamental_domain(Gamma)` PNG — `F_Gamma = Gamma.fundamental_domain(p0) : HyperbolicPolytope` in $HH^n$ with `F_Gamma.as_subset(HH^n)` and `F_Gamma.show()`.

Intended owners: `categories/modular/congruence_subgroups.py` (`Gamma(N),Gamma1(N),Gamma0(N)` $: \operatorname{Sub}(\mathrm{SL}_2(ZZ))$ with `level,index,generators,cusps`), `categories/groups/arithmetic_subgroups.py` (`ArithmeticSubgroup(G)` $\subset G(Q)$ commensurable with $G(ZZ)$), `categories/modular/modular_curves.py` ($X(\Gamma)=\Gamma\backslash HH^* : \mathsf{Curve}/Q$ with $Y(\Gamma)=\Gamma\backslash HH$ open, integral model, moduli $F_N$), `categories/schemes/curve_genus.py` (`genus`, `hodge_numbers`, `canonical`, `RiemannHurwitz`), `categories/modular/fundamental_domains.py` (`FundamentalDomain` $\Gamma\to F_\Gamma\subset HH^n$ as `HyperbolicPolytope` with side-pairings), `categories/hyperbolic/polytopes.py` + `categories/hyperbolic/visualization.py` (`HyperbolicPolytope` `F_Gamma.visualize()` in $HH^2,HH^3$). Not a free `fundamental_domain(Gamma)` list — `Gamma.fundamental_domain()` as polytope object in $HH^n$ with $HH^n$ owning the metric.

## Desired capability: del Pezzo, Hirzebruch, common surfaces; toric surfaces classification and Fulton — note 2026-09-16

Need del Pezzo, Hirzebruch, and other common algebraic surfaces; lattice cones/polytopes wired to honest lattices; character/cocharacter lattices as preamble lattices; classification of toric surfaces with recognition; all of Fulton *Introduction to toric varieties* operationalized.

* Common surfaces: $\text{Hirzebruch } F_e = P(O_{P^1}\oplus O_{P^1}(-e)) : \mathsf{Surfaces}/k$ for $e : NN$, $\operatorname{Pic}(F_e)=ZZ\cdot C_0 \oplus ZZ\cdot f$ with $C_0^2=-e$, $f^2=0$, $C_0\cdot f=1$, $K_{F_e}=-2C_0-(e+2)f$, $(-1)$-curves etc.; $\text{del Pezzo } dP_n = \operatorname{Bl}_{p_1\ldots p_n} P^2$ $0\leq n\leq 8$ with $p_i$ in general position (no 3 collinear, no 6 on conic), $K_{dP_n}^2=9-n$, degree $d=9-n$, $\operatorname{Pic}(dP_n)=ZZ\cdot H \oplus\bigoplus_{i}ZZ\cdot E_i$ with $H^2=1$, $E_i^2=-1$, $K=-3H+\sum E_i$, $(-1)$-curves = $240,27,16\ldots$ Weyl orbit $W(E_n)$.
  $P^1\times P^1 = F_0$, $\operatorname{Bl}_p P^2 = F_1$ etc., plus $K3$ ($K=0$), `Enriques`, rational/ruled.
  Interface `Hirzebruch(e)` $\to F_e : \mathsf{Surfaces}$, `DelPezzo(n, points)` $\to dP_n : \mathsf{Surfaces}$ with `dP_n.picard_lattice()` $\to\operatorname{Pic}\cong\mathsf{Lattices}$, `dP_n.minus_one_curves()` $\to [C]$, not bare self-intersection numbers.

* Classification of toric surfaces and recognition: smooth complete toric surface $S_\Sigma$ from fan $\Sigma\subset N_R$ $\dim N_R=2$ with $\Sigma$ complete ($|\Sigma|=N_R$), $\Sigma$ smooth iff each cone $\sigma=\operatorname{cone}(v_i,v_{i+1})$ has $\det(v_i,v_{i+1})=\pm 1$ ($v_i\in N$ primitive).
  $S_\Sigma$ determined by cyclic ordered rays $v_0\ldots v_{r-1}\in N$ with $v_{i+r}=v_i$, relation $v_{i-1}+v_{i+1}=a_i v_i$ with $a_i = -D_i^2 = -\text{self-intersection}$ of torus-invariant divisor $D_i = V(v_i) \cong P^1$.
  Isomorphism type recognition: toric surface is either $P^2$ ($r=3$) or $F_e$ or blow-up of $F_e$ (iterated toric blow-up adding ray $v_i+v_{i+1}$ subdividing cone).
  Algorithm: from $S : \mathsf{Surfaces}/k$ smooth complete, test if $S$ toric via `Cox ring` / `T-invariant divisor` basis, and if so compute $\Sigma(S)$ by $N=X_*(T)$ and rays = primitive generators of $T$-invariant prime divisors ($D_\rho$), then match cyclic $v_i$ sequence up to $\operatorname{GL}_2(ZZ)$ ($\operatorname{Aut}(N)$) to recognize as $F_e$ / $\operatorname{Bl}$ / $P^2$.

* Character and cocharacter lattices as preamble-owned lattices: for split torus $T\cong G_m^r : \mathsf{Tori}/k$, $X^*(T)=\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathsf{GrpSch}}(T,G_m) \cong ZZ^r$ character lattice, $X_*(T)=\operatorname{Hom}(G_m,T) \cong ZZ^r$ cocharacter lattice, both as `Lattices` objects $X^*,X_* : \mathsf{Lattices}$ (`Str Lattices` even unimodular $ZZ^r$ with standard pairing $X^*\times X_*\to ZZ$, $(\chi,\lambda)\mapsto\chi\circ\lambda \in \operatorname{Hom}(G_m,G_m)=ZZ$), anti-equivalence $T\mapsto X^*(T) : \mathsf{Tori} \to \mathsf{Lattices}^{\mathrm{op}}$ ($T\cong\operatorname{Spec} k[X^*(T)]$). Interface `T.character_lattice()` $\to X^*(T) : \mathsf{Lattices}$ and `T.cocharacter_lattice()` $\to X_*(T) : \mathsf{Lattices}$ as honest `Lattices` (with `Lattices.gram_matrix` etc.), not as bare $ZZ^r$ vectors; pairing `X^*(T).pairing(X_*(T))` $\to ZZ$ as `BilinearForm`. Already `categories/group/characters.py` owns $X^*(T)$ as group, but `Lattices` owner supplies lattice structure (`X^*(T).as_lattice()` returns same object with `Lattices` refinement).

* Lattice cones and polytopes as independent constructions wired to honest preamble lattices: cone $\sigma\subset N_R=N\otimes R$ strongly convex rational polyhedral $\sigma=\operatorname{cone}(v_i)$, $v_i\in N$, with $\sigma\cap N$ monoid $S_\sigma$, $\sigma^\vee\subset M_R$ dual $\sigma^\vee=\{u\in M_R \mid \langle u,v\rangle\geq 0\ \forall v\in\sigma\}$ with $M=X^*(T)$, $N=X_*(T)$ dual lattices; polytope $P\subset M_R$ lattice polytope $P=\operatorname{conv}(m_i)$, $m_i\in M$, with vertices $\operatorname{vert}(P)\subset M$, faces $\operatorname{face}(P)$ as `LatticePolytope` with $P\subset M_R$ retaining $M : \mathsf{Lattices}$ as ambient, normal fan $\Sigma_P\subset N_R$.
  Both are independent first-class objects `LatticeCone(N, rays)` and `LatticePolytope(M, vertices)` with `sigma.ambient_lattice()` $\to N$, `P.ambient_lattice()` $\to M$, `sigma.dual()` $\to\sigma^\vee\subset M_R$, `P.normal_fan()` $\to\Sigma\subset N_R$, `P.face_lattice() -> Poset`. Wired into honest `Lattices`: no `sigma.ambient` stored as bare $ZZ^2$ matrix but as `Lattices` object with `N.dual() = M`. Reuse `polyhedral_cones.RationalPolyhedralCones` and `schemes/polytopes.py` as adapters for `SageCone` / `Polyhedron` private engines, but public objects retain $N,M : \mathsf{Lattices}$.
  Interface `LatticeCone(N, [v_i])` $\to\sigma\subset N_R$ with `sigma.dual_cone()` $\to\sigma^\vee$, `LatticePolytope(M, [m_i])` $\to P$ with `P.normal_fan()`.

* Fulton operationalized — all computational results from W. Fulton *Introduction to toric varieties* as owned functors: for toric $X_\Sigma : \mathsf{ToricVarieties}/k$ with fan $\Sigma\subset N_R$ already intaken via `RationalPolyhedralFans` / `ToricSchemes`, need $T$-invariant prime divisors $D_\rho = V(\rho)$ for $\rho\in\Sigma(1)$ ray with $v_\rho\in N$ primitive, $T$-Weil divisor $D=\sum_{\rho} a_\rho D_\rho : \operatorname{WeilDiv}(X_\Sigma)$ ($a_\rho: ZZ$), $T$-Cartier via support function $\psi: |\Sigma|\to R$ piecewise linear with $\psi|_\sigma \in M$ and $D_\psi = -\sum \psi(v_\rho)D_\rho$; exact $0\to M\to\oplus_{\rho} ZZ\cdot D_\rho \to \operatorname{Cl}(X_\Sigma)\to 0$ with $\operatorname{Cl} \cong \operatorname{Pic}$ when smooth, $0\to M\to\oplus ZZ\cdot D_\rho \to \operatorname{Pic} \to 0$?
  For smooth complete, $\operatorname{Pic}(X_\Sigma) \cong \oplus ZZ\cdot D_\rho / M$ with $M\to\oplus$ by $m\mapsto \sum\langle m,v_\rho\rangle D_\rho$, $A^*(X_\Sigma)=Z[D_\rho]/(SR+\text{linear})$ Chow ring with Stanley-Reisner $SR=(\prod_{\rho\in S} D_\rho \mid S\notin\Sigma)$ and linear $\sum\langle m,v_\rho\rangle D_\rho=0$.
  Cohomology $H^i(X_\Sigma, O(D))$ via Demazure vanishing / $H^i = \oplus_{m\in M} H^i_{m}$ with $H^i_m = \tilde H^{i-1}(|\Sigma|\setminus\ldots)$ combinatorial via $\Sigma_D$, $\chi(O(D))$ via `Ehrhart` of polytope $P_D=\{m \mid \langle m,v_\rho\rangle\geq -a_\rho\}$ when $D$ nef: $H^0(O(D))$ has basis $\chi^m$ for $m\in P_D\cap M$ and $h^0=|P_D\cap M|$.
  Need `X_Sigma.divisor_class_group()`, `X_Sigma.chow_ring()`, `X_Sigma.cohomology(D,i)`, etc., all via $M,N$ lattices.

Intended owners: `categories/schemes/surfaces/hirzebruch.py` (`Hirzebruch(e)` $\to F_e$ with $\operatorname{Pic}=ZZ^2$), `categories/schemes/surfaces/del_pezzo.py` (`DelPezzo(n)` $\to dP_n$ with $\operatorname{Pic}=ZZ^{n+1}$, $(-1)$-curves), `categories/schemes/surfaces/common_surfaces.py` ($P^1\times P^1$, K3, Enriques, rational), `categories/schemes/toric/surfaces.py` (`ToricSurface` $\Sigma\subset N_R$ smooth complete, rays $v_i\in N$), `categories/schemes/toric/classification.py` (`is_toric_surface`, `recognize_toric_surface` via $\Sigma$ and $v_{i-1}+v_{i+1}=a_i v_i$), `categories/tori/character_lattices.py` (`T.character_lattice()` $\to X^* : \mathsf{Lattices}$, `T.cocharacter_lattice()` $\to X_*$, pairing $\langle,\rangle$), `categories/cones/lattice_cones.py` (`LatticeCone(N,rays)` $\subset N_R$ with `dual`), `categories/polytopes/lattice_polytopes.py` (`LatticePolytope(M,verts)` $\subset M_R$ with `normal_fan`, `face_lattice`), `categories/schemes/toric/fulton.py` (`TDivisor` $D_\rho$, `WeilDiv` $\sum a_\rho D_\rho$, `Cartier` via $\psi$, $\operatorname{Cl},\operatorname{Pic}$, Chow $SR+\text{linear}$, $H^i(O(D))$ via $P_D\cap M$). Not a free `toric_divisor(fan)` list — `X_Sigma.divisor(D_rho)` on the `ToricVariety` with $N,M : \mathsf{Lattices}$ as dual pair.

## Desired capability: rational maps, birational maps, blowups, blowdowns, contractions — note 2026-09-16

Need support for rational maps, birational maps, resolving indeterminacy, blowups (at least along points), blowdowns, contractions of curves (at least on surfaces).

* Rational map is not a morphism with missing points ignored.
  For $X,Y : \mathsf{Sch}/k$ integral separated finite type, $\varphi: X \dashrightarrow Y$ is $U\subset X$ open dense with $\varphi: U\to Y$ morphism, with $U = \operatorname{dom}(\varphi)$ maximal domain of definition, equivalently $\varphi \in \operatorname{Hom}_{\mathsf{Sch}/k}(\operatorname{Spec} k(X), Y)$ where $k(X)=\operatorname{Frac} O_X$ function field.
  Composition is only where domains intersect: $\psi\circ\varphi$ defined on $\varphi^{-1}(\operatorname{dom}\psi)\cap\operatorname{dom}\varphi$.
  Interface `X.rational_map(Y, dom, morphism)` as object `RationalMap` with `phi.domain()` $\to U : \operatorname{Open}(X)$ and `phi.morphism()` $\to U\to Y$.

* Birational map is not a rational map with an inverse as set map.
  For $X,Y$ integral, $\varphi: X \dashrightarrow Y$ birational means $\exists\psi: Y \dashrightarrow X$ with $\psi\circ\varphi = \operatorname{id}_X$ as rational maps (equality on dense open where both defined) and $\varphi\circ\psi=\operatorname{id}_Y$, equivalently $k(X)\cong k(Y)$ as fields via $\varphi^*$.
  Group $\operatorname{Bir}(X)=\operatorname{Aut}_{\operatorname{Bir}}(X)$ is group of birational self-maps.
  Interface `X.birational_map(Y, phi, psi)` with `phi.is_birational() -> Bool` via $k(X)\cong k(Y)$, `phi.inverse()` returning $\psi$.

* Resolving indeterminacy is the theorem $\varphi: X \dashrightarrow Y$ with $Y$ proper and $X$ smooth (or normal) admits $\pi: \tilde X\to X$ proper birational (sequence of blowups) with $\tilde \varphi = \varphi\circ\pi : \tilde X\to Y$ morphism.
  At least for surfaces, resolution is by point blowups: $\pi = \operatorname{Bl}_{p_r}\circ\ldots\circ\operatorname{Bl}_{p_1}$ where $p_i\in X_{i-1}$.
  Need `phi.resolve_indeterminacy()` $\to (\pi: \tilde X\to X, \tilde \varphi: \tilde X\to Y)$ with $\pi$ as `Blowup` composition and $\tilde \varphi$ regular.
  Hardness is Hironaka in general; at least point case is required.

* Blowup at a point $p: \operatorname{Spec} k\to X$ (closed point, $X$ smooth of dimension $n$) is $\operatorname{Bl}_p X = \operatorname{Proj} \oplus_{d\geq 0} I_p^d : \mathsf{Sch}/k$ with $\pi: \operatorname{Bl}_p X\to X$ proper birational, iso over $X\setminus\{p\}$, exceptional $E=\pi^{-1}(p)\cong P^{n-1}_k$ with $O_E(E)=O_{P^{n-1}}(-1)$, $\operatorname{Pic}(\operatorname{Bl}_p X)=\pi^*\operatorname{Pic}(X)\oplus ZZ\cdot E$, $K_{\operatorname{Bl}}=\pi^*K_X+(n-1)E$.
  For surface $n=2$, $E\cong P^1$, $E^2=-1$, $K\cdot E=-1$.
  Interface `X.blowup(p)` $\to (\operatorname{Bl}_p X, \pi, E)$ with $\pi: \operatorname{Bl}_p X\to X$ and $E: \operatorname{Div}(\operatorname{Bl}_p X)$.

* Blowdown is the inverse: given $S$ smooth surface with $(-1)$-curve $E\subset S$, $E\cong P^1$, $E^2=-1$, Castelnuovo criterion gives $\pi: S\to S'$ with $S'=S$ contracted $E\mapsto \mathrm{pt}$ smooth, $S=\operatorname{Bl}_{\pi(E)} S'$.
  More generally blowdown of exceptional locus of $\pi: \tilde X\to X$ recovers $X$.
  Interface `S.blowdown(E)` $\to S'$ when $E$ is contractible, with certificate $E^2=-1$ and $E\cong P^1$.

* Contraction of curves on surfaces (at least): for $S : \mathsf{Surfaces}/k$ smooth projective and $C\subset S$ curve (effective divisor $C=\cup C_i$), contraction $c: S\to S'$ with $c(C)=\mathrm{pt}$, $c: S\setminus C \cong S'\setminus\{\mathrm{pt}\}$ iso, $S'$ normal (smooth when $C$ is $(-1)$-curve, singular otherwise).
  Grauert criterion: $C$ contractible iff intersection matrix $(C_i\cdot C_j)$ negative definite.
  For $C\cong P^1, C^2=-1$ get smooth $S'$ (Castelnuovo); for $C^2=-2$ chain get du Val singularity on $S'$.
  Toric contraction is $v_i$ removal from fan (coarsening).
  Interface `S.contract_curve(C)` $\to (S', c)$ with $c: S\to S'$ and `S'.singularity_at(c(C))` when not $(-1)$.

Intended owners: `categories/schemes/rational_maps.py` (`RationalMap` $X\dashrightarrow Y$ with `domain`, `morphism`), `categories/schemes/birational_maps.py` (`BirationalMap` with `is_birational` via $k(X)\cong k(Y)$), `categories/schemes/blowups.py` ($\operatorname{Bl}_p X$ with $\pi, E\cong P^{n-1}$, $\operatorname{Pic}$ splitting), `categories/schemes/resolution.py` (`resolve_indeterminacy` via $\operatorname{Bl}_{p_i}$ sequence), `categories/schemes/surfaces/contractions.py` (`contract_curve(C)` $\to S'$ with `Grauert`/`Castelnuovo` $E^2=-1$). Not a free `blowup(X,point)` matrix — `X.blowup(p)` on the scheme object with exceptional $E$.

## Desired capability: singularities — normal forms, Milnor/Tjurina, Kodaira fibers, dual graphs — note 2026-09-16

Need normal form classification of isolated point singularities, computing and classifying singularities in other codimensions when possible, Milnor and Tjurina numbers, fibrations with Kodaira classification, and dual graphs of exceptional curves.

* Isolated hypersurface singularity $f: (C^n,0)\to(C,0)$ with $f(0)=0$, $df(0)=0$, isolated means $\mu<\infty$ where $\mu=\dim_C C\{x_1\ldots x_n\}/(\partial f)$ finite.
  Normal forms: for $n=2$, $A_k: x^2+y^{k+1}$, $D_k: x^2y+y^{k-1}$, $E_6: x^3+y^4$, $E_7: x^3+xy^3$, $E_8: x^3+y^5$ (simple / du Val / rational double points, $\mu=\tau$); for $n\geq 3$ add $A_k$ suspension etc. Classification is Arnold's $A$-$D$-$E$ plus unimodal etc. Interface `f.normal_form() -> (type, k)` with $\text{type} \in \{A_k,D_k,E_6,E_7,E_8\}$ when simple, `Unknown` else, computed via $\mu,\tau$ and resolved via `SINGULAR` adapter when available, not by brute Groebner in preamble.

* Milnor $\mu(f)=\dim_C C\{x\}/J(f)$ where $J(f)=(\partial f/\partial x_i)$ Jacobian ideal, and Tjurina $\tau(f)=\dim_C C\{x\}/(f,J(f))$ where $T(f)=(f,J(f))$.
  For isolated $f$, $\tau \leq \mu$ with $\tau=\mu$ iff $f$ weighted homogeneous.
  Also $\mu = \operatorname{rank} H_{n-1}(F)$ Milnor fiber $F=f^{-1}(\delta)\cap B_\varepsilon$ ($F \simeq \vee_{i=1}^\mu S^{n-1}$), $\tau = \dim T^1_{f}$ base of miniversal deformation.
  For complete intersection $f=(f_1\ldots f_c)$, $\mu$ via $\Omega$ etc. Interface `f.milnor_number()` $\to \mu : NN$, `f.tjurina_number()` $\to \tau : NN$ on the germ object `HypersurfaceSingularity(f)` with $C\{x\}$ as `Rings` completion.

* Computing and classifying singularities in other codimensions when possible: for $X\subset A^n$ defined by $I=(f_1\ldots f_m)$, singular locus $\operatorname{Sing} X = V(I+\operatorname{Jac}_c)$ where $\operatorname{Jac}_c$ is ideal of $c\times c$ minors of Jacobian $\operatorname{Jac}(f)$ ($c=\operatorname{codim} X$), $\dim \operatorname{Sing} X$ is codim of singularity.
  Classify $p\in X$ as $\text{regular} \Leftrightarrow \operatorname{rank} \operatorname{Jac}(p)=c$, else `singular` with $\operatorname{embdim} = n - \operatorname{rank}$, $\operatorname{mult} = \operatorname{ord}_p(I)$, `type` via $\mu,\tau$ when isolated hypersurface, otherwise via `Singular` stratification (`Whitney`, `Hilbert-Samuel`). Find algorithms means delegate to maintained `SINGULAR`/`Oscar` for $\operatorname{Sing}$, $\operatorname{mult}$, $\mu,\tau$ when isolated, and report `Unknown` with reason when not decidable (non-isolated requires different invariants).
  Do not claim classification by Hilbert polynomial alone.

* Fibrations operationalized: flat proper morphism $f: S\to C$ with $S$ surface, $C$ curve, generic fiber $F = S_\eta$ smooth curve over $k(C)$.
  For elliptic fibration, $F$ is genus-1 curve with section $\sigma: C\to S$.
  Need `f.fibers()` $\to [F_p]$ over $p\in C$ with $F_p = f^{-1}(p)$ divisor, smooth vs singular.

* Kodaira classification of singular fibers for minimal elliptic $f: S\to C$ with section (Kodaira-Néron): each singular fiber $F_p$ is Kodaira type with dual graph affine Dynkin: $I_n$ ($n\geq 0$, $I_0$ smooth, $I_1$ nodal rational, $I_n$ $n$-gon of $P^1$s), $I_n^*$ ($D_{n+4}$), $\mathrm{II}$ (cusp, $1$ rational with cusp), $\mathrm{III}$ ($2$ rationals tangent), $\mathrm{IV}$ ($3$ concurrent), $\mathrm{II}^*$ ($E_8$), $\mathrm{III}^*$ ($E_7$), $\mathrm{IV}^*$ ($E_6$) with multiplicities $m_i$ and self-intersections $F_{p,i}^2=-2$.
  Fiber given by valuations $v_p(g_2), v_p(g_3), v_p(\Delta)$ in Weierstrass $y^2=4x^3-g_2x-g_3$, $\Delta=g_2^3-27g_3^2$.
  Interface `f.kodaira_fiber(p) -> Type` with $\text{type} \in \{I_n,I_n^*,\mathrm{II},\mathrm{III},\mathrm{IV},\mathrm{II}^*,\mathrm{III}^*,\mathrm{IV}^*\}$ and $F_p = \sum m_i \Theta_i$.

* Dual graphs of exceptional curves in resolutions: for $\pi: \tilde S\to S$ resolution of $p\in S$ (e.g. du Val), exceptional $\operatorname{Exc}(\pi)=\cup_{i}E_i$ with $E_i\cong P^1$, graph $\Gamma(\operatorname{Exc})$ with vertex $v_i$ per $E_i$, edge $v_i\text{---}v_j$ when $E_i\cdot E_j=1$ (or $>0$), vertex weight $w(v_i)=E_i^2$ (usually $-2$ for du Val, $-1$ for blowup), edge multiplicity $E_i\cdot E_j$.
  For $A_k$, $\Gamma=A_k$; $D_k\to D_k$, $E_6/E_7/E_8\to E_6/E_7/E_8$; for general cusp, $\Gamma$ is cycle.
  Interface `pi.dual_graph()` $\to \Gamma : \mathsf{WeightedGraph}$ with `Gamma.vertices()` $=E_i$, `Gamma.edge_weight(v_i,v_j)` $=E_i\cdot E_j$, `Gamma.vertex_weight(v_i)` $=E_i^2$.

Intended owners: `categories/schemes/singularities/isolated.py` (`HypersurfaceSingularity(f)` with `normal_form` $A_k/D_k/E_6/E_7/E_8$, `is_isolated`), `categories/schemes/singularities/milnor_tjurina.py` (`milnor_number` $\mu=\dim C\{x\}/J$, `tjurina_number` $\tau=\dim C\{x\}/(f,J)$), `categories/singularities/classification.py` (`classify(p in X)` via $\operatorname{Sing} X$ minors, `mult`, `embdim`), `categories/schemes/fibrations.py` (`Fibration` $f: S\to C$ with `fibers`), `categories/schemes/elliptic_surfaces/kodaira.py` (`kodaira_fiber(p)` $I_n/I_n^*/\mathrm{II}/\mathrm{III}/\mathrm{IV}/\mathrm{II}^*/\mathrm{III}^*/\mathrm{IV}^*$ via $v_p(g_2),v_p(g_3),v_p(\Delta)$), `categories/schemes/resolution/dual_graphs.py` (`dual_graph(Exc)` $\to \Gamma$ weighted).
Not a free `kodaira_type(fiber)` string — `f.kodaira_fiber(p)` on the fibration with Weierstrass data.

## Desired capability: generalized weighted graphs, Coxeter/Dynkin/s.e. laced, bilinear $\leftrightarrow$ graph — note 2026-09-16

Need generalized weighted-edge-and-vertex graphs, specializing to Coxeter diagrams (nodes weights $\pm 2$ or 4), further to Dynkin ($\pm 2$), further to simply laced, with smooth passage between any bilinear form and associated (di)graph.

* Generalized graph is $D=(V,E,w_V,w_E)$ with $V : \mathsf{FiniteOrderedSets}$ vertices $v_i$, $E\subset\{\{i,j\}\mid i\neq j\}$ undirected (or directed for `(di)graph` variant $E\subset V\times V$ with $i\neq j$), vertex weight $w_V: V\to ZZ$ ($w_V(v_i)=b(v_i,v_i)$) and edge weight $w_E: E\to ZZ$ ($w_E(\{i,j\})=b(v_i,v_j)$ when nonzero).
  Allow loops via $w_V$, but edge only when $b(v_i,v_j)\neq 0$.
  Need object `WeightedGraph` with `D.vertex_weight(v_i)`, `D.edge_weight(e)`, `D.adjacency_matrix()` $\to\operatorname{Mat}_{|V|}(ZZ)$ with $\operatorname{diag}=w_V$, $\text{off-diag}=w_E$.

* Specializations as full subcategories with weight restrictions: $\mathsf{WeightedGraphs} \supset \mathsf{CoxeterGraphs} \supset \mathsf{DynkinGraphs} \supset \mathsf{SimplyLacedDynkinGraphs}$ where `CoxeterGraphs` is $w_V(v)\in\{\pm 2,\pm 4\}$ and $w_E$ corresponds to Coxeter label $m_{ij}\in\{2,3,4,6,\infty\}$ via $w_E = -2\cos(\pi/m_{ij})$ normalized to $\pm 1,\pm\sqrt{2}$ etc., but in this integral normalization `Coxeter` means vertex weights $\pm 2$ (long/short root lengths $2$ vs $4$) with edge weights $w_E\in\{0,\mp 1,\mp 2\}$ encoding bond multiplicity; `DynkinGraphs` further restricts $w_V\in\{\pm 2\}$ (all roots length $2$ or all $\pm 2$ after scaling), `SimplyLacedDynkinGraphs` further restricts $w_V=2$ and $w_E\in\{0,-1\}$ (i.e. $A$-$D$-$E$ with single bonds).
  More precisely per prompt: nodes $w_V=\pm 2$ or $4$ for Coxeter, $\pm 2$ for Dynkin, $2$ (with $w_E\in\{0,-1\}$) for simply laced.
  Interface must enforce via `CoxeterGraphs` predicate `D.vertex_weights()` $\subset\{\pm 2,\pm 4\}$ and `Dynkin` $\subset\{\pm 2\}$.

* Smooth passage between any bilinear form and associated (di)graph with nodes $v_i$ of weight $b(v_i,v_i)$ and edges weight $b(v_i,v_j)$ when nonzero: for $(M,b)$ where $M : \mathsf{Modules}(ZZ)$ free of rank $n$ with basis $v=(v_i)_{i<n}$ ($v_i \in M$) and $b: M\times M\to ZZ$ bilinear (not necessarily symmetric; symmetric for lattices, but general for Coxeter), define $\operatorname{graph}(M,b,v) \to D$ with `D.vertex_weight(v_i)` $=b(v_i,v_i)$ and for $i\neq j$, if $b(v_i,v_j)\neq 0$ or $b(v_j,v_i)\neq 0$ put edge $\{i,j\}$ with $w_E(\{i,j\})=b(v_i,v_j)$ (or $b(v_j,v_i)$ for directed, or unordered with $w_E = b(v_i,v_j)$ when symmetric).
  Conversely, from $D$ with $|V|=n$, define $M_D = \oplus_{i<n} ZZ\cdot v_i : \mathsf{Lattices}$ (free $ZZ$-module) with $b_D(v_i,v_i)=w_V(v_i)$, $b_D(v_i,v_j)=w_E(\{i,j\})$ when $\{i,j\}\in E$ else $0$, extended bilinearly.
  Then $\operatorname{graph}(M_D,b_D,\mathrm{canonical\_basis})=D$ and $\operatorname{module}(\operatorname{graph}(M,b)) \cong (M,b)$ when basis is the graph's basis.
  Need functors $\mathsf{BilinearModule}\to\mathsf{WeightedGraph}$ and $\mathsf{WeightedGraph}\to\mathsf{BilinearModule}$ inverse on framed objects (choice of basis / vertex ordering).
  For (di)graph, directed variant keeps $b(v_i,v_j)$ and $b(v_j,v_i)$ separately as two directed edge weights.

Intended owners: `categories/graphs/weighted_graphs.py` (`WeightedGraph` $D=(V,E,w_V,w_E)$ with `vertex_weight`, `edge_weight`), `categories/coxeter/coxeter_diagrams.py` (`CoxeterGraphs` $w_V\in\{\pm 2,\pm 4\}$, `DynkinGraphs` $w_V\in\{\pm 2\}$, `SimplyLaced` $w_V=2,w_E\in\{0,-1\}$ as subcats), `categories/forms/bilinear_graphs.py` (`bilinear_to_graph(M,b)` $\to D$, `graph_to_bilinear(D)` $\to (M_D,b_D)$), `categories/lattices/graph_lattices.py` (`GraphLattice` $M_D$). Not a free `graph_of_bilinear_form(matrix)` list — `(M,b).associated_graph(basis)` $\to D$ on the bilinear module with basis datum.

## Desired capability: invariants/coinvariants under $G\to O(M,b)$, folding, converse $G\to\operatorname{Aut}(D)\to O(M,b)$ — note 2026-09-16

Need invariants and coinvariants under action $G\to O(M,b)$, induced folding of associated diagrams, and conversely actions $G\to\operatorname{Aut}(D)$ inducing $G\to O(M,b)$.

* Invariants and coinvariants: for $G : \mathsf{Groups}$ (finite, or finitely generated with action) acting on $(M,b) : \mathsf{BilinearModules}$ via $\rho: G\to O(M,b)$ ($O(M,b)=\{g\in\operatorname{GL}(M) \mid b(gx,gy)=b(x,y)\ \forall x,y\}$ orthogonal group of $b$), invariants $M^G = \{m\in M \mid gm=m\ \forall g\in G\} = \ker(\oplus_{g}(g-1))$ as $\operatorname{Sub}(M)$ pure submodule (fixed sublattice when $M$ lattice, $b|_{M^G}$ restricted), coinvariants $M_G = M / \langle gm-m \mid g\in G,m\in M\rangle = M / \operatorname{span}(g-1)M$ as $\operatorname{Quot}(M)$ with quotient map $\pi: M\to M_G$ and induced $b_G$ when $G$ preserves $b$ and quotient is torsion-free (or with torsion retained as $\operatorname{Quot}$). Need `M.invariants(G,rho)` $\to M^G : \operatorname{Sub}(M)$ and `M.coinvariants(G,rho)` $\to M_G : \operatorname{Quot}(M)$ on the $G$-module object $M_G = ZZ[G]\text{-}\mathsf{Mod}$ structure, with $M^G = \operatorname{Hom}_{ZZ[G]}(ZZ,M)$ and $M_G = ZZ\otimes_{ZZ[G]}M$.

* Induced folding of associated diagrams: from $(M,b)$ with basis $v=(v_i)$ giving $D=\operatorname{graph}(M,b,v)$ (above), $G\to O(M,b)$ permutes basis up to isometry.
  Folding is quotient graph $D/G$ where orbit $O_i=G\cdot v_i$ becomes vertex $w_O$ with weight $w(w_O)= \sum_{x,y\in O} b(x,y)$ / $|O|$?
  More invariantly, choose $G$-stable partition of $V$ and folded bilinear form $b^G$ on $M^G$ or on orbit sums $s_O = \sum_{v\in O} v \in M^G$ with $b^G(s_O,s_{O'}) = \sum_{x\in O,y\in O'} b(x,y)$.
  Diagram folding $\operatorname{fold}(D,G) \to D/G$ has vertices $O$, vertex weight $b^G(s_O,s_O)$, edge weight $b^G(s_O,s_{O'})$ when nonzero, specializing to Dynkin folding $A_{2n-1}\to B_n$, $D_{n+1}\to B_n$, $E_6\to F_4$, $D_4\to G_2$ when $G$ graph automorphism.
  Need `D.fold(G, action)` $\to D/G : \mathsf{WeightedGraphs}$.

* Conversely, action $G\to\operatorname{Aut}(D)$ of the (di)graph inducing $G\to O(M,b)$: for $D=(V,E,w)$ weighted graph, $\operatorname{Aut}(D)=\{g\in\operatorname{Sym}(V) \mid w_V(gv)=w_V(v), w_E(g e)=w_E(e)\}$ as `Groups` (finite), and $G\to\operatorname{Aut}(D)$ as permutation representation.
  Then $M_D=\oplus ZZ\cdot v_i$ with $b_D$ as above inherits $G\to O(M_D,b_D)$ via $g\cdot v_i = v_{g(i)}$ extending linearly, preserving $b_D$ because $w$ preserved: $b_D(gv_i,gv_j)=w_E(g\{i,j\})=w_E(\{i,j\})=b_D(v_i,v_j)$.
  So graph automorphisms give orthogonal bimodule automorphisms.
  Need functor `AutGraphToOrthogonal` $: \operatorname{Aut}(D)\to O(M_D,b_D)$ and $G\to\operatorname{Aut}(D) \Rightarrow G\to O(M_D,b_D)$ composition.
  Both directions together give equivalence between $G$-stable weighted graphs and $G$-bilinear modules with permutation basis.

Intended owners: `categories/lattices/invariants.py` (`invariants(G,rho)` $\to M^G = \ker(g-1) : \operatorname{Sub}$), `categories/lattices/coinvariants.py` (`coinvariants(G,rho)` $\to M_G = M/\langle gm-m\rangle : \operatorname{Quot}$), `categories/graphs/folding.py` (`fold(D,G)` $\to D/G$ with $b^G(s_O,s_{O'})$), `categories/lattices/group_actions.py` (`GroupAction` $G\to O(M,b)$ with $M^G, M_G$, and `GraphAction` $G\to\operatorname{Aut}(D) \to G\to O(M_D,b_D)$). Not a free `invariants(gens)` list — `M.invariants(rho)` with `rho: G -> O(M,b)` morphism as `G-Module` structure.

## Desired capability: vector fields on Lie groups and manifolds, brackets, flows, ad/Ad — note 2026-09-16

Need vector fields on Lie groups (and manifolds more generally), their brackets (Lie and Poisson), associated flows, $\operatorname{ad}(X,Y)$ and $\operatorname{Ad}_X(Y)$.

* Vector field on $M : \mathsf{Man}$ (`SmMfd`, `RiemMfd`, `ComplexMfd`) is $X: TM\to M$ section $X\in\Gamma(TM)$ with $X_p\in T_pM$, $X: C^\infty(M)\to C^\infty(M)$ derivation $X(fg)=X(f)g+fX(g)$.
  On Lie group $G : \mathsf{LieGroups}$ with $g=\operatorname{Lie}(G)=T_eG$, left-invariant $X^L \leftrightarrow X_e\in g$ via $X^L_g = dL_g(X_e)$ where $L_g: G\to G$ left translation, similarly right-invariant $X^R_g=dR_g(X_e)$.
  Space $\Gamma(TG)^L \cong g$ as `LieAlgebras` via $X^L\mapsto X_e$ with bracket preserved.
  For general $M$, $\Gamma(TM)$ is $\mathsf{LieAlgebras}_R$ infinite-dimensional with $C^\infty(M)$-module structure.

* Lie bracket $[X,Y]\in\Gamma(TM)$ is `LieAlgebras` bracket: $[X,Y](f)=X(Y(f))-Y(X(f))$ as derivation, satisfying Jacobi $[[X,Y],Z]+[[Y,Z],X]+[[Z,X],Y]=0$ and $[fX,Y]=f[X,Y]-Y(f)X$.
  For left-invariant fields on $G$, $[X^L,Y^L]=[X,Y]^L$ corresponds to $g$ bracket.
  Need `X.bracket(Y)` $\to [X,Y] : \Gamma(TM)$ on the `Man` object, not as matrix commutator list.

* Poisson bracket $\{f,g\}\in C^\infty(M)$ for Poisson manifold $(M, \pi)$ with bivector $\pi\in\Gamma(\wedge^2 TM)$ via $\{f,g\}=\pi(df,dg)$ and Hamiltonian vector field $X_f = \pi^\sharp(df)$ with $X_f(g)=\{f,g\}$, $[X_f,X_g]=X_{\{f,g\}}$.
  When $M=T^*Q$ or $g^*$ dual of Lie algebra, $\{\ ,\ \}$ is canonical / Lie-Poisson $\{f,g\}(\xi)=\langle\xi,[df_\xi,dg_\xi]\rangle$.
  Need `PoissonBracket(f,g)` $\to\{f,g\}$ with Jacobi from $\pi$ integrability $[\pi,\pi]_S=0$ Schouten, not as $\{f,g\}=\partial f\partial g$ string.

* Flow of $X\in\Gamma(TM)$ is $\varphi_X^t: M\to M$ (local flow) with $d/dt\, \varphi_X^t(p)=X_{\varphi_X^t(p)}$, $\varphi_X^0=\operatorname{id}$, $\varphi_X^{t+s}=\varphi_X^t\circ\varphi_X^s$ where defined, complete when $M$ compact or $X$ left-invariant on $G$ (then $\varphi_X^t = R_{\exp(tX_e)}$ or $L_{\exp(tX_e)}$ depending on invariance).
  For $G$, exponential $\exp: g\to G$ is flow at $e$: $\exp(X)=\varphi_{X^L}^1(e)$.
  Need `X.flow(t)` $\to \varphi_X^t : \mathsf{Diffeomorphisms}(M)$ with $\varphi_X^t(p)$ ODE, and `X.is_complete()` predicate.

* $\operatorname{ad}$ and $\operatorname{Ad}$: for $G$ with $g=\operatorname{Lie}(G)$, $\operatorname{Ad}: G\to\operatorname{GL}(g)$ adjoint representation $\operatorname{Ad}_g = d(c_g)_e$ where $c_g(h)=ghg^{-1}$, so $\operatorname{Ad}_g(Y)=gYg^{-1}$ for matrix $G$, $\operatorname{Ad}: G\to\operatorname{Aut}_{\mathsf{LieAlg}}(g)$ group morphism with $\operatorname{Ad}_{gh}=\operatorname{Ad}_g\circ\operatorname{Ad}_h$.
  At Lie algebra level $\operatorname{ad}: g\to\operatorname{gl}(g)$, $\operatorname{ad}_X(Y)=[X,Y]$ with $\operatorname{ad} = \operatorname{Lie}(\operatorname{Ad}) = d(\operatorname{Ad})_e$, so $\operatorname{ad}_X = d/dt|_0 \operatorname{Ad}_{\exp(tX)}$.
  Relation $\operatorname{Ad}_{\exp X}=\exp(\operatorname{ad}_X)$ ($\exp: \operatorname{gl}(g)\to\operatorname{GL}(g)$). Interface must be `g.ad(X,Y)` $\to [X,Y]=\operatorname{ad}_X(Y) : g$ and `G.Ad(g,Y)` $\to \operatorname{Ad}_g(Y) : g$ with $\operatorname{ad} = d\operatorname{Ad}$, $\operatorname{Ad}_{\exp}$ vs $\exp\circ\operatorname{ad}$ compatibility, computed via matrix $\operatorname{Ad}$ when $G$ linear, else via $L_g,R_g$ derivatives.
  Not a free `ad_matrix` — `X.ad(Y)` on $g$ and `g.Ad(Y)` on $G$ objects with $g=\operatorname{Lie}(G)$ retained.

Intended owners: `categories/manifolds/vector_fields.py` (`VectorField` $X\in\Gamma(TM)$ on $M: \mathsf{Man}$ with `bracket`, `flow`), `categories/lie/vector_fields.py` (`LeftInvariantVectorField` $X^L\leftrightarrow g$, $\exp$ via flow), `categories/geometry/brackets.py` (`LieBracket` $[X,Y]$, `PoissonBracket` $\{f,g\}$ with $\pi$), `categories/dynamics/flows.py` (`Flow` $\varphi_X^t: M\to M$ with $d/dt\, \varphi = X$), `categories/representations/adjoint.py` ($\operatorname{ad}_X(Y)=[X,Y]: g\to\operatorname{gl}(g)$, $\operatorname{Ad}_g(Y)=gYg^{-1}: G\to\operatorname{GL}(g)$ with $\operatorname{ad}=\operatorname{Lie}(\operatorname{Ad})$, $\operatorname{Ad}_{\exp}=\exp\circ\operatorname{ad}$). Not a free `bracket(X,Y)` list — `X.bracket(Y)` and `g.Ad(Y)` on the manifold/Lie objects.

## Desired capability: canonical sheaves, Serre duality — note 2026-09-16

Need canonical sheaves, operationalize Serre duality.

* Canonical sheaf $\omega_X$ is not a line bundle assigned by hand.
  For $X: \mathsf{Sm}/k$ smooth of dimension $n$ (or $X: \mathsf{Sch}/k$ smooth proper), $\omega_X = \wedge^n \Omega^1_{X/k} = \det \Omega^1_{X/k} : \operatorname{Pic}(X)$ as $\mathsf{QCoh}(X)$ invertible, with $\omega_X = \det T^*X$.
  For $X$ normal Gorenstein, $\omega_X$ is dualizing sheaf; for $X$ proper, $\omega_X \cong f^! O_{\operatorname{Spec} k}$ where $f: X\to\operatorname{Spec} k$ and $f^!$ from six functors.
  Need `X.canonical_sheaf()` $\to \omega_X : \operatorname{Pic}(X)$ as object $\omega_X : \mathsf{InvertibleSheaves}(X)$ with $\omega_X = \det \Omega^1$, not a divisor class number.

* For singular or non-proper settings, dualizing complex $\omega_X^\bullet = f^! O_{\operatorname{Spec} k} : D^b_{\mathrm{coh}}(X)$ in $D(\mathsf{QCoh})$ with $\omega_X = H^{-n}(\omega_X^\bullet)$ when $X$ smooth $n$-dim; $\omega_X^\bullet$ is the $f^!$ object from `six_functors` already intaken.
  Interface `X.dualizing_complex()` $\to \omega_X^\bullet$ and `X.canonical_sheaf()` $\to \omega_X$ when $X$ smooth.

* Serre duality is not $h^i = h^{n-i}$ equality of numbers.
  For $X : \mathsf{SmProj}/k$ smooth projective $n$-dim and $F : \mathsf{Coh}(X)$ coherent, duality is natural isomorphism $\operatorname{Ext}^i_X(F, \omega_X) \cong H^{n-i}(X, F)^\vee$ ($k$-dual), functorially $R\Gamma(F)^\vee \cong R\operatorname{Hom}_X(F, \omega_X[n])$ in $D(k)$, and $\operatorname{Ext}^i(F, \omega_X) \cong H^{n-i}(F)^*$.
  In derived form $R\operatorname{Hom}_X(F, \omega_X[n]) \cong R\Gamma(F)^\vee$ with shift $[n]$, and $Rf_* R\operatorname{Hom}(F, f^! G) \cong R\operatorname{Hom}(Rf_*F, G)$ adjunction for $f: X\to\operatorname{Spec} k$ proper.
  For vector bundle $F$, $H^i(X, F)^\vee \cong H^{n-i}(X, F^\vee\otimes\omega_X)$ via $\operatorname{Ext}^i(F,\omega)=H^i(F^\vee\otimes\omega)$.
  Trace $\operatorname{tr}: H^n(X, \omega_X) \to k$ is the duality pairing.

* Operationalize means: from $X: \mathsf{SmProj}$ with $\omega_X$ as above and $F: \mathsf{QCoh}$ with $R\Gamma(F) : D(k)$ effective via Čech $\check{C}(U,F)$ already intaken, construct $R\operatorname{Hom}(F, \omega_X)$ and the Yoneda pairing $\operatorname{Ext}^i\otimes H^{n-i}\to k$ via $\cup$ and $\operatorname{tr}$, and certify $\dim \operatorname{Ext}^i = \dim H^{n-i}$ with explicit $k$-linear iso $\mathrm{SD}_{X,F,i}: \operatorname{Ext}^i(F,\omega_X) \to H^{n-i}(F)^\vee$ as $\mathsf{Vect}_k$ isomorphism, not as dimension equality.
  When $F=O_X(D)$ for divisor $D$, $\mathrm{SD}$ gives $h^i(O(D))^\vee = h^{n-i}(O(K-D))$.

Intended owners: `categories/sheaves/canonical.py` (`canonical_sheaf` $X\to\omega_X=\wedge^n\Omega^1 : \operatorname{Pic}$ and `dualizing_complex` $f^!O$), `categories/duality/serre.py` (`SerreDuality` $\operatorname{Ext}^i(F,\omega_X) \cong H^{n-i}(F)^\vee$, $R\operatorname{Hom}(F,\omega_X[n]) \cong R\Gamma(F)^\vee$ via $Rf^!$), delegating to `categories/sheaves/six_functors.py` ($f^!$, $Rf_* \dashv f^!$), `categories/schemes/canonical.py` (`X.canonical_divisor()` $\to K_X$ with $O(K_X)=\omega_X$). Not a free `serre_dual(F)` number — `X.serre_duality(F,i)` $\to \operatorname{Hom}(\operatorname{Ext}^i, H^{n-i\vee})$ on the scheme with $\omega_X$.

## Desired capability: Siegel half-spaces — note 2026-09-16

Need Siegel half-spaces.

* Siegel upper half-space $HH_g = \{ Z\in\operatorname{Mat}_{g\times g}(C) \mid Z^t=Z, \operatorname{Im} Z >0 \}$ ($\operatorname{Im} Z$ positive definite Hermitian $g\times g$) as `ComplexMfd` of dimension $n=g(g+1)/2$ ($n : NN$), with $HH_1 = HH$ upper half-plane, $HH_g = \operatorname{Sp}_{2g}(RR)/U(g)$ as symmetric space $G/K$ where $G=\operatorname{Sp}_{2g}(RR)$, $K=U(g)$ maximal compact, $\operatorname{Sp}_{2g} = \{ M\in\operatorname{GL}_{2g} \mid M^t J M = J \}$, $J=(0\ I_g;\ -I_g\ 0)$.
  As `ComplexMfd`, $HH_g$ has bounded domain model via Cayley $Z\mapsto(Z-iI)(Z+iI)^{-1}$.

* Action $\gamma\cdot Z = (AZ+B)(CZ+D)^{-1}$ for $\gamma=(A\ B;\ C\ D)\in\operatorname{Sp}_{2g}(RR)$ with $A,B,C,D\in\operatorname{Mat}_{g\times g}(RR)$ ($\gamma^t J \gamma =J$), $\gamma\cdot Z\in HH_g$ when $Z\in HH_g$, transitive with stabilizer $U(g)$ at $Z_0=iI_g$.
  Arithmetic subgroup $\Gamma_g = \operatorname{Sp}_{2g}(ZZ) \subset \operatorname{Sp}_{2g}(Q)$ acting properly discontinuously, quotient $A_g = \Gamma_g\backslash HH_g : \mathsf{QuasiProjVar}$ is moduli of principally polarized abelian varieties of dimension $g$ ($A_g \cong M_{\mathrm{ppav},g}$), with $A_1 = \operatorname{SL}_2(ZZ)\backslash HH = M_{1,1}$ elliptic curves.
  Need Siegel modular forms $f: HH_g\to C$ with $f(\gamma\cdot Z)=\det(CZ+D)^k f(Z)$.

* As generalization of $HH=HH_1$, need $HH_g$ as object `SiegelHalfSpace(g)` $: \mathsf{HermitianSymmetricSpaces}$ with `HH_g.dimension()` $\to g(g+1)/2$, `HH_g.group()` $\to\operatorname{Sp}_{2g}$, `HH_g.arithmetic_subgroup()` $\to\operatorname{Sp}_{2g}(ZZ)$, and `HH_g.quotient()` $\to A_g$.

Intended owners: `categories/modular/siegel_half_space.py` (`SiegelHalfSpace` $HH_g=\{Z\mid Z^t=Z,\operatorname{Im} Z>0\} : \mathsf{ComplexMfd}$ with $\operatorname{Sp}_{2g}(RR)/U(g)$, action $(AZ+B)(CZ+D)^{-1}$), `categories/hermitian/siegel.py` (`Siegel domain` as `HermitianSymmetric`), `categories/moduli/ppav.py` ($A_g=\operatorname{Sp}_{2g}(ZZ)\backslash HH_g$ moduli).
Not a free `siegel_half_space(g)` matrix — `SiegelHalfSpace(g)` object in `ComplexMfd` with $\operatorname{Sp}_{2g}$ action.

## Desired capability: derivations of modules and augmented ZZ-algebras, Fox free differential calculus — note 2026-09-16

Need derivations of modules and augmented $ZZ$-algebras, more generally Fox's algebraic differential forms (the free differential calculus).

* Derivation of augmented algebra: for $R=ZZ$ (or $R : \mathsf{CommRings}$), $A : \mathsf{Alg}_R$ augmented means $\varepsilon: A\to R$ as $R$-algebra morphism with section $\eta: R\to A$ ($\varepsilon\circ\eta=\operatorname{id}_R$), equivalently $A = R\oplus I$ with $I=\ker \varepsilon$ augmentation ideal as $R$-module.
  For $A$-module $M$, $R$-derivation $d: A\to M$ is $R$-linear with Leibniz $d(ab)=a\cdot d(b)+d(a)\cdot b$ and $d(r)=0$ for $r\in R$ (via $\eta$), with $d(1)=0$.
  Universal derivation $d_A: A\to\Omega_{A/R}$ with $\Omega_{A/R}$ Kähler differentials as $A$-module generated by $da$ with relations $d(ab)=a\, db + b\, da$, and $\operatorname{Der}_R(A,M)=\operatorname{Hom}_A(\Omega_{A/R},M)$ natural.
  For augmented $A$, $\Omega_{A/R}\otimes_A R \cong I/I^2$ via $da\mapsto a-\varepsilon(a)$.

* Derivation of module: for $R$-module $M$ with $G$-action or $A$-module structure, derivation $d: G\to M$ or $d: A\to M$ with appropriate $g\cdot d(h)+d(g)$ etc. In group case $d: G\to M$ satisfies $d(gh)=d(g)+g\cdot d(h)$ ($1$-cocycle $Z^1(G,M)$), which is same as algebra derivation when $A=ZZ[G]$ augmented via $\varepsilon: ZZ[G]\to ZZ$, $g\mapsto 1$.
  Need `M.derivations()` as $\operatorname{Der}$ object.

* Fox free differential calculus is the case $A=ZZ[F_n]$ group ring of free group $F_n=\langle x_1\ldots x_n\rangle$, augmented via $\varepsilon: ZZ[F_n]\to ZZ$, $x_i\mapsto 1$, $I=(x_i-1)$.
  Fox derivatives $\partial/\partial x_i: ZZ[F_n]\to ZZ[F_n]$ are $ZZ$-linear with $\partial x_j/\partial x_i=\delta_{ij}$, $\partial(uv)/\partial x_i = \partial u/\partial x_i + u\cdot\partial v/\partial x_i$ (twisted Leibniz with left $u$), and $u-\varepsilon(u)= \sum_{i} (x_i-1)\cdot\partial u/\partial x_i$ fundamental formula, and $\partial(u^{-1})/\partial x_i = -u^{-1}\cdot\partial u/\partial x_i$.
  Universal $d: ZZ[F_n]\to\oplus_{i=1}^n ZZ[F_n]\cdot dx_i$ free $A$-module $\Omega_{ZZ[F_n]/ZZ} \cong \oplus A\cdot dx_i$ with $d(u)=\sum \partial u/\partial x_i\, dx_i$.
  So $\operatorname{Der}$ is free.
  This computes $H_1(F_n, M)$ etc., and $I/I^2$ etc.

* Need functors: `AugmentedAlgebra(A,eps)` $\to\operatorname{Der}_R(A,M)=\operatorname{Hom}(\Omega,M)$ with $\Omega_{A/R}$ as $A$-module, `FoxDerivatives(F_n)` $\to\partial/\partial x_i$ on $ZZ[F_n]$, and more generally for $F$ free group / free algebra $T(V)$ / $R\langle X\rangle$, $\Omega$ free with basis $dx_i$.
  Interface `A.derivations(M)` $\to\operatorname{Der}_R(A,M) : \mathsf{Modules}$, `ZZ[F_n].fox_derivative(x_i, u)` $\to\partial u/\partial x_i$, `A.kahler_differentials()` $\to\Omega_{A/R}$ as $A$-module with $d_A: A\to\Omega$.

Intended owners: `categories/algebras/derivations.py` ($\operatorname{Der}_R(A,M)=\operatorname{Hom}_A(\Omega_{A/R},M)$ with $\Omega$ Kähler, augment case $\Omega\otimes_A R \cong I/I^2$), `categories/algebras/augmented_algebras.py` (`AugmentedAlgebra` $(A,\varepsilon: A\to R)$ with $I=\ker \varepsilon$), `categories/algebras/fox_calculus.py` (`FoxDerivatives` $\partial/\partial x_i: ZZ[F_n]\to ZZ[F_n]$ with $\partial(uv)=\partial u+u\partial v$, $\Omega_{ZZ[F_n]}=\oplus A\cdot dx_i$ free), `categories/modules/derivations.py` (`ModuleDerivation` $d: G\to M$ $d(gh)=d(g)+g\cdot d(h)$ as $Z^1$). Not a free `fox_derivative(word)` string — `ZZ[F_n].fox_derivative(x_i)` on the augmented group algebra with $\Omega$ free.

## Desired capability: content ideal $c_M(x)$ of an element $x\in M$ — note 2026-09-16

Let $R : \mathsf{CommRings}$ and $M : R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod}$.
An element $x\in M$ determines a map

$$
\operatorname{ev}_x: \operatorname{Hom}_R(M,R) \to R,\quad f \mapsto f(x) \tag{3}
$$

This map is $R$-linear: $\operatorname{ev}_x(r\cdot f)=r\cdot f(x)=r\cdot \operatorname{ev}_x(f)$ and $\operatorname{ev}_x(f+g)=f(x)+g(x)$, hence $\operatorname{im}(\operatorname{ev}_x) \triangleleft R$ is an ideal.
Denote $c_M(x) = \operatorname{im}(\operatorname{ev}_x) \subset R$ and call it the content of $x$.
Interface must be `M.content_ideal(x)` $\to c_M(x) : \mathsf{Ideals}(R)$ as object $c_M(x) : \mathsf{Ideals}(R)$ with $c_M(x) = \{ f(x) \mid f\in\operatorname{Hom}_R(M,R) \}$, not a bare set of evaluations.

* Lemma 3.4. If $M$ is free of finite rank and $x\in M$ is non-zero, then $c_M(x)$ is a non-zero ideal in $R$.
  Proof via a basis $M \cong R^n$ with $x=(r_1,\ldots,r_n) \neq 0$, then $\operatorname{Hom}_R(M,R) \cong R^n$ via dual basis $e_i^*$, $\operatorname{ev}_x(e_i^*) = r_i$, so $c_M(x) = (r_1,\ldots,r_n) \triangleleft R$ is the ideal generated by the coordinates, non-zero because some $r_i\neq 0$.
  Need `M.is_free()`, `M.rank() : NN`, `M.basis()`, $\operatorname{Hom}_R(M,R)$ dual module, and `Ideal(R)` construction to state and prove the lemma as $c_M(x) \neq 0$ when $x\neq 0$ and $M\in\mathsf{FreeFiniteRank}$.

Intended owners: `categories/modules/content.py` (`content_ideal` `M.content_ideal(x)` $\to c_M(x) = \operatorname{im}(\operatorname{Hom}_R(M,R) \to R, f\mapsto f(x)) : \mathsf{Ideals}(R)$), `categories/modules/dual.py` ($\operatorname{Hom}_R(M,R) : R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod}$ dual), `categories/modules/free_modules.py` (`FreeFiniteRank` with `basis`, `rank`), `categories/rings/ideals.py` (`Ideals(R)`). Not a free `content_of_element(M,x)` returning list of values — `M.content_ideal(x)` on the module with $\operatorname{Hom}_R(M,R)$ dual.

## Desired capability: shortcuts for common matrices — Jordan blocks, banded, tridiagonal, antidiagonal, symplectic form — note 2026-09-16

Need shortcuts for matrices which are extremely common as constructors, not as hand-rolled `Matrix(R, [[...]])` lists.

* Jordan block $J_n(\lambda) : \operatorname{Mat}_n(R)$ for $R : \mathsf{CommRings}$, $n : NN$, $\lambda : R$ is $J_n(\lambda) = \lambda\cdot I_n + N_n$ where $N_n$ is nilpotent with $(N_n)_{i,i+1}=1$ for $1\leq i<n$ and $0$ else, so $J_n(\lambda) = [[\lambda,1,0,\ldots],[0,\lambda,1,\ldots],\ldots,[0,\ldots,\lambda]]$.
  Interface `JordanBlock(n, lambda)` $\to J_n(\lambda) : \operatorname{Mat}_n(R)$ with `JordanBlock(1,lambda)=[lambda]`, `JordanBlock(n,0)=N_n`. Need `J_n(lambda).charpoly()` $= (T-\lambda)^n$ and $J_n(\lambda)$ as $\operatorname{End}_R(R^n)$ with $R^n : R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod}$ free.

* Banded matrix of bandwidth $(k,l)$ (or $w = \max(k,l)$) is $A : \operatorname{Mat}_{m\times n}(R)$ with $A_{ij}=0$ if $j-i > k$ (super-band) or $i-j > l$ (sub-band).
  Specialization $w=1$ with $k=l=1$ is tridiagonal.
  Need `BandedMatrix(m,n,k,l, entries)` constructor where entries are given as bands $a^{(d)}_i = A_{i,i+d}$ for $-l\leq d\leq k$, not as full $m\times n$ list.
  The matrix is sparse by construction, not by zero-filling.

* Tridiagonal $T_n(a,b,c) : \operatorname{Mat}_n(R)$ with diagonal $a = (a_1,\ldots,a_n) : R^n$, superdiagonal $b = (b_1,\ldots,b_{n-1})$, subdiagonal $c = (c_1,\ldots,c_{n-1})$, $T_{ii}=a_i$, $T_{i,i+1}=b_i$, $T_{i+1,i}=c_i$, $0$ else.
  Interface `TridiagonalMatrix(diag=a, super=b, sub=c)` $\to T : \operatorname{Mat}_n(R)$ and `TridiagonalMatrix(n, a,b,c)` variant.
  Includes symmetric tridiagonal when $b=c$.

* Antidiagonal $A_n(v) : \operatorname{Mat}_n(R)$ for $v=(v_1,\ldots,v_n) : R^n$ is $A_{i, n+1-i}=v_i$ and $0$ else, so $A = [[0,\ldots,v_1],[0,v_2,0,\ldots],\ldots,[v_n,\ldots,0]]$ with anti-diagonal $v$.
  Permuted identity `J_n = AntidiagonalMatrix(1,...,1)` is the reversal matrix $J_n^2=I_n$, $J_n A J_n$ reverses.
  Interface `AntidiagonalMatrix(vec)` $\to A : \operatorname{Mat}_n(R)$ with `vec : R^n` as module element.

* Basic symplectic form matrix $J_{2g} : \operatorname{Mat}_{2g}(R)$ is $J_{2g} = [0\ I_g;\ -I_g\ 0]$ block matrix with $I_g : \operatorname{Mat}_g(R)$ identity, $0 : \operatorname{Mat}_g(R)$ zero, so $J_{2g} = [[0, I_g],[-I_g,0]]$ with $J_{2g}^t = -J_{2g}$, $J_{2g}^2 = -I_{2g}$, $\det J_{2g}=1$.
  This is the standard symplectic form $\langle x,y\rangle = x^t J_{2g} y$ preserved by $\operatorname{Sp}_{2g}(R) = \{ M \mid M^t J_{2g} M = J_{2g} \}$.
  Variant with $R=ZZ$ gives $\operatorname{Sp}_{2g}(ZZ)$.
  Need `SymplecticFormMatrix(g)` $\to J_{2g} : \operatorname{Mat}_{2g}(R)$ and `SymplecticFormMatrix(g, R)` over $R$, with $J_2 = [0\ 1;\ -1\ 0]$ for $g=1$.
  When $R$ not commutative, use appropriate sign.

Intended owners: `categories/matrices/common_matrices.py` (`JordanBlock(n,lambda)`, `BandedMatrix(m,n,k,l)`, `TridiagonalMatrix`, `AntidiagonalMatrix`, `SymplecticFormMatrix(g)`), `categories/matrices/jordan.py` (`JordanBlock` with $N_n$), `categories/matrices/banded.py` (`BandedMatrix` with bandwidth), `categories/matrices/symplectic.py` ($J_{2g} = [0\ I_g;\ -I_g\ 0]$ with $\operatorname{Sp}_{2g}$). Not a free `Matrix([[lambda,1],[0,lambda]])` — `JordanBlock(n, lambda)` etc. on $R$ with $\operatorname{Mat}_n(R)$ parent.

## Desired capability: rational canonical form (Frobenius normal form) — note 2026-09-16

Need rational canonical form as owned normal form, not as hand-rolled `Matrix.diagonal([companion(...)])` list.

* Companion matrix $C(p) : \operatorname{Mat}_d(F)$ for monic $p(x)=x^d + a_{d-1}x^{d-1}+\ldots+a_0 \in F[x]$ (or $R[x]$ monic over PID) is $C(p) = [[0,0,\ldots,0,-a_0],[1,0,\ldots,0,-a_1],[0,1,\ldots,0,-a_2],\ldots,[0,\ldots,1,-a_{d-1}]]$ with $1$ on subdiagonal and $-a_i$ in last column, so $\operatorname{charpoly}(C(p))=p$ and $m_{C(p)}=p$, and $F^d \cong F[x]/(p)$ as $F[x]$-module via $x\cdot v = C(p)v$.
  For $\deg p =0$ ($p=1$), $C(p)$ is $0\times 0$ empty block.

* Rational canonical form $\operatorname{RCF}(A) : \operatorname{Mat}_n(F)$ for $A : \operatorname{Mat}_n(F)$ over field $F$ (or PID $R$ with $R[x]$ PID) is $\operatorname{RCF}(A) = \operatorname{diag}(C(p_1),\ldots,C(p_r))$ block diagonal with invariant factors $p_1 \mid p_2 \mid \ldots \mid p_r$ monic in $F[x]$, $p_r = m_A$ minimal polynomial, $\prod_{i=1}^r p_i = \chi_A$ characteristic polynomial, and $r =$ number of invariant factors = $\dim_F$ of $F^n$ as $F[x]$-module decomposition $F^n \cong \oplus_{i=1}^r F[x]/(p_i)$ where $x\cdot v = A v$.
  Similarly elementary divisors refine via factorization $p_i = \prod q_j^{e_{ij}}$ with $q_j$ irreducible.
  The invariant factors are obtained from Smith normal form of $xI_n - A : \operatorname{Mat}_n(F[x])$ via $\operatorname{diag}(1,\ldots,1,p_1,\ldots,p_r)$ with $p_i \mid p_{i+1}$.
  Two matrices are similar $B = P A P^{-1}$ iff $\operatorname{RCF}(B)=\operatorname{RCF}(A)$ (i.e. same $p_i$). Jordan form over algebraic closure is refinement when $p_i$ splits: $J_n(\lambda)$ blocks are $C((x-\lambda)^k)$ companion.

* As $F[x]$-module structure, the invariant factors classify: $M_A = F^n$ with $x\cdot m = A m$ is finite $F[x]$-module, $M_A \cong \oplus F[x]/(p_i)$ with $p_i\mid p_{i+1}$, and $\operatorname{RCF}(A)$ is the matrix of $x$ on $\oplus F[x]/(p_i)$ in the basis $1, x, \ldots, x^{d_i-1}$ per summand.
  Over PID $R$, same with $R[x]$ and $p_i\in R[x]$ monic.

* Interface must be `A.rational_canonical_form()` $\to\operatorname{RCF}(A) : \operatorname{Mat}_n(F)$ as object $\operatorname{RCF}(A) : \operatorname{Mat}_n(F)$ with `RCF(A).invariant_factors()` $\to [p_1,\ldots,p_r] : \operatorname{List}(F[x])$ monic with $p_i\mid p_{i+1}$, `RCF(A).companion_blocks()` $\to [C(p_i)]$, and `A.rational_canonical_form_transformation()` $\to P : \operatorname{GL}_n(F)$ with $P A P^{-1} = \operatorname{RCF}(A)$ when $P$ exists (e.g. via Smith).
  For $F = QQ$, `QQbar`, $F_p$, $R=ZZ$, need `F[x] = PolynomialRing(F)` as `Rings` owner.
  Do not use `A.jordan_form()` as substitute over non-splitting fields.

Intended owners: `categories/matrices/rational_canonical.py` (`RationalCanonicalForm` $\operatorname{RCF}(A)=\operatorname{diag}(C(p_i))$ with $p_i\mid p_{i+1}$, $m_A=p_r$, $\chi_A=\prod p_i$), `categories/matrices/companion.py` (`CompanionMatrix(p)` $\to C(p) : \operatorname{Mat}_{\deg p}(F)$), `categories/modules/frobenius_form.py` ($F[x]$-module $M_A = R^n$ with $x\cdot v = A v$ and $M_A \cong \oplus R[x]/(p_i)$). Not a free `diagonal_matrix([companion(p) for p in factors])` — `A.rational_canonical_form()` on the matrix with $F[x]$ Smith.

## Desired capability: annihilators and element-wise methods via annihilator ideals — note 2026-09-16

For $R : \mathsf{CommRings}$ and $M : R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod}$, and for $x\in M$ and $N\leq M$ submodule, define annihilators as ideals of $R$:

* Element annihilator $\operatorname{Ann}_R(x) = \{ r\in R \mid r\cdot x = 0 \} \triangleleft R$ as `Ideals(R)` with $0\in\operatorname{Ann}_R(x)$, closed under $+$ and $R\cdot\operatorname{Ann}\subset\operatorname{Ann}$.
  The annihilator is the kernel of the orbit map $R \to M, r\mapsto r\cdot x$, so $R/\operatorname{Ann}_R(x) \cong R\cdot x \leq M$ as $R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod}$.
  Interface `M.annihilator(x)` $\to\operatorname{Ann}_R(x) : \mathsf{Ideals}(R)$ with `M.annihilator(x)` $= \{r \mid r\cdot x=0\}$.

* Module and submodule annihilators $\operatorname{Ann}_R(M) = \{ r\in R \mid r\cdot M=0 \} = \cap_{x\in M} \operatorname{Ann}_R(x) \triangleleft R$ and $\operatorname{Ann}_R(N) = \cap_{x\in N} \operatorname{Ann}_R(x) \triangleleft R$ as `Ideals(R)` (possibly intersection over infinite $M$, but ideal is well-defined; compute via generating set when $M$ finitely generated $M = \sum R\cdot x_i$ then $\operatorname{Ann}_R(M)=\cap_i \operatorname{Ann}_R(x_i)$). Need `M.annihilator()` $\to\operatorname{Ann}_R(M) : \mathsf{Ideals}(R)$ for the whole module and `M.annihilator_of_submodule(N)` $\to\operatorname{Ann}_R(N)$.

* Element-wise methods replaced with properties of annihilators (as ideals, not as element tests):

  - `M.is_zero()` $\Leftrightarrow M=0 \Leftrightarrow \operatorname{Ann}_R(M)=R \Leftrightarrow 1\in\operatorname{Ann}_R(M)$ (the zero module is the unique module annihilated by $1$), not by enumerating $x\in M$.

  - `M.is_faithful()` $\Leftrightarrow \operatorname{Ann}_R(M)=0$ in $R$ (faithful means no non-zero $r$ kills all of $M$), not by testing $r\cdot x$ for all $r,x$.

  - `x.is_zero()` $\Leftrightarrow x=0 \Leftrightarrow \operatorname{Ann}_R(x)=R \Leftrightarrow 1\cdot x=0$.

  - `x.is_torsion()` $\Leftrightarrow \operatorname{Ann}_R(x)\neq 0$ when $R$ domain (torsion means non-zero $r$ with $r\cdot x=0$); more generally $x$ is $I$-torsion for $I\triangleleft R$ when $I\subseteq\operatorname{Ann}_R(x)$.
    This replaces ad-hoc `x.is_torsion()` that would test $r\cdot x$ by enumerating $r$.

  - Similarly `M.is_torsion()` means $\forall x\in M, \operatorname{Ann}_R(x)\neq 0$ (or $\operatorname{Ann}_R(x)$ contains a regular element), not a separate predicate.
    The annihilator ideal carries the uniform interface: `Ann_R(x).is_zero_ideal()`, `Ann_R(x).is_whole_ring()`, `Ann_R(x).contains(r)`, `Ann_R(x).is_nonzero()`.

Intended owners: `categories/modules/annihilator.py` (`annihilator` `M.annihilator(x)` $\to\operatorname{Ann}_R(x) : \mathsf{Ideals}(R)$, `M.annihilator()` $\to\operatorname{Ann}_R(M)$, with $R/\operatorname{Ann}_R(x) \cong R\cdot x$), `categories/modules/torsion.py` (`is_torsion` via $\operatorname{Ann}_R(x)\neq 0$), `categories/modules/faithful.py` (`is_faithful` via $\operatorname{Ann}_R(M)=0$, `is_zero` via $\operatorname{Ann}_R(M)=R$), `categories/rings/ideals.py` (`Ideals(R)` as lattice).
Not a free `is_zero_element(x)` or `annihilator(x)` returning list of $r$ — `M.annihilator(x)` on the module with `Ideals(R)`.

## Desired capability: matrix spaces over $R$ as fibered category $\mathsf{Mat}(R)$ and module morphisms via matrices as honest functor — note 2026-09-16

Reformulate matrix spaces over $R$ via the category fibered over rings whose objects are $NN$ and whose morphisms are $\operatorname{Mat}_{n,m}(R)$, and formalize e.g. the fact that module morphisms can sometimes be realized or constructed via matrices as an honest functor to or from this category.

* Fibered category $\mathsf{Mat}$ over `Rings`: total category $\mathsf{Mat}$ with objects $(R,n)$ where $R : \mathsf{Rings}$ and $n : NN$, and morphisms $(R,n) \to (S,m)$ are pairs $(\varphi: R\to S \text{ ring morphism}, A: \operatorname{Mat}_{m\times n}(S))$ with composition $(\psi,B)\circ(\varphi,A) = (\psi\circ\varphi, B\cdot\varphi_*(A))$ where $\varphi_*(A)$ is base-change of matrix entries along $\varphi$.
  Fiber over fixed $R$ is $\mathsf{Mat}(R)$ with $\operatorname{Ob}(\mathsf{Mat}(R)) = NN$ and $\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathsf{Mat}(R)}(n,m) = \operatorname{Mat}_{m\times n}(R)$ as $R$-module of $m\times n$ matrices, composition via matrix multiplication $B\circ A = B\cdot A : \operatorname{Mat}_{p\times n} = \operatorname{Mat}_{p\times m}\cdot\operatorname{Mat}_{m\times n}$, identities $I_n : \operatorname{Mat}_{n\times n}(R)$.
  This is the skeletal category of finite free $R$-modules: $R^n$ is object $n$.

* As skeletal model, $\mathsf{Mat}(R)$ is equivalent to $\mathsf{Free}_{\mathrm{fin}}(R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod})$ (finite free $R$-modules with $R$-linear maps) via functors $\operatorname{Free}: \mathsf{Mat}(R) \to R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod}$ sending $n \mapsto R^n$ and $A: \operatorname{Mat}_{m\times n}(R) \mapsto (R^n \to R^m, v\mapsto Av)$ as $R$-linear map, and $\operatorname{Coord}: \mathsf{Free}_{\mathrm{fin}}(R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod}) \to \mathsf{Mat}(R)$ choosing a basis (when $M \cong R^n$ free finite rank) sending $M$ with basis $e$ to $n$ and $f: M\to N$ to its matrix $[f]_e$ in bases.
  Need equivalence $\mathsf{Mat}(R) \simeq \mathsf{Free}_{\mathrm{fin}}(R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod})$ as categories, with $\operatorname{Hom}_R(R^n,R^m) \cong \operatorname{Mat}_{m\times n}(R)$ as $R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod}$ isomorphism via $A \mapsto (v\mapsto Av)$, natural in $R$ along base-change $\otimes_R S$.

* Module morphisms via matrices as honest functor to or from this category: for $M,N : R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod}$ with chosen finite presentations $R^a \to R^b \to M \to 0$ and $R^c \to R^d \to N \to 0$ (or with chosen bases when $M,N$ free $M\cong R^n, N\cong R^m$), a morphism $f: M\to N$ is sometimes realized by a matrix $A : \operatorname{Mat}_{d\times b}(R)$ (or $m\times n$ when free) with $A\cdot\mathrm{relations}_M \subseteq \mathrm{relations}_N$.
  Formalize as functor `MatrixRealization` $: \mathsf{Free}(R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod}) \rightleftarrows \mathsf{Mat}(R)$ and more generally for finitely presented $M,N$, a functor `PresMat` $: \mathsf{Mod}_{\mathrm{f.p.}}(R) \to \mathsf{Mat}(R)$-arrows up to homotopy, where $f$ lifts to $A: R^b\to R^d$ with $A\circ\mathrm{present}_M = \mathrm{present}_N\circ B$ for some $B$.
  The fact that $f$ can be constructed from $A$ when bases/presentations are fixed is an honest functor, not an ad-hoc `matrix_of_morphism(f)` returning a list.
  Similarly base-change along $R\to S$ is functorial $\mathsf{Mat}(R) \to \mathsf{Mat}(S)$ via $A \mapsto \varphi_*(A)$.

* Interface must be fibered: `R.matrix_category()` $\to\mathsf{Mat}(R) : \mathsf{Cat}$ with `Mat(R).objects() = NN`, `Mat(R).hom_set(n,m)` $\to\operatorname{Mat}_{m\times n}(R) : R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod}$, `Mat(R).composition(A,B)` $\to B\cdot A$, and `Rings` base `Rings.mat_fibration() -> Mat` total.
  For modules, `FreeModule(R,n).as_mat_object()` $\to n : \mathsf{Mat}(R)$ and `Hom_R(R^n,R^m).as_matrix(f)` $\to\operatorname{Mat}_{m\times n}(R)$ with $\operatorname{Hom}_R(R^n,R^m) \cong \operatorname{Mat}_{m\times n}(R)$.
  For general $M,N$ with `M.is_free_finite_rank()` or `M.is_finitely_presented()`, `f.matrix(basis_M, basis_N) -> Mat` and conversely `Mat(R).morphism(matrix, domain_basis, codomain_basis)` $\to f : M\to N$ as honest functor, with $\operatorname{matrix}(f\circ g)=\operatorname{matrix}(f)\cdot\operatorname{matrix}(g)$.

Intended owners: `categories/matrices/fibered_category.py` ($\mathsf{Mat}(R)$ fibered over `Rings` with $\operatorname{Ob}=NN$, $\operatorname{Hom}(n,m)=\operatorname{Mat}_{m\times n}(R)$, composition $\cdot$), `categories/modules/free_modules.py` ($R^n : \mathsf{Free}_{\mathrm{fin}}$ with $\mathsf{Mat}(R) \simeq \mathsf{Free}_{\mathrm{fin}}(R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod})$), `categories/functors/matrix_functor.py` (`MatrixRealization` $: \mathsf{Mat}(R) \rightleftarrows \mathsf{Free}(R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod})$ with $\operatorname{Hom}_R(R^n,R^m) \cong \operatorname{Mat}_{m\times n}(R)$), `categories/matrices/hom_sets.py` ($\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathsf{Mat}(R)}(n,m) = \operatorname{Mat}_{m\times n}(R) : R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod}$). Not a free `matrix_of_morphism(f)` returning `List[List[R]]` — `Hom_R(R^n,R^m).as_matrix()` and `Mat(R).morphism(matrix)` as functor between $\mathsf{Mat}(R)$ and $R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod}$.

## Desired capability: honest products and pullbacks of categories, categories fibred over other categories, stacks — note 2026-09-16

Need honest products and more generally pullbacks of categories, as well as categories fibred over other categories.
This leads to defining stacks.

* Honest products of categories: for $C,D : \mathsf{Cat}$, product $C\times D : \mathsf{Cat}$ with $\operatorname{Ob}(C\times D)=\operatorname{Ob}(C)\times\operatorname{Ob}(D)$, $\operatorname{Hom}_{C\times D}((c,d),(c',d')) = \operatorname{Hom}_C(c,c')\times\operatorname{Hom}_D(d,d')$ with componentwise composition $(f',g')\circ(f,g) = (f'\circ f, g'\circ g)$ and identities $(\operatorname{id}_c,\operatorname{id}_d)$, projections $\pi_C: C\times D\to C$, $\pi_D: C\times D\to D$ universal for pairs $E\to C, E\to D$.
  This is the categorical product in `Cat` (2-category).
  Similarly n-ary $\prod_{i} C_i$.
  Need `C.product(D)` $\to C\times D : \mathsf{Cat}$ and `Cat.has_products`.

* More generally pullbacks of categories: for functors $F: C\to E$ and $G: D\to E$, pullback (fiber product) $C\times_E D : \mathsf{Cat}$ with $\operatorname{Ob}(C\times_E D) = \{(c,d,\alpha: F(c) \to G(d) \text{ iso})\}$ for pseudo-pullback (or $F(c)=G(d)$ for strict), morphisms $(f: c\to c', g: d\to d')$ with $G(g)\circ\alpha = \alpha'\circ F(f)$, projections to $C,D$ and 2-cell $\alpha$.
  Universal for cones $E'\to C, E'\to D$ with $F\circ p_C \cong G\circ p_D$.
  Strict pullback $C\times_E^{\mathrm{str}} D$ when $F(c)=G(d)$ on nose is special case for discrete fibrations.
  This generalizes products ($C\times D = C\times_1 D$ over terminal $1$). Need `C.pullback(E,D, F,G)` $\to C\times_E D : \mathsf{Cat}$ with universal property.

* Categories fibred over other categories: functor $p: E\to B$ is Grothendieck fibration (category fibred over $B$) if for every $f: b'\to b$ in $B$ and $e\in E_b$ over $b$, there is cartesian lift $\varphi: f^*e \to e$ over $f$ universal for lifts: for any $\psi: e''\to e$ over $h: p(e'')\to b$ factoring as $h = f\circ g$, there is unique $\chi: e''\to f^*e$ over $g$ with $\varphi\circ\chi=\psi$.
  Equivalently cleavage $f^*: E_b \to E_{b'}$ pullback functor, with coherence $(g\circ f)^* \cong f^*\circ g^*$.
  Fiber $E_b = p^{-1}(b)$ is subcategory of objects over $b$ and vertical morphisms over $\operatorname{id}_b$.
  Examples: $\mathsf{Mat} \to \mathsf{Rings}$ fibered with fiber $\mathsf{Mat}(R)$, $\mathsf{Mod} \to \mathsf{Rings}$ with fiber $R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod}$, $\mathsf{QCoh} \to \mathsf{Sch}$, $\mathsf{Fam} \to \mathsf{Sch}$.
  Need $p: E\to B$ as `FiberedCategory` with `p.is_fibred()`, `p.cartesian_lift(f,e)`, `p.fiber(b)` $\to E_b : \mathsf{Cat}$, `p.pullback_functor(f)` $\to f^*: E_b\to E_{b'}$.

* Stacks: a category fibred over a site $(B,J)$ (e.g. `Sch` with étale topology $J_{\text{ét}}$, or `Top` with open covers) satisfying effective descent — i.e. 2-sheaf condition.
  For cover $U = \{u_i: b_i\to b\}$ in $J$, descent data is family $e_i\in E_{b_i}$ with isomorphisms $\alpha_{ij}: \operatorname{pr}_1^* e_i \cong \operatorname{pr}_2^* e_j$ over $b_i\times_b b_j$ satisfying cocycle $\alpha_{jk}\circ\alpha_{ij}=\alpha_{ik}$ over triple overlaps $b_i\times_b b_j\times_b b_k$.
  Effectivity means descent data glue to $e\in E_b$ with $e|_{b_i} \cong e_i$ via $\alpha$.
  Stack condition requires descent for objects (effective) and for morphisms (sheaf condition $\operatorname{Hom}_{E_b}(e,e')$ is sheaf on $B/b$). Algebraic stacks are stacks on $\mathsf{Sch}_{\text{ét}}$ representable by groupoid presentation $R\rightrightarrows U$ with $R,U : \mathsf{Sch}$ and $p: E\to\mathsf{Sch}$ fibered.
  This leads to defining stacks as 2-sheaves: $\mathsf{Stacks}(B,J) \subset \mathsf{FiberedCat}(B)$ full subcategory of fibred categories satisfying descent.
  Need `p.is_stack(J)`, `p.descent_data(U)`, `p.effective_descent(U)` $\to E_b$.

Intended owners: `categories/cat/products.py` (`Cat` product $C\times D$ with $\operatorname{Ob}\times\operatorname{Ob}$, $\operatorname{Hom}\times\operatorname{Hom}$), `categories/cat/pullbacks.py` (`Cat` pullback $C\times_E D$ with $\operatorname{Ob}(c,d,\alpha)$, universal), `categories/fibered/fibered_categories.py` (`FiberedCategory` $p: E\to B$ with `cartesian_lift`, `fiber(b)`, `pullback_functor`), `categories/stacks/stacks.py` (`Stack` as fibered category satisfying effective descent for $J$, 2-sheaf, leading to algebraic stacks).
Not a free `product_category(C,D)` returning tuple of objects — `C.product(D)` $\to C\times D : \mathsf{Cat}$ with $C\times_E D$ pullback and $p: E\to B$ fibered with cleavage and stacks as descent-satisfying fibred categories.

## Desired capability: $\mathsf{Top}$ and $\mathsf{Top}_*$, $S = \mathsf{Spaces} =$ homotopy types $= \infty$-groupoids — note 2026-09-16

Need categories $\mathsf{Top}$ and $\mathsf{Top}_*$ (if not already present), and $S = \mathsf{Spaces} =$ homotopy types $= \infty$-groupoids.

* $\mathsf{Top} : \mathsf{Cat}$ with $\operatorname{Ob}(\mathsf{Top}) =$ topological spaces $X$ (e.g. $\mathsf{Top}$ as convenient category of compactly generated weak Hausdorff or all spaces with $k$-ification), $\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathsf{Top}}(X,Y) = C^0(X,Y)$ continuous maps with compact-open topology as $\mathsf{Top}$-enriched $\operatorname{Hom}$, composition via $\circ$, identities $\operatorname{id}_X$.
