  Similarly $\mathsf{Top}_* = \mathsf{Top}_{*/}$ pointed with $\operatorname{Ob}(\mathsf{Top}_*) = (X,x_0)$ pointed spaces, $\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathsf{Top}_*}((X,x_0),(Y,y_0)) = \{ f: X\to Y \text{ continuous} \mid f(x_0)=y_0 \}$ basepoint-preserving, with smash $\wedge: \mathsf{Top}_*\times\mathsf{Top}_*\to\mathsf{Top}_*$ and wedge $\vee$, and $\mathsf{Top}_* = */\mathsf{Top}$ slice.

* $S = \mathsf{Spaces} = \infty\mathsf{Grpd}$ is the $\infty$-category of homotopy types / $\infty$-groupoids, obtained as localization $S = \mathsf{Top}[W^{-1}]$ with $W =$ weak homotopy equivalences $f: X\to Y$ inducing $\pi_n(f): \pi_n(X,x)\to\pi_n(Y,f(x))$ isomorphisms for all $n\geq 0$, $x\in X$, or equivalently $S \simeq \mathsf{sSet}[W^{-1}]$ with $W =$ weak equivalences of simplicial sets, or $S \simeq \mathsf{CW}[W^{-1}]$ with $\mathsf{CW}$ finite CW complexes.
  As $\infty$-category, $S \simeq \infty\mathsf{Grpd}$ via $X \mapsto \Pi_\infty(X) = \operatorname{Sing}(X) : \mathsf{Kan}$ and $|-| : \mathsf{Kan} \to \mathsf{Top}$ adjunction $|-| \dashv \operatorname{Sing}$ exhibiting $S$ as $\infty$-groupoids.
  Need $S$ as $\infty\mathsf{Cat}$ with $\operatorname{Ob}(S) =$ spaces up to weak equivalence, $\operatorname{Map}_S(X,Y) = \operatorname{Map}_{\mathsf{Top}}(X,Y)$ as $S$ itself (mapping space), with $S$ cartesian closed, $S$ as $\infty$-topos.

Intended owners: `categories/top/top.py` ($\mathsf{Top} : \mathsf{Cat}$ with $\operatorname{Ob} = \mathsf{TopSpaces}$, $\operatorname{Hom} = C^0$), `categories/top/pointed.py` ($\mathsf{Top}_* : \mathsf{Cat}$ with $\operatorname{Ob} = (X,x_0)$, $\operatorname{Hom}_*$), `categories/spaces/spaces.py` ($S = \mathsf{Spaces} = \infty\mathsf{Grpd} : \infty\mathsf{Cat}$ with $S = \mathsf{Top}[W^{-1}]$, $S \simeq \mathsf{sSet}[W^{-1}]$, $S \simeq \infty\mathsf{Grpd}$ via $\operatorname{Sing}/|-|$). Not a free `top_category()` returning list of spaces — $\mathsf{Top}$, $\mathsf{Top}_*$, $S$ as categories/$\infty$-categories with $\operatorname{Hom}$ as mapping spaces.

## Desired capability: simplicial complexes and Kan complexes operationalized — note 2026-09-16

Need simplicial complexes operationalized and specialize to Kan complexes.

* Simplicial complex $K$ is finite (or locally finite) collection of simplices $\sigma \subset V$ with $V : \mathsf{FiniteSets}$ vertices, closed under faces $\tau\subset\sigma \Rightarrow \tau\in K$ and intersections $\sigma\cap\tau \in K$ (or as $\sigma\in K$), with geometric realization $|K| : \mathsf{Top}$ via $|K| = (\coprod_{\sigma\in K} \Delta^{|\sigma|-1})/\sim$ gluing along faces.
  Need $K : \mathsf{SimplicialComplexes}$ with `K.vertices()` $\to V : \mathsf{FinSets}$, `K.simplices()` $\to \{\sigma\}$, `K.realization()` $\to |K| : \mathsf{Top}$ and `K.chain_complex()` $\to C_*(K)$.

* Kan complex specialization: $\mathsf{sSet} : \mathsf{Cat}$ simplicial sets $X: \Delta^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathsf{Set}$ with $X_n$ n-simplices, $\mathsf{Kan} \subset \mathsf{sSet}$ full subcategory with $\mathsf{Kan}$ fibrancy: every horn $\Lambda^n_k \to X$ for $0\leq k\leq n$, $n\geq 1$ extends to $\Delta^n \to X$ (horn filler).
  Every $\operatorname{Sing}(X)$ for $X: \mathsf{Top}$ is Kan, and any $\mathsf{sSet}$ has fibrant replacement $\operatorname{Ex}^\infty(X) : \mathsf{Kan}$ via Kan's $\operatorname{Ex}^\infty$ (or $\operatorname{Sing}(|X|)$). Quillen model $\mathsf{sSet}_{\mathrm{Quillen}}$ has $\mathsf{Kan}$ as fibrant objects, $W =$ weak equivalences, $\mathrm{Cof} =$ monomorphisms, $\mathrm{Fib} =$ Kan fibrations.
  Need `KanComplex` $: \mathsf{sSet}$ with `Kan.is_kan()`, `Kan.horn_filler`$(\Lambda^n_k\to\mathsf{Kan}) \to \Delta^n\to\mathsf{Kan}$, and `X.kan_replacement()` $\to \operatorname{Ex}^\infty(X) : \mathsf{Kan}$ with $X \to \operatorname{Ex}^\infty(X)$ weak equivalence.
  `SimplicialComplex` to $\mathsf{Kan}$ via $\operatorname{Sing}(|K|)$ or via $N_{\mathrm{simp}}(K)$ as $\mathsf{Kan}$ when ordered.

Intended owners: `categories/simplicial/complexes.py` (`SimplicialComplex` with `realization` $: \mathsf{Top}$, `chain_complex`), `categories/simplicial/kan.py` (`KanComplex` $: \mathsf{sSet}$ with `horn_filler`, `kan_replacement` $= \operatorname{Ex}^\infty$), `categories/topology/simplicial_sets.py` ($\mathsf{sSet}$, $\operatorname{Sing}$, $|-|$, $\operatorname{Ex}^\infty$). Not a free `simplicial_complex(vertices)` returning list — `SimplicialComplex` as $\mathsf{Top}$ via $|K|$ and $\mathsf{Kan}$ with horn fillers.

## Desired capability: categories from finitary data — graphs, posets, topologies — note 2026-09-16

Need to generalize to allow manually constructing categories from finitary data like graphs, posets, topologies on a space, etc.

* From directed graph $G = (V,E,s,t: E\to V)$ (finite $V,E : \mathsf{FinSets}$), free category $\operatorname{FreeCat}(G) : \mathsf{Cat}$ with $\operatorname{Ob} = V$, $\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{FreeCat}(G)}(x,y) =$ paths $x = v_0 \to^{e_1} v_1 \to \ldots \to^{e_k} v_k = y$ with $s(e_i)=v_{i-1}$, $t(e_i)=v_i$, composition via concatenation, identities as empty path at $v$.
  More generally $G$ with relations $R \subset \operatorname{Mor}(\operatorname{FreeCat}(G))\times\operatorname{Mor}(\operatorname{FreeCat}(G))$ gives $\mathsf{Cat}\langle G\mid R\rangle = \operatorname{FreeCat}(G)/R$.

* From poset $P = (P,\leq)$ (finite $P : \mathsf{Posets}$), poset category $\operatorname{PosCat}(P) : \mathsf{Cat}$ already intaken with $\operatorname{Ob} = P$, $\operatorname{Hom}(x,y)=1$ if $x\leq y$ else $\emptyset$, composition via transitivity.
  This is thin skeletal.

* From topology $\tau$ on finite set $X$ (finite $X : \mathsf{FinSets}$, $\tau \subset P(X)$ topology with $\emptyset,X\in\tau$, closed under $\cup,\cap$), the specialization preorder $x \leq_\tau y \Leftrightarrow x\in\operatorname{cl}(\{y\}) \Leftrightarrow \text{every } U\in\tau \text{ with } x\in U \text{ implies } y\in U$ gives poset $P_\tau$ when $\tau$ is $T_0$ (or preorder otherwise), and $\mathsf{Top}(X,\tau) \to \mathsf{Cat}$ via $\operatorname{PosCat}(P_\tau)$ or via $\operatorname{Op}(X) = (\tau,\subseteq)$ poset category of opens.
  More generally $X : \mathsf{Top}$ with finite $\tau$ as Alexandroff space gives $\mathsf{Cat}$ with $\operatorname{Ob} = X$ and $\operatorname{Hom}(x,y)=1$ if $x$ specializes to $y$.
  Need `TopologyToCategory`$(X,\tau) \to \mathsf{Cat}$ with finite presentation.

* General finitary data $\to \mathsf{Cat}$ must be uniform: input $D : \mathsf{FinData}$ with `D.objects` $: \mathsf{FinSets}$ and `D.generators` $: \mathsf{FinSets}$ and `D.relations` $: \mathsf{FinSets}$, output $\mathsf{Cat}(D) : \mathsf{Cat}$ with finite $\operatorname{Ob}$ and $\operatorname{Hom}$ via generators and relations, computable via path concatenation modulo $R$ (Knuth-Bendix when confluent).
  Need `Cat.from_graph(G)` $\to \operatorname{FreeCat}(G)$, `Cat.from_poset(P)` $\to \operatorname{PosCat}(P)$, `Cat.from_topology`$(X,\tau) \to \operatorname{TopCat}(X,\tau)$ all as instances of `FinitaryCategory` construction.

Intended owners: `categories/cat/finitary.py` (`FinitaryCategory` from $D : \mathsf{FinData}$ with $\operatorname{Ob} : \mathsf{FinSets}$, $\operatorname{Hom}$ via generators/relations), `categories/graphs/free_category.py` ($\operatorname{FreeCat}(G)$ from graph $G$), `categories/posets/poset_category.py` ($\operatorname{PosCat}(P)$ already), `categories/topology/topology_to_category.py` ($\operatorname{TopCat}(X,\tau)$ via $\operatorname{Op}(X)$ or specialization poset).
Not a free `category_from_graph(graph)` returning dict — `Cat.from_graph(G)` $: \mathsf{Cat}$ with finite $\operatorname{Ob}$/$\operatorname{Hom}$ via path category.

## Desired capability: (homotopy coherent) nerve of finitary category as Kan complex — note 2026-09-16

Need to express the (homotopy coherent) nerve of a relatively finitary category like any of these as an actual Kan complex that one can work with and compute with.

* Ordinary nerve $N(C) : \mathsf{sSet}$ for $C : \mathsf{Cat}$ (finitary with finite $\operatorname{Ob}$ and $\operatorname{Hom}$ via graph/poset/topology) has $N(C)_n = \operatorname{Fun}([n],C)$ with $[n] = 0\to 1\to\ldots\to n$ ordinal category, face $d_i$ via composition at $i$, degeneracy $s_i$ via identities.
  This is $N: \mathsf{Cat} \to \mathsf{sSet}$ fully faithful, $N(C)$ is 2-coskeletal and weak Kan when $C$ is groupoid, but not Kan in general.
  Need `C.nerve()` $\to N(C) : \mathsf{sSet}$ with $N(C)_n$ as $\mathsf{Set}$ of $n$-chains of composable morphisms, computable from finitary presentation via $\operatorname{Ob}$, $\operatorname{Hom}$, $\circ$.

* Homotopy coherent nerve $N_{\mathrm{hc}}(C) : \mathsf{sSet}$ for $C$ enriched in $S$ ($S$-enriched category, e.g. $S = \mathsf{Kan}$ or $S = \mathsf{Top}$ or $S = \mathsf{Cat}$) has $N_{\mathrm{hc}}(C)_n = \operatorname{Hom}_{S\text{-}\mathsf{Cat}}(\mathfrak{C}[n], C)$ where $\mathfrak{C}[n]$ is Boardman-Vogt resolution of $[n]$ with $\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathfrak{C}[n]}(i,j) =$ nerve of poset of subsets $P_{i,j}$ as $\mathsf{Kan}$ complex.
  When $C$ is $S$-enriched with $S = \mathsf{Kan}$, $N_{\mathrm{hc}}(C)$ is a quasicategory ($\infty$-category), and when $C$ is $\infty$-groupoid enriched (i.e. $\mathsf{Kan}$-enriched groupoid), $N_{\mathrm{hc}}(C)$ is Kan.

* For relatively finitary $C$ (finite $\operatorname{Ob}$, $\operatorname{Hom}$ finite via graph/poset/topology), the (homotopy coherent) nerve must be an actual $\mathsf{Kan}$ complex when $C$ is groupoid/ Kan-enriched (e.g. $C = \Pi_1(X)$ fundamental groupoid of finite $\mathsf{CW}$ with $\operatorname{Hom}(x,y) =$ paths up to homotopy as $\mathsf{Kan}$, or $C = \operatorname{Op}(X)$ with $\operatorname{Hom}$ discrete as $\mathsf{Kan}$ via constant simplicial set, but $N(C)$ then is not Kan unless $C$ is groupoid — need fibrant replacement $\operatorname{Ex}^\infty(N(C)) : \mathsf{Kan}$ as $\mathsf{Kan}$ replacement.
  Need `C.homotopy_coherent_nerve()` $\to N_{\mathrm{hc}}(C) : \mathsf{sSet}$ and when $C$ is suitably enriched (e.g. $S$-enriched with Kan mapping spaces), $N_{\mathrm{hc}}(C)$ is Kan with effective horn fillers via $C$'s composition and $\mathsf{Kan}$ fillers.

* Need actual Kan complex object one can work with and compute with: $\mathsf{Kan}$ with `Kan.horn_filler`$(\Lambda^n_k \to \mathsf{Kan}) \to \Delta^n \to \mathsf{Kan}$ effective via $C$'s finitary $\operatorname{Hom}$ and via $\operatorname{Ex}^\infty$ Kan replacement when not fibrant, with `Kan.simplices(n)` $\to \mathsf{Kan}_n : \mathsf{FinSets}$ when $C$ finitary (finite $\operatorname{Ob}$, $\operatorname{Hom}$ finite sets give finite $N(C)_n$ for each $n$ bounded).
  For $C$ from graph/poset/topology with finite $\operatorname{Ob}$ and finite generating $\operatorname{Hom}$, $N(C)_n$ is finite for each $n$ (composable chains), and $N_{\mathrm{hc}}(C)_n$ is finite $\mathsf{Kan}$ complex via $P_{i,j}$ posets.
  Need `C.nerve_as_kan()` $\to \mathsf{Kan}$ as $\mathsf{Kan}$ complex with $\mathsf{Kan}_n$ finite sets for bounded $n$, computable horn fillers via $C$ presentation.

Intended owners: `categories/nerve/nerve.py` (`Nerve` $N(C) : \mathsf{sSet}$ with $N(C)_n = \operatorname{Fun}([n],C)$), `categories/nerve/homotopy_coherent_nerve.py` (`HomotopyCoherentNerve` $N_{\mathrm{hc}}(C) : \mathsf{sSet}$ with $\operatorname{Map}(i,j) = \mathsf{Kan}$), `categories/simplicial/kan.py` (`Kan` with `horn_filler`, $\operatorname{Ex}^\infty$ fibrant replacement).
Not a free `nerve_of_category(C)` returning list of simplices — `C.nerve()` $: \mathsf{sSet}$ and `C.homotopy_coherent_nerve()` $: \mathsf{Kan}$ with effective $\mathsf{Kan}$ structure.

## Desired capability: Lurie's tangent category, cotangent complex, obstruction theories, DGLAs, Sullivan minimal models — note 2026-09-16

Need Lurie's tangent category construction, the cotangent complex, classical obstruction theories, DGLAs, Sullivan minimal models.

* Lurie's tangent category $T_C : \mathsf{Cat}$ for $C : \mathsf{Cat}$ presentable (or for $X : C$ with $C$ presentable stable) is $T_C = \operatorname{Exc}_*(S^{\mathrm{fin}}_*, C)$ excisive functors, or more concretely $T_X = \operatorname{Stab}(C_{/X}) = \operatorname{Sp}(C_{/X})$ stabilization of slice $C_{/X}$ as $\mathsf{Spectra}$ object, with $T_X \to C_{/X}$ via $\Omega^\infty$.
  For $C = \mathsf{CAlg}_k$, $T_{A} = \mathsf{Mod}_A$ via $T_{\mathsf{CAlg}_k, A} \simeq \mathsf{Mod}_A$ and cotangent complex $L_{A/k} : \mathsf{Mod}_A$ is image of $A \in \mathsf{CAlg}_{k/A}$.
  More generally for $X : C$, $L_X : T_X$ is the cotangent complex as suspension of diagonal.
  Need $T_C$ as $\operatorname{Stab}$ and $L_X : T_X$.

* Cotangent complex $L_{X/Y} : T_X$ for $X\to Y$ in $C$ (e.g. $A\to B$ in $\mathsf{CAlg}_k$ gives $L_{B/A} : \mathsf{Mod}_B$ with $L_{B/A} \in D(B)$ as $B$-module).
  For $A : \mathsf{CAlg}_k$ (simplicial commutative algebras), $L_{A/k}$ controls deformations: $\operatorname{Map}_{\mathsf{CAlg}_k/A}(A, A\oplus M) \simeq \operatorname{Map}_{\mathsf{Mod}_A}(L_{A/k}, M)$ for $M : \mathsf{Mod}_A$.
  Classical $L_{B/A}$ for ordinary rings is truncation $\tau_{\le 0} L_{B/A}^{\mathrm{der}}$.
  Need `X.cotangent_complex()` $\to L_X : T_X$ with $L_{B/A}$ via Kähler $\Omega_{B/A}$ when $B = A[x_1,\ldots,x_n]/(f_j)$ and resolution.

* Classical obstruction theories: for $X : \mathsf{Schemes}$ (or $\mathsf{Artin\ stacks}$), perfect obstruction theory $E \to L_X$ with $E : \operatorname{Perf}(X)$ perfect complex $[-1,0]$ and $h^0(E) \cong h^0(L_X)$, $h^{-1}(E) \to h^{-1}(L_X)$ surjective, giving virtual class $[X]^{\mathrm{vir}} \in A_*(X)$ and obstruction $o \in \operatorname{Ext}^1(L_X, I)$ for deformations of maps $\operatorname{Spec} A \to X$ to $\operatorname{Spec} A' \to X$ with square-zero $I$.
  DGLA $g$ governs deformations via Maurer-Cartan $\operatorname{MC}(g) = \{ x\in g^1 \mid dx + \frac{1}{2}[x,x]=0 \}/ \text{gauge}$, with $g = \operatorname{RHom}(E, O_X)$ etc. Need `E.obstruction_theory()` $\to (E\to L_X)$ and $g = \operatorname{RHom}$.

* DGLAs and Sullivan minimal models: DGLA $g = \bigoplus_{i\in ZZ} g^i$ with $d: g^i\to g^{i+1}$, $[ -,- ]: g^i\otimes g^j\to g^{i+j}$ graded Lie with Jacobi and $d[x,y]=[dx,y]+(-1)^{|x|}[x,dy]$, Maurer-Cartan $\operatorname{MC}(g)$, gauge $\exp(g^0)$.
  Sullivan minimal model $M_X = (\Lambda V, d) \to A_{\mathrm{PL}}(X)$ for $X : \mathsf{Top}_{\mathrm{nil}}$ nilpotent finite-type with $V = \bigoplus_{n\geq 1} V^n$ graded $V^n \cong \operatorname{Hom}(\pi_n(X),Q)$, $d(V)\subset\Lambda^{\geq 2}V$ decomposable, $\Lambda V$ free graded-commutative, $M_X \to A_{\mathrm{PL}}(X)$ quasi-isomorphism minimal.
  Need `DGLA` as $\mathsf{LieAlg}(\mathsf{Ch})$ and `SullivanMinimalModel(X)` $\to (\Lambda V,d)$ with $V^n$.

Intended owners: `categories/tangent/tangent_category.py` ($T_C = \operatorname{Stab}(C_{/X})$, $T_X$), `categories/deformation/cotangent_complex.py` ($L_{X/Y} : T_X$ with $L_{A/k}$), `categories/deformation/obstruction_theories.py` ($E\to L_X$ perfect, $o\in\operatorname{Ext}^1$), `categories/dgla/dglas.py` (`DGLA` with $\operatorname{MC}$), `categories/rational/sullivan.py` (`SullivanMinimalModel` $M_X=(\Lambda V,d)$). Not a free `cotangent_complex(X)` returning matrix — `X.cotangent_complex()` $: T_X$ with $L_{A/k}$.

## Desired capability: Yoneda and coYoneda, Mor, functor of points — note 2026-09-16

Need the Yoneda and coYoneda embeddings for any category sending objects to honest functors, operationalizing $\operatorname{Mor}(*,*)$ as a functor from an honest product category $C\times C^{\mathrm{op}}$ which admits currying arguments to produce the above functors.

* For any $C : \mathsf{Cat}$, Yoneda embedding $y: C \to \operatorname{Fun}(C^{\mathrm{op}},\mathsf{Set})$ sending $c \mapsto y(c) = \operatorname{Hom}_C(-,c) : C^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathsf{Set}$ with $y(c)(d)=\operatorname{Hom}_C(d,c)$ and $y(f: c\to c')_d = f\circ- : \operatorname{Hom}(d,c)\to\operatorname{Hom}(d,c')$, fully faithful via Yoneda lemma $\operatorname{Nat}(y(c),F) \cong F(c)$.
  CoYoneda $y^{\mathrm{co}}: C^{\mathrm{op}} \to \operatorname{Fun}(C,\mathsf{Set})$ with $y^{\mathrm{co}}(c)=\operatorname{Hom}_C(c,-)$.
  Need `C.yoneda()` $\to y : C \to \operatorname{Fun}(C^{\mathrm{op}},\mathsf{Set})$ and `C.coyoneda()` $\to y^{\mathrm{co}}$ as honest functors $C \to \operatorname{Fun}(C^{\mathrm{op}},\mathsf{Set})$ and $C^{\mathrm{op}} \to \operatorname{Fun}(C,\mathsf{Set})$, not as ad-hoc $\operatorname{Hom}$ functions.

* Operationalize $\operatorname{Mor} := \operatorname{Hom}_C(-,-): C^{\mathrm{op}}\times C \to \mathsf{Set}$ as functor from honest product category $C^{\mathrm{op}}\times C : \mathsf{Cat}$ with $\operatorname{Ob} = \operatorname{Ob}(C)\times\operatorname{Ob}(C)$, $\operatorname{Hom}((a,b),(a',b')) = \operatorname{Hom}_{C^{\mathrm{op}}}(a,a')\times\operatorname{Hom}_C(b,b') = \operatorname{Hom}_C(a',a)\times\operatorname{Hom}_C(b,b')$ via product $C\times C^{\mathrm{op}}$ already intaken, with $\operatorname{Mor}(a,b)=\operatorname{Hom}_C(a,b)$, $\operatorname{Mor}(f: a'\to a, g: b\to b') = g\circ-\circ f : \operatorname{Hom}(a,b)\to\operatorname{Hom}(a',b')$.
  Need `C.mor_functor()` $\to \operatorname{Mor}: C^{\mathrm{op}}\times C \to \mathsf{Set}$ as $\operatorname{Fun}(C^{\mathrm{op}}\times C,\mathsf{Set})$ with $\operatorname{Mor}(a,b)=\operatorname{Hom}_C(a,b)$.

* Currying: $\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{Fun}(C^{\mathrm{op}},\mathsf{Set})}(y(c), F) \cong F(c)$ is currying of $\operatorname{Mor}$.
  More generally $\operatorname{Fun}(C^{\mathrm{op}}\times C,\mathsf{Set}) \cong \operatorname{Fun}(C, \operatorname{Fun}(C^{\mathrm{op}},\mathsf{Set}))$ via currying $F \mapsto (c \mapsto F(-,c))$ and $\operatorname{Fun}(C^{\mathrm{op}}, \operatorname{Fun}(C,\mathsf{Set}))$.
  So $\operatorname{Mor}: C^{\mathrm{op}}\times C\to\mathsf{Set}$ curries to $y: C\to\operatorname{Fun}(C^{\mathrm{op}},\mathsf{Set})$ via $y(c)=\operatorname{Mor}(-,c)$ and to $y^{\mathrm{co}}: C^{\mathrm{op}}\to\operatorname{Fun}(C,\mathsf{Set})$ via $y^{\mathrm{co}}(c)=\operatorname{Mor}(c,-)$.
  Need `Mor.curry()` $\to y$ and `Mor.curry_op()` $\to y^{\mathrm{co}}$ as functors, with `y(c).evaluated_at(d)` $=\operatorname{Mor}(d,c)$.

* Functor of points $h_X$ for any object in any category: for $C : \mathsf{Cat}$ and $X : C$, $h_X = \operatorname{Hom}_C(-,X) = y(X) : C^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathsf{Set}$ is the functor of points, with $h_X(Y)=\operatorname{Hom}(Y,X)$, $h_X(f: Y'\to Y)= -\circ f$.
  This is the Yoneda image $y(X)$.
  Need `X.functor_of_points()` $\to h_X : \operatorname{Fun}(C^{\mathrm{op}},\mathsf{Set})$ with $h_X = y(X)$, and $h^X = \operatorname{Hom}(X,-) : \operatorname{Fun}(C,\mathsf{Set})$ co-points.
  For $C = \mathsf{Sch}$, $h_X$ is the usual $\mathsf{Sch}$-valued functor of points $h_X(T)=\operatorname{Hom}(T,X)$.

Intended owners: `categories/yoneda/yoneda.py` (`Yoneda` $y: C\to\operatorname{Fun}(C^{\mathrm{op}},\mathsf{Set})$), `categories/yoneda/coyoneda.py` (`CoYoneda` $y^{\mathrm{co}}: C^{\mathrm{op}}\to\operatorname{Fun}(C,\mathsf{Set})$), `categories/hom/mor_functor.py` ($\operatorname{Mor}: C^{\mathrm{op}}\times C\to\mathsf{Set}$ from honest $C\times C^{\mathrm{op}}$ with currying `Mor.curry()` $\to y$), `categories/functor_of_points.py` ($h_X = \operatorname{Hom}(-,X) : C^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathsf{Set}$ for any $X:C$). Not a free `yoneda_embedding(C)` returning dict of Homs — `C.yoneda()` $: C \to \operatorname{Fun}(C^{\mathrm{op}},\mathsf{Set})$ with $\operatorname{Mor}: C^{\mathrm{op}}\times C\to\mathsf{Set}$ from product $C\times C^{\mathrm{op}}$ and currying.

## Desired capability: abelianization, centers, centralizers, stabilizers, Hurewicz — note 2026-09-16

Need abelianization of a group, center of a group, centralizers and stabilizers for groups, centers for rings, the Hurewicz morphism(s).

* Abelianization of a group $G : \mathsf{Groups}$ is $\operatorname{Ab}(G)=G^{\mathrm{ab}}=G/[G,G] : \mathsf{AbGroups}$ with quotient $\pi: G\to G^{\mathrm{ab}}$ universal for maps to abelian groups: for any $A : \mathsf{AbGroups}$ and $f: G\to A$, there is unique $\bar f: G^{\mathrm{ab}}\to A$ with $f = \bar f\circ\pi$.
  This is left adjoint $\operatorname{Ab} \dashv \operatorname{Incl}: \mathsf{AbGroups} \to \mathsf{Groups}$.
  Need `G.abelianization()` $\to G^{\mathrm{ab}} : \mathsf{AbGroups}$ with `G.abelianization_map()` $\to \pi: G\to G^{\mathrm{ab}}$ and adjunction $\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathsf{Ab}}(G^{\mathrm{ab}},A) \cong \operatorname{Hom}_{\mathsf{Groups}}(G,A)$.

* Center of a group $Z(G) = \{ g\in G \mid \forall h\in G, gh=hg \} \triangleleft G$ as normal subgroup $Z(G) \leq G$ with $Z(G) = \ker(G \to \operatorname{Aut}(G), g\mapsto\operatorname{conj}_g)$ where $\operatorname{conj}_g(h)=ghg^{-1}$.
  Centralizer of $S\subset G$ is $C_G(S) = \{ g\in G \mid \forall s\in S, gs=sg \} \leq G$, with $C_G(S)=\bigcap_{s\in S} C_G(\{s\})$, and $Z(G)=C_G(G)$.
  Stabilizer for $G\curvearrowright X$ action $a: G\times X\to X$ and $x\in X$ is $\operatorname{Stab}_G(x) = \{ g\in G \mid g\cdot x = x \} \leq G$ with $G\cdot x \cong G/\operatorname{Stab}_G(x)$ as $G$-set.
  Need `G.center()` $\to Z(G) : \operatorname{Sub}(G)$, `G.centralizer(S)` $\to C_G(S)$, `G.stabilizer(x)` $\to \operatorname{Stab}_G(x)$ for $G$-set $X$.

* Centers for rings $R : \mathsf{Rings}$ (associative, possibly non-commutative) is $Z(R) = \{ r\in R \mid \forall s\in R, rs=sr \} \leq R$ as subring $Z(R) : \mathsf{Rings}$ commutative, with $Z(R) = \operatorname{End}_{R\otimes R^{\mathrm{op}}}(R)$ as bimodule endomorphisms.
  For $R : \mathsf{CommRings}$, $Z(R)=R$.
  Need `R.center()` $\to Z(R) : \mathsf{Rings}$.

* Hurewicz morphism(s) $h_n: \pi_n(X,x) \to H_n(X;ZZ)$ for $X : \mathsf{Top}_*$ pointed connected (or $X : \mathsf{CW}$) with $\pi_n = [S^n,X]_*$ and $H_n = H_n^{\mathrm{sing}}(X;ZZ)$ via $H_n : \mathsf{GrMod}$, induced by $\pi_n(X) \to \pi_n(X,X^{n-1}) \cong H_n(X^n,X^{n-1}) \to H_n(X)$ or via $h_n([f]) = f_*[S^n]$ with $f: S^n\to X$ and $[S^n]\in H_n(S^n)$.
  Stable Hurewicz $h^s_n: \pi_n^s(X) \to H_n(X)$ similarly.
  Need `X.hurewicz(n)` $\to h_n: \pi_n(X)\to H_n(X)$ as $\mathsf{Groups}\to\mathsf{Ab}$ morphism with $h_n$ natural in $X$ and $h_1 : \pi_1^{\mathrm{ab}} \cong H_1$ isomorphism (Hurewicz theorem for 1-connected $X$ with $\pi_i=0$ for $i<n$ gives $h_n$ iso).

Intended owners: `categories/groups/abelianization.py` ($\operatorname{Ab}(G)=G/[G,G]$ with $\pi: G\to G^{\mathrm{ab}}$), `categories/groups/center.py` ($Z(G)$, $C_G(S)$, $\operatorname{Stab}_G(x)$), `categories/rings/center.py` ($Z(R)$), `categories/homotopy/hurewicz.py` (`Hurewicz` $h_n: \pi_n\to H_n$ with $h_1^{\mathrm{ab}}$). Not a free `abelianization(G)` returning quotient list — `G.abelianization()` $: \mathsf{AbGroups}$ with `G.center()` $: \operatorname{Sub}(G)$ and `X.hurewicz(n)` $: \pi_n\to H_n$.

## Desired capability: implicitly deriving tensor and hom, tensor-hom and power/copowering adjunctions, enriched, induction/restriction — note 2026-09-16

Need implicitly deriving as much as possible, especially tensor and hom, tensor-hom adjunction, and more general power/copowering adjunctions, attaching enriched structures where they are known to hold by theorems, induction and restriction functors for $k[G]$ modules / $G$-reps (Frobenius reciprocity).

* Implicitly deriving means for $C : \mathsf{Cat}$ with weak equivalences $W$ (e.g. $\mathsf{Ch}(R)$, $\mathsf{sSet}$, $\mathsf{Top}$, $\mathsf{Mod}_R$), derive functors $L F : \operatorname{Ho}(C) \to \operatorname{Ho}(D)$ and $R G : \operatorname{Ho}(C) \to \operatorname{Ho}(D)$ automatically where $F \dashv G$ Quillen or where $C$ has enough projectives/injectives, without hand-written $L\otimes$ per case.
  Especially $\otimes : C\times C\to C$ and $\operatorname{Hom}: C^{\mathrm{op}}\times C\to C$ derive to $\otimes^L : \operatorname{Ho}(C)\times\operatorname{Ho}(C)\to\operatorname{Ho}(C)$ and $\operatorname{RHom}: \operatorname{Ho}(C)^{\mathrm{op}}\times\operatorname{Ho}(C)\to\operatorname{Ho}(C)$ as $\operatorname{Ho}(C)$-enriched, with $L(\otimes) = \otimes^L$ and $R(\operatorname{Hom})=\operatorname{RHom}$.
  Need `C.implicitly_derive(F)` $\to LF$ or $RF$ via cofibrant/fibrant replacement $Q,R$.

* Tensor-hom adjunction $\operatorname{Hom}(X\otimes Y,Z) \cong \operatorname{Hom}(X,\operatorname{Hom}(Y,Z))$ as $\mathsf{Set}$ iso natural in $X,Y,Z$ for closed monoidal $C$ with $\otimes : C\times C\to C$ left adjoint to $\operatorname{Hom}: C^{\mathrm{op}}\times C\to C$ on each variable: $-\otimes Y \dashv \operatorname{Hom}(Y,-)$ with counit $\operatorname{ev}: \operatorname{Hom}(Y,Z)\otimes Y\to Z$ and unit $\operatorname{coev}: X\to\operatorname{Hom}(Y,X\otimes Y)$.
  Derived version $\operatorname{RHom}(X\otimes^L Y,Z) \cong \operatorname{RHom}(X,\operatorname{RHom}(Y,Z))$ in $\operatorname{Ho}(C)$.
  More generally power $X^K$ and copower $K\cdot X$ for $K : S$ ($S = \mathsf{Set}, \mathsf{sSet}, \mathsf{Spaces}$) with adjunctions $\operatorname{Hom}(K\cdot X,Y) \cong \operatorname{Hom}(K,\operatorname{Map}(X,Y)) \cong \operatorname{Hom}(X,Y^K)$ for $C$ tensored/cotensored over $S$.

* Power/copowering adjunctions: for $C$ enriched over $V$ and tensored/copowered over $V$ (e.g. $\mathsf{sSet}$ over $\mathsf{Set}$, $\mathsf{Top}$ over $S$, $\mathsf{Ch}(R)$ over $\mathsf{Ch}(ZZ)$), power $X^K : C$ cotensor and copower $K\cdot X : C$ tensor over $K : V$ with $\operatorname{Map}(K\cdot X,Y) \cong \operatorname{Map}(K,\operatorname{Map}(X,Y)) \cong \operatorname{Map}(X,Y^K)$ in $V$.
  Need `C.power(K,X)` $\to X^K$ and `C.copower(K,X)` $\to K\cdot X$ with adjunctions.

* Attaching enriched structures where they are known to hold by theorems: e.g. $\mathsf{Top}$ is $S$-enriched via $\operatorname{Map}(X,Y)\in S$, $\mathsf{Cat}$ is $\mathsf{Cat}$-enriched (2-category) via $\operatorname{Fun}(C,D)$, $\mathsf{sSet}$ is $\mathsf{sSet}$-enriched, $\mathsf{Ch}(R)$ is $\mathsf{Ch}(R)$-enriched, $\mathsf{Spectra}$ is $\mathsf{Spectra}$-enriched, etc. Need `C.enrichment()` $\to V$ and `C.enriched_hom(X,Y)` $\to \operatorname{Map}_C(X,Y) : V$ when theorem holds (e.g. $\mathsf{Top}$ is cartesian closed and $S$-enriched, $\mathsf{Cat}$ is cartesian closed as 2-category).

* Induction and restriction functors for $k[G]$ modules / $G$-reps (Frobenius reciprocity): for $H \leq G$ groups and $k : \mathsf{CommRings}$, restriction $\operatorname{Res}^G_H: k[G]\text{-}\mathsf{Mod} \to k[H]\text{-}\mathsf{Mod}$ via $k[H]\to k[G]$ and induction $\operatorname{Ind}_H^G = k[G]\otimes_{k[H]} - : k[H]\text{-}\mathsf{Mod} \to k[G]\text{-}\mathsf{Mod}$ left adjoint to $\operatorname{Res}$, with Frobenius reciprocity $\operatorname{Hom}_{k[G]}(\operatorname{Ind}_H^G V,W) \cong \operatorname{Hom}_{k[H]}(V,\operatorname{Res}_H^G W)$ natural, and $\operatorname{CoInd}_H^G = \operatorname{Hom}_{k[H]}(k[G],-) : k[H]\text{-}\mathsf{Mod} \to k[G]\text{-}\mathsf{Mod}$ right adjoint to $\operatorname{Res}$ with $\operatorname{Hom}_{k[H]}(\operatorname{Res} V,W) \cong \operatorname{Hom}_{k[G]}(V,\operatorname{CoInd} W)$.
  Similarly for $G$-reps $\operatorname{Rep}_k(G) = k[G]\text{-}\mathsf{Mod}$ with same.
  Need $\operatorname{Res}_H^G$, $\operatorname{Ind}_H^G$, $\operatorname{CoInd}_H^G$ with adjunctions.

Intended owners: `categories/derived/implicit_deriving.py` (`ImplicitlyDerive` $LF$, $RF$ for $\otimes, \operatorname{Hom}$), `categories/monoidal/tensor_hom.py` (`TensorHomAdj` $\operatorname{Hom}(X\otimes Y,Z)\cong\operatorname{Hom}(X,\operatorname{Hom}(Y,Z))$), `categories/enriched/power_copower.py` (`Power` $X^K$, `Copower` $K\cdot X$), `categories/enriched/enriched.py` (`EnrichedCategory` with $\operatorname{Map}$), `categories/representation/induction_restriction.py` ($\operatorname{Res}^G_H$, $\operatorname{Ind}_H^G \dashv \operatorname{Res}$, `FrobeniusReciprocity`). Not a free `derive_tensor(X,Y)` returning list — `C.derived_tensor(X,Y)` $\to X\otimes^L Y$ with $\operatorname{RHom}$ and adjunctions.

## Desired capability: axiomatic subcategories of $\mathsf{Cat}$, `show(C)`, limits/colimits, monads, $QX$, interchange — note 2026-09-16

Need existing categories placed into axiomatic subcategories of $\mathsf{Cat}$: Abelian, Complete, Cocomplete, Bicomplete, HasProducts, HasCoproducts, HasPullbacks, etc., enrich `show(C)` to display known properties like this so that they can be seen and audited by users, reasonable computational abilities of limits and colimits at least in filtered or $ZZ$-indexed cases; equalizers and coequalizers, etc., monads, algebras over monads, $QX$ for a topological space, basic interchange for limit/colimit calculus.

* Place existing categories $C : \mathsf{Cat}$ into axiomatic subcategories of $\mathsf{Cat}$ via $C \in \mathsf{Abelian}$, $C \in \mathsf{Complete}$, $C \in \mathsf{Cocomplete}$, $C \in \mathsf{Bicomplete}$, $C \in \mathsf{HasProducts}$, $C \in \mathsf{HasCoproducts}$, $C \in \mathsf{HasPullbacks}$, $C \in \mathsf{HasPushouts}$, $C \in \mathsf{HasEqualizers}$, $C \in \mathsf{HasCoequalizers}$, $C \in \mathsf{CartesianClosed}$, etc., as $\mathsf{Cat}$ with axiom `with_axiom(HasProducts)` etc. Need `C.is_abelian()`, `C.is_complete()`, `C.has_products()` as predicates via $C \in \mathsf{Cat}_{\mathsf{Abelian}}$ etc., not as separate `has_products(C)` functions.

* Enrich `show(C)` to display known properties like this so that they can be seen and audited by users: `C.show()` or `show(C)` in REPL/notebook must display $C$ with its $\mathsf{Cat}$ axioms (`Abelian`, `Complete`, `HasProducts`, …) as tags, e.g. `Abelian(C) = AbGroups` shows `Abelian, Complete, Cocomplete, HasLimits, HasColimits`, so users can audit which $\mathsf{Cat}$ axioms a category satisfies.

* Reasonable computational abilities of limits and colimits, at least in filtered or $ZZ$-indexed cases; equalizers and coequalizers, etc.: $\lim: \operatorname{Fun}(I,C)\to C$ for $I : \mathsf{Cat}$ small, with $\lim_I : \operatorname{Fun}(I,C)\to C$ right adjoint to $\operatorname{diag}: C\to\operatorname{Fun}(I,C)$, and $\operatorname{colim}: \operatorname{Fun}(I,C)\to C$ left adjoint.
  Need at least filtered colimits $\operatorname{colim}_{i\in I}$ with $I$ filtered ($ZZ$-directed $I = ZZ$ with $i\leq j$ gives $ZZ$-indexed $\operatorname{colim}_{n\in ZZ} X_n$ as sequential colimit, similarly $\lim_{n\in ZZ} X_n$ for $ZZ^{\mathrm{op}}$-indexed inverse limit), and equalizers $\operatorname{Eq}(f,g) = \lim( X \rightrightarrows Y )$ for $f,g: X\to Y$ and coequalizers $\operatorname{Coeq}(f,g) = \operatorname{colim}( X \rightrightarrows Y )$, with products $X\times Y = \lim(X\to 1\leftarrow Y)$ and coproducts $X\oplus Y = \operatorname{colim}(1\leftarrow 0\to 1)$ etc. Need `C.limit(diagram)` $\to \lim_I F : C$ with universal cone $\lim \to F(i)$ and `C.colimit(diagram)` $\to \operatorname{colim}_I F$ when $I$ filtered or $ZZ$.

* Monads, algebras over monads: monad $T: C\to C$ with $\eta: \operatorname{Id}_C \to T$, $\mu: T^2 \to T$ satisfying $\mu\circ T\eta = \operatorname{id} = \mu\circ\eta T$ and $\mu\circ T\mu = \mu\circ\mu T$, and $T$-algebras $\operatorname{Alg}_T$ with $\operatorname{Ob} = (X, a: TX\to X)$ with $a\circ\eta_X = \operatorname{id}_X$, $a\circ\mu_X = a\circ T(a)$, morphisms $f: (X,a)\to(Y,b)$ with $f\circ a = b\circ T(f)$.
  Examples: $\operatorname{List}$ monad on $\mathsf{Set}$, $\operatorname{FreeGroup}$ on $\mathsf{Set}$, $\operatorname{Free}_R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod}$ on $\mathsf{Set}$, $\operatorname{Powerset}$ monad, etc. Need `Monad`$(T,\eta,\mu)$ and `C.algebras(T)` $\to \operatorname{Alg}_T : \mathsf{Cat}$ with forgetful $U: \operatorname{Alg}_T\to C$ and free $F: C\to\operatorname{Alg}_T$.

* $QX$ for a topological space $X : \mathsf{Top}$: e.g. $QX = \Omega^\infty\Sigma^\infty X = \operatorname{colim}_n \Omega^n \Sigma^n X$ as stabilization $Q = \Omega^\infty\Sigma^\infty : \mathsf{Top}\to\mathsf{Top}$ with $QX$ the free infinite loop space on $X$, or $QX$ as $Q$-construction for $K$-theory, or $QX$ as rationalization depending on context.
  In this intake, $QX$ is the $Q$-construction for $X : \mathsf{Top}$ as $QX = \Omega^\infty\Sigma^\infty X$ (or $B^{\infty}X$ for $X$ connected), with $Q: \mathsf{Top} \to \mathsf{Top}$ monad $X \mapsto QX$ and $QX \simeq \operatorname{colim}_{n} \Omega^n \Sigma^n X$ via $\Sigma \dashv \Omega$.
  Need `X.Q()` $\to QX : \mathsf{Top}$ with $QX = \operatorname{colim}_n \Omega^n \Sigma^n X$.

* Basic interchange for limit/colimit calculus: moving through $\operatorname{Hom}$ arguments, commuting with adjoints, etc.: $\operatorname{Hom}_C(\lim_i X_i, Y) \cong \lim^{\mathrm{op}}_i \operatorname{Hom}_C(X_i,Y)$ (limit in first variable becomes colimit), $\operatorname{Hom}_C(X, \lim_i Y_i) \cong \lim_i \operatorname{Hom}_C(X,Y_i)$, $\operatorname{Hom}_C(\operatorname{colim}_i X_i, Y) \cong \lim_i \operatorname{Hom}_C(X_i,Y)$, $\operatorname{Hom}_C(X, \operatorname{colim}_i Y_i) \cong \operatorname{colim}_i \operatorname{Hom}_C(X,Y_i)$ when $X$ compact, and $L \dashv R$ gives $L(\operatorname{colim}_i X_i) \cong \operatorname{colim}_i L(X_i)$ and $R(\lim_i X_i) \cong \lim_i R(X_i)$ (left adjoints preserve colimits, right adjoints preserve limits), Fubini $\lim_i \lim_j X_{i,j} \cong \lim_{i,j} X_{i,j} \cong \lim_j \lim_i X_{i,j}$ and $\operatorname{colim}_i \operatorname{colim}_j \cong \operatorname{colim}_{i,j}$, and $\lim$/$\operatorname{colim}$ commute with $\times$ when $C$ has appropriate limits.
  Need $\operatorname{Hom}(\lim, -)$, $\operatorname{Hom}(-, \lim)$, $L(\operatorname{colim})$, $R(\lim)$ interchanges.

Intended owners: `categories/cat/axioms.py` (`CatAxioms` `Abelian`, `Complete`, `Cocomplete`, `HasProducts`, etc.), `categories/cat/show.py` (`show(C)` displaying $\mathsf{Cat}$ axioms), `categories/limits/filtered.py` (`FilteredColimit` $\operatorname{colim}_{i\in I}$ for $I$ filtered/$ZZ$), `categories/limits/equalizers.py` (`Equalizer` $\operatorname{Eq}(f,g)$, `Coequalizer` $\operatorname{Coeq}(f,g)$), `categories/monads/monads.py` (`Monad` $T: C\to C$ with $\eta,\mu$ and $\operatorname{Alg}_T$), `categories/top/qx.py` ($QX = \Omega^\infty\Sigma^\infty X$ for $X : \mathsf{Top}$), `categories/limits/interchange.py` (`Interchange` $\operatorname{Hom}(\lim, -) \cong \lim \operatorname{Hom}$, $L(\operatorname{colim}) \cong \operatorname{colim} L$). Not a free `limit_of_diagram(diagram)` returning list — `C.limit`$(I, F: I\to C) \to \lim_I F : C$ with $\lim$ as right adjoint to $\operatorname{diag}$ and `show(C)` displaying axioms.

## Desired capability: attaching homological notions to modules — free resolutions then apply functor and take homology for derived functors — note 2026-09-16

Attaching homological notions to modules.
Operationalize taking a free resolution and applying a functor and taking homology in order to compute derived functors.

* For $R : \mathsf{Rings}$ (commutative $R : \mathsf{CommRings}$ or general) and $M : R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod}$, attach homological notions $\operatorname{Tor}^R_n(M,N)$, $\operatorname{Ext}^n_R(M,N)$, $L_nF(M)$, $R^nF(M)$ as objects $L_nF(M) : R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod}$ or $\mathsf{Ab}$ with $L_nF(M) = H_n(F(P_\bullet))$ etc., as graded $H_* : \mathsf{GrMod}$ with `H_*.graded_piece(n)` $=H_n$.
  The notions are attached to the module $M$, not as free `Tor(M,N)` functions returning numbers.

* Operationalize via free (or projective) resolution $P_\bullet \to M \to 0$ with $P_\bullet : \mathsf{Ch}(R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod})$ and $P_i$ free finite rank (or $P_i = R^{b_i}$) with differential $d_i: P_i \to P_{i-1}$ and augmentation $\varepsilon: P_0 \to M$ exact: $\ldots \to P_1 \to^{d_1} P_0 \to^{\varepsilon} M \to 0$ and $H_i(P_\bullet)=0$ for $i>0$, $H_0(P_\bullet) \cong M$.
  Need `M.free_resolution()` $\to P_\bullet : \mathsf{Ch}$ with $P_i$ free, $P_\bullet$`.augmentation()` $\to M$, and $P_\bullet$`.is_exact()`.

* Then apply functor $F : R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod} \to \mathsf{Ab}$ (or $R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod} \to R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod}$, $R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod} \to D(\mathsf{Ab})$) — e.g. $F = \operatorname{Hom}_R(-,N)$ contravariant, $F = -\otimes_R N$ covariant, $F = \operatorname{Hom}_R(P,-)$, $F = G\circ F$ etc. — to $P_\bullet$ (or to $P_\bullet$ without augmentation) to get complex $F(P_\bullet) : \mathsf{Ch}(\mathsf{Ab})$ with $F(P_\bullet)_n = F(P_n)$ and differential $F(d_n): F(P_n) \to F(P_{n-1})$ (or $F(d^n)$ for contravariant with degree shift).
  Then take homology $H_n(F(P_\bullet)) = \ker F(d_n) / \operatorname{im} F(d_{n+1}) : \mathsf{Ab}$ as $\mathsf{GrMod}$ graded $H_*(F(P_\bullet)) : \mathsf{GrMod}$ with `H_*.graded_piece(n)` $=H_n$.
  For left exact $F$ (e.g. $\operatorname{Hom}_R(-,N)$), this computes right derived $R^nF(M) = H^n(F(P_\bullet))$ after appropriate $\operatorname{Hom}$ and $\operatorname{Ext}^n_R(M,N) = H^n(\operatorname{Hom}_R(P_\bullet,N))$; for right exact $F$ (e.g. $-\otimes_R N$), this computes left derived $L_nF(M)=H_n(F(P_\bullet))$ and $\operatorname{Tor}_n^R(M,N)=H_n(P_\bullet\otimes_R N)$.

* This is the standard free-resolution model for derived functors: $L_nF(M) = H_n(F(P_\bullet))$ with $P_\bullet \to M$ free resolution, and $R^nF(M) = H^n(F(I^\bullet))$ for injective $I^\bullet$ when needed, but the operationalized version requested is free resolution + apply $F$ + take homology.
  Need `M.derived_functor(F, n)` $\to H_n(F(P_\bullet)) : \mathsf{Ab}$ with $P_\bullet =$ `M.free_resolution()`, $F(P_\bullet) : \mathsf{Ch}$, $H_n : \mathsf{GrMod}$.
  For $F = -\otimes_R N$, this is $\operatorname{Tor}_n^R(M,N)$; for $F = \operatorname{Hom}_R(-,N)$, this is $\operatorname{Ext}^n_R(M,N)$ as $\operatorname{Ext}^n = R^n \operatorname{Hom}$.

* Interface must be on the module with its resolution: `M.free_resolution()` $\to P_\bullet : \mathsf{Ch}(R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod})$ with $P_i$ free, `M.apply_functor_to_resolution(F)` $\to F(P_\bullet) : \mathsf{Ch}$, $H_n(F(P_\bullet)) \to L_nF(M) : \mathsf{Ab}$ as $\mathsf{GrMod}$.
  For $F = \operatorname{Hom}_R(-,N)$, `M.ext(N,n)` $\to \operatorname{Ext}^n_R(M,N) = H^n(\operatorname{Hom}_R(P_\bullet,N))$; for $F = -\otimes_R N$, `M.tor(N,n)` $\to \operatorname{Tor}_n^R(M,N) = H_n(P_\bullet\otimes_R N)$.
  The free resolution is the computational engine; the derived functor is the homology of the resulting complex.

Intended owners: `categories/modules/homological.py` (`HomologicalNotions` attached to $M : R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod}$ with $\operatorname{Tor}$, $\operatorname{Ext}$, $L_nF$), `categories/homological/free_resolutions.py` (`FreeResolution` $P_\bullet \to M$ with $P_i$ free, `M.free_resolution()` $\to P_\bullet$), `categories/derived/derived_functors.py` (`DerivedFunctor` $L_nF(M)=H_n(F(P_\bullet))$, $R^nF(M)=H^n(F(P_\bullet))$), `categories/modules/free_modules.py` (`FreeFiniteRank` with `basis`). Not a free `free_resolution(M)` returning list of matrices — `M.free_resolution()` $: \mathsf{Ch}$ with $P_\bullet$ as complex of free modules and $H_n(F(P_\bullet)) : \mathsf{GrMod}$ as derived functor.

## Desired capability: Yoneda product on $\operatorname{Ext}$ operationalized — note 2026-09-16

Operationalize Yoneda product on $\operatorname{Ext}$.

* Yoneda product is not a formal $\operatorname{Ext}^p\otimes\operatorname{Ext}^q\to\operatorname{Ext}^{p+q}$ symbol.
  For $R : \mathsf{Rings}$ (or $R : \mathsf{CommRings}$) and $A,B,C : R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod}$ (or $D(R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod})$), the product

  $$
  \operatorname{Yoneda}: \operatorname{Ext}^p_R(B,C) \otimes \operatorname{Ext}^q_R(A,B) \to \operatorname{Ext}^{p+q}_R(A,C)
  $$

  is composition in the derived category $D(R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod})$ via $\operatorname{Ext}^n_R(A,B) = \operatorname{Hom}_{D(R)}(A, B[n])$ ($B[n]$ shift).
  Explicitly, for $\xi \in \operatorname{Ext}^p(B,C)$ represented by $\xi: B \to C[p]$ in $D(R)$ and $\eta \in \operatorname{Ext}^q(A,B)$ represented by $\eta: A \to B[q]$, the Yoneda product is $\xi\cdot\eta = (\xi[q] \circ \eta) : A \to C[p+q]$ where $\xi[q]: B[q] \to C[p+q]$ is shift of $\xi$.
  Equivalently via extensions, $\xi$ is $0\to C\to E_1\to\ldots\to E_p\to B\to 0$ and $\eta$ is $0\to B\to F_1\to\ldots\to F_q\to A\to 0$, splice at $B$ to $0\to C\to E_1\to\ldots\to E_p\to F_1\to\ldots\to F_q\to A\to 0$ of length $p+q$, modulo Yoneda equivalence (pushout/pullback).
  Need $\operatorname{Ext}^p \otimes \operatorname{Ext}^q \to \operatorname{Ext}^{p+q}$ as $R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod}$ morphism with $p,q : NN$.

* Operationalize via free resolutions and chain maps: choose free resolution $P_\bullet \to A \to 0$ with $P_i$ free finite rank, then $\operatorname{Ext}^q_R(A,B) = H^q(\operatorname{Hom}_R(P_\bullet,B))$ with cocycle $f: P_q \to B$ representing class, similarly $Q_\bullet \to B$ for $\operatorname{Ext}^p(B,C)$.
  Lift $f$ to chain map $F_\bullet: P_{\bullet+q} \to Q_\bullet$ covering $f: P_q \to B$, then compose with $g: Q_p \to C$ representing $\operatorname{Ext}^p$ to get $g\circ F_q: P_{p+q} \to C$ representing $\operatorname{Yoneda}(g,f) \in \operatorname{Ext}^{p+q}(A,C)$.
  Concretely via $\operatorname{RHom}_R(A,B) = \operatorname{Hom}_R(P_\bullet,B) : \mathsf{Ch}$ and composition $\operatorname{RHom}(B,C) \otimes^L \operatorname{RHom}(A,B) \to \operatorname{RHom}(A,C)$ as chain map composition.

* Graded structure: $\operatorname{Ext}^*_R(A,B) = \bigoplus_{n\geq 0} \operatorname{Ext}^n_R(A,B) : \mathsf{GrMod}$ with `Ext^n.graded_piece(n)` $=\operatorname{Ext}^n$ and Yoneda product makes $\operatorname{Ext}^*_R(A,A) : \mathsf{GrAlg}$ graded $R$-algebra with $\operatorname{Ext}^p\otimes\operatorname{Ext}^q\to\operatorname{Ext}^{p+q}$ and $\operatorname{Ext}^0(A,A)=\operatorname{Hom}_R(A,A)$, and $\operatorname{Ext}^*_R(A,B)$ as graded $\operatorname{Ext}^*(A,A)$-module.
  Similarly $\operatorname{Tor}_*$ has shuffle product.

* Interface must be on the modules with resolutions: `A.ext(B,q)` $\to \operatorname{Ext}^q_R(A,B) : R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod}$ as $\operatorname{Hom}_{D(R)}(A,B[q])$, `B.ext(C,p)` $\to \operatorname{Ext}^p_R(B,C)$, then `Ext^p(B,C).yoneda_product(Ext^q(A,B))` $\to \operatorname{Ext}^{p+q}(A,C) : R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod}$ with `Ext^p.graded_piece` compatibility, and $\operatorname{RHom}_R(A,B) \otimes \operatorname{RHom}_R(B,C) \to \operatorname{RHom}_R(A,C)$ via composition.
  For $F = \operatorname{Hom}_R(P_\bullet,-)$ and $F = -\otimes_R N$, Yoneda product is induced by $\operatorname{Hom}$ composition and $\otimes^L$ shuffle.

Intended owners: `categories/homological/yoneda_product.py` (`YonedaProduct` $\operatorname{Ext}^p(B,C)\otimes\operatorname{Ext}^q(A,B)\to\operatorname{Ext}^{p+q}(A,C)$ via $\operatorname{Hom}_{D(R)}(B,C[p])\otimes\operatorname{Hom}_{D(R)}(A,B[q])\to\operatorname{Hom}_{D(R)}(A,C[p+q])$, splice, chain maps), `categories/derived/derived_category.py` ($D(R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod})$ with $\operatorname{Hom}(A,B[n])=\operatorname{Ext}^n$), `categories/homological/ext_tor.py` ($\operatorname{Ext}^n$, $\operatorname{Tor}_n$), `categories/homological/free_resolutions.py` ($P_\bullet\to A$ with $\operatorname{Hom}(P_\bullet,B)$). Not a free `yoneda_product(ext1, ext2)` returning list — `Ext^p(B,C).yoneda(Ext^q(A,B))` $\to \operatorname{Ext}^{p+q}(A,C)$ on the $\operatorname{Ext}$ graded objects with $\operatorname{RHom}$ composition.

## Desired capability: $\mathrm{MU}$ and $\mathrm{BP}$, spectra, $E_n$ and $A_n$ ring spectra, free $E_n$ algebras — note 2026-09-16

Operationalize $\mathrm{MU}$ and $\mathrm{BP}$, spectra, $E_n$ ring spectra, $E_n$ module spectra, $A_n$ ring spectra, free $E_n$ algebra functors.

* Spectra $\mathsf{Sp} : \mathsf{Cat}$ as stable $\infty$-category $\mathsf{Sp} = \operatorname{Sp}(\mathsf{Top}_*) = \lim(\ldots \to \mathsf{Top}_* \to^{\Sigma} \mathsf{Top}_* \to^{\Sigma} \ldots)$ or as $\mathsf{Spectra}$ sequential spectra $X = (X_n, \sigma_n: \Sigma X_n \to X_{n+1})$ with $X_n : \mathsf{Top}_*$, $\Omega$-spectra when $\sigma_n^\flat: X_n \to \Omega X_{n+1}$ is weak equivalence, with smash $\wedge: \mathsf{Sp}\times\mathsf{Sp}\to\mathsf{Sp}$ and $S = \Sigma^\infty S^0$ sphere spectrum.
  Need $\mathsf{Sp}$ as stable $\infty\mathsf{Cat}$ with $\Sigma \dashv \Omega$ as $\Sigma^\infty \dashv \Omega^\infty$ and $\mathsf{Sp}$ is $\infty$-categorical stabilization of $S$.

* $\mathrm{MU} = \operatorname{Thom}(\mathrm{MU})$ complex cobordism as $E_\infty$ ring spectrum $\mathrm{MU} = \operatorname{colim}_n \operatorname{Thom}(\mathrm{BU}(n))$ with $\mathrm{MU}_* = \pi_*(\mathrm{MU}) = ZZ[x_1,x_2,\ldots]$ $|x_i|=2i$ ($x_i = [\mathrm{CP}^i]$), Thom spectrum $\mathrm{MU} = \operatorname{Thom}(\mathrm{BU} \to \mathrm{BO})$ via $J: \mathrm{BO}\to\mathrm{BGL}_1(S)$.
  Similarly $\mathrm{BP}$ at prime $p$ is summand of $\mathrm{MU}_{(p)}$ via Quillen idempotent $e: \mathrm{MU}_{(p)} \to \mathrm{MU}_{(p)}$ with $e^2=e$ and $\mathrm{BP} = e \mathrm{MU}_{(p)}$ as $p$-local $E_\infty$ (or $E_4$) ring spectrum with $\mathrm{BP}_* = ZZ_{(p)}[v_1,v_2,\ldots]$ $|v_i|=2(p^i-1)$ and $\mathrm{BP}_*\mathrm{BP} = \mathrm{BP}_*[t_1,t_2,\ldots]$.
  Need $\mathrm{MU} : \mathsf{CAlg}(\mathsf{Sp})$ and $\mathrm{BP} : \mathsf{CAlg}(\mathsf{Sp})_{(p)}$ with $\mathrm{MU}_*$, $\mathrm{BP}_*$.

* $E_n$ ring spectra as $E_n$-algebras in $\mathsf{Sp}$ for $E_n$ operad ($n = 1$ associative $A_\infty$, $n = \infty$ $E_\infty$ commutative): $\operatorname{Alg}_{E_n}(\mathsf{Sp})$ with $E_n$-operad $E_n(k) \simeq \operatorname{Conf}_k(R^n)$ little $n$-cubes, $A : \operatorname{Alg}_{E_n}(\mathsf{Sp})$ has multiplications $E_n(k) \otimes A^{\otimes k} \to A$.
  $E_n$ ring spectrum $R$ is $R : \operatorname{Alg}_{E_n}(\mathsf{Sp})$ with $\pi_*(R)$ graded ring.
  Example $\mathrm{MU}$ is $E_\infty$, $\mathrm{BP}$ is $E_4$ (or $E_\infty$ for $p>2$ recent), Morava $E_n$ is $E_\infty$.
  $E_n$ module spectra $\operatorname{Mod}_{E_n}(\mathsf{Sp})$ is $\mathsf{Mod}_R = \operatorname{Fun}_{E_n}(R, \mathsf{Sp})$ with $R\text{-}\mathsf{Mod}$ stable.
  Need $E_n$-algebras and modules as $\operatorname{Alg}_{E_n}(\mathsf{Sp})$ and $\mathsf{Mod}_R$.

* Free $E_n$ algebra functors $\operatorname{Free}_{E_n}: \mathsf{Sp} \to \operatorname{Alg}_{E_n}(\mathsf{Sp})$ left adjoint to forgetful $U: \operatorname{Alg}_{E_n}\to\mathsf{Sp}$, with $\operatorname{Free}_{E_n}(X) = \coprod_{k\geq 0} E_n(k) \otimes_{\Sigma_k} X^{\otimes k}$ as $\coprod E_n(k) \times_{\Sigma_k} X^{\wedge k}$ in $\mathsf{Sp}$, and $\operatorname{Free}_{E_n}(S^0) \simeq \coprod_{k} B\Sigma_k$ etc. For $n=\infty$, $\operatorname{Free}_{E_\infty}(X) \simeq \operatorname{Sym}^*(X) = \coprod_{k} X^{\wedge k}_{h\Sigma_k}$.
  $A_n$ ring spectra are $A_n$-algebras for $A_n$ operad ($A_n(k)=*$ for $k\leq n$ else $\emptyset$, with $A_\infty = E_1$).

Intended owners: `categories/spectra/spectra.py` ($\mathsf{Sp}$ as $\mathsf{Spectra}$ stable $\infty$-category), `categories/spectra/mu.py` ($\mathrm{MU} : \mathsf{CAlg}(\mathsf{Sp})$ with $\mathrm{MU}_*$), `categories/spectra/bp.py` ($\mathrm{BP} : \mathsf{CAlg}(\mathsf{Sp})_{(p)}$ with $\mathrm{BP}_*$), `categories/spectra/e_n_ring.py` ($E_n$-algebras $\operatorname{Alg}_{E_n}(\mathsf{Sp})$), `categories/spectra/e_n_modules.py` ($E_n$-modules $\mathsf{Mod}_R$), `categories/spectra/free_e_n.py` ($\operatorname{Free}_{E_n}: \mathsf{Sp} \to \operatorname{Alg}_{E_n}$), `categories/spectra/a_n.py` ($A_n$-algebras).
Not a free `e_n_algebra(X)` returning list — $\operatorname{Free}_{E_n}(X) : \operatorname{Alg}_{E_n}(\mathsf{Sp})$ with $E_n(k)\otimes_{\Sigma_k} X^{\otimes k}$.

## Desired capability: Morava $K(n)$ and $K(n)$-local spheres, heights, $p$-local/$p$-complete, fibers/cofibers in spectra — note 2026-09-16

Operationalize Morava $K(n)$ and $K(n)$-local spheres, $p$-local and $p$-complete spheres, and more generally fibers and cofibers in spectra.
Heights of Morava $E$-theories.

* Morava $K(n)$ at prime $p$ height $n$ is $K(n)$ as $E_\infty$ (or $A_\infty$) ring spectrum with $K(n)_* = F_p[v_n^{\pm 1}]$ $|v_n|=2(p^n-1)$ and $K(n)^* K(n) =$ Morava stabilizer etc., with $K(0)=Q$, $K(\infty)=F_p$.
  $K(n)$-localization $L_{K(n)}: \mathsf{Sp} \to \mathsf{Sp}_{K(n)}$ is Bousfield localization at $K(n)$ with $L_{K(n)}X$ initial $K(n)$-local under $X\to L_{K(n)}X$ with $K(n)_*(X\to L_{K(n)}X)$ iso, and $K(n)$-local sphere $L_{K(n)}S^0$ as $L_{K(n)}$ of sphere $S^0$.
  Height $n = \operatorname{ht}(E)$ for Morava $E = E_n = E(k,\Gamma)$ Lubin-Tate spectrum $E_* = W(k)[[u_1,\ldots,u_{n-1}]][u^{\pm 1}]$ with $|u|=-2$, $|u_i|=0$, height $n$ of formal group $\Gamma$ over $k : \mathsf{Fields}$ char $p$.

* $p$-local sphere $S^0_{(p)} = L_{M(p)}S^0$ localization at $M(p)=S/p$ Moore spectrum, and $p$-complete sphere ${S^0}^{\wedge}_p = \lim_k S^0/p^k = L_{S/p}S^0$ as $p$-completion $X^{\wedge}_p = \lim_k X/p^k$ with $X/p^k = \operatorname{cofib}(X \to^{p^k} X)$.
  More generally $L_{S/p}$ Bousfield at $S/p$.
  Need $S^0_{(p)} : \mathsf{Sp}$ and ${S^0}^{\wedge}_p : \mathsf{Sp}$ with $\pi_*(S^0_{(p)}) = ZZ_{(p)}$ etc.

* Fibers and cofibers in spectra more generally: for $f: X\to Y$ in $\mathsf{Sp}$, fiber $\operatorname{fib}(f) = X\times_Y 0$ as pullback $0\to Y$ and cofiber $\operatorname{cofib}(f)= Y \cup_X 0$ as pushout $X\to Y$ with $0 : \mathsf{Sp}$ zero object, and $\mathsf{Sp}$ is stable so $\operatorname{fib}(f) \simeq \operatorname{cofib}(f)[-1]$ and $\operatorname{cofib}(f) \simeq \operatorname{fib}(f)[1]$ and distinguished triangles $\operatorname{fib}\to X\to Y\to\operatorname{fib}[1]$.
  Need `f.fiber()` $\to \operatorname{fib}(f) : \mathsf{Sp}$ and `f.cofiber()` $\to \operatorname{cofib}(f) : \mathsf{Sp}$ with $\operatorname{fib} \simeq \Sigma^{-1}\operatorname{cofib}$.

Intended owners: `categories/spectra/morava_k.py` ($K(n) : \mathsf{CAlg}(\mathsf{Sp})$ with $K(n)_*$, $L_{K(n)}$), `categories/spectra/morava_e.py` ($E_n : \mathsf{CAlg}(\mathsf{Sp})$ Lubin-Tate with height $n$), `categories/spectra/localization.py` ($L_{K(n)}$, $p$-local $S^0_{(p)}$, $p$-complete ${S^0}^{\wedge}_p$), `categories/spectra/fibers_cofibers.py` ($\operatorname{fib}(f)$, $\operatorname{cofib}(f)$ in $\mathsf{Sp}$ stable, $\operatorname{fib} \simeq \operatorname{cofib}[-1]$). Not a free `localize_sphere(n)` returning number — $L_{K(n)}S^0 : \mathsf{Sp}$ with height $n$.

## Desired capability: framed manifolds, factorization homology, $(-)^{hG}$ and $(-)_{hG}$, $\mathrm{MString}$ and $\mathrm{tmf}$ — note 2026-09-16

Operationalize framed manifolds, factorization homology, $(*)^{hG}$ and $(*)_{hG}$ functors, $\mathrm{MString}$ and $\mathrm{tmf}$ spectra.

* Framed $n$-manifold is $M : \mathsf{Mfld}^{\mathrm{fr}}_n$ with $M : \mathsf{Man}$ smooth $n$-manifold and framing $\varphi: TM \cong M\times R^n$ as $\mathsf{Vect}$ trivialization, equivalently lift of classifying map $M \to \mathrm{BO}(n)$ to $\mathrm{BString}$?
  actually $\mathrm{BO}(n)$ via $\mathrm{EO}(n)$, with $\mathsf{Mfld}^{\mathrm{fr}}_n : \mathsf{Cat}$ with morphisms embeddings $M\hookrightarrow N$ preserving framing.
  Need `FramedManifold` with $TM$ framing.

* Factorization homology $\int_M A$ for $E_n$-algebra $A : \operatorname{Alg}_{E_n}(\mathsf{Sp})$ (or $\operatorname{Alg}_{E_n}(C)$ for $C : \mathsf{Cat}$ like $\mathsf{Ch}$, $\mathsf{Sp}$, $\mathsf{Vect}$) and framed $M : \mathsf{Mfld}^{\mathrm{fr}}_n$ is $\int_M A = \operatorname{colim}_{\mathsf{Disk}^{\mathrm{fr}}_{n/M}} A$ as colimit over $\mathsf{Disk}^{\mathrm{fr}}_{n/M} = \{ U = \coprod_{i\in I} R^n \hookrightarrow M \}$ with $A(U)=A^{\otimes I}$, with $\int_{R^n} A \simeq A$ and excision $\int_{M \cup_{M_0\times R} N} A \simeq \int_M A \otimes_{\int_{M_0\times R} A} \int_N A$.
  For $A$ as $E_\infty$ algebra, $\int_M A \simeq A\otimes \Sigma^\infty_+ M$ etc. Need `A.factorization_homology(M)` $\to \int_M A : C$ with $A$ as $E_n$-algebra.

* $(-)^{hG}$ and $(-)_{hG}$ functors: for $G : \mathsf{Groups}$ finite (or $G : \mathsf{Spaces}$ as $\infty$-group $BG$), $X : \mathsf{Sp}^{BG} = \operatorname{Fun}(BG,\mathsf{Sp})$ is spectrum with $G$-action, homotopy fixed points $X^{hG} = \lim_{BG} X = \operatorname{Map}_{\mathsf{Sp}^{BG}}(1, X) : \mathsf{Sp}$ as limit over $BG$ and homotopy orbits $X_{hG} = \operatorname{colim}_{BG} X = 1 \otimes_{G} X : \mathsf{Sp}$ as colimit, with $X^{hG} = (X)^{G}$ derived and $X_{hG}=X/G$ derived, norm $\operatorname{Nm}: X_{hG} \to X^{hG}$ and Tate construction $X^{tG}=\operatorname{cofib}(\operatorname{Nm})$ with $X^{tG}= (X^{hG})_{?}$ etc. Need `X.homotopy_fixed_points(G)` $\to X^{hG} : \mathsf{Sp}$ and `X.homotopy_orbits(G)` $\to X_{hG} : \mathsf{Sp}$.

* $\mathrm{MString}$ and $\mathrm{tmf}$ spectra: $\mathrm{MString} = \operatorname{Thom}(\mathrm{MString})$ as $E_\infty$ ring spectrum $\mathrm{MString} = \operatorname{Thom}(\mathrm{BString} \to \mathrm{BO})$ with $\mathrm{BString} = \mathrm{BSpin}\langle\frac{1}{2}p_1\rangle$ and $\pi_*(\mathrm{MString})$ string bordism, and $\mathrm{tmf} = O^{\mathrm{top}}(M_{\mathrm{ell}})$ topological modular forms as $E_\infty$ ring spectrum $\mathrm{tmf}$ with $\pi_*(\mathrm{tmf})$ via $\mathrm{tmf}_* =$ $E_\infty$ with $\mathrm{tmf} \to \mathrm{MString}$ orientation $\sigma: \mathrm{MString} \to \mathrm{tmf}$ Witten genus, $\mathrm{TMF} = \mathrm{tmf}[\Delta^{-1}]$ periodic, $\mathrm{Tmf}$ compactified.
  Need $\mathrm{MString} : \mathsf{CAlg}(\mathsf{Sp})$ and $\mathrm{tmf} : \mathsf{CAlg}(\mathsf{Sp})$ with $\sigma$.

Intended owners: `categories/manifolds/framed.py` (`FramedManifold` $\mathsf{Mfld}^{\mathrm{fr}}_n$), `categories/factorization/factorization_homology.py` ($\int_M A : C$ for $E_n$-algebra $A$), `categories/spectra/homotopy_fixed_points.py` ($X^{hG}=\lim_{BG} X$), `categories/spectra/homotopy_orbits.py` ($X_{hG}=\operatorname{colim}_{BG} X$), `categories/spectra/mstring.py` ($\mathrm{MString} : \mathsf{CAlg}(\mathsf{Sp})$), `categories/spectra/tmf.py` ($\mathrm{tmf} : \mathsf{CAlg}(\mathsf{Sp})$ with $\sigma: \mathrm{MString}\to\mathrm{tmf}$). Not a free `factorization_homology(M,A)` returning list — `A.factorization_homology(M)` with $\int_M A$.

## Desired capability: sieves, Grothendieck topologies, pullbacks as intersections, topoi — note 2026-09-16

Operationalize sieves, Grothendieck topologies, pullbacks as intersections, and topoi.

* Sieve $S \subset h_U$ on $U : C$ is subfunctor $S \subset \operatorname{Hom}_C(-,U) : C^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathsf{Set}$ with $S$ closed under precomposition: if $f \in S(V)$ and $g: W\to V$ then $f\circ g \in S(W)$.
  Equivalently collection of morphisms into $U$ stable under precomposition.
  Need `Sieve(C,U)` with `S.subobject(h_U)` and `S.is_sieve()`.

* Grothendieck topology $J$ on $C : \mathsf{Cat}$ is collection $J(U) = \{ S \subset h_U \mid S \text{ covering}\}$ for each $U : C$ with axioms: maximal $h_U \in J(U)$, stability $S\in J(U)$ and $f: V\to U$ implies $f^*S = \{ g: W\to V \mid f\circ g \in S \} \in J(V)$, transitivity $S\in J(U)$ and $T \subset h_U$ with $f^*T \in J(V)$ for all $f\in S$ implies $T\in J(U)$.
  Covering families $\{U_i\to U\}$ generate sieves $S = \{ f: V\to U \mid f \text{ factors through some } U_i\to U \}$.
  Need `GrothendieckTopology` with `J.covering_sieves(U)`.

* Pullbacks as intersections operationalized: in $C = \operatorname{Op}(X)$ poset of opens of $X : \mathsf{Top}$ with $U \leq V$ iff $U\subseteq V$, pullback $U\times_X V = U\cap V$ as intersection, and more generally for $C = \operatorname{Sub}(X)$ or $C = \mathsf{FinSets}$, pullback is intersection $A\times_C B = A\cap B$ when $A,B \subset C$ as subobjects.
  In general $\mathsf{Cat}$, pullback $V\times_U W$ for $V\to U\leftarrow W$ is fiber product, and when $C$ is poset, it is meet $V\wedge W$.
  Need `C.pullback`$(V\to U\leftarrow W) \to V\times_U W : C$ with $V\times_U W = V\cap W$ when $C$ is $\operatorname{Op}(X)$ or $\operatorname{Sub}$.

* Topos $E : \mathsf{Topoi}$ as Grothendieck topos $E = \operatorname{Sh}(C,J)$ sheaves on site $(C,J)$ with finite limits, colimits, exponentials, subobject classifier $\Omega$, and $E$ is $\infty$-topos $E = \operatorname{Sh}_\infty(C,J)$ when $S$-valued; $E = \operatorname{PSh}(C)$ presheaves and sheafification $a: \operatorname{PSh}(C) \to \operatorname{Sh}(C,J)$ left exact left adjoint to inclusion, with $E$ as left exact localization of $\operatorname{PSh}(C)$; $E$ has $E = \operatorname{Sh}(C,J) = \operatorname{PSh}(C)[J^{-1}]$.
  Need $\mathsf{Topoi}$ as 2-category with $\operatorname{Sh}(C,J)$ as objects, geometric morphisms $f: E\to F$ as $f^*: F\to E$ left exact left adjoint.
  Also elementary topos axioms.

Intended owners: `categories/sites/sieves.py` (`Sieve` $S\subset h_U$), `categories/sites/grothendieck_topology.py` ($J$ with $J(U)$ covering sieves), `categories/limits/pullbacks_as_intersections.py` (`pullback` as $\cap$ for $\operatorname{Op}(X)$), `categories/topoi/topoi.py` (`Topos` $E=\operatorname{Sh}(C,J)$ with $a: \operatorname{PSh}\to\operatorname{Sh}$), `categories/topoi/sheaves.py` ($\operatorname{Sh}(C,J)$). Not a free `sieve_on(U)` returning list — `Sieve(C,U)` with $S\subset h_U$ and `J.covering_sieves(U)`.

## Desired capability: superficie.info as oracle source for surface invariants, and interactive geography/classification tool for lattices — intake 2026-09-19

Intake item from https://superficie.info/ (Belmans–Commelin, repository: https://github.com/superficie/superficie-algebriche).

* **Source of oracle calculations for algebraic surfaces:**

  * `superficie.info` catalogs minimal complex algebraic smooth surfaces structured by the Enriques–Kodaira classification ($\kappa \in \{-\infty, 0, 1, 2\}$) and pairs of Chern numbers $(c_1^2, c_2)$.

  * Numerical invariants provided: Kodaira dimension $\kappa$, Chern numbers $(c_1^2, c_2)$, topological Euler characteristic $e = c_2$, holomorphic Euler characteristic $\chi(\mathcal{O}_X) = \frac{1}{12}(c_1^2 + c_2)$, irregularity $q = h^{0,1}$, geometric genus $p_g = h^{0,2}$, Betti numbers $b_1, b_2, b_3, b_4$, and full Hodge diamond $h^{p,q}$.

  * Preamble integration: serves as an oracle and specimen test-fixture database for preamble surface invariants and scheme constructions (`categories/schemes/surfaces/`, `Surfaces/C`), validating intersection numbers, canonical divisor properties $K_X^2 = c_1^2$, and Hodge numbers against literature citations.

* **TODO: Produce a similar tool for lattices:**

  * Design and implement a lattice geography / classification explorer analogous to `superficie.info` (and related sites `fanography.info`, `grassmannian.info`, `hyperkaehler.info`).

  * Mathematical domain: integral lattices $(L, b)$ up to isometry, classified by signature $(p, q)$ (definite and indefinite/Lorentzian), rank $n = p + q$, determinant $\det(L)$, discriminant group $A_L = L^\vee / L$, discriminant quadratic/bilinear form $q_L: A_L \to \mathbb{Q}/2\mathbb{Z}$, parity (even/odd), genus of lattices (via Conway–Sloane / Nikulin $p$-adic invariants), root systems (roots of norm $\pm 2$, Dynkin diagrams), reflection groups / Coxeter–Vinberg polyhedra, and special geometric lattices (hyperbolic lattices, $E_8$, Leech $\Lambda_{24}$, K3 lattice $\mathrm{II}_{3,19}$, Enriques lattice $E_8(-1) \oplus U$, Coble lattices, and their primitive embeddings).

  * Interactive tool architecture:

    * 2D geography / parameter space grid (e.g. signature $(p, q)$, or $(n, \det(L))$, or $(\mathrm{rank}, \mathrm{length}(A_L))$).

    * Filtering by mathematical properties: unimodular, even/odd, definite/indefinite, signature, reflective, projective/hyperbolic.

    * Detail view per lattice/genus: Gram matrix representative, discriminant form, automorphism group $\mathrm{O}(L)$, root system components, embedding relations (e.g. primitive sublattices and orthogonal complements in $\mathrm{II}_{3,19}$ or $\mathrm{II}_{1,25}$).

    * Exportable test specimens / oracle fixtures formatted for direct verification in `tests/lattices/` and preamble lattice modules.

Intended owners: `categories/schemes/surfaces/` (surface invariants and oracle verification), `categories/lattices/` (`Lattices` category, discriminant forms, genus classification, invariants), and `computations/` or `src/dzack_research/` / web visualizer (interactive lattice geography explorer).

## Desired capability: Fano 3-folds geography, classification, and derived categories (Fanography) — intake 2026-09-19

Intake item from https://fanography.info/ (Pieter Belmans, repository: https://github.com/fanography/fanography).

* **Source of oracle calculations for Fano 3-folds:**

  * Exhaustive catalog of the 105 deformation families of smooth complex Fano 3-folds classified by Iskovskikh and Mori–Mukai.

  * Stratification by Picard rank $\rho(X) \in \{1, \dots, 10\}$:

    * $\rho = 1$: 17 families (e.g. index $r=4$: $\mathbb{P}^3$; index $r=3$: quadric $Q^3 \subset \mathbb{P}^4$; index $r=2$: del Pezzo 3-folds $V_1, \dots, V_5$; index $r=1$: prime Fano 3-folds $X_{2g-2}$ with genus $g \in \{2, \dots, 10, 12\}$).

    * $\rho = 2$: 36 families.

    * $\rho = 3$: 31 families.

    * $\rho = 4$: 13 families.

    * $\rho = 5$: 3 families.

    * $\rho = 6$: 1 family (blowup of $\mathbb{P}^3$ at 6 points).

    * $\rho = 7$: 1 family.

    * $\rho = 8$: 1 family.

    * $\rho = 9$: 1 family.

    * $\rho = 10$: 1 family ($\mathbb{P}^1 \times S_1$ where $S_1$ is del Pezzo surface of degree 1).

  * Numerical invariants provided per family:

    * Picard rank $\rho = b_2$, Fano index $r = \max \{m \in \mathbb{Z}_{>0} \mid -K_X = m H \text{ for } H \in \mathrm{Pic}(X)\}$.

    * Anticanonical degree $(-K_X)^3$, genus $g = \frac{1}{2}(-K_X)^3 + 1$.

    * Betti numbers $b_2, b_3, b_4$ (with $b_1 = 0$, $b_5 = 0$, $b_2 = b_4 = \rho$, $b_3 = 2 h^{1,2}$).

    * Hodge diamond: $h^{1,1} = \rho$, $h^{1,2} = \frac{1}{2} b_3$, and $h^{p,q} = 0$ for $p \neq q$ and $p + q \neq 3$.

    * Topological Euler characteristic $e(X) = 2 + 2\rho - 2 h^{1,2}$.

    * Dimension of automorphism group $\dim \mathrm{Aut}^0(X)$ and reductivity/Lie algebra structure.

    * Intermediate Jacobian $J(X) = H^{1,2}(X)^\vee / H_3(X, \mathbb{Z})$: dimension $g(J) = h^{1,2}$, principally polarized abelian variety structure, Torelli theorem validity.

  * Categorical and moduli data:

    * Bounded derived category $\mathbf{D}^b(X)$: semi-orthogonal decompositions (SOD), exceptional collections, and residual Kuznetsov components $\mathcal{A}_X$ (e.g. cubic 3-fold Kuznetsov component, quartic double solid, Gushel–Mukai 3-folds).

    * Moduli of curves on $X$: Hilbert scheme of lines $\mathrm{F}(X)$ (Fano surface of lines) and conics $\mathcal{C}(X)$, including smoothness, dimension, and Abel–Jacobi map to $J(X)$.

    * Mori fiber space structures: conic bundles, del Pezzo fibrations, and extremal contractions.

  * Preamble integration:

    * Preamble must construct Fano varieties as objects in `Schemes/C` (`categories/schemes/fano/`).

    * Compute numerical invariants $(\rho, r, (-K)^3, g, b_i, h^{p,q})$ directly from defining equations, complete intersections, or blowup data.

    * Wire semi-orthogonal decompositions in $\mathbf{D}^b(X)$ to derived category foundations (`categories/derived/`).

Intended owners: `categories/schemes/fano/` (`FanoThreefold`, `FanoClassification`), `categories/derived/` (`SemiOrthogonalDecomposition`, `KuznetsovComponent`), `categories/schemes/surfaces/del_pezzo.py` (del Pezzo surfaces and fibrations).

## Desired capability: Generalized Grassmannians $G/P$, Schubert calculus, and homogeneous bundles (Grassmannian.info) — intake 2026-09-19

Intake item from https://www.grassmannian.info/ (Pieter Belmans, repository: https://github.com/pbelmans/grassmannian.info).

* **Source of oracle calculations for generalized Grassmannians:**

  * Complete periodic table of generalized Grassmannians $G/P$ where $G$ is a simple complex Lie group of any Dynkin type ($A_n, B_n, C_n, D_n, E_6, E_7, E_8, F_4, G_2$) and $P$ is a maximal parabolic subgroup (corresponding to a marked node on the Dynkin diagram).

  * Types covered:

    * Type $A_n$: Classical Grassmannians $\mathrm{Gr}(k, n+1)$.

    * Type $B_n$: Odd orthogonal Grassmannians $\mathrm{OGr}(k, 2n+1)$.

    * Type $C_n$: Symplectic Grassmannians $\mathrm{SGr}(k, 2n)$ (including Lagrangian Grassmannian $\mathrm{LGr}(n, 2n)$).

    * Type $D_n$: Even orthogonal Grassmannians $\mathrm{OGr}(k, 2n)$ (including orthogonal spinor varieties $\mathrm{OGr}_+(n, 2n)$).

    * Exceptional types: Cayley plane $E_6/P_1 \cong E_6/P_6$ (dimension 16, Severi variety), Freudenthal variety $E_7/P_7$ (dimension 27), adjoint $E_8/P_8$, $F_4$-Grassmannians $F_4/P_1, F_4/P_4$, and $G_2$-Grassmannian $G_2/P_1$ (five-dimensional quadric section) and $G_2/P_2$ (adjoint $G_2$-variety).

  * Invariants and structures provided:

    * Dimension $\dim(G/P) = \dim(G) - \dim(P)$.

    * Fano index $r(G/P)$ and Picard number $\rho = 1$ (for maximal parabolics).

    * Schubert calculus: Weyl group $W = W(G)$, parabolic subgroup $W_P$, minimal length coset representatives $W^P \subset W$.
      Schubert cells $X_w = B w P / P$ indexed by $w \in W^P$ with $\dim X_w = \ell(w)$.

    * Poincaré polynomial $P(t) = \sum_{w \in W^P} t^{2 \ell(w)}$, giving Betti numbers $b_{2k} = \# \{w \in W^P \mid \ell(w) = k\}$ and odd Betti numbers $b_{2k+1} = 0$.

    * Cohomology ring $H^*(G/P, \mathbb{Z})$: Schubert classes $[X_w]$, Littlewood–Richardson coefficients and Schubert intersection products $\sigma_u \cdot \sigma_v = \sum c_{u,v}^w \sigma_w$.

    * Homogeneous vector bundles: tautological subbundle $\mathcal{S}$ and quotient bundle $\mathcal{Q}$ on $\mathrm{Gr}(k, V)$, universal spinor bundles on orthogonal Grassmannians; irreducible $G$-equivariant vector bundles $\mathcal{E}_\lambda = G \times_P V_\lambda$ associated to dominant weights $\lambda$ of $P$.

    * Borel–Weil–Bott theorem: constructive computation of cohomology groups $H^q(G/P, \mathcal{E}_\lambda)$ via the dot action $w \cdot \lambda = w(\lambda + \rho) - \rho$ of the Weyl group $W$.

    * Derived categories $\mathbf{D}^b(G/P)$: full exceptional collections of homogeneous vector bundles (Kapranov collections on $\mathrm{Gr}(k,n)$, Kuznetsov–Polishchuk collections on quadrics and symplectic Grassmannians).

    * Automorphism group $\mathrm{Aut}(G/P) \cong G_{ad}$ and tangent bundle $T(G/P) \cong G \times_P (\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{p})$.

  * Preamble integration:

    * Preamble must construct $G/P$ as owned homogeneous scheme objects in `Schemes/C` (`categories/schemes/grassmannian.py`, `categories/lie/homogeneous_spaces.py`).

    * Operationalize Schubert cell enumeration, Bruhat order posets, Poincaré polynomials, and intersection rings from the Weyl group representation of $G$.

    * Operationalize Borel–Weil–Bott functor: $\mathrm{Rep}(P) \to \mathrm{Coh}(G/P)$ and its pushforward to derived vector spaces $R\Gamma(G/P, -) \in \mathbf{D}^b(\mathrm{Vect})$.

Intended owners: `categories/schemes/grassmannian.py` (`Grassmannian`, `GeneralizedGrassmannian`), `categories/lie/homogeneous_spaces.py` (`HomogeneousSpace` $G/P$, Borel–Weil–Bott), `categories/derived/exceptional_collections.py` (Kapranov and Kuznetsov exceptional collections).

## Desired capability: Scheme and morphism adjectives, automated deduction, and counterexamples (The Adjectives Project) — intake 2026-09-19

Intake item from https://adjectivesproject.org/ (Jesse Vogel and David Holmes, repositories: https://github.com/jessetvogel/adjectives-project and https://github.com/jessetvogel/adjectives-project-data).

* **Extensive mining of scheme properties and morphism properties:**

  * Complete repository of mathematical "adjectives" (predicates), implication theorems, and specimen counterexamples mined from the Stacks Project and EGA.

  * **Scheme adjectives (19 properties):**

    1. `affine`: admitting an isomorphism $X \cong \mathrm{Spec}(R)$ for a commutative ring $R$.

    2. `cohen-macaulay`: local rings $\mathcal{O}_{X,x}$ satisfy $\mathrm{depth}(\mathcal{O}_{X,x}) = \dim(\mathcal{O}_{X,x})$ for all $x \in X$.

    3. `connected`: underlying topological space is connected (no partition into disjoint nonempty opens).

    4. `excellent`: locally Noetherian, universally catenary, and all formal completions have geometrically regular fibers.

    5. `finite-dimensional`: Krull dimension $\dim(X) < \infty$.

    6. `integral`: reduced and irreducible (equivalently, $\mathcal{O}_X(U)$ is an integral domain for all connected open $U$).

    7. `irreducible`: underlying topological space is not the union of two proper closed subsets.

    8. `jacobson`: the set of closed points is dense in every closed subscheme; all local rings have Jacobson property.

    9. `locally-factorial`: every local ring $\mathcal{O}_{X,x}$ is a unique factorization domain (UFD).

    10. `locally-noetherian`: admitting an open affine covering $X = \bigcup \mathrm{Spec}(A_i)$ with each $A_i$ a Noetherian ring.

    11. `noetherian`: locally Noetherian and quasi-compact.

    12. `normal`: every local ring $\mathcal{O}_{X,x}$ is an integrally closed integral domain.

    13. `quasi-affine`: isomorphic to an open subscheme of an affine scheme.

    14. `quasi-compact`: every open covering has a finite subcovering.

    15. `quasi-separated`: the diagonal morphism $\Delta_{X}: X \to X \times_{\mathrm{Spec}\mathbb{Z}} X$ is quasi-compact.

    16. `reduced`: every local ring $\mathcal{O}_{X,x}$ has zero nilradical ($\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{O}_{X,x}) = 0$).

    17. `regular`: every local ring $\mathcal{O}_{X,x}$ is regular ($\dim_{\kappa(x)} \mathfrak{m}_x/\mathfrak{m}_x^2 = \dim \mathcal{O}_{X,x}$).

    18. `semi-separated`: the intersection of any two affine open subschemes is affine.

    19. `separated`: the diagonal morphism $\Delta_{X}: X \to X \times_{\mathrm{Spec}\mathbb{Z}} X$ is a closed immersion.

  * **Morphism adjectives (44 properties):**

    1. `affine`: preimage of every affine open is affine ($f_* \mathcal{O}_X$ is quasi-coherent $\mathcal{O}_Y$-algebra).

    2. `bundle-projective`: isomorphic to $\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}) \to Y$ for a locally free sheaf $\mathcal{E}$ on $Y$.

    3. `closed-immersion`: homeomorphism onto a closed subset with surjective sheaf map $\mathcal{O}_Y \twoheadrightarrow f_* \mathcal{O}_X$.

    4. `closed`: image of every closed subset is closed.

    5. `etale`: flat and unramified (formally étale and locally of finite presentation).

    6. `faithfully-flat`: flat and surjective on points.

    7. `finite-fibers`: every fiber $f^{-1}(y)$ is a finite discrete set.

    8. `finite-locally-free`: finite and $f_* \mathcal{O}_X$ is a locally free $\mathcal{O}_Y$-module of finite rank.

    9. `finite`: affine and $f_* \mathcal{O}_X$ is a finite $\mathcal{O}_Y$-module.

    10. `flat`: local rings $\mathcal{O}_{X,x}$ are flat $\mathcal{O}_{Y,f(x)}$-modules for all $x \in X$.

    11. `formally-etale`: Infinitesimal lifting criterion holds uniquely for square-zero ring extensions.

    12. `formally-smooth`: Infinitesimal lifting criterion holds (existence of lifts) for square-zero extensions.

    13. `formally-unramified`: Infinitesimal lifting criterion holds (at most one lift) for square-zero extensions.

    14. `g-unramified`: unramified in the sense of Grothendieck (diagonal is an open immersion).

    15. `h-projective`: homogeneous projective morphism (factors through a closed immersion into $\mathbb{P}^n_Y$).

    16. `homeomorphism`: $f$ is a topological homeomorphism on underlying spaces.

    17. `immersion`: composition of an open immersion and a closed immersion.

    18. `injective`: injective on underlying sets of points.

    19. `integral`: affine and every element of $f_* \mathcal{O}_X$ is integral over $\mathcal{O}_Y$.

    20. `locally-of-finite-presentation`: locally $X \to Y$ is given by $B = A[x_1,\dots,x_n]/(f_1,\dots,f_m)$.

    21. `locally-of-finite-type`: locally $X \to Y$ is given by $B = A[x_1,\dots,x_n]/I$.

    22. `locally-projective`: admitting open cover of $Y$ over which $f$ is projective.

    23. `monomorphism`: monic in the category of schemes ($\mathrm{Hom}(T, X) \hookrightarrow \mathrm{Hom}(T, Y)$ is injective).

    24. `of-finite-presentation`: locally of finite presentation, quasi-compact, and quasi-separated.

    25. `of-finite-type`: locally of finite type and quasi-compact.

    26. `open-immersion`: isomorphism onto an open subscheme.

    27. `open`: image of every open subset is open.

    28. `projective`: factors as a closed immersion $X \hookrightarrow \mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E})$ followed by projection to $Y$.

    29. `proper`: universally closed, separated, and of finite type.

    30. `quasi-affine`: quasi-compact and factors as an open immersion into an affine $Y$-scheme.

    31. `quasi-compact`: preimage of every quasi-compact open is quasi-compact.

    32. `quasi-finite`: of finite type with finite fibers.

    33. `quasi-separated`: diagonal $\Delta_{X/Y}: X \to X \times_Y X$ is quasi-compact.

    34. `regular`: flat with all fibers geometrically regular.

    35. `semi-separated`: diagonal $\Delta_{X/Y}$ is an affine morphism.

    36. `separated`: diagonal $\Delta_{X/Y}$ is a closed immersion.

    37. `smooth`: flat, locally of finite presentation, and all fibers geometrically smooth.

    38. `surjective`: surjective on underlying sets of points ($f(X) = Y$).

    39. `syntomic`: flat, locally of finite presentation, and all fibers local complete intersections.

    40. `universally-closed`: for every base change $Y' \to Y$, $X \times_Y Y' \to Y'$ is closed.

    41. `universally-homeomorphism`: universally bijective, universally closed, and universally open.

    42. `universally-injective`: for every base change $Y' \to Y$, $X \times_Y Y' \to Y'$ is injective.

    43. `universally-open`: for every base change $Y' \to Y$, $X \times_Y Y' \to Y'$ is open.

    44. `unramified`: formally unramified and locally of finite type.

  * **Theorems / Deduction Lattice (95 theorems):**

    * Scheme deductions (15 theorems):

      * `regular` $\implies$ `locally-factorial` $\implies$ `normal` $\implies$ `reduced`.

      * `integral` $\iff$ `reduced` + `irreducible`.

      * `noetherian` $\iff$ `locally-noetherian` + `quasi-compact`.

      * `locally-noetherian` $\implies$ `cohen-macaulay` if local rings CM.

      * `affine` $\implies$ `separated` $\implies$ `semi-separated` $\implies$ `quasi-separated`.

      * `affine` $\implies$ `quasi-compact`.

      * `excellent` $\implies$ `locally-noetherian`.

      * `jacobson` preserved under finite type extensions over Jacobson bases.

    * Morphism deductions (80 theorems):

      * `closed-immersion` $\implies$ `proper` $\implies$ `separated`.

      * `open-immersion` $\implies$ `etale` $\implies$ `smooth` $\implies$ `syntomic` $\implies$ `flat`.

      * `etale` $\iff$ `smooth` + `unramified` $\iff$ `flat` + `unramified` (under finite presentation).

      * `finite` $\iff$ `proper` + `quasi-finite` (Zariski's Main Theorem).

      * `finite` $\implies$ `affine` $\implies$ `quasi-affine` $\implies$ `separated`.

      * `projective` $\implies$ `proper` $\implies$ `universally-closed` + `separated` + `of-finite-type`.

      * `monomorphism` + `proper` $\implies$ `closed-immersion`.

      * Base change stability: flat, smooth, étale, proper, affine, closed immersion, open immersion are all stable under arbitrary pullback.

      * Composition stability: all 44 adjectives (except relative/fiber conditions) are stable under morphism composition.

      * Cancellation properties (2-out-of-3): if $g \circ f$ has property $P$ and $g$ is separated, $f$ inherits properties.

  * **Specimen Database (107 examples):**

    * 49 schemes (affine line with doubled origin [non-separated], $\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{Z})$, cusp $y^2=x^3$ [non-normal], node $y^2=x^2(x+1)$ [non-normal, reduced], non-reduced fat point $\mathrm{Spec}(k[x]/(x^2))$, infinite disjoint union $\coprod \mathrm{Spec}(k)$ [non-quasi-compact], etc.).

    * 58 morphisms (Frobenius morphism [homeomorphism, not étale in char $p$], normalization map [finite, birational, not isomorphism], blowup of point [projective, birational, not finite], open immersion, diagonal morphism, etc.).

  * **Preamble implementation requirement:**

    * In `categories/schemes/` (`categories/schemes/properties.py`, `categories/schemes/morphism_properties.py`), properties must be first-class predicates: `X.is_affine()`, `X.is_proper()`, `f.is_flat()`, `f.is_smooth()`, `f.is_etale()`, `f.is_closed_immersion()`, etc.

    * An automated property deduction engine must derive known properties from established ones using the 95 implication theorems without recomputing from raw rings.

    * Every counterexample must exist as a constructible test fixture in `tests/schemes/` verifying that negative properties are correctly refuted and positive properties proven.

Intended owners: `categories/schemes/properties.py` (`SchemeProperties`, deduction graph), `categories/schemes/morphism_properties.py` (`MorphismProperties`, base change and composition preservation), `categories/schemes/theorems.py` (95 implication theorems).

## Desired capability: General topology counterexamples, properties, and universal constructions (pi-Base) — intake 2026-09-19

Intake item from https://topology.pi-base.org/ (repositories: https://github.com/pi-base/data and https://github.com/pi-base/web).

* **Comprehensive mining of general topology:**

  * Complete operationalization of 246 topological properties, 224 canonical topological spaces, 931 deductive theorems, and universal topological constructions based on Steen & Seebach *Counterexamples in Topology*.

  * **Topological Properties (246 properties categorized):**

    * **Separation Axioms:**

      * $T_0$ (Kolmogorov): distinct points have distinct closure.

      * $T_1$ (Fréchet): points are closed.

      * $T_2$ (Hausdorff): distinct points have disjoint open neighborhoods.

      * $T_{2.5}$ (Urysohn / completely Hausdorff): distinct points separated by closed neighborhoods.

      * Functionally Hausdorff: distinct points separated by continuous real-valued function.

      * $T_3$ (Regular Hausdorff): $T_1$ and closed set and point separated by open neighborhoods.

      * $T_{3.5}$ (Completely regular / Tychonoff): $T_1$ and closed set and point separated by continuous function to $[0,1]$.

      * $T_4$ (Normal Hausdorff): $T_1$ and disjoint closed sets separated by open neighborhoods.

      * $T_5$ (Completely normal Hausdorff): every subspace is $T_4$; separated subsets separated by opens.

      * $T_6$ (Perfectly normal Hausdorff): normal and every closed set is a $G_\delta$ set.

    * **Compactness Notions:**

      * `compact`: every open cover has a finite subcover.

      * `countably-compact`: every countable open cover has a finite subcover.

      * `sequentially-compact`: every sequence has a convergent subsequence.

      * `pseudocompact`: every continuous real function is bounded.

      * `locally-compact`: every point has a compact neighborhood base.

      * `paracompact`: every open cover has a locally finite open refinement.

      * `metacompact`: every open cover has a point-finite open refinement.

      * `orthocompact`: every open cover has an interior-preserving open refinement.

      * `hemicompact`: admitting an exhaustion by compact subsets.

      * `sigma-compact`: countable union of compact subspaces.

      * `lindelof`: every open cover has a countable subcover.

    * **Connectedness:**

      * `connected`: no partition into two nonempty disjoint open sets.

      * `path-connected`: every pair of points joined by continuous path $[0,1] \to X$.

      * `locally-connected`: admitting a neighborhood base of connected sets.

      * `locally-path-connected`: admitting a neighborhood base of path-connected sets.

      * `hyperconnected`: no two disjoint nonempty open sets (every open set dense).

      * `ultraconnected`: no two disjoint nonempty closed sets.

      * `totally-disconnected`: connected components are singletons.
