**Source:** https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.03792 (arXiv:2501.03792v2, 2026-08-09) + https://ar5iv.org/html/2501.03792v2 — Toshimasa Ishige, Akitoshi Takayasu — *Computer-assisted proofs for finding the monodromy of Picard–Fuchs differential equations for a family of K3 toric hypersurfaces*. Code base for rendered results: Takayasu lab kv library 0.4.57 (`kv::allsol`, `kv::odelong_maffine`) and the companion CAP repo https://github.com/taklab-org/CAP_finding_monodromy (already intaken 2026-09-15 as generic validated LocSys transport).
This intake operationalizes the *K3-specific on-the-nose instantiation* — exact $M_{\Sigma_i}\in\mathrm{GL}_4(\mathbb{Z})$ for the paper's family, as the model for any K3 (and CY) Picard–Fuchs population.

**Family and Picard–Fuchs ODE.** The paper studies the 2-parameter K3 toric hypersurface family whose periods satisfy the rank-4 Picard–Fuchs Pfaffian system $d\Phi = \Omega\Phi$, $\Omega = A(x,y)dx + B(x,y)dy$ with rational $A,B\in\mathrm{Mat}_4(\mathbb{C}(x,y))$ having simple poles along the singular divisor $\mathcal{S}\subset\mathbb{C}^2$ (Eqs.
1.8, 2.4). The Gauss–Manin connection $\nabla_{GM}$ on $H^2_{dR}(X/S)$ restricts to this $\Omega$ after trivialization; the PF operator is the cyclic-vector image in $D_S$.
Generic line $\ell: 2(x-x_0)+(y-y_0)=0$ (2.5) is chosen through base point $(x_0,y_0)$ to reduce to $B = \ell\setminus\mathcal{S}$ with $\ell\cap\mathcal{S}=\{p_1,\dots,p_6\}$.

**Base point, singular locus, and loops.** Base point $(\lambda_0,\mu_0)$ with $\lambda_0=2^{-10}$, $\mu_0$ small, maps via $(x,y)=(\mu(\lambda-1/4)^{-2},\mu(\lambda-1/4)^{-3})$ to $(x_0,y_0)\approx(0.0157478,-0.0632382)$ (2.3). Intersection $\mathcal{S}\cap\ell$ is computed rigorously as 4 real roots $p_1<\dots<p_4$ and a complex-conjugate pair $p_5,\bar p_5=p_6$, enclosed as disjoint interval boxes via `kv::allsol` interval Newton on the system $\{xy(4x+y)[(36x+27y/2+1)^2-(1-12x)^3]=0,\;2(x-x_0)+(y-y_0)=0\}$ (Sec.
3.1). Loops $\Sigma_i$ encircling each $p_i$ from $p_0=(x_0,y_0)$ are prescribed explicitly as 3-segment piecewise smooth contours $x=c_x+r e^{\pm i\pi t},\; y=c_y-2r e^{\pm i\pi t}$ with centres/radii chosen to avoid other singularities (Sec.
2.3, 3.4). These $\Sigma_i$ generate $\pi_1(B,p_0)$.

**4-step CAP pipeline (paper Secs.
1.1, 2–3, Thm.
1.3, App.
A).**

1. **Fundamental matrix at $b_0$.** Particular solutions $\varphi^k(\lambda,\mu)=\sum_{l,m\ge0} a_{lm}\lambda^l\mu^m\cdot(\log\lambda)^{(\cdot)}(\log\mu)^{(\cdot)}$ $(k=1..4)$ of Theorem 2.3 (Prop.
   3.7) with $a_{lm}=(2l+4m)!/(l+m)!l!(m!)^3$ are evaluated at $(\lambda_0,\mu_0)$: truncated sums ($N_\mathrm{trunc}=41$) plus rigorous truncation bounds via majorants $\beta_N,\gamma_N,\delta_i(N)$ (Thms.
   3.4–3.7, App.
   A\) give interval enclosures of $\varphi^k,\varphi^k_x,\varphi^k_y,\varphi^k_{xy}$ and hence of $\Phi(b_0)\in\mathrm{GL}_4(\mathbb{C})$ (2.56). Operation: `fundamental_matrix(nabla,b_0,N)` $\to$ interval $\mathrm{Mat}_4$ with certified $|\mathrm{error}|<\delta$.

2. **Loop selection.** Explicit $\Sigma_i$ ($i=1..6$) as above; no topology inference — loops are data that generate $\pi_1$ for this arrangement.

3. **Rigorous analytic continuation.** Along each $\Sigma_i$ solve the variational ODE $d\Phi/dt = (\dot x(t)A(x(t),y(t))+\dot y(t)B(x(t),y(t)))\Phi(t)$, $\Phi(0)=\Phi(b_0)$, with $kv$ rigorous integrator `odelong_maffine`: Taylor order 15, interval / double-double / `interval<dd>` arithmetic, affine-arithmetic remainder to control wrapping effect, validated step-size control (Sec.
   3, Rem.
   1.2). Returns interval matrix $\Phi(1)\ni$ true analytic continuation $\tilde\Phi(b_0)$ after traversing $\Sigma_i$.
   Hard codes are $p=15$, `dd` precision; method is generic for any integrable $\Omega$ and any piecewise-smooth $\gamma$.

4. **Monodromy extraction and on-the-nose integer recovery.** $M_{\Sigma_i}=\Phi(b_0)^{-1}\Phi(1)\in\mathrm{GL}_4(\mathbb{C})$ as interval matrix (2.57). Unimodularity ($\det M_{\Sigma_i}=1$, from K3 lattice preservation) plus enclosure width $<0.5$ entrywise certifies the unique integer matrix inside — the on-the-nose $M_{\Sigma_i}\in\mathrm{GL}_4(\mathbb{Z})$ of Theorem 1.3 (Sec.
   1.3). Since $M_{\Sigma_i}$ generate the monodromy group, this solves the monodromy problem up to conjugacy (fixed $\Phi(b_0)$ presentation).
   Remark 1.4 notes the matrices agree with the $K3$-lattice-theoretic values of [5] — the CAP route is independent of $K3$ lattice theory and therefore generalizes.

**What is K3-specific vs. what is general (verbatim hard-vs-soft split, cf.
intake 2026-09-15).**

- *K3-specific data:* the polynomial defining $\mathcal{S}$, the line $\ell$, the matrices $A,B$ (via $a..e,B_0..C_3,h=1+20x+9y$), the coefficient $a_{lm}$, the numbers $N=4,r=41,\lambda_0=2^{-10}$, the centres/radii of $\Sigma_i$, and the closed-form $\beta_N,\gamma_N,\delta_i$ proofs for this $a_{lm}$.

- *General method:* Input $(B=\mathbb{C}^k\setminus\mathcal{S},\; n,\; \Omega\in\mathrm{Mat}_n(\Omega^1(\log\mathcal{S}))$ integrable regular-singular, $b_0$, presentation of $\Phi(b_0)$ as series) $\to$ functor $\mathrm{LocSys}(\nabla):\pi_1(B,b_0)\to\mathrm{GL}_n$ $\to$ interval $M_\gamma$ with $M_\gamma\ni\rho([\gamma])$.
  Transport step (4) is fully general; step (1) is general up to replacing $a_{lm}$ growth analysis.

**Preamble operationalization — on-the-nose population.**

Operationalize as the validated-transport specialization of the Gauss–Manin / local-system pipeline already named in this file:

- **Families:** $f:X\to S$ with $f.\mathrm{as\_family}(S,\mathrm{flat}/\mathrm{smooth}/\mathrm{proper})$ (intake 2026-09-15), e.g. Dwork pencil / toric K3 / $M$-polarized $K3$ / mirror CY. Require $S$ smooth, $U=S\setminus\Delta$ with $\Delta$ the discriminant (= image of $\mathcal{S}$), $B=U^{an}$.

- **Cohomology and connection:** $H^2_{dR}(X/S)=R^2f_*\Omega^\bullet_{X/S}:\mathrm{QCoh}(S)$ with $\nabla_{GM}:H\to H\otimes\Omega^1_S$ in $\mathrm{FlatConn}^{rs}(S,\Delta)$; Pfaffian $\Omega$ is its matrix in a trivialization $H|_U\simeq\mathcal{O}_U^n$.
  PF ideal $\mathrm{Ann}_{D_S}(w)$ for $w(z)=\int_{\gamma_z}\Omega_z$ is the $D_S$-picture; monodromy lives on $\mathrm{Sol}(\nabla_{GM})\simeq R^2f^{an}_*\mathbb{C}\in\mathrm{LocSys}(U^{an})$ via Riemann–Hilbert.

- **Monodromy object:** $\rho=\mathrm{LocSys}(\nabla_{GM}):\pi_1(U^{an},b_0)\to\mathrm{GL}_n(\mathbb{Z})$ (or $\mathrm{O}(L)$ for polarized $K3$ lattice $L$), image $\mathrm{Mon}(\nabla_{GM})\le\mathrm{GL}_n(\mathbb{Z})$.
  Interface on $\nabla$ / $\mathrm{LocSys}$: `nabla.monodromy_representation(b0)` $\to \operatorname{Rep}(\pi_1)$, `nabla.monodromy_matrices(generators)` $\to$ family $\{M_{\Sigma_i}:\mathrm{GL}_n(\mathbb{Z})\}$, `nabla.monodromy_group()` $= \langle M_{\Sigma_i}\rangle$.
  Honest functors, not free functions `monodromy(...)`.

- **Validated transport adapter (private):** `ValidatedMonodromy(nabla,b_0,gamma,Phi(b_0))` using maintained interval libraries (kv, Arb, IntervalArithmetic.jl, CAPD) — $b_0$-enclosure via series majorant, rigorous ODE `odelong_maffine`-style Taylor+affine transport, interval $M_\gamma$.
  Certification to integer when $\rho$ is integral ($\mathrm{O}(L)$, $\mathrm{Sp}$, $\mathrm{SL}$) and $\text{width}<0.5$; otherwise returns tight interval.
  This adapts the Ishige–Takayasu code pattern without importing it: evaluator $\Omega(x)$ is the parameter, library choice is the backend, preamble owns the categories.

- **Generality beyond this PF equation:** any family where the PF/Pfaffian is known (Doran–Malmendier twists, Fano/K3 mirrors of arXiv:2403.07349, Batyrev–Borisov / Dolgachev–Nikulin polarizations, Lairez Griffiths–Dwork `Periods` adapter) and where $b_0$ admits explicit series/regular-singular expansion.
  Population grows with the PF operator population — no new monodromy formalism per family.

**Intended owners (refines the generic CAP row).**

- `categories/schemes/monodromy.py` — `MonodromyRepresentation`, `MonodromyGroup` on $\mathrm{FlatConn}^{rs}$ / $D^{rh}_S$, `LocalSystem.monodromy()`.

- `categories/schemes/periods.py` + `categories/cohomology/gauss_manifold.py` — $H^k_{dR}(X/S)$, $\nabla_{GM}$, $PF(w)=\mathrm{Ann}_{D_S}(w)$, link to `ValidatedMonodromy`.

- `categories/differential_equations/validated_monodromy.py` — private adapter `ValidatedTransport` (kv/Arb/IntervalArithmetic.jl/CAPD), `FundamentalMatrixEnclosure`, `RigorousAnalyticContinuation`; depends on `categories/topology/fundamental_group.py` ($\pi_1$) and `categories/differential_equations/picard_fuchs.py` / `categories/schemes/k3/` for the K3 specimens.

- Not a free `compute_monodromy(A,B,loops)` — method on the connection/local-system: `nabla.validated_monodromy(b0, loops)`.

**References kept with intake.**

- Paper PDF: `references/pdfs/2501.03792-Ishige-Takayasu-K3-PF-monodromy.pdf` (fetch via arXiv 2501.03792).

- Code pattern: `taklab-org/CAP_finding_monodromy` (kv 0.4.57, C++17, OpenMP, Ubuntu 22.04, EPYC 9754) — already intaken as generic; K3 PF data are `src/parameters.hpp`, `src/variable_coefficients.hpp`, `src/validated_integration.cpp`.

- Related preamble capabilities: families (`f.as_family`), periods / PF via Griffiths–Dwork (`Periods`), Gauss–Manin, six functors / $R f_*$, $\pi_1$/CW / graded, validated LocSys transport.

**Status and next specimen.** Proposed.
First on-the-nose specimen is the paper's Theorem 1.3 matrices $M_{\Sigma_i}$ ($i=1..6$) — certify they equal the lattice-theoretic matrices of [5] and generate $\mathrm{Mon}\le\mathrm{O}(L)$ for the K3 lattice $L$ of this family.
Second specimen is transport of the same connection along a different generating set of $\pi_1$ (e.g. a braid of $\Sigma_i$) and verification of the expected braid relations in $\mathrm{Mon}$.
Subsequent specimens are any other K3 PF Pfaffian (e.g. Doran–Malmendier $L_{3,N}$) run through the same `ValidatedMonodromy` without new code.

## Tamagawa Numbers

Availability of $\tau(G)$ for smooth affine group schemes, computability in some cases.
The Tamagawa measure.
Smith-Minkowski-Siegel mass formula.
$O_q(RR)$ as a compact real Lie group for $q$ definite.
Groups $GU$, $GSp$, etc. Semantic double cosets.
Orientations of R-modules (generator of $\det(R)$). Whitehead product and graded lie algebra structure on $\pi_*(X,x)$.
Selmer group.
Tate dual.
Cyclotomic characters.
Factorization homology.
Recursive formulas for Weil-Petersson volumes from https://www.math.stonybrook.edu/~mlyubich/Archive/Geometry/Teichmuller%20Space/Mirz3.pdf, moduli spaces $M_{g,n}(\vec{L})$.
$L_\infty$ algebras, formal moduli problems, $MC(\mathfrak{g})$.
Checking prestability, semistability, stability of marked nodal curves.
Dual graphs of pointed prestable nodal curves.
Dyer-Lashof algebra.
Poisson algebras.
